# Megawide EPC Procurement â€” WPM Dashboard

## Project Overview
Work Package Management (WPM) Dashboard for Megawide Construction Corporation EPC projects. Tracks procurement work packages, award status, budgets, and contractors across multiple projects.

**Live URL:** https://pmodepartment.github.io/prc-app (login: `/login.html`)
**Staging URL:** https://fmlozano-pmo.github.io/prc-app-dev (login: `/login.html`)
**Stack:** Vanilla HTML/CSS/JS (no build step) + Supabase (PostgreSQL + Auth) + GitHub Pages hosting
**GitHub (prod):** https://github.com/PMODepartment/prc-app
**GitHub (staging):** https://github.com/fmlozano-pmo/prc-app-dev â€” branch `staging`, push via `git push dev staging:main`
**Supabase (prod):** `https://cayjeqeleenizbdzrums.supabase.co`
**Supabase (staging):** `https://duivwgmjcbxtfagkiqyj.supabase.co`

---

## Architecture

No build step â€” edit files directly, push to GitHub, GitHub Pages auto-deploys (~1â€“2 min).

### Key Files
| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `assets/js/auth.js` | Supabase auth wrapper â€” `AppAuth.requireLogin()`, `AppAuth.requireAdmin()`, `getSB()`, profile cache |
| `assets/js/db.js` | All DB operations via `WPDb.*` â€” also `computeStats()`, `Fmt.*`, `renderUserBar()` |
| `assets/js/ui.js` | Shared UI helpers â€” sidebar init, modals, toast, hamburger menu, iOS pinch-zoom prevention |
| `assets/css/dashboard.css` | Global styles, CSS variables, responsive breakpoints, view-tabs, mobile fixes |
| `supabase-schema.sql` | Full DB schema for reference |
| `assets/js/onboarding.js` | Role-aware guided-tour modal (`window.Onboarding`) â€” auto-opens on first login, re-openable via topbar **?** button. Self-contained (injects own CSS, follows dark mode via CSS vars). |
| `onboarding.html` | Standalone 13-slide onboarding deck for training (keyboard nav, F=fullscreen, P=print). Not wired into the app. |
| `WPM_Dashboard_Onboarding.pptx` | Editable PowerPoint version of the onboarding deck (same content). |

> `assets/js/` + `assets/css/` files are canonical. The stale **root-level duplicate** `auth.js`/`db.js`/`ui.js`/`charts.js`/`dashboard.css` copies (and `firebase-config.js`, `auth-guard.js`, etc.) were **deleted** 2026-07 (they were unreferenced dead code — see Known Issues #9). Don't recreate them.

### Pages
| File | Auth | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `login.html` | public | Sign-in + Step 2 project picker. Shows Portfolio Overview card (admins), project list, Add New Project (admin/super_admin). On create â†’ `project.html?id=<newId>`. |
| `register.html` | public | Self-registration (creates `pending` user) |
| `pending.html` | public | Shown to unapproved users |
| `forgot-password.html` | public | Password reset request â€” `redirectTo` points to `/prc-app/reset-password.html` (was `login.html`, which had no recovery handling) |
| `reset-password.html` | public | **Set-new-password page** â€” the target of the reset email link. Self-contained (own Supabase client + inline dark mode, like `forgot-password.html`). On load, `detectSessionInUrl` (default) parses the recovery token from the URL and fires `onAuthStateChange('PASSWORD_RECOVERY')`; the page then reveals New/Confirm password fields and calls `sb.auth.updateUser({password})`, signs out, and redirects to `login.html`. Shows an "invalid/expired link" error (with a link back to Forgot Password) when no recovery session settles (~1.8s) or the URL carries `error_description`. **REQUIRES** the redirect URL to be allow-listed in Supabase (see below). |
| `index.html` | user | Portfolio Overview â€” consolidated dashboard, 6 tabs (Dashboard tab merged into Overview) |
| `project.html` | user | Single project dashboard â€” 3 tabs |
| `wp-form.html` | user | Add / edit work package |
| `review.html` | user+ | **All Work Packages** — bulk view/edit surface (Grid + Cards). No approval tabs: every writing role auto-approves. Admin-only legacy-cleanup strip. |
| `admin.html` | admin + manager | User management + project management (admin/super_admin); Performance tab (all three roles) |
| `claim-form.html` | user | Add / edit claim or CO â€” **hidden feature, not yet active** |
| `my-wps.html` | user | Officer's WP list |
| `project-selector.html` | user | Standalone picker â€” **unused, not linked** |

## Database (Supabase)

**URL:** `https://cayjeqeleenizbdzrums.supabase.co`

### Tables
- **`projects`** â€” `id` (text PK e.g. 'AVR101'), name, location, status, budget_bcb, start_date, end_date, **`group_head`** (Group Head tag â€” see below)
- **`users`** â€” `id` (UUID FK â†’ auth.users), name, email, role (`super_admin|admin|user|viewer`), status (`pending|approved|rejected`), projects (text[]), last_login
- **`work_packages`** â€” all WP fields. **Generated columns (never INSERT into):** `total_awarded` (= `awarded_cost + additionals`), `awarding_lead_time` (= `actual_awarding_date - awarding_date`), `variance` (= `approved_budget_bcb - total_awarded`). Use `awarded_cost` and `lead_time` instead. `unmap()` in `db.js` strips all three automatically.
- **`claims`** â€” `id`, `project_id`, `claim_no`, `claim_type`, `party` (`Client|Vendor`), `description`, `wp_no`, `contractor`, `date_filed`, `amount_claimed`, `basis`, `status`, `approved_amount`, `date_resolved`, `review_status`, `review_notes`, `remarks`, `submitted_by`

### WPDb API (db.js)
```js
WPDb.getProjects()                         // all projects
WPDb.getProject(id)
WPDb.createProject(data)
WPDb.updateProject(id, data)
WPDb.archiveProject(id)                    // sets status='archived'
WPDb.unarchiveProject(id)
WPDb.deleteProject(id)                     // also deletes all WPs

WPDb.getApprovedWPs(pid)                   // approved WPs for one project
WPDb.getAllApprovedWPs()                   // all approved WPs (single query)
WPDb.getApprovedWPsForProjects(ids)        // approved WPs for array of IDs â€” avoids N+1
WPDb.getAllWPs(pid)                        // all WPs regardless of status
WPDb.getPendingWPs()                       // pending_review WPs (admin)
WPDb.submitWP(data, user)                  // inserts with review_status='pending_review'; throws on error
WPDb.updateWP(id, data)                    // update (resets to pending_review); throws on error
WPDb.updateWPDirect(id, data)             // update without status change; throws on error
WPDb.approveWP(id)
WPDb.rejectWP(id, _, reason)
WPDb.deleteWP(id)                          // permanently deletes one WP; throws on error
WPDb.getAllUsers()
WPDb.updateUser(id, updates)
WPDb.deleteUser(id)                        // COMPLETE remove: purges public.users profile + auth.users login via admin_delete_user() RPC (admin "Remove")
WPDb.updateLastLogin(userId)
```

### Auth Flow
1. `getSB()` â€” returns `window.__sb` (Supabase client, UMD bundle, created synchronously on page load)
2. `AppAuth.requireLogin(cb)` â€” checks session â†’ loads profile from `sessionStorage` cache (`wpm_prof_{userId}`) or fetches from DB â†’ checks `status === 'approved'` â†’ calls cb(user, profile)
3. `AppAuth.requireAdmin(cb)` â€” wraps `requireLogin`, requires role in `['admin', 'super_admin']`
4. `AppAuth.logout()` â€” clears `wpm_prof_*` sessionStorage keys, signs out, redirects to login
5. Role in `window.__wpmRole`, profile in `window.__profile`

### Supabase Settings
- **Email confirmation disabled** â€” users go straight to `pending` for admin approval
- **Plan: PRO** — org `PMODepartment`, billing account `pmodepartment2@megawide.com.ph`, moved off Free 2026-08-10 for Storage. **Pro projects do NOT pause for inactivity**, so the free-tier "pauses after 7 days → 5–30s cold start" behaviour no longer applies. `.github/workflows/keep-alive.yml` and the UptimeRobot ping are therefore redundant — left in place only as insurance against a future downgrade.
- **Outbound email is the ONE thing this stack cannot do at volume — on ANY plan.** Supabase’s **built-in** sender is rate-limited and documented as development-only on **every** tier, Pro included; Pro raises the configurable auth rate limit but does not make the shared sender production-grade. This has never bitten because the app sends email in exactly ONE place — `resetPasswordForEmail` in `forgot-password.html`. Both signup paths (`register.html`, `vendor-register.html`) call `signUp` with email confirmation disabled and send **nothing**. **Any feature that emails vendors at scale needs custom SMTP** — see the Resend note below and “Vendor self-service at scale” under Vendor Management.
- **Password-reset flow** (Auth â†’ URL Configuration): the **Redirect URLs allow-list must contain `https://pmodepartment.github.io/prc-app/**`** (wildcard). It was previously **empty**, so Supabase ignored `resetPasswordForEmail`'s `redirectTo` and fell back to the **Site URL** (`/prc-app/` = the portfolio, which needs login and has no reset form) â€” the reset link "went nowhere useful." With the wildcard added, the email link lands on **`reset-password.html`**, which handles the recovery token. If you add other post-auth redirect targets, they must match this allow-list (or add them).
- **Custom SMTP (Resend)**: configured then **reverted** (toggled OFF 2026-07 at user request) â€” back on the built-in email service. The Resend config (domain `megawide.com.ph` pending verification, `supabase-smtp` API key, sender/host/username) remains **saved** in Supabase; re-enabling is just the "Enable custom SMTP" toggle. DNS records to verify the domain live in the handoff Artifact. This is independent of the reset-link fix above.
- **Sign in with Microsoft (2026-08, `login.html`)** â€” a "Sign in with Microsoft" button (`#btnMicrosoft`) calls `sb.auth.signInWithOAuth({provider:'azure', ...})`, redirecting the whole page to Microsoft and back. **Requires manual setup, not yet done (as of this writing):** (1) an **Entra ID (Azure AD) app registration** in the Megawide tenant â€” Single tenant account type (this is what actually restricts sign-in to `@megawide.com.ph`, not app code), redirect URI `https://<supabase-project-ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback`, a client secret, and **admin consent granted** for the `User.Read` delegated permission; (2) in Supabase **Authentication â†’ Providers â†’ Azure**, enable it and paste the Application (client) ID, client secret, and Tenant ID. Until both are done the button will error (provider not enabled). **First-time sign-in bootstrap:** a brand-new Microsoft identity has an `auth.users` row but no `public.users` profile yet â€” `ensureProfile(user)` in `login.html` inserts one (`name`/`email` from the Microsoft identity, `role:'user'`, `status:'pending'`) exactly like `register.html`'s self-registration insert, so they land on `pending.html` and go through the **same admin-approval queue as everyone else** (no auto-approve, by design/user request) â€” an admin still has to approve and assign role/projects/manager. The same `ensureProfile()` runs in the auto-restore-session check at page load, so it also covers a returning Microsoft user whose profile row hasn't been created yet. Relies on Supabase's automatic identity-linking for a verified email matching an existing `auth.users` row (so an existing email/password account with the same `@megawide.com.ph` address doesn't get duplicated) â€” verify this is enabled in the project's Auth settings when configuring the provider.

## DB Migrations â€” Run in Supabase SQL Editor (all IF NOT EXISTS, safe to re-run)

**⚠️ ALL migration/seed/one-off SQL now lives in `migrations/`, named `YYYY-MM-DD_<name>.sql`
(2026-09-01 reorg).** The date is the day the file was FIRST COMMITTED, recovered from
`git log --diff-filter=A` — not the filesystem mtime, which OneDrive sync and checkouts make
meaningless. The redundant `MIGRATION_` prefix was dropped (the folder says it); `seed_` /
`update_` / `add_` were kept lowercase because they still say something the folder does not.
`supabase-schema.sql` stays in the repo root — it is the reference schema, not a migration.

- **⚠️ FILENAME ORDER IS NOT RUN ORDER — `migrations/README.md` is authoritative.** Sorting the
  folder is wrong in at least one place: eight files share `2026-08-10` and alphabetically
  `vendor_invite_rls_fix` sorts BEFORE `vendor_management`, yet it depends on the tables that
  file creates. A date prefix cannot encode within-day sequence. The README carries the ordered
  list, the `_rates_wp_link` → `_fix` and `_field_ownership` → `_self_view` dependencies, and the
  rule that `vendor_edit_guard_consolidated` runs LAST and its five predecessors must never be
  re-run after it.
- **⚠️ `.gitignore` gained a GLOB GUARD (`SEED_vendor_*.sql`, `*seed_vendor_*.sql`) — do not
  remove it.** The real-data seeds were ignored by BARE FILENAME, which still matches at any
  depth, so `migrations/SEED_vendor_masterlist.sql` was safe — but a seed regenerated under this
  folder's new convention (`migrations/2026-08-20_seed_vendor_masterlist.sql`) was **NOT**
  matched and could have been committed, republishing ~2,400 real vendor names, TINs, contacts
  and addresses on the public Pages site (Known Issues #20). Verified both spellings are now
  ignored at any depth while the harmless `seed_demo_project` stays tracked.
- References were rewritten repo-wide to the new paths — 177 full + 4 extension-less, across
  `CLAUDE.md`, `db.js`, `import-monitoring.html`, `project.html`, `vendors.html`, `wp-form.html`
  and the migrations' own cross-references. Verified: 0 stale names and 0 dangling paths. The
  stale checkouts under `.claude/worktrees/` were deliberately left alone (git-excluded).

```sql
-- WP form fields
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS works text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS type_of_works text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS scope text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS actual_delivery date DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS type_of_service text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS charging_type text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS contract_package_no text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS co_description text DEFAULT NULL;
-- Bond / payment / retention
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS surety_bond text DEFAULT 'No';
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS performance_bond text DEFAULT 'No';
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS warranty_bond text DEFAULT 'No';
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS payment_terms_days integer;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS dp_percent numeric(5,4);
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS dp_terms text;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS dp_release_date date;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS dp_amount numeric(18,2);
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS retention_percent numeric(5,4);
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS retention_amount numeric(18,2);
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS approver_name text;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS approval_date date;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS submittal_document_type text;
-- Columns missing from original live DB
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS approver text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS support_team text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS delivery_status text DEFAULT 'Not Awarded';
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS submittal_type text DEFAULT 'Not Required';
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS dp_notes text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS retention_period text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS awarding_status text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS purchase_request text DEFAULT NULL;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS osm text DEFAULT 'No';   -- OSM flag (Yes/No). Column + WP-form field + importer RETAINED; the OSM FILTER was removed from both dashboard WP Lists (2026-07-23), and the OSM TAG was removed GLOBALLY from the UI (2026-07-24): the WP-form field (`f-osm`), the review-grid column, the importer's OSM rule bullet + KPI tile + preview column, and project.html's `_osmMatch` dead code. The DB column is UNTOUCHED — `wp-form` still saves `osm:'No'` and the importer still writes `osm` from Remarks, so existing rows and the column keep working; only the visible tag is gone.
-- Per-WP audit trail ("last change" log) — see migrations/2026-07-23_wp_audit_trail.sql (also recreates wp_view_public)
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS updated_at timestamptz;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS updated_by uuid;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS updated_by_name text;
-- Free-of-charge awards — see migrations/2026-08-25_wp_free_of_charge.sql and Known Issues #28c.
-- A REAL award at ₱0 (vendor provides the scope free), as opposed to "Awarded, cost not
-- encoded yet". NEVER back-fill this by inferring `awarded_cost = 0` — a literal 0 in this
-- data is usually an importer default on a row whose cost was never entered.
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS free_of_charge boolean DEFAULT false;
-- Buyback WPs — see migrations/2026-08-11_wp_buyback.sql and Known Issues #28b.
-- dep % is stored as a DECIMAL fraction (0.30 = 30%), like dp_percent.
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS buyback boolean DEFAULT false;
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS buyback_depreciation_percent numeric(5,4);
-- EITHER a depreciation % OR an exact recovered amount is entered (never both).
ALTER TABLE work_packages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS buyback_amount numeric(18,2);
-- Users: activity tracking. WITHOUT this, getAllUsers() returns last_login=undefined for
-- every row and admin.html shows EVERY approved user as "Active" (the activity badge
-- degrades gracefully when the column is missing). auth.js/login.html already write it.
ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS last_login timestamptz;
-- Viewer role
ALTER TABLE users DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS users_role_check;
ALTER TABLE users ADD CONSTRAINT users_role_check CHECK (role IN ('super_admin','admin','user','viewer'));
```

> The live DB was created from an early schema version. Any "column not found" error means a column needs to be added above â€” all statements are idempotent.

## BCB Baselines (BCB0 … BCB2)

A project's procurement budget is **re-baselined** over time, so the same work package can show a **saving against BCB0 and a loss against BCB1** (the re-baseline ate the savings). Run **`migrations/2026-07-23_bcb_baselines.sql`** once — it adds `budget_bcb0..budget_bcb2 numeric(18,2)` and backfills `budget_bcb0` from `approved_budget_bcb`.

- **`approved_budget_bcb` still holds the CURRENT baseline** (newest non-blank), so all ~110 existing read sites — charts, KPIs, tables, PDF, CSV, importer — keep working untouched. **Do not repoint them at a baseline column.**
- **The switcher re-maps the data, not the readers** (`applyBcbBaseline(wps, level)` in `db.js` returns a copy with `approved_budget_bcb` set to that baseline). `project.html` keeps the loaded rows in **`_rawWPs`** and `index.html` in **`_rawAll`**; `allWPs` / `_allWPs` are the mapped views. When you add a load path, map it the same way or the switcher will silently stop applying.
- **Carry-forward:** `bcbEffective(w, level)` returns that baseline's figure, else the newest EARLIER one — a WP not re-baselined at BCB2 keeps its BCB1 value. `bcbValue()` is the raw per-baseline figure (BCB0 falls back to the legacy single column so pre-migration rows still read).
- **A project may have NO BCB0.** QHL706's sheet heads its budget `BUDGET 1 - BCB1 (VAT EX)`, so all 170 WPs import into `budget_bcb1` and `budget_bcb0` is empty. `bcbLevelsPresent()` therefore reports **only populated levels** (it no longer hardcodes `bcb0:true`) and **`BcbBaseline.resolve(wps)`** renders the user's stored pick only if that baseline has data here, else the lowest level that does. Without this, QHL706 would show a **BCB0 tab displaying BCB1's own figures** (the `approved_budget_bcb` fallback). Rows imported before baselines existed have no level populated at all — BCB0 is then offered and stands for the single stored budget. **Both dashboards call `resolve()`, not `get()`.**
- **Default is BCB0**
- **UI:** per-project switcher `#ov-bcb-switch` on the Overview (beside Charging Scope, with a note counting re-baselined WPs); portfolio switcher `#idx-bcb-switch` in the filter bar. Both re-render everything — KPIs, batteries, period charts, backlog, WP List.
- **WP form:** Budget & Contract now has **Budget BCB0 (Net) \*** (the original, required) plus optional **Revised BCB1/BCB2**; blank means "unchanged at that baseline". Each field shows a live delta vs the previous baseline and, once an Awarded Cost is entered, whether that baseline turns the award into a **saving** or **OVER**. Save writes all four columns and sets `approved_budget_bcb` to the newest non-blank.
- **Review grid:** the Budget & Contract section shows **Budget BCB0 (required) / BCB1 / BCB2** — the SAME three fields as the WP form. There is deliberately **no "current" column**: `approved_budget_bcb` is DERIVED (the newest non-blank baseline), so showing it beside BCB0 duplicated the same figure and let the two drift. Editing ANY baseline recomputes and stages `approved_budget_bcb` (bcb2 ?? bcb1 ?? bcb0) in `_xlSet`, because every dashboard/chart/export reads that field — without it the KPIs wouldn't move. `xlVal` still falls back to `approved_budget_bcb` when reading BCB0 on rows imported before baselines existed.

## Group Heads (project tag, 2026-08-06)

Every project can be tagged with the **Group Head** that owns it. Run **`migrations/2026-08-06_project_group_head.sql`** once — it adds `projects.group_head text` (nullable; NULL/blank = **Unassigned**).

- **Roster lives in ONE place: `window.GROUP_HEADS` in `db.js`** — `['Tan','Rodrin','Ronquillo','Calimag','Fellowes','Flores']`. The column is deliberately plain `text` (not an enum/FK) so the roster can change with no DB migration. Companion helpers, also in `db.js`: **`window.ghLabel(v)`** (blank → the `window.GH_UNASSIGNED` sentinel `'Unassigned'`) and **`window.ghOptions(cur)`** (builds the `<option>` list, pre-selects `cur` case-insensitively, and **prepends an off-roster/legacy value** so editing a project tagged with a name later dropped from the roster can't silently clear it — same intent as `fillSelectLegacy()` in wp-form.html). **Use `ghLabel()` rather than re-inlining `|| 'Unassigned'`**, or the filter/group keys stop matching.
- **Where it's set:** the **Edit Project** modal in `project.html` (`#ep-group-head`, options rebuilt on every open so the legacy-value rescue applies), and both **New Project** modals — the shared global one in `db.js` (`#gnp-group-head`) and `admin.html`'s own (`#np-group-head`, options filled in the modal-open path). All three write `group_head: <value> || null`.
- **Where it's shown:** `project.html`'s topbar subtitle (`page-sub`, as `Location · Group Head: X · Description`, blank parts omitted), the Portfolio project **cards** (`renderProjectCard`'s `.pc-meta` line, after the location) and the Portfolio project **table** (new **Group Head** column).
- **Deploy-order-safe:** `db.js`'s `createProject`/`updateProject` detect the `group_head` "column does not exist"/schema-cache error and **retry without the field** (`_isMissingGroupHeadCol`/`_stripGroupHead`), the same guard pattern already used for the audit-trail and BCB-baseline columns — so the code is safe to ship before the migration runs (the tag just won't persist until it does).

### Portfolio Overview — filter / sort / group by Group Head (`index.html`)

**The Group Head FILTER lives in the project-selection dropdown, not the cards/table toolbar (2026-08-06, per user request: "so the charts can be filtered by group heads").** A first cut put a standalone `#proj-gh-filter` select in the cards/table toolbar — but that only narrowed the cards/table display, leaving every chart, KPI, and battery on the page still scoped to whatever projects were checked in the project-selection dropdown (`#proj-filter-wrap` / `_activeIds`). Group Head needed to work like project selection itself, not a display-only filter layered on top of it. Fixed by moving the picker into `#proj-filter-panel` (the same popover as the project checklist) as **`#pf-gh`**, a plain `<select>` above the search box.

- **`window.setFilterGroupHead(gh)`** — picking a head **replaces `_activeIds`** with exactly that head's non-archived projects (`'all'` restores the normal default: every non-archived permitted project). Because `_activeIds` is what every chart, KPI, battery, and table on the whole page reads, this one call re-scopes the entire dashboard to that Group Head — not just the project cards/table block. `updateFilterLabel()` + `renderFilterList()` + `renderAll()` run same as a manual pill toggle.
- **`_syncPfGhOptions()`** rebuilds `#pf-gh`'s options from the heads actually present among `permitted` (roster order, then any off-roster legacy value, then Unassigned if any) — called once at page init, not on every `renderAll()` (the roster only changes when `permitted` does, i.e. never mid-session).
- **Consistency guard:** toggling an individual project pill (`toggleProjectPill`), or clicking **All**/**None**, resets `#pf-gh` back to `'all'` — a manual toggle can break a picked head's exact project set (e.g. adding one extra project outside that head), so the picker is reset rather than left silently misrepresenting the selection.
- **`#proj-sort`** (Code · Name · Group Head · WPs · Awarded · Award rate · Budget) and **`#proj-gh-group`** ("Group by Group Head", **defaults CHECKED**, 2026-08-07 — was unchecked) remain in the cards/table toolbar (`.proj-gh-controls`) as **display-only** controls — they only affect how the (already `_activeIds`-scoped) cards/table are ordered/grouped, not which projects are included. Both call **`window.onProjGhChange()`**, which re-renders the cards and (if visible) the table.
- **Search (`#proj-table-search`) + Status (`#proj-table-status`) moved into this same toolbar, beside Sort (2026-08-07)** — they used to live in `#project-table-wrap`'s own panel header, so they only existed (and only filtered) when the Table view was showing. Now they sit in the always-visible `.proj-gh-controls` row and are folded into **`_projRows()`** itself (via `_projSearchVal()`/`_projStatusVal()`), so they filter **both** Cards and Table through the one shared pipeline — same as Sort/Group-by. `renderProjectTable()` no longer filters on its own; it just consumes `_projRows()`'s output. `renderProjectCards()`'s existing active-vs-archived split needed no changes: filtering to `status=archived` simply leaves its "active" bucket empty (blank main grid) while its own "Archived (N)" section — which reads from the SAME filtered `rows` — holds everything, exactly like it already did for the table alone.
- **One pipeline feeds BOTH views.** `_projRows()` maps `_tableData` (already scoped to the current project selection) into row objects, applies search + status, then sorts via **`_GH_SORTS`** (every comparator tie-breaks on `id` so ordering is stable). When grouping is on it sorts by Group Head FIRST — so a group's rows stay contiguous — and the chosen sort orders *within* each group. `renderProjectCards()` and `renderProjectTable()` both start from `_projRows()`, so a change to the filter/sort/group logic only needs wiring once.
- **`renderProjectCards()` was extracted out of `renderAll()`** so the toolbar controls can re-render just the cards. `renderAll()` must therefore set **`_tableData` BEFORE calling it** (it previously set `_tableData` after the inline card loop). Archived projects still go to the separate collapsible "Archived (N)" block.
- **Grouped rendering — NOT a solid-red pill (2026-08-06, per user feedback: "no red overlay behind the Group Head tag").** The first cut used a full solid-red pill for the group label (`background:#EE3124;color:#fff`), which read louder than intended — red is reserved elsewhere in this app for the small project-CODE pill only, not a whole label block. Both the cards divider and the table's `.gh-group-row` header now use a light tinted chip (`var(--mw-red-light,#FDECEA)` background, `border-left:3px solid var(--mw-red)`, dark text) instead. Cards get a full-width divider per head (`grid-column:1/-1`) — `.project-cards` is a CSS grid, so the divider must span it that way; the table's `.gh-group-row` uses `colspan="10"`. **The table now has 10 columns** (Group Head was inserted at position 4), so `colspan` and the viewer cost-hide rule moved: `body.budget-hidden #proj-data-table th/td:nth-child(8)` (was 7) hides Budget, plus `.gh-budget-opt` hides the "Sort: Budget" option. **If you add another column to this table, update both the `N_COLS` constant in `renderProjectTable()` and that `nth-child`.**
- **`sortProjTable()`/`_tableSortCol`/`_tableSortDir` were REMOVED** — a click-to-sort stub no `<th>` ever called, so the table was always id-ascending. `#proj-sort` is the sort control now; don't reintroduce the stub.

## Role-Based Access Control

| Role | Projects Visible | Edit WPs | Auto-Approve | Admin Rights | Cost Data |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `super_admin` | All | All | âœ… | Full | âœ… |
| `admin` | All | All | âœ… | Users + Projects | âœ… |
| `specialist` | All (read) | Assigned only | âœ… | None | âœ… |
| `manager` | Assigned | Assigned | âœ… | None | âœ… |
| `user` | Assigned | Assigned | ✅ | None | ✅ |
| `viewer` | Assigned | None | â€” | None | âŒ |
| `viewer_budget` | Assigned | None | â€”  | None | âœ… |

**`viewer_budget` (added 2026-08-03)** â€” identical to `viewer` in every read-only respect (no edit/add/delete anywhere, redirected off `wp-form.html`/`review.html`/`claim-form.html`, assigned-projects-only, same single-page stacked layout, Tools/Status-Tracker hidden) but CAN see budget/cost data. The one flag that used to conflate "read-only" and "hide cost" is now two flags: **`window.__isViewer`** (`true` for both `viewer` and `viewer_budget` â€” gates every read-only/layout behavior below, unchanged) and **`window.__hideBudget`** (`true` ONLY for `viewer` â€” gates every cost-hiding behavior). A parallel CSS class **`body.budget-hidden`** (toggled from `__hideBudget`) carries all the cost-hiding selectors that used to live under `body.viewer-mode`; `body.viewer-mode` itself keeps only the read-only/layout selectors (view-tabs hidden, single-page layout, Tools/Status-Tracker hidden, etc.) â€” those still apply to `viewer_budget` too. **Data path:** `db.js` `_wpRel()` now gates on `window.__wpmRole==='viewer'` specifically (not `__isViewer`), so `viewer_budget` reads `work_packages` directly (full cost columns) via RLS instead of the cost-free `wp_view_public` view â€” `viewer` is the only role still routed through that view. **Server-side:** `migrations/2026-08-03_viewer_budget_role.sql` (run once) widens `users_role_check` to allow the value and updates the `wp_insert`/`wp_update`/`wp_delete`/`claims_insert`/`claims_update` RLS policies to deny `viewer_budget` too (`not in ('viewer','viewer_budget')`, was `<> 'viewer'`); `wp_select` is untouched (viewer_budget was never excluded from it, so it already reads the base table once the client stops routing it to the view). Wired into: `login.html` (skip-picker redirect, role-label map), `wp-form.html`/`review.html`/`claim-form.html` (redirect guards â€” claim-form.html had no viewer redirect at all before this and now has one), `admin.html` (role `<select>`s + `roleLabel()`/`roleStyle()`), `onboarding.js` `tierOf()` (mapped into the `viewer` tier). **When adding a new cost-bearing UI element, gate it on `window.__hideBudget` (or `body.budget-hidden`), not `window.__isViewer` (or `body.viewer-mode`)** â€” the latter now also matches `viewer_budget`, which must keep seeing cost data.


**Auto-approve roles** (`AppAuth.isAutoApprove(profile)`): `super_admin`, `admin`, `specialist`, `manager`, `user` (added 2026-07 — regular users add/edit/delete WPs freely, no manager/WP-approver sign-off; only `viewer` is read-only). **ALL write paths must gate auto-approve on `AppAuth.isAutoApprove({role})`, never on a hardcoded `['admin','super_admin']` list** — the project.html quick-add modal (`saveQuickWP`) had that bug (fixed 2026-07): a `user`/`manager`/`specialist` quick-adding a WP left it stuck `pending_review` (invisible on dashboards, which query `review_status='approved'`) with no self-service approval. `wp-form.html`, the Status Tracker, and CSV import were already correct. **Archived projects are READ-ONLY for everyone** (incl. admins — they can only unarchive): `project.html` sets `window.__archived` + `body.archived-readonly` (CSS hides `#add-wp-link`/`#sidebar-tools`/`#status-tracker`; edit/delete links gated by `window.__archived`), and `openAddWPModal`/`saveQuickWP`/`updateWpStatus`/`deleteWPFromDetail`/`importWPsFromCSV` all early-return when archived; `wp-form.html` `submitForm()`/`deleteCurrentWP()` block if the selected project's status is `archived`. In the **Portfolio Overview** archived projects start **UNSELECTED** (`_activeIds` excludes `status==='archived'`) so they're out of the charts by default (their pill can still be toggled on). The **project-filter label** (`updateFilterLabel`) counts against **active (non-archived) projects only** — reads `"N of M active projects selected"` (e.g. `6 of 6 active projects selected`, not `6 of 7 projects selected`), appending ` (+K archived)` only when archived pills are toggled on. â€” WPs save directly as `approved`; no `pending_review` step.

**Specialist** sees all projects in login picker and `index.html` (same as admin) but `canAccessProject()` still limits editing to `profile.projects`. Uses `getAllApprovedWPs()` (single query, no N+1).

**Viewer restrictions** (`body.viewer-mode` + `window.__isViewer`): cost KPIs hidden, Budget tab hidden, Financial WP List tab hidden, cost columns excluded via `_getActiveCols()`/`getActiveCols()`, Add WP + Edit buttons hidden, Tools section hidden, `wp-form.html` redirects immediately. **Viewers must not see ANY budget / cost / award-amount in charts, graphs or tables** — enforced by `body.viewer-mode` CSS rules in BOTH pages plus a few JS guards. Hidden for viewers: the **Top 5 panels** (`.top5-row` — High Value / Savings / Overbudget), the **% Awarded by Value** battery (`#ov-battery-val` / `#idx-ov-battery-val` — the **% by WP** battery + count stats table stay), the **Procurement Budget (BCB) & Awarded by Period** chart panels (`#dash-period-panel`/`#idx-dash-period-panel`) and the **backlog period** chart panels (`#backlog-period-panel`/`#idx-bl-period-panel`), the **WP-List Filtered Analysis** (project `#pwa-analysis` via JS, portfolio `#wp-mon-analysis` via CSS), the **Status Tracker** (`#status-tracker`, JS), the project **Budget** + **Works** tabs (`#tab-works`/`#tab-works-btn`, plus a `switchTab` redirect to overview), the Schedule **Budget** data-mode toggle (`#sch-btn-budget` — chart stays in #WPs count mode), the backlog-table budget column (project `#backlog-table` col 6, portfolio `#bl-table` col 7), the portfolio **project-table Budget column** (`#proj-data-table` col 7) and the **project-card Budget/Awarded ₱ line** (guarded by `window.__isViewer` in `renderProjectCard`). When adding any new money-bearing chart/panel/column, add a matching `body.viewer-mode` hide rule.

**Viewer UI simplification (2026-07-30, `project.html`)** — because viewers are read-only with most panels stripped, the leftover UI was made lighter: the WP-List **"Set Procurement & Delivery status inline" hint** (`#wplist-edit-hint`) is hidden (viewers can't edit); the **Overview top row** (`.ov-top-row`) is centered with `max-width:860px` so the lone WP-status summary reads as a deliberate summary instead of a stranded half-width block (cost KPIs, period chart and Top 5 are all hidden for viewers). All `body.viewer-mode` CSS.

**Viewer single-page layout (2026-07-30, `project.html`)** — viewers get **no main tab bar** (`#view-tabs` hidden). Instead of switching between Overview / Backlog / WP List, **all three views are shown stacked on one scrolling page**, each under a red `.viewer-page-head` section header (Overview / Backlog / Work Packages). `switchView(v)` short-circuits to **`_viewerSinglePage()`** for viewers: it shows all three `#view-*` containers, injects the section header once each (`el.dataset.vhead` guard), sets `currentView='table'` (so the WP-List filter/search handlers that gate on it stay live), and renders all three (`renderOverview`/`renderBacklog`/`buildTable`). Non-viewers keep the normal tabbed behavior untouched.

Within the viewer single-page layout the **Work Packages section keeps the normal tabbed WP List** — the Overview/Schedule/Submittals sub-tab switcher (`#proj-view-tabs`) stays clickable and the single paged table renders as usual (an interim "stacked read-only sub-sections" experiment — `renderViewerWpSections`/`#viewer-wp-sections` — was **reverted** at user request). `renderCell` remains extracted to module scope as **`_wpRenderCell(key,wp,_stickyLeft)`** (+ helper `_fmtPct`) — a harmless refactor still used by `buildTable`. The Submittal-status donut is visible to viewers (not hidden). Award/All sub-tabs stay hidden for viewers (cost).

**Viewer single-page layout also applied to the Portfolio Overview (2026-07-30, `index.html`)** — same treatment, one level up: viewers get **no main tab bar** (`#view-tabs` hidden) and see **Overview → Backlog → Schedule → Work Packages stacked on one scrolling page** under red `.viewer-page-head` section headers (`_VIEWER_TABS`/`_VIEWER_TAB_HEADS`). **Budget and Works are excluded** — they're money-only tabs already fully hidden for viewers (`#tab-budget`/`#tab-budget-btn`/`#tab-works`/`#tab-works-btn`), same as before. `window.switchTab(tab)` short-circuits to **`_viewerSinglePage()`** for viewers (ignores the requested tab, ignores/ never sets `_activeTab`); `renderAll()`'s final "render the active tab" step also branches to `_viewerSinglePage()` for viewers so a project-filter change re-renders all four stacked sections instead of only `_activeTab`. Each section's `_renderTab(t,...)` call is unchanged — this only changes which tabs render and how they're laid out. The WP List section here keeps its own `wp-vtab` Overview/Award/Schedule/Submittals/All sub-tab switcher untouched (Award/All already hidden for viewers), matching the project-page decision not to further sub-divide it. `#idx-ov-battery-val`/Top 5/period-chart/etc. viewer cost-hides are unaffected.

**Viewer single-page layout, round 2 (2026-07-30) — project picker first, collapsible sections, sticky nav, WP % chart mode:**
- **Project selector relocated into the topbar (`index.html`).** `#proj-filter-wrap` (the "All projects ▾" dropdown) now lives in `.topbar-left` next to the READ-ONLY badge on desktop, via **`_viewerRelocateProjFilter()`** (called at the top of `_viewerSinglePage()`) — `window.matchMedia('(min-width:768px)')` decides: desktop moves it into `#topbar-proj-filter-slot` (a new empty slot in the topbar markup); narrow viewports move it back to `#proj-filter-anchor`, a stable marker left in its original spot inside `#filter-tabs-bar` (so it's never lost off-screen; re-evaluated on every `_viewerSinglePage()` call, i.e. every filter change / resize-triggered rerender). Compact chip styling via `body.viewer-mode .topbar-left #proj-filter-wrap` (smaller padding, no shadow, `--surface-2` fill, label ellipsis-clamped to 160px). `#idx-bcb-switch` (BCB baseline switch, when applicable) stays in `#filter-tabs-bar` untouched — it isn't cost data, so it was never gated for viewers.
- **Project cards/table moved to the very top of the page, ahead of Overview (`index.html`).** The cards+table markup (Cards/Table toggle, `#project-cards`, `#project-table-wrap`) is wrapped in **`#idx-viewer-projects`**; `_viewerSinglePage()` does `mainContent.insertBefore(projSec, mainContent.firstElementChild)` so it becomes the first thing on the page, given its own collapsible **"Projects"** section head. Non-viewers never call `_viewerSinglePage()`, so for them this markup stays exactly where it was authored (end of the Overview tab, after Top 5) — no DOM move, no behavior change. Rendering (`renderProjectCard`/`filterProjectTable`/etc.) is untouched since it targets the same element ids regardless of their position in the DOM.
- **Section dividers are now collapsible (both pages).** `.viewer-page-head` gained a caret (`.vph-caret`, rotates -90° when `.collapsed`), `cursor:pointer`, hover/`:focus-visible` states, and `role="button" tabindex="0"` + Enter/Space handling. Implementation: **`_mkCollapsibleHead(container,label,icon)`** — defined once per page (`index.html` and `project.html` each have their own copy, consistent with this codebase's no-shared-JS-module convention) — moves ALL of a section container's existing children into a new `.viewer-section-body` wrapper, then appends a clickable head + that body. Click toggles `body.style.display`. Idempotent via `container.dataset.vhead`. **Default state is expanded** on every page load (per user's explicit choice — collapse is a manual declutter action, not a default). Applied to: `#idx-viewer-projects` (Projects), `#tab-overview`/`#tab-backlog`/`#tab-schedule`/`#tab-wplist` (index.html), and `#view-overview`/`#view-backlog`/`#view-table` (project.html).
- **Backlog tab — Due Only filter + reordered layout + negative-aging convention (2026-08-05, both pages).** Three related changes to the Backlog table/tab on `index.html` and `project.html`:
- **Table moved ABOVE the charts** (was: charts first, table last — see the "Backlog table below/last" comments, now reworded). Both pages' Backlog tab/view now render the filter toolbar + table first, then the aging/status/period/submittal chart panels below. Pure markup reorder — no id/function changes, so nothing downstream needed touching.
- **New "Due Only / Not Due Yet" filter** (`#bl-due-filter` on both pages, a plain 3-option `<select>` next to the Trade filter — `All (Due + Not Due) / Due Only / Not Due Yet`) wired into `renderBlTable()` (index.html) / `renderBacklog()` (project.html). **Due = the planned award date has already passed** — the exact same definition already used by the WP-status batteries (`renderIdxOvBattery`/`renderOvBattery`: `w.awarding_date && new Date(w.awarding_date)<today`), reused here for consistency. A WP with no planned award date can't be judged either way, so it's excluded from "Due Only" and included in "Not Due Yet".
- **Aging sign convention FLIPPED** (per explicit user request): the `Aging (d)` column previously showed `today − awarding_date` (positive = overdue). It now shows **`awarding_date − today`** — **overdue WPs read NEGATIVE, not-yet-due WPs read POSITIVE.** Changed in `index.html`'s `_renderBlRow` (`renderBlTable`) and `project.html`'s `_renderRow` (`renderBacklog`) — both the raw day-count formula AND the `agStyle`/`agCls` overdue-severity color thresholds were mirror-flipped (old `aging>60`/`aging>30` red/amber → new `aging<-60`/`aging<-30`). The `+`/`-`-prefixed display formatting (index.html) needed no change — it already prints the JS number's own sign. **The "Most Overdue First" sort** (`bl-sort-by` on index.html, the default `_blCmp` branch on project.html) was re-derived to keep the SAME user-facing behavior (`_blCmp`'s date-based comparator needed no change at all — it never used a day-diff variable; index.html's inline sort comparator was flipped to `agA-agB` ascending on `awarding_date-now`, still landing most-overdue-first). **NOT touched:** `charts.js`'s `wpAgingBuckets` (the aging-bucket bar chart) — it only emits fixed bucket-count labels like "&gt;60d overdue", never displays the raw signed number to the user, so its internal (still old-convention) day-diff calc needed no change. Also left untouched: `project.html`'s dead `renderDashBacklog()`/`dash-backlog-tbody` path (see Known Issues — its DOM target doesn't exist in the current markup, self-no-op, out of scope).

**WP List — Charging Type filter (Main Contract / Change Order), both pages (2026-08-05).** A new plain `<select>` (`#wp-mon-charging` on `index.html`, `#f-charging` on `project.html` — 3 options: `Main Contract + CO` / `Main Contract` / `Change Order`) sits beside the existing Trade filter. Uses the same `_isCO(w)` test already established in `project.html` (`/change\s*order/i` on `charging_type`, blank counts as Main Contract) — **index.html gained its own copy of `_isCO`** (didn't have one before) defined right above `filterWPTable`. **index.html**: wired into the single `filterWPTable()` predicate (the one function that produces `_wpMonFiltered`, reused by the table, cards, and Filtered Analysis panel — so one edit covers all consumers) and added to `resetWPMonFilters()`'s clear-list. **project.html**: wired into all THREE parallel filter-predicate functions that must stay in sync per the existing convention (`getFiltered`, `getFilteredSorted`, `_pwaFilteredWPs` — see the WP List Filtered Analysis entry above, "same predicates as getFilteredSorted") and added to the `['f-status','f-charging']` change-listener array (Trade has its own listener since it also resets the Works/Vendor cascade; Charging doesn't cascade anything, so it joins Status's plain re-filter listener).

**Backlog / WP List / Schedule-summary group tables start collapsed for viewers, on `index.html` only** (project.html wasn't part of this ask — its Backlog/WP List trade-groups still default expanded for everyone, unchanged). Three separate collapse mechanisms, one seed each:
  - **Backlog table** (two-level Project→Trade): `_blCollapseState` Map, key `'__P__'+pid`. Seeded `true` **once per key** (`if(window.__isViewer && !_blCollapseState.has(pKey)) _blCollapseState.set(pKey,true)`) right where the render loop first computes `pKey` — the `Map.has` guard means a viewer who manually expands a project stays expanded across re-renders (filter changes), it's only ever collapsed automatically on first encounter.
  - **WP List table** (two-level Project→Trade): same pattern on `_projCollapseState`, key = bare `pid`.
  - **Schedule "summary by project" table**: no persisted Map (this table is fully string-rebuilt every render, same for all roles) — instead the initial HTML itself renders the trade-header rows with `display:none` and the project indicator glyph as `+` (not `−`) whenever `window.__isViewer` (`const pStartCollapsed = !!window.__isViewer`). `toggleSchProj`'s existing `isHidden` detection (reads `tradeHdrs[0].style.display`) picks this up correctly with no changes to the toggle function itself.
  - In all three cases only the OUTER (project) grouping level starts collapsed — trade-level sub-groups, once a project is opened, render in their normal (expanded) state.
- **Sticky section-jump nav rail, both pages** — mirrors `wp-form.html`'s `#form-nav` pattern exactly (same CSS shape, same IntersectionObserver scroll-spy approach), adapted to a new class name (`.viewer-side-nav`) since it lives on a different set of pages. Markup: `<nav class="viewer-side-nav" id="viewer-side-nav">` inserted as the first child of `#main-content-region`, hidden (`display:none`) until built. `body.viewer-mode #main-content-region{display:flex;gap:20px;align-items:flex-start}` turns it into a flex row with `#main-content`/`#loading` (`flex:1;min-width:0`) — inert for non-viewers since the region stays `display:block` for them. **Desktop**: sticky **40px** left rail (narrowed 2026-07-30, was 200px — see follow-up note below), `top:76px` (topbar 60px + gap), `max-height:calc(100vh - 92px)`, own scrollbar. **Mobile (≤900px)**: becomes a sticky horizontal scroll-spy strip under the topbar (`top:52px`), pill-style active tab (bottom border instead of left border) — same breakpoint/values as wp-form. Section containers get `scroll-margin-top:76px` (`112px` on mobile) so `scrollIntoView` lands below the sticky chrome. **`window._viewerBuildNav()`** (called at the end of every `_viewerSinglePage()`) filters a candidate list down to sections currently `style.display !== 'none'` (so Budget/Works never appear in the nav) and rebuilds the link list + rewires `_viewerWireNavSpy(nav)`, which: (a) wires click → `scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth'})`, auto-**expanding** the target section first if its head has `.collapsed` (`head.click()` before scrolling, so a nav click never lands on a blank collapsed section); (b) runs an `IntersectionObserver` (`rootMargin:'-80px 0px -55% 0px'`) that highlights whichever observed section is nearest the top, suppressed for 700ms after a click (`_viewerNavClickLock`) to avoid spy flicker during the scroll animation — identical timing to wp-form's `clickLock`. index.html's candidate list: Projects/Overview/Backlog/Schedule/Work Packages; project.html's: Overview/Backlog/Work Packages (no Projects/Schedule sections there).

**Sticky nav rail narrowed to 40px with rotated labels (2026-07-30, both pages)** — the original 200px labeled rail (icon + horizontal text, one per line) ate too much horizontal content width per user feedback. Desktop now uses a **40px rail with each label rotated 90°** (`writing-mode:vertical-rl; transform:rotate(180deg)` on the `<a>`, `display:block; text-align:center` — deliberately NOT `display:flex`, since mixing flexbox alignment with `writing-mode` on the same element is unreliable across engines) and **no icon** (`.viewer-side-nav a i{display:none}` — icons don't rotate sensibly under vertical writing-mode without a separate counter-rotation, and the option chosen was "rotated text, no icons"). `white-space:nowrap` is load-bearing here: without it a long label like "Work Packages" could wrap into a second vertical line and widen the rail past 40px. The **mobile (≤900px) horizontal strip is unchanged in spirit** but now explicitly resets `writing-mode:horizontal-tb; transform:none` and restores `display:flex; align-items:center; gap:9px` (icon shown again) — a rotated rail only saves space in a vertical sidebar layout, not a horizontal top-strip one, so mobile keeps the original icon+upright-label pill design. `.viewer-side-nav-title` ("Sections" heading) is now `display:none` unconditionally (both breakpoints) — it didn't fit a 40px column and was dropped rather than rotated.

**Project-header pill style (2026-07-30, `index.html` only) — Backlog table + WP List table project headers now match the Schedule Summary by Project look.** Per user feedback that Schedule's row style ("a light `#f0f0f0` row + a small red project-code pill + plain-text project name + grey WP-count/rate + a grey `+`/`−` toggle badge") reads better than a full-width solid-red bar. Both the **Backlog table's two-level Project→Trade header** (`_idxBlRenderTable`'s `twoLevel` branch, project-level `pKey` row) and the **WP List's `projheader` item row** (`renderWPMonTable`) were restyled from `background:#EE3124` (solid red, white text, colspan-wide) to the pill style: `background:#f0f0f0`, a `<span>` pill (`background:#EE3124;color:#fff;padding:2px 7px;border-radius:4px`) holding just the project ID, plain-text project name (`permitted.find(...).name`, NOT the old combined "ID — Name" `_blProjLabel`/`_projLabel` strings — those two helpers are unchanged and still used by the (dead, mode-locked) single-level Backlog branch and the mobile card view respectively), and a grey `+`/`−` indicator (`background:#e0e0e0;color:#555`, was `rgba(255,255,255,.25)` on red). **Backlog's WP-count+budget text is now a single inline span** (`N WPs · ₱X.XM`, hidden budget suffix for viewers) rather than two separately-floated spans, to fit naturally after the pill+name on one line. **The WP List's project header keeps its two-`<td>` sticky/non-sticky split** (`tdSticky` colspan = frozen-column count, `tdRest` = the remainder) — this is load-bearing per Known Issues #12 (contiguous sticky-column offsets; a single full-colspan cell has nothing to stick against horizontally) and was preserved exactly, only the colors/content changed. The trade-level sub-header rows (light grey `#fafafa`/`#f8f8f8`, already close to this style) and the mobile card view's `.wpc-projgroup` chip (already light-bg/red-accent) were untouched — only the solid-red PROJECT-level desktop-table headers changed. **`project.html`'s own Backlog/WP-List project headers still use the solid-red style** — this pass was scoped to `index.html` per the request; ask if you want the per-project dashboard's tables restyled to match too.
- **"WP %" toggle added to the Schedule tab's "Work Package by Period (Planned vs Actual)" chart (`index.html`).** This chart (`#c-idx-sch-period`) previously only had **# WPs / Budget** modes (`_idxSchDataMode`, `setIdxSchDataMode`); Budget is money and already viewer-hidden (`body.viewer-mode #sch-btn-budget{display:none}`). Added a third button `#sch-btn-wppct` → mode `'wppct'`, **visible to viewers** (it's counts, not money — no new hide rule needed, it simply isn't matched by the existing Budget-only selector). No `charts.js` changes were needed: `wpCountByPeriodMonthly`/`wpCountByPeriod` already accepted an `opts.countPct` flag (added for the Overview tab's existing "# WPs %" mode, `_periodCountPct` — Planned/Actual WP counts as a % of the portfolio's total WP count, with cumulative Planned %/Actual %/Forecast % lines, same S-curve treatment as Budget %). `_renderIdxSchPeriodChart()` just gained a `_idxSchDataMode==='wppct'` branch calling the same chart functions with `{countPct:true}` instead of no-opts; `setIdxSchDataMode` now also toggles `#sch-btn-wppct`'s active state via `_segBtn`.

**Admin role restriction**: admins cannot assign `super_admin` or `specialist` roles to other users (only `super_admin` can).

**User â†’ Manager assignment**: `assigned_admin` DB column stores the manager's UUID. `admin.html` uses `WPDb.getManagerUsers()` to populate the dropdown (role=`manager` only). The "Assign to Manager" section is visible to `super_admin` only. Column header in user table shows "Manager".

**DB constraint** (run in Supabase SQL Editor):
```sql
ALTER TABLE users DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS users_role_check;
ALTER TABLE users ADD CONSTRAINT users_role_check CHECK (role IN ('super_admin','admin','specialist','manager','user','viewer'));
```

Both approval modal (`modalRoleSelect`) and Change Role modal (`crm-role-select`) in `admin.html` include all 6 roles.

---

## Navigation & Sidebar

**Collapsible sidebar + topbar logo** (`initMobileMenu` in `ui.js`, central â€” applies to every page with a `.topbar`): the `.btn-menu` hamburger is now visible on **all** sizes. On desktop/tablet (â‰¥768px) it toggles `body.sidebar-collapsed` (CSS slides `.sidebar` out via `translateX(-100%)` and sets `.main { margin-left:0 }` to reclaim the space); state persisted in `localStorage.wpm_sidebar_collapsed` and restored on load with a `.no-transition` guard to avoid a slide animation on first paint. **Default is COLLAPSED (2026-08-03)** â€” `initMobileMenu` collapses the sidebar on first load unless the user has explicitly expanded it before (`localStorage.getItem('wpm_sidebar_collapsed') === '0'`); previously the default was expanded, so every new session required a hamburger click just to get the sidebar out of the way. On mobile (<768px) the hamburger still opens the slide-in drawer + overlay. `initMobileMenu` also injects the **Megawide mark** (`assets/img/megawide-mark.png`, `.topbar-logo`, ~26px) into `.topbar-left` right after the menu button (idempotent) â€” mirrors the Planning App topbar (hamburger + mark + title).

**Sidebar lists ACTIVE projects only:** both `project.html` and (since 2026-07-23) `index.html` filter `status!=='archived'` out of the sidebar project list — an archived project (TES101) is not something you jump into to manage WPs. They stay reachable from the login picker, a direct URL, and the portfolio project cards/table (where their pill starts unselected). The Overview search box's >5-project visibility test counts active projects too.

**Sidebar project search:** both `project.html` (`#sidebar-proj-search` → `renderSidebarProjects()`) and `index.html` (`#nav-proj-search` → `window.__sidebarRefresh`) filter the sidebar project list by id or name. On the Overview it appears only when the user has **more than 5 projects**, is `position:sticky` at the top of the section, and shows a "No matching projects" empty state. Pinned/unpinned ordering (`SidebarPrefs.sortProjects`) is applied to the FILTERED list.

**Sidebar scrolling — ONE scroller only:** `.sidebar` is the single scroll container (`overflow-y:auto`, thin custom scrollbar in `dashboard.css`). The project list (`#sidebar-proj-list`) previously had its own `max-height:240px;overflow-y:auto`, which rendered **two scrollbars side by side**; it now grows naturally and the search box is `position:sticky` at the top of the section so it stays reachable. Don't reintroduce an inner scroll region inside the sidebar.

**Nav context** (`sessionStorage` key `wpm_nav_ctx`): stores project ID or `'consolidated'`. Set by `project.html` and `index.html`; read by `admin.html` and `review.html` for context-aware sidebar.

### Sidebar per Page

**`project.html`**
```
Current Project â†’ [project name]
Work Packages â†’ Add Work Package | Review WPs (admin, pending badge)
Claims & Change Order Register â†’ HIDDEN (display:none)
Tools â†’ Download Template
Admin (admin only) â†’ Portfolio Overview | User Management | Pinned/Recent Projects
```

**`wp-form.html`**
```
Current Project â†’ Back to [project]
Work Packages â†’ WP Form | Review WPs (admin)
Navigation â†’ Portfolio Overview
Tools â†’ Download Template
Admin (admin only) â†’ User Management
```

**`review.html`**
```
Overview â†’ Portfolio Overview
Current Project â†’ Back to [project] (project context only)
Projects â†’ [list]
Work Packages â†’ Add Work Package | Review WPs (active)
Admin â†’ User Management
```

**`admin.html`** â€” consolidated context
```
Projects â†’ [searchable list] | New Project
Admin â†’ User Management (active) | Portfolio Overview
```

**`admin.html`** â€” project context
```
Current Project â†’ Back to [project]
Admin â†’ User Management (active) | Portfolio Overview
```

**Key rules:**
- "Add Work Package" must NOT appear in `admin.html`
- `admin.html` uses plain project links (no pin/star)
- `project.html` and `review.html` use `SidebarPrefs.projectLink()`
- `SidebarPrefs`: pins in `localStorage` key `wpm_sidebar_{userId}`; `window.__sidebarRefresh` callback re-renders after pin toggle

---

## All Work Packages Page (`review.html`) — approval workflow retired

**The approval workflow is gone (2026-07-23).** Every writing role is auto-approve (`AppAuth.isAutoApprove` = `super_admin, admin, specialist, manager, user`; only `viewer` is excluded, and viewers are redirected off this page), so **no write path can create a `pending_review` WP any more** — `wp-form.html`, the quick-add modal, CSV/Excel import and the Status Tracker all save straight to `approved`. The Pending / Approved / Rejected tabs and the Approve/Reject buttons were therefore dead controls and were **removed**, along with the reject-reason modal, `switchTab`, `currentTab` and `rejectingId`.

The page is now the **bulk view-and-edit surface for all work packages**:
- Retitled **"Work Packages"** (sub: "View and bulk-edit all work packages"); the sidebar link on `review.html`, `project.html`, `wp-form.html` and `claim-form.html` reads **"All Work Packages"** (`ti-table` icon, was "Review WPs" / `ti-clipboard-check`).
- **Grid (Excel) is the default view** (`_reviewView` starts `'grid'`; the Grid button leads the switcher, Cards is the alternate). One card list (`#panel-cards`) shows every filtered WP — no status tabs — sorted by project then WP No., with an **Edit** button that degrades to a disabled **"View only"** when `canEditProject` is false.
- **Legacy cleanup strip** (`#legacy-strip`, admins only, dismissible per session via `sessionStorage.wpm_legacy_dismissed`): if any rows still carry a non-`approved` `review_status` — only possible for WPs predating the auto-approve change — it reports the count and offers **Approve all N** (`approveLegacy()` loops `WPDb.approveWP`, busts the WP cache, reloads). Those rows also show a `PENDING (legacy)` / `REJECTED (legacy)` chip and a per-card Approve button. Once cleared, the strip never appears again.
- The grid's status dropdown labels the states honestly: **All work packages / Approved (live) / Pending review (legacy) / Rejected (legacy)**.
- `WPDb.getPendingWPs()` and the sidebar pending badges (`project.html`, `wp-form.html`) are retained — they now surface exactly these legacy leftovers and read 0 once cleaned up. `updateWP` (which resets `review_status='pending_review'`) is still called on the non-auto-approve branch of the Status Tracker and the grid save; that branch is currently unreachable but is kept so re-introducing a review-required role is a one-line change to `isAutoApprove`.

## Review Page — Excel-like Grid View (`review.html`)

`review.html` has a **Cards | Grid (Excel)** switcher (`setReviewView('cards'|'grid')`, state `_reviewView`) in the filter bar. Cards = the original review-card list (with the Pending/Approved/Rejected tab row); **Grid** = a full spreadsheet over the same `allWPs`, with the tab row hidden. The Trade + Search filters drive BOTH views (`applyFilters` is wrapped to re-render the grid). **The grid's own "Show" status dropdown (`#xl-status-filter`: All/Approved/Pending/Rejected) was removed (2026-08-06)** — the approval workflow is retired, it was almost always "All" anyway, and the legacy-strip already surfaces + one-click-fixes any pre-auto-approve rows without needing a filter. `renderGrid()`'s `statusF` is now hardcoded `'all'`.

**Engine (all inline in `review.html`, no libraries):**
- **Columns are grouped by the 8 WP-form sections** (`XL_GROUPS`: Identity & Classification, Approval Matrix, Insurance Bonds, Material/Subcon Submittals, Procurement Schedule, Budget & Contract, Payment Terms, Procurement Status). Every column carries `g:'<group>'` and `XL_COLS` is ordered by section, so the grid mirrors the form top-to-bottom. The thead is **two sticky rows** — a clickable group row (`tr.xl-grp`, `top:0`) above the column row (`th.xl-colh`, `top:26px`).
  - **Collapsible sections:** clicking a group header toggles it (`toggleXlGroup(g)`); **Expand all / Collapse all** buttons sit in the toolbar. State lives in `_xlCollapsed` and persists per user in `localStorage['wpm_grid_groups_<uid>']` (`_xlLoadGroups`/`_xlSaveGroups`, loaded in `requireLogin`). `XL_GROUPS_DEFAULT_COLLAPSED = ['approval','bonds','payment']` on a first visit so the grid opens at a workable width.
  - A collapsed group keeps its `keep:true` columns (Project + WP No. in Identity — the frozen keys) or, if it has none, a **narrow read-only placeholder column** (`ph:true`, hatched) so its header stays on screen and clickable — without it a collapsed section could never be re-opened. The header shows `+N` for how many columns are hidden.
  - **`VC` (visible columns) is the coordinate space.** `_xlBuildVC()` runs at the top of every render, and **every r/c coordinate — selection, nav bounds, copy/paste, fill-down, editors, dirty repaint — indexes into `VC`, never `XL_COLS`**. Only the required-field tally (`_xlMissingCells`) and the save-time `_xlRowMissing` scan `XL_COLS`, so a collapsed section can't hide a missing required field from the count or from save gating. If you add a coordinate-based lookup, use `VC`.
- **OPTIONAL two-level grid: Trade (level 1, collapsible + subtotals) → work packages (level 2) (2026-08-17).** **Off by default** — a toolbar checkbox **"Group by trade"** (`#xl-group-trades` → `window.xlSetGroupTrades(on)`, state `_xlGrouped`, persisted per user in `localStorage['wpm_grid_tradegroup_<uid>']` and loaded by `_xlLoadGroupMode()` inside `_xlLoadPrefs()`) turns it on; unchecked, the grid keeps its original flat project → WP-No. order and emits no group rows at all. When on, `_gridRows` sorts **by trade first** (`XL_TRADE_ORDER`, the canonical 10-trade order; unknown/blank trades sort last as `(No trade)`), then project, then WP No., so same-trade rows form one contiguous run. `_xlGroupRow(key, rows)` emits ONE `<tr class="xl-grouprow">` per run: a chevron in the gutter, the trade name + WP count in the first non-placeholder column, and money **subtotals rendered under their own columns** for every key in `XL_SUBTOTAL_COLS` (`budget_bcb0/1/2`, `awarded_cost`, `total_awarded`, `_variance`, `buyback_amount`, `_buyback_value`). Sums read through **`xlVal(w,k)`**, so they reflect **staged, unsaved edits** and the BCB0 carry-forward. Draft (`__new`) rows keep riding at the very top under their own **"New work packages"** group (`_xlGroupKeyOf` returns the sentinel `' new'`).
  - **Collapsible trades.** Clicking a group row calls `window.xlToggleTradeGroup(key)` (state `_xlTradeCollapsed`, a `Set`, session-only — deliberately not persisted, unlike the column-section collapse). A collapsed trade's member rows are **filtered out of `_gridRows`**, i.e. out of the coordinate space, exactly like the existing "Only incomplete" checkbox. **Its subtotal still shows every WP in the trade** — `_xlGroups` is built from the rows BEFORE the collapse filter runs, and `_xlGroupRow` sums that captured full set. **Staged edits on a hidden row are never lost:** `gridSave()` looks a dirty id up in `_gridRows` **and falls back to `allWPs`**, so collapsing can't silently drop an unsaved change. (`_xlMissingCells()`'s required-empty tally DOES only count visible rows — same pre-existing behaviour as "Only incomplete".)
  - **Header placement is index-keyed, and the fully-collapsed cases are the ones to get right.** `_groupStart[r]` holds the group whose first VISIBLE row sits at `_gridRows` index `r`; a collapsed group has no visible member, so its header is queued in `_collapsedAt[r]` at the index it should precede (`g.__pendingAt`, the running visible-row count at the time it was skipped). `_headsBefore(r)` emits collapsed headers then the starting group's header, and is called once per row **plus once more with `_gridRows.length`** so groups collapsed at the very end still render. The `!_gridRows.length` early-return is also gated on `!(_xlGrouped && _xlGroups.length)` — otherwise collapsing every trade would render "No work packages match the current filters" with no headers left to click, permanently trapping the user. Traced against collapse-first / -middle / -last / -all before shipping.
  - **CRITICAL — the group rows are OUTSIDE the grid's coordinate space, by construction.** They carry **no `data-r`/`data-c` attributes** and are not members of `_gridRows`. Every coordinate-based mechanism in this grid either indexes into `_gridRows` (selection bounds, nav, copy/paste, fill, `_xlRowMissing`) or looks a cell up via `_xlTd(r,c)`'s `[data-r][data-c]` query — neither is positional over `tbody`'s children — so inserting these rows changed **nothing** about selection, keyboard nav, Ctrl+C/V, fill-handle, undo, or dirty repainting. **If you add another injected non-data row, keep it attribute-free the same way**; the moment a decorative row carries `data-r`/`data-c` it becomes selectable and the whole model breaks.
  - Known cosmetic side effect, accepted: the zebra stripe is `tbody tr:nth-child(even)`, which now counts the group rows too, so stripe parity can flip from one trade group to the next. Not worth a rewrite (`:nth-child` can't skip a class).
  - CSS: `.xl-grouprow td.xl-grouphead` (red-tinted `#FDECEA` / dark `#33211F`, red top border, `!important` so it beats the zebra/data-row-hover rules), `.xl-grouplbl`, `.xl-groupn`, `.xl-groupsum`. **The group-row `:hover` rule must sit AFTER the base rule in source order** — both carry `!important` at equal specificity, so the later one wins; putting hover first (the first cut) silently produced no hover feedback on a clickable row.
  - **`_xlLoadPrefs()` is called at the TOP of `renderGrid()`, not mid-way.** It used to run after the row filtering, which was harmless while it only loaded column widths/freeze/section-collapse — but `_xlGrouped` drives the sort and grouping, so loading it late meant the stored "Group by trade" pref was ignored on the first render of every page load. Any new pref that affects row selection or ordering has the same requirement.
- **`XL_COLS`** — 58 column defs `{g,k,label,w,type,opts,ro,req,reqIfAwarded,keep,dynOpts,users}` covering every WP-form field (identity, approval matrix, bonds, submittals, schedule, budget, payment terms, status). Two extra editor types beyond the grid's original set: **`pct`** (stored as a decimal, shown and entered as whole percent — `dp_percent`, `retention_percent`, matching the form) and **`bool`** (`requires_approval`, a Yes/No select over a real boolean).
  - **Cascading Trade → Works → Type (2026-07):** `works` and `type_of_works` are `type:'select'` with **`dynOpts:'works'`/`'type'`** — their dropdown options are computed **per row** from that row's Trade via `XL_TRADE_WORKS` (the same ladder as the WP form — keep in sync) in `_xlWorksOpts(w)`/`_xlTypeOpts(w)`, exactly like `onTradeChange`/`_setTypeOptions`. Off-list/legacy stored values are preserved (unshifted). Because dynOpts options vary per row, the header **▾ "set all"** menu falls back to a free-text input for these two (can't list one shared option set).
  - **User-directory fields (2026-07):** `responsible_team`/`approver`/`support_team` carry **`users:true`** — their editor is a text `<input>` wired to a shared `<datalist id="xl-users-dl">` of names from `WPDb.getAllUsers()` (loaded once via `_xlLoadUsers()` in `requireLogin`, stored in `_xlUsers`). Autocomplete from the real user list while still allowing multiple comma-separated names (matches the WP form's multi-select intent).
  - **Column order — Description before Cost Code (2026-07):** in the Identity group the grid lists Project, WP No., **Description, then Cost Code** (Cost Code moved right of Description). A contiguous left-freeze captures everything to the pin's left, so ordering Description first lets "freeze up to Description" stop before the often-empty Cost Code column.
- **Readability (2026-07-23):** even rows are zebra-striped (`--surface-2`, excluding frozen + missing + dirty cells so those keep their own colour), hovering a row tints it `--mw-red-light` and reddens its gutter number (`.xl-rownum`) so you can track a row across the wide horizontal scroll. The dense keyboard-shortcuts paragraph is collapsed into a **`<details class="xl-help">`** disclosure ("How to use this grid — works like Excel", `<kbd>` chips), closed by default. The required-empty tally is amber (`#B45309`), not alarm-red — it's a worklist, not a failure (save gating already blocks bad saves). **Toolbar legend polish (2026-07):** the two colour swatches (`required, empty` / `unsaved`) are grouped in a rounded `.xl-legend-box` chip with a single explanatory `title` tooltip, and a thin `.xl-sep` divider separates the legend from the Sections controls. **The `.xl-help` disclosure arrow was a corrupted glyph** — `summary::before { content:'B8' }` (a stray control char + "B8" from a bad edit) literally rendered "B8" beside "How to use this grid"; fixed to `content:'▸'` (rotates when open). **Required-column `*` placement:** the red required marker was `th.xl-req::after`, which put it AFTER the pin/caret/resize icons and wrapped it to a second line under the header; it's now an inline `<span class="xl-req-star">*</span>` rendered right after the label text inside a `.xl-hlbl` span. **Save/Discard buttons:** Save carries a `ti-device-floppy` icon + a `#xl-save-lbl` span (so `_xlUpdateBar`/`gridSave` update the LABEL, not the whole button — preserving the icon) and gets a red shadow only when there are pending edits; Discard is a quiet `.ghost` button with a `ti-arrow-back-up` icon; both sit in a divider-led `.xl-actions` group.
- **Resizable columns:** every column header carries a 7px drag grip (`.xl-rz`) at its right edge — **drag to resize, double-click to restore that column's default**, plus a **Reset widths** button in the toolbar. Overrides live in `_xlWidths{k:px}` (clamped 44–900) and persist per user in `localStorage['wpm_grid_widths_<uid>']`. **All width reads go through `_xlW(col)`** (default `col.w` unless overridden) — the colgroup, `_xlLeft()` frozen offsets and `_xlTotalW()`. The drag live-updates only the `<col>` + table width (no re-render), then re-renders on release so the frozen columns' sticky `left` offsets follow the new widths.
- **LAYOUT GOTCHA (was a live rendering bug):** `table.xl` had `table-layout:fixed` **together with `width:max-content`**. A max-content width resolves through the *auto* table algorithm, which **discards `table-layout:fixed` and every `<col>` width** — so one long `description` (the QHL706 plumbing WPs are ~600 chars) stretched its column to ~4700px, blew the table to ~10000px and pushed every other column off-screen (the user's screenshot: only Project + WP No. visible, text bleeding across the grid). **`renderGrid()` now sets an explicit pixel `width` on the table** (`_xlTotalW()` = gutter + the visible columns) and the CSS no longer sets `max-content`. Never reintroduce a content-based width on this table — with `table-layout:fixed` the width must be definite.
- **Grid prefs load lazily** via `_xlLoadPrefs()` at the top of `renderGrid` (guarded by `_xlPrefsLoaded`), NOT from the `requireLogin` callback — loading there depends on the callback firing *after* the module's `let` declarations initialise, which is true only because Supabase auth is async. The lazy load removes the ordering hazard entirely.
- **Only the ACTIVE cell gets a real `<input>`/`<select>`** (`_xlBeginEdit`, absolutely positioned inside `#xl-wrap`), so thousands of cells stay fast. Cells are plain `<td>` text otherwise.
- **Excel keyboard model:** click to select, **type any character** or **Enter/F2** to edit, **Tab/arrows** to move, **Shift+click / Shift+arrows** to range-select, **Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V** (TSV — round-trips with real Excel, multi-cell block paste from the anchor), **Ctrl+D** fill down, **Del** clear range, **Ctrl+Z** undo (batch stack `_xlUndo`), **Esc** cancel. **Ctrl+Arrow / Ctrl+Shift+Arrow (2026-08-06)** — jump (or extend the selection) to the edge of the current data block, matching real Excel: `_xlJumpEdge(r,c,dr,dc)` walks in the arrow's direction — from a filled cell it stops at the last filled cell before a gap/edge; from an empty cell, or when the very next cell is empty, it skips forward to the next filled cell (or the edge). "Filled/empty" is judged via `xlVal()`, the same value the grid displays. Wired into the same `nav` block as the plain-arrow case in the grid's `keydown` handler — `jump = e.ctrlKey||e.metaKey` picks `_xlJumpEdge(...)` over the old ±1-cell step as the destination, then Shift decides whether that destination extends the range (`_xlAnchor`) or just moves the active cell. **Guided-fill Tab/Enter + type-to-filter dropdowns (2026-08-06):** `_xlNextRequiredEmptyInRow(r,fromC,forward)` searches row `r` (wrapping, never crossing rows) for the next required-but-empty VISIBLE cell (indexes `VC`, never `XL_COLS` — a required field hidden behind a collapsed section can't be jumped to). **Tab** (`_xlTabTarget(r,c,forward)`, used both by the outer `keydown` handler and the cell editor's own Tab) always prefers that jump over the plain ±1-column step, falling back to normal movement once the row has no more missing required cells — safe for any row since Tab was never used for cross-row bulk edit. **Enter** only takes the jump on a freshly-added draft row (`w.__new`) — deliberately NOT on ordinary saved rows, where "Enter down one column across many existing rows" is an established bulk-edit pattern (e.g. fixing one field on 30 incomplete rows) that a row-scoped jump would otherwise hijack sideways every time that row has some other unrelated missing field. Combined with `xlAddRow()`'s existing "land on Description, ready to type" behavior, this makes filling in a new WP a straight sequence of Tab/Enter presses through exactly its required fields, in form order. **Dropdown cell editors are now split two ways:** `asUserSelect` (Responsible/Approver/Support) and `type:'bool'` columns stay a native `<select>` (a `<datalist>` filters its popup to the current text, which would hide everything when the stored value is an abbreviation not in the option list — bool only has 2 options anyway); every other `type:'select'` column (fixed `col.opts` OR per-row `dynOpts` like Works/Type) is now a text `<input list="xl-ed-datalist">` so a long option list can be narrowed by typing instead of scrolling. **`xlCoerce(raw, col, ctx)` gained a 3rd `ctx` arg** (the row, or a partial row-shaped object with at least `.trade`) so it can resolve `dynOpts` option lists via `_xlWorksOpts`/`_xlTypeOpts` for normalization — previously a paste into Works/Type was never normalized at all, and the existing select-column match only handled case, not extra/collapsed whitespace. Both `_xlSet` (typed edits, cell/block paste) and `_xlPasteWholeWPs` (whole-WP paste) now pass that context through.
- **Toolbar layout — dead space reclaimed, twice (2026-08-06):** first pass moved the "How to use this grid" disclosure onto the same row as the missing/dirty-count + Discard/Save actions (a `.xl-bar2` wrapper). **Reverted the same day per user feedback** — the actual dead space they meant was the gap AFTER the "Sections: Expand all / Collapse all / Reset widths" controls on the FIRST toolbar row. Final layout: the missing/dirty-count, autosave-indicator, and Discard/Save actions moved onto `.xl-bar`'s first (and now only) row, right after the Sections controls, pinned right via a `<span style="flex:1">` spacer — filling exactly the space that used to sit empty. `<details class="xl-help">` reverted to being a plain standalone element below `.xl-bar` (its original position) — with the actions now sharing row 1, it no longer needed a shared-row trick of its own. The `.xl-bar2`-scoped CSS from the first pass was removed as dead code.
- **Toolbar/table visual polish (2026-08-06):** (1) **Button hierarchy** — plain `.xl-btn` buttons (Select all/Copy WPs/Paste WPs/Discard-adjacent) had no `:hover` state at all, reading as static/flat; added a red-tinted hover (`var(--mw-red-light)` border+bg, matching the row-hover/menu-hover convention used elsewhere in the app) plus a `transition`. The "Sections" trio (Expand all/Collapse all/Reset widths) — infrequent layout tools, not primary content actions — got a new **`.xl-btn-mini`** tertiary style (borderless/muted `--text-hint`, same hover tint) so they read as visually lower-priority than Select all/Copy/Paste WPs, instead of all `.xl-btn`s looking the same weight. Three-tier hierarchy is now: primary (solid red — Add WP, Save changes) → secondary (bordered, hover-tinted — Select all, Copy/Paste WPs) → tertiary (borderless, muted — Sections mini-tools).
