(2) **Header/zebra contrast** — `table.xl thead th`'s background was the exact SAME `var(--surface-2)` as the even-row zebra stripe (and the `.xl-freeze` header override), so the column-header row visually blended into the data instead of reading as a header band. Both header rules now use the group-header row's existing tone (`#F3F3F3` light / `#3a3a3a` dark) plus a firmer `border-bottom:2px solid var(--border-md)` on the column-header row — a hard border line is a more reliable separator than a small color-luminance gap, especially in dark mode where nearby grays are hard to tell apart. The zebra stripe itself was also strengthened beyond the app-wide `var(--surface-2)` default (barely-there ~2% contrast) to a table-scoped `#F1F1F1` light / `#363736` dark, kept deliberately short of the header's tone so rows and header stay distinguishable from EACH OTHER too. (3) **`.xl-wrap`'s `max-height: calc(100vh - Npx)` reserve** trimmed from 250px → 215px → **205px** (2026-08-06, second pass) to reclaim dead space below the table — flag if this still leaves a visible gap or clips on a given screen size; it's a static estimate (same magic-number pattern already used elsewhere, e.g. `.viewer-side-nav{max-height:calc(100vh - 92px)}`), not something verifiable without a live render.
- **Toolbar regression fix — grouped into two explicit flex children, not one flat wrapping list (2026-08-06, same day, reported "the top section significantly looked worse"):** putting the missing/dirty-count + Discard/Save directly into `.xl-bar`'s single long flex-wrap list (the first pass above) worked at very wide viewports but broke unpredictably at normal widths — the row was already using most of its width for the existing controls, so adding more items just pushed the wrap point around, sometimes splitting mid-group into a ragged 3-line toolbar (main controls / counts+actions / help disclosure) instead of the intended 2. Fixed by wrapping the content in two explicit children — **`.xl-bar-left`** (Add WP through Reset widths, itself `flex-wrap:wrap` so it degrades gracefully on its own) and **`.xl-bar-right`** (counts + Discard/Save, `margin-left:auto`, `flex-shrink:0`) — so `.xl-bar` (now `flex-wrap:wrap` over just these 2 children) can only ever produce two outcomes: both groups share one row (right pinned to the end, filling what would otherwise be dead trailing space), or the right group wraps as ONE unit onto its own row while the left group wraps its own children independently. Never a 3-way ragged split regardless of viewport width. **Lesson: when adding content to an already-near-full flex-wrap row, group the addition into its own flex child rather than appending flat items — a flat list wraps unpredictably at the individual-item level, a grouped child wraps as a whole.**
- **Toolbar FINAL layout — a dedicated 2nd row, not wrap-dependent (2026-08-06, 3rd and last iteration).** Even grouped into `.xl-bar-left`/`.xl-bar-right`, row 1 was still full enough at real viewport widths that the right group wrapped to its own line with a wide dead gap to its left, and `<details class="xl-help">` pushed to a 3rd line — the same complaint a third time. Root problem: **the layout was still being decided by flex-wrap at runtime rather than by the markup.** Final structure is two explicit toolbar rows: **row 1 `.xl-bar`** = `.xl-bar-left` only (Add WP → Reset widths); **row 2 `.xl-bar.xl-bar2`** = the help disclosure (left) + `.xl-bar-right` (counts + Discard/Save, `margin-left:auto`). The help toggle now fills what was the dead space, and the toolbar is **always exactly 2 rows** regardless of width. `.xl-bar2 .xl-help[open]{flex-basis:100%}` keeps the expanded multi-line help on its own line so it can never squeeze the actions off-screen. **Verified geometrically with a throwaway harness** (real `dashboard.css` + the real toolbar markup, since the live page needs a Supabase login): at 1650/1400/1200px the help and actions share one line with the actions flush right (0px gap to the bar's right edge) and the toolbar measures 64px = exactly 2 rows; expanded, the help takes the full 1618px row and the actions drop below it, still flush right and on-screen. **Don't re-solve this by adding items back into row 1 and trusting flex-wrap — that failed three times; put new toolbar content in an explicit row.**
- **Toolbar squeezed to ONE row at any desktop width — icon-only secondary buttons (2026-08-17).** With the sidebar expanded the content column drops to ~1000px and the toolbar still spilled the Sections trio onto a second row. Rather than retune the wrapping again (which failed repeatedly — see the iterations below), the fix was to **cut the content**: `Select all` and the three Sections buttons became **icon-only** (`.xl-btn.xl-btn-ico`, each keeping a full `title` **and** an `aria-label` since there's no visible text), the `Sections:` label was dropped, `Only incomplete` shortened to `Incomplete`, and the `#xl-count` row-count moved **into the footer** beside the other status readouts (it's information, not an action). **`Add WP`, `Copy WPs` and `Paste WPs` keep their text labels** — Add WP is the primary action, and Copy/Paste are the cross-project workflow rather than an obvious-from-the-icon utility (they were briefly made icon-only with a count badge and reverted the same day at user request; `_xlSyncPasteBtn()` still rewrites the label to `Paste N WPs`). **`.xl-btn-ico` needs the two-class selector `.xl-btn.xl-btn-ico`**: its rule sits above `.xl-btn`/`.xl-btn-mini` in source order, so at equal single-class specificity their padding would win. **Measured with a throwaway harness** (real `dashboard.css` + the real toolbar markup, with Paste at its longest `Paste 100 WPs` state): natural content width **1330px → 726px**, one row at every width down to **730px** — comfortably under the ~1000px content column you get with the sidebar expanded.
- **Toolbar FINAL-final: one row above the grid, a footer below it (2026-08-06, 4th iteration).** Even at 2 rows the chrome above the table was too tall and a gap persisted under the grid. Redistributed by *purpose* rather than by wrapping: **Discard/Save moved up onto the shared `.rv-filter-bar`** (search shrunk from a greedy `flex:1` to `flex:1 1 200px;max-width:420px` to free the width) as `<span class="rv-grid-actions xl-actions" id="rv-grid-actions">` — they're grid-only, so **`setReviewView()` hides them in Cards view**; **the help disclosure + the missing/dirty/autosave counts moved BELOW the table** into `<div class="xl-bar xl-foot">`. That leaves exactly **one** toolbar row (Add WP → Reset widths) between the filter bar and the data. Verified with a harness at 1650/1300px: filter bar = 1 row (34px) holding project/trade/search/view-switch/Discard/Save; toolbar = 1 row; footer sits below the grid.
- **Grid height is now MEASURED, not a hard-coded `calc()` (2026-08-06).** `.xl-wrap`'s `max-height: calc(100vh - Npx)` was tuned three times (250 → 215 → 205px) and still left a dead gap under the table, because no single constant can cover every combination of filter-bar height, toolbar wrapping, legacy strip, and draft banner. **`_xlFitHeight()`** now walks the `offsetTop` chain to get the wrap's document-relative top, subtracts the footer's measured height and 14px of breathing room from `window.innerHeight`, and sets `maxHeight` inline (floor 240px). Called at both `renderGrid()` exit points, on `resize`, and on the help `<details>` `toggle` event (opening it changes the footer height). **Uses the `offsetTop` chain, NOT `getBoundingClientRect().top`** — the rect is viewport-relative, so growing the wrap can scroll the page, which changes `rect.top`, which changes the computed height: a feedback loop. Verified: `top 102 + grid 597 + footer 17 + 14 = 730` = exactly the viewport height. The CSS `calc()` remains only as a pre-JS fallback. **When adding anything above the grid, don't retune a constant — `_xlFitHeight()` already absorbs it.**
- **Grid view no longer scrolls the PAGE — only the grid scrolls (2026-08-06, `body.rv-grid-fixed`).** A spreadsheet with two nested scrollbars is wrong: the filter bar and toolbar could scroll away from the data. In Grid view `setReviewView()` now toggles **`body.rv-grid-fixed`**, whose CSS pins the shell to the viewport and makes `.xl-wrap` the only scroller via a **flex CHAIN** — `.app-shell{height:100vh;overflow:hidden}` → `.main` → `.content` → `#main-content` → `#grid-view` → `.xl-wrap{flex:1}`, with **`min-height:0` on every link** (a flex item's default `min-height:auto` refuses to shrink below content size and would push the page taller than the viewport again). **This replaced arithmetic with layout**: `_xlFitHeight()`'s hand-computed height kept leaving a gap because no constant could know about **`.content`'s 48px `padding-bottom`** — the real source of the last stubborn dead space. Flex is self-correcting: verified that opening the help disclosure shrinks the grid 688px→455px and showing the draft banner shrinks it to 622px, with the page never scrolling and the footer never clipped. **Scoped deliberately:** Cards view removes the class (it's a normal scrolling document, verified still scrolling), and the CSS is gated to `@media (min-width:768px)` so mobile keeps normal page scrolling — one breakpoint owns it, so a window resize re-evaluates with no JS listener. `_xlFitHeight()` early-returns (clearing its inline `max-height`) when the class is active AND the media query matches, so the two sizing mechanisms never fight; below 768px it still owns the height as the measured fallback.
  - **GOTCHA that broke the first attempt:** `#main-content` starts with an inline `style="display:none"` and the load path sets an inline `display:block` — an inline style **outranks a class rule**, so `body.rv-grid-fixed #main-content{display:flex}` silently didn't apply, the flex chain broke at that link, and the wrap grew to its content height (5234px) with the footer clipped 4532px off-screen by the `overflow:hidden` above it. Fixed with `display:flex !important`. Same inline-beats-class trap as the `panel-fake-fullscreen` bug in Known Issues #30 — **when a class must override an element this app also styles inline from JS, it needs `!important`.** `#grid-view` needed no such treatment because `setReviewView` sets its display to `''` (removing the inline value) rather than a literal.
- **Sidebar-expanded wrap + search dead gap (2026-08-06, final pass).** Two leftovers from the row consolidation: (a) with the sidebar EXPANDED the content column drops to ~1000px and the toolbar spilled **"Collapse all"/"Reset widths" onto a 3rd row**, split away from the `Sections:` label they belong to; (b) with the sidebar COLLAPSED the `max-width:420px` search cap left a visible dead gap between the search and the Grid/Cards switcher. Fixes: **the Sections label + its 3 buttons are now one `<span class="xl-sections">` inline-flex unit** (`flex-shrink:0`) so they can only ever wrap as a whole group, never split; **the colour legend (`.xl-legend-box`, ~180px) moved out of the toolbar into the footer's `.xl-bar-right`** beside the counts — it's explanatory content like the help text, not an action, and its width was what forced the wrap; and **the search's `max-width` cap was dropped** (kept `flex:1 1 200px;min-width:160px`) so it absorbs leftover width on wide layouts but still shrinks to let Discard/Save share the row when tight. Verified per-project (badge shown, project dropdown hidden — the real layout) at 900–1900px content widths: **filter bar 1 row + toolbar 1 row = 2 rows at every width ≥1000px**, gap after the search a constant 10px (just the flex gap), search growing 301→1201px. Only below ~900px does the toolbar take a 2nd row, and the Sections group moves intact. **Measure with a harness before changing this toolbar — the visible row count is not what a naive `getBoundingClientRect().top` comparison reports** (`align-items:center` gives different-height items tops that differ by a few px within the SAME row; group by vertical centre with a ~12px tolerance instead).
- **Cell selection drew a red ring around the WHOLE grid (2026-08-06).** `#xl-wrap` carries `tabindex="0"` (it's the grid's keyboard-event target) and the grid calls `wrap.focus()` after every cell mousedown and every editor commit. That matched `dashboard.css`'s global a11y rule `:where(…, [tabindex], …):focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--mw-red) !important }`, so **clicking any cell outlined the entire scroll container** — reported as "selecting a cell highlights red the whole outline of the grid". Note this fired on plain mouse clicks, not just keyboard tabbing: Chromium treats a *programmatic* `.focus()` on a tabindex container as focus-visible. Fixed with a page-scoped `.xl-wrap:focus-visible:has(td.xl-sel){outline:none !important}` — suppressed only while a cell is actually selected, because `.xl-sel` already paints a 2px red outline on the active cell and is a far more precise focus indicator than ringing the whole container. The `:has()` guard deliberately **keeps** the container ring for the edge case of an empty grid / no selected cell, so a keyboard user is never left with no focus indicator at all. **Don't remove `tabindex="0"` from `#xl-wrap` to "fix" this** — the grid's whole keyboard model (arrows/Tab/Ctrl+C/V/Z) depends on that element being focusable.
- **Excel mouse model (2026-07-23):** selection is driven by a document **`mousedown`** handler (replaced the old `click` handler — a click handler fires AFTER mouseup and would clobber a drag range). **Drag to select a range** (`_xlDragMode==='select'`, anchor set lazily on first move so a plain click stays a single cell); **Shift+click** still extends. **Fill handle** — a floating `#xl-fill-handle` square pinned to the bottom-right of the selection (`_xlSyncFill()`, called from `_xlSelect`/`_xlPaintRange`, repositioned on wrap scroll, hidden while editing); **drag it up/down to fill** (`_xlDragMode==='fill'`, live `.xl-fillprev` preview, `_xlFillValue` repeats the source block like Excel, commit via `_xlCommitFill` into the undo stack). **Per-column bulk set** — a header **▾** (`.xl-colset`, shown for editable non-key columns) opens `#xl-colmenu` (`_xlColMenu`): select/bool columns list their options as buttons, other types get a value input; `_xlColApply`/`_xlColApplyInput` → `_xlSetColumn(k,val)` stages the value on **every visible row** (respecting edit-scope; confirm first; undoable). Great for the dropdowns (Charging, Procurement/Submittal/Delivery Status). CSS: `#xl-fill-handle`, `.xl-fillprev`, `.xl-colset`, `#xl-colmenu`.
- **Range-highlight visibility fix (2026-08-06):** dragging a multi-cell range only ever showed the red outline on the LAST cell touched (`.xl-sel`) — the range fill (`.xl-range`) was an 8%-opacity `background` with no dark-mode variant, and lost the CSS specificity fight against `table.xl tbody tr:hover td{background:...}` while the mouse was still over the row being dragged through, so the rest of the selected block looked unhighlighted (reported: "not sure if the bunch are selected or not"). `.xl-range` now uses an **inset `box-shadow` overlay** (`box-shadow:inset 0 0 0 9999px rgba(238,49,36,.22)`, dark-mode `rgba(255,122,110,.28)`) instead of `background` — a box-shadow paints on top of whatever background the cell already has (zebra stripe, `.xl-dirty` amber, `.xl-missing` red, the hover tint) rather than competing with it for the same CSS property, so it can never be silently defeated by another rule's specificity again. Also added an explicit **`#xl-sel-info`** readout ("N cells selected (R rows × C cols)"), populated in `_xlPaintRange()`, shown only when the range is more than 1 cell — a definitive confirmation independent of the visual highlight. **Moved from the toolbar into the footer's `.xl-bar-right` (2026-08-06)**, beside `#xl-missing-count`, so all four transient status readouts (selection / required-empty / unsaved / autosave) sit together below the grid instead of competing for toolbar width. `_xlPaintRange()` looks it up by id, so the DOM move needed no JS change. Verified with all four strings populated at their longest simultaneously: footer stays 1 row (23px) with no overflow at 1000–1900px content widths. **If you add another cell-state class that sets `background` on `table.xl td`, prefer `box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 9999px <color>` over `background` for anything meant to layer OVER other cell states (selection, previews, flags) — `background` only ever shows one winner.**
- **"Last Change" audit columns + "Review" column removed (2026-07-23):** the grid now has a **`Last Change`** group (`g:'audit'`) with read-only **Last Edited By** (`updated_by_name`) and **Last Edited On** (`updated_at`, `type:'datetime'` — new format in `xlFmt`) so officers can see who last touched every WP without opening the detail panel (the grid has no detail panel). The vestigial read-only **`review_status` "Review" column was removed** — the approval workflow is retired so it always read "approved". (The SHOW dropdown that used to filter on this field was itself removed 2026-08-06 — see the Grid-view entry above.)
- **BCB0 shows the CURRENT baseline:** for rows imported before per-baseline columns existed (their single budget lives in `approved_budget_bcb`, `budget_bcb0` null), the grid's **BCB0** cell falls back to `approved_budget_bcb` in `xlVal` (mirrors the dashboard's `bcbValue()` carry-forward) instead of showing blank; and **editing BCB0 also moves `approved_budget_bcb`** when no higher baseline (BCB1/BCB2) is set (in `_xlSet`), so the "current" column and all dashboard KPIs stay in step.
- **Add a WP from the grid** (`xlAddRow()`): an **Add WP** button in the toolbar creates a **draft row** (`_newRows`, id `new_N`, `__new:true`) pinned to the TOP of the grid and styled green, with a `×` in the row gutter to discard it (`xlRemoveNewRow`). Drafts ignore the Trade/Search/status/"Only incomplete" filters so a row you just added can never be filtered out from under you. The cursor lands on Description ready to type. Sensible defaults are pre-set (OSM No, Charging Main Contract, Procurement Not Started, Award Not Yet Awarded, Submittal Not Required, bonds No) so only the genuinely required fields highlight red; **WP No. auto-numbers** to `max whole number + 1` for that project counting saved AND draft rows, and stays editable. Project comes from the page context; in the **consolidated grid a project picker** (`#xl-new-proj`) appears next to the button listing only projects the user may edit (archived + DEMO excluded).
  - **Insert happens on Save, through the same path as the WP form / quick-add**: `WPDb.submitWP(payload, profile)` then `WPDb.approveWP` for auto-approve roles. `_xlDraftPayload(w)` builds the payload from `XL_COLS`, skipping `ro`/placeholder/`_`-prefixed keys so **no generated column is ever written**. Drafts must pass the FULL required-field check (unlike untouched existing rows, which only highlight), and a **duplicate WP No. guard** runs first against both saved rows and other drafts — the DB has a `(project_id, wp_no)` unique constraint. A draft that fails to insert stays staged (its edits are preserved) while successful ones are cleared. **`_xlDraftPayload` also writes the derived `approved_budget_bcb`** (newest non-blank of `budget_bcb0/1/2`) — that column has no grid cell of its own but every dashboard/chart reads it, so without this a grid-added/pasted WP's budget wouldn't move the KPIs. (Same rule as `_xlSet`/the WP form; applies to BOTH Add-WP drafts and pasted rows.)
  - **Add MULTIPLE blank rows at once (2026-08-04):** `xlAddRow()` reads a count box **`#xl-add-count`** (1–100, default 1) beside the Add WP button and pushes that many drafts, each auto-numbered (`_xlNextWpNo` reads `_newRows`, so each push bumps the next number). A count of 1 keeps the old behavior (land the cursor on Description ready to type); >1 just selects the first new row and toasts. A shared **`_xlNewDraft(pid, seed)`** builds a draft with the sensible defaults (used by both add and paste); `seed` pre-fills fields.
  - **Copy work packages from one project and paste them as new rows in ANOTHER (2026-08-04):** three toolbar controls — **Select all** (`xlSelectAllCells()`, also **Ctrl+A**: sets `_xlAnchor`/`_xlSel` to span the whole grid), **Copy WPs** (`xlCopyWPs()`), **Paste WPs** (`xlPasteWPs()`, `#xl-paste-wps`). **Copy** serializes the selected rows' full field data (via `_xlCopyableCols()` — every editable column, skipping `ro`/`ph`/`_`-prefixed/`project_id`; values read with `xlVal` so staged edits + the BCB0 carry-forward are captured) into **`localStorage['wpm_wp_clipboard']`** as `{v:1, source, rows:[{k:v}]}` — localStorage so it **survives navigating to another project's grid** (same origin, `review.html?project=<id>`) — and ALSO drops a TSV (with header labels) on the OS clipboard for Excel. **Paste** reads that key and re-creates each row as a draft (`_xlNewDraft` + the copied fields as `seed`) in whatever project's grid is open; **WP Nos. that clash in the target are auto-renumbered** (normalized-key `taken` Set + `maxNum` bump, considering saved rows, existing drafts, and rows pasted earlier in the same batch); `award_status` is re-derived from the pasted `procurement_status`. Nothing is written until **Save** (same insert path as Add WP). The Paste button is disabled + relabeled (`_xlSyncPasteBtn()`, called from `_xlUpdateBar` + after copy) to show the pending clipboard count/source, e.g. "Paste 87 WPs". `Ctrl+C`/`Ctrl+V` cell-level TSV copy/paste (Excel interop, existing) is untouched — the new buttons are the whole-WP, cross-project path.
- **Staged, not live:** every edit goes into `_gridEdits = {wpId:{field:value}}` and paints the cell amber (`.xl-dirty`); nothing hits the DB until **Save**. `gridSave()` batches one update per WP — `WPDb.updateWPDirect` for auto-approve roles, `WPDb.updateWP` (back to `pending_review`) otherwise, mirroring the WP form. `gridDiscard()` drops all staged edits; a `beforeunload` guard warns on unsaved changes.
- **Read-only cells** (`.xl-ro`): `project_id`, `review_status`, the generated `total_awarded`, the computed `_variance` (recomputed live from the staged budget/awarded), and **`award_status`** — which stays **derived from Procurement Status** (Known Issues #18): editing Procurement Status also stages `award_status`. Generated columns are stripped from the patch before the write.
- **Row-level edit scope:** cells are locked (and paste skips them, with a toast) unless `AppAuth.canEditProject(profile, w.project_id)` — so a specialist/manager/user editing the consolidated grid can only touch their assigned projects. RLS enforces the same server-side. Viewers never reach this page (redirected to `index.html`).
- **Save-time safeguard:** an Awarded WP missing an Awarded Cost or Awarded Vendor blocks the whole save with a message naming the WP Nos. — same rule as `wp-form.html`.
- **Required-field highlighting:** columns carry `req:true` (WP No., Description, Trade, Charging, Planned Award, Target Delivery, Target Completion, Budget (BCB)) or `reqIfAwarded:true` (Awarded Cost, Awarded Vendor — required only while `award_status==='Awarded'`), mirroring `wp-form.html` `save()`. Their headers get a red `*` (`.xl-req`), and any required-but-empty cell is tinted red with a red left bar (`.xl-missing`, dark-mode aware). `_xlMissing(w,col)` / `_xlRowMissing(w)` / `_xlMissingCells()` drive it; `_xlRefreshRow` re-evaluates on every edit so the Awarded-only requirements appear and disappear as Procurement Status changes. The toolbar shows a live **"N required fields empty in M WPs"** tally plus a colour legend, and an **"Only incomplete"** checkbox filters the grid to rows with gaps (the count reads `3 work packages (of 6)`). **Save gating is edit-scoped:** a row you EDITED must have all its required fields or the save is blocked with a per-WP list of the missing labels; rows you didn't touch are highlighted but never block, since many arrived incomplete from the monitoring-sheet imports. This replaced the narrower Awarded-cost/vendor-only check.
- **Derived-award gotcha (fixed):** the `procurement_status` → `award_status` derivation in `_xlSet` must compare against **`xlVal(w,'award_status')` (the current effective value), not the stored `w.award_status`** — comparing to the stored value left a stale staged `award_status` when a user flipped a WP to Awarded and back, so it stayed "Awarded" (and kept demanding a cost/vendor) despite Procurement Status reading Sourced. When the derived value returns to the stored one the override is deleted rather than re-staged.
- Sticky header + **user-configurable frozen columns** (default Project + WP No.) after a row-number gutter; offsets computed by `_xlLeft(i)` (contiguous chain — Known Issues #11/#12). Each header shows a **📌 pin** (`.xl-pin`) — click it to **freeze columns up to and including that one** (click the current boundary again to reset to default). `XL_FREEZE` is now a `let`, recomputed each render by `_xlComputeFreeze()` from **`_xlFreezeKey`** (the KEY of the last frozen column, so the freeze survives group collapse/expand — falls back to the default `XL_FREEZE_DEFAULT=2` if that key is hidden). Persisted per user in `localStorage['wpm_grid_freeze_<uid>']` (`_xlLoadFreeze`/`_xlSaveFreeze`, loaded in `_xlLoadPrefs`). `window.xlSetFreeze(key)` toggles + re-renders. Styles live in the page's `<style>` under "Excel-like grid view" (`.xl-*`, dark-mode aware).

---

## Consolidated Dashboard (index.html)

Single Supabase query: `getAllApprovedWPs()` (admin) or `getApprovedWPsForProjects(ids)` (user). **Never revert to per-project N+1 calls.**

Lazy rendering: `_rendered` flags per tab â€” charts render on first open, reset on filter change.

**Tabs** (Claims tab hidden):

| Tab | Key content |
|---|---|
| Overview | Mirrors the per-project Overview layout (**Dashboard tab removed** â€” merged here). Top row: Cost Overview **4 KPIs (BCB, Actual Award, Balance to Award [amount + WP qty remaining], Savings / Loss [â‚± + %]) â€” matches the per-project Overview** (`.ov-cost-grid` is 2-col; `card()` takes `sub`/`subColor` for the qty/% sub-line; `balanceAmt=ctc`, `savings=hm.budgetAwarded-awardedCost`) left + two WP Status cards (donut % by WP/Value toggle + stats table) right. Then **Procurement Budget (BCB) and Awarded by Period** chart (`c-idx-dash-period`, Monthly/Quarterly + Budget/# WPs toggles, `_idxDashRenderPeriod`) + **Top 5 panels** (High Value / Savings / Overbudget). **Portfolio-specific:** clickable **project cards/table** (`setProjectView`) come **last, after the Top 5**. Rendered by the `tab==='overview'` branch of `_renderTab` (the former Dashboard render body was merged in; the old status-bars/summary/dashboard-backlog statements remain but self-no-op since their DOM was removed). |
| Action Center | **Risk & decision-first "what needs attention now" view (2026-08-10).** 4 summary tiles + 4 tables, all computed from the selected-projects approved WP set (`_lastWPs`), tables only (no canvas), every row opens `openWPDetail(id)`. Sections: **Overdue Awards** (not-resolved WPs whose planned `awarding_date` < today, sorted most-overdue-first, BCB at risk), **Awarded Over Budget** (`isMoneyAwarded` && `effectiveAwardedCost` > own BCB, overrun ₱), **Data Gaps** (unified 2026-08-12 — see below), **Upcoming Award Pipeline** (not-resolved, planned date ≥ today, bucketed 0–30/31–60/61–90 days + table). `renderActionsTab(wps)` in the `tab==='actions'` branch of `_renderTab`; uses shared `window.isResolved`/`isMoneyAwarded`/`effectiveAwardedCost` (#28) so "Not to be Awarded" WPs are correctly excluded from all problem lists. Tables cap at 50 rows with a "top 50 of N" note, collapsible, sortable, sticky-headed (see Known Issue #34). Desktop non-viewer only (viewers get the single-page layout; `switchTab` short-circuits and `_VIEWER_TABS` omits it). First of three planned analytical features (Vendor Analytics + Cycle-Time still to come). **Moved out of the main tab bar, super_admin-only via the profile menu (2026-08-10)** — see below. **Data Gaps folded together (2026-08-12, explicit request):** what was a separate 5th "Missing Planned Award Date" section is now ONE row-type within Data Gaps alongside missing-cost/missing-vendor — a WP is either "Awarded but missing cost/vendor" or "not resolved with no planned award date" (the two never overlap on one WP), tagged in a shared `Missing` column (`Cost`/`Vendor`/`Planned Date`) and filterable via the same dropdown (`All`/`Cost`/`Vendor`/`Planned Date`). The summary tile row dropped from 5 to 4 (the standalone "Missing Planned Date" tile was removed; its count folded into the "Data Gaps" tile). `window.wpMissingPlanCount(wps)` (the chart-footnote helper, Known Issue #32) is UNCHANGED and independent of this table merge — it's a different consumer (the period-chart footnotes on `index.html`/`project.html`), not read by `renderActionsTab`. |
| Backlog | **Charts first** (aging chart + status donut + period chart + submittal donut), then the Backlog — Not Awarded WPs table **below** (so charts are visible on tab open). Table filter bar: Trade, Sort, **BCB min/max (`#bl-bmin`/`#bl-bmax`, ₱M → ₱, applied in `renderBlTable`)** — mirrors the per-project backlog budget filter — and Search. **The table is ALWAYS grouped two-level Project → Trade** (mirrors the WP List) — `_idxBlGroupMode` is a fixed `'project-trade'` const and the old **Flat / By Trade / By Project toggle (`#idx-bl-group-toggle` / `toggleIdxBlGroupMode`) was removed** (2026-07-30). The render draws a prominent **red Project header** (collapse key `__P__<pid>`), then **Trade subheaders** under it (key `<pid>||<trade>`, independent per project), both collapsible via `toggleBlGroup` (`_blCollapseState`), with the Project AND Trade columns hidden from rows (`showProj`/`showTrade` flags rebuild the thead + `nCols`). Project groups order by the `permitted` list; trade groups by `TRADE_ORDER`. (`renderBlTable` still contains the now-dead single-level flat/grouped branches — harmless, never reached while the mode is fixed.) The BCB inputs are hidden for viewers (`body.viewer-mode #bl-bmin/#bl-bmax`). |
| Budget | Cost KPIs + budget-by-period + budget-by-trade HBar + Budget vs Awarded by Project grouped bar + budget table by trade. **KPI cards use `helpingMetrics(wps)` — the SAME authoritative basis as the Overview tab — so BCB / Awarded Cost / CTC / EAC / SavY-Loss reconcile across tabs.** The old naive `Σ total_awarded` over ALL rows over-counted provisional costs on Not-Yet-Awarded WPs (see #17), making the Budget-tab figures disagree with the Overview. The 5th/6th cards are **Savings / Loss** (`budgetAwarded − awardedCost` = `BCB − EAC`) + **% Savings / Loss** (`savings ÷ budgetAwarded`) — renamed from "Variance"/"% Variance". The budget-by-trade table's Awarded column + TOTAL also use the strict `award_status==='Awarded' && total_awarded>0` predicate so the table reconciles with the cards (the per-trade **Variance / % Var** column headers are unchanged). |
| Schedule | Period chart (# WPs Planned vs Actual default; Budget toggle available via `_idxSchDataMode`) + WP by Trade + WP by Status + collapsible schedule summary table |
| Works | Stacked period chart + donuts by trade + collapsible BCB by Period per Scope table + BCB & Awarded by Period per Scope table |
| WP List | **Project-grouped â†’ Trade-grouped** (two-level: a prominent red Project group header above each project's Trade subheaders), 5 view tabs (Overview/Award/Schedule/Submittals/All), sortable headers, virtual pagination, slide-in detail panel |

**IDX_TRADE_ORDER** (used for Budget, Schedule, Works tables):
```js
['General Requirements','Site Works','Structural Works','Architectural Works',
 'Mechanical Works','Electrical Works','Auxiliary Works','Plumbing Works',
 'Fire Protection Works','Allied Services','Site Development Works']
```

**WP List** (`index.html`): `_WPC` column defs + `_WP_VIEWS` + `_getActiveCols()`. **Award Status appears in ALL FIVE views (2026-08-22)** — it was only in Overview/Submittals/All; `'status'` (index.html) / `'award_status'` (project.html) was added to the **Award** and **Schedule** view key lists on both pages so a WP's awarding state is readable from every tab. Keep any new view in line with that. `renderWPMonTable()` rebuilds colgroup/thead per view and also populates `#wp-mon-cards` (mobile card view). `setWPListView(view)` switches + resets sort. `openWPDetail(w)` / `closeWPDetailModal()` â€” slide-in panel. All tab hidden for viewers.
  - An **OSM filter** (`wp-mon-osm`: All/Yes/No, filters `w.osm==='Yes'`) sits in the filter bar too. **Cascading multiselect filters (Trade â†’ Works â†’ Vendor)**: Trade is a single native `<select>` (`wp-mon-trade`, `onWpMonTradeChange()`); **Works and Vendor are custom checkbox-dropdown multiselects** (`.msf`/`.msf-btn`/`.msf-pop`, state in `_msfSel={works:Set,vendor:Set}`). `_recomputeFilterOptions()` cascades: Works options = distinct works of the trade-filtered set; Vendor options = distinct vendors (`_wpVendors(w)` splits `contractor||proposed_vendors` on `/[\r\n;|]+/` â€” newline/semicolon/pipe only, NEVER comma or space so multi-word names and "Company, Inc." stay intact; source data must use one of these delimiters) of the trade+works-filtered set. Selecting a Trade clears Works+Vendor and repopulates; selecting Works narrows the Vendor list and drops now-invalid vendor selections. `msfToggle/msfOnToggle/msfSelectAll/msfFilterOpts` drive the popovers; outside-click closes them. `filterWPTable()` reads the two Sets (empty Set = all). The custom buttons are fixed-width (`min/max-width` 150â€“190px) so the filter bar stays a **single row** regardless of project selection (replaced the old wide native selects that wrapped to 3 rows when all projects were selected).
  - **Filtered Analysis KPIs**: 4 cards â€” Work Packages, Total Budget (BCB), Total Awarded, **Total to be Awarded** (`wpmon-sum-tba`, amber â€” sum of `approved_budget_bcb` for `award_status!=='Awarded'` rows). The period chart uses `{tidyLabels:true}` (see charts.js `_dlTidy`) to declutter dense month/quarter bars.
  - **Budget / # WPs toggle** (`setWpMonDataMode('budget'|'count')`, state `_wpMonDataMode`, buttons `wpmon-btn-budget`/`wpmon-btn-count`): mirrors the Overview-tab chart's toggle. `budget` (default) → `budgetAwardedByPeriod(Monthly)` (BCB + Awarded cost, distinct-colour `_dlTidy` labels); `count` → `wpCountByPeriod(Monthly)` (Planned vs Actual WP counts). In **table** view the count mode renders a Planned/Actual-WPs count table (Planned by `awarding_date`, Actual by `actual_awarding_date` clamped to today) instead of the Budget/Awarded ₱ table. Applies to BOTH chart and table view. Same toggle added to the per-project WP List Analysis (`setPwaDataMode`, `_pwaDataMode`, `pwa-btn-budget`/`pwa-btn-count`).
  - **Overview period chart — 3-way toggle + default # WPs (2026-07):** the Overview "Procurement Budget (BCB) & Awarded by Period" chart (`c-dash-period` project / `c-idx-dash-period` portfolio) now has **# WPs / Budget / Budget %** and **defaults to # WPs** (`_dashDataMode`/`_idxDashDataMode` = `'count'`; buttons reordered). **Budget %** passes `{sharePct:true}` to `budgetAwardedByPeriodMonthly`/`budgetAwardedByPeriod`, which early-return into **`_periodSharePct(id,labels,budgetData,awardedData)`** — a plain bar chart of each period's Budget (BCB) and Awarded as a **share of the project's total budget** (bars, no cumulative lines, `%` axis/labels). `_periodSharePct` reuses the already-÷1e6 period arrays so ₱M cancels in the ratio. `sCurve(id,wps,mode)` is likewise `mode`-aware (`'wp'`|`'budget'`|`'budgetpct'` → cumulative % complete) for when the S-curve is re-added to a page. **Stable toggle buttons (2026-07):** the period-chart header title changes length per mode, which shifted the toggle buttons; the title `<span>` is now `flex:1 1 auto;min-width:0` with `text-overflow:ellipsis;white-space:nowrap` and the button group `flex-shrink:0`, so the buttons stay pinned at the panel's right edge regardless of title text (project + portfolio). All three modes' legends already share `font.size` 10 (desktop) — verified uniform.

**Top 5 rank tables (2026-07):** several ideas were tried and dropped — frozen WP-name column (covered the values), name truncation (unreadable), inner ◂▸ content-slide (user wanted the whole card to move, not the content). **Final = card-expand accordion** (`initTop5Accordion` in ui.js): clicking a card's title widens that whole `.panel` to `flex:1 1 100%` (full row width) so its table reads at a glance; the other two shrink to slim 128px title-only cards (`rank-list` hidden); click again to return to 3-up. **Row-detail vs card-expand are SEPARATE interactions (2026-07-30):** clicking a WP **row** (`.rank-list`) only opens that WP's detail panel (`openWPDetail`) — it NEVER expands/collapses the card; expand/collapse fires only from the card title/caret or empty chrome. Previously a row click in the default 3-up state both opened the panel AND enlarged the card (the guard only skipped the toggle when already expanded); the click handler now returns early for ANY `e.target.closest('.rank-list')`. **Layout:** collapsed cards group **on top** (`order:1`), GROW to share the full top row (`flex:1 1 0;min-width:0`) so the two chips stretch across the whole width with no dead space (each ~half the row), one-line title (`white-space:nowrap`, ellipsis safety net), 46px chip height and the expanded card takes its **own full-width row below** them (`order:2`, `flex:1 1 100%` — basis 100% forces the wrap so it spans the full width). (An interim `flex:1 1 0%` "master-detail" put the expanded card beside the collapsed strip; reverted per user — they wanted collapsed-on-top, expanded-below.) **Collapsed titles stay HORIZONTAL** — the first accordion rotated them vertically and a long title ("TOP 5 HIGH VALUE PACKAGES") then forced a ~530px row = a giant empty box; horizontal keeps collapsed cards ~75–90px tall. Widths set **inline `!important`** (saved/restored from `dataset.t5Flex/t5MinW`) so they beat the cards' own inline `flex:`; **no `transition:flex`** (non-interpolatable %↔px basis breaks it). Desktop only (`matchMedia('(min-width:768px)')` + `@media` guard; mobile already stacks full-width). `buildRankTable` (db.js) itself is back to plain full-name rows with `overflow-x:auto`. Called after `initExpandableCharts()` on index + project. Verified: expanded card 338px (270px table, no empty box), collapsed 75–90px, restores to 3-up.

**Top 5 High Value Packages — full parity with Savings/Overbudget format (2026-08-06):** `buildRankTable(id, items, type)`'s `isValue` branch (`type==='value'`, the High Value card) now renders **BCB | Award | Savings/Loss | % | %WT** — the exact same 5 value columns as the Savings/Overbudget tables. First column header renamed `Budget`→**`BCB`** for consistency. Unlike the Savings/Overbudget tables (one fixed sign per whole table — always green or always red), a High-Value WP can be either a saving or an overbudget depending on its own numbers, so **color is picked PER ROW**: `rowColor = item.savings<0 ? '#EE3124' : '#2D9B6F'` applied to the Savings/Loss, %, and %WT cells (BCB/Award cells stay neutral gray/black, matching the other two tables). `item.savings` = `variance(w)` (`bcb − effectiveAwardedCost(w)`, signed); `pct` = signed `savings ÷ bcb`; `wt` = signed `savings ÷ totBCB` (reuses the same `wtPct` helper the Savings/Loss tables already had). Callers (`buildRankTable('idx-rank-value', …)` in index.html, `buildRankTable('rank-value', …)` in project.html) compute all three the same way `rank-gains`/`rank-losses` do — just unfiltered by sign (High Value keeps its top-5-by-budget sort, not top-5-by-variance). **If you add a 4th Top-5 card, mirror this column set**; if any row's sign can vary, use the per-row `rowColor` pattern instead of one accent color for the whole table.

**Top 5 cards — equal-width columns (2026-08-06):** the 3 panels in `.top5-row` (`index.html` + `project.html`) previously had uneven flex weights (`flex:0.8` for High Value, `flex:1.25` for Savings/Overbudget) from when High Value showed only 1 value column against the other two's 4. Now that all three show the same 5-column table, all three panels are `flex:1;min-width:250px` so they render evenly wide in the default 3-up layout. `initTop5Accordion()` (ui.js) is unaffected — it caches whatever `style.flex` a card has at init (`dataset.t5Flex`) and restores it on collapse, so the accordion still works with the new equal weights.

**Budget % period chart (2026-07):** `_periodSharePct(id,labels,budgetData,awardedData,cumB,cumADisp,forecast)` renders per-period share **bars PLUS the cumulative Budget %, Cumulative Awarded % and Forecast % lines** — the same S-curve treatment as the # WPs and Budget modes, just ÷total×100. The callers (`budgetAwardedByPeriodMonthly`/`budgetAwardedByPeriod`) now compute `cumB`/`cumADisp`/`forecast` first and pass them in (the sharePct early-return moved below that). Cumulative Budget % ends at 100%; Forecast % ends at projected spend (below 100% by savings, like the ₱ mode). Labels via **`_dlTidy`** (bars <6% of the series max unlabelled) — kills the 0.0%/tiny-% clutter. The earlier trailing-empty-period trim was removed so the forecast line can extend (bars there are 0-height/invisible; _dlTidy handles labels).

**Chart panels — real fullscreen (2026-07):** `initExpandableCharts` (ui.js) now toggles the **Fullscreen API** on the `.panel` (was a 2.2× taller in-page panel, which didn't fit large displays / smart TVs). `fullscreenchange` sets the wrap to `calc(100vh - 96px)`, calls **`Charts.resize(id)`** (new — `c.resize()`+`update('none')`) to re-fit the canvas, and toggles data labels; `.panel:fullscreen` CSS paints `--surface` over the API's black backdrop. Button icon `ti-arrows-maximize`↔`ti-arrows-minimize`.
  - **Chart / Table toggle** (`setWpMonViewMode('chart'|'table')`, state `_wpMonViewMode`): Chart|Table switch beside Monthly|Quarterly. Table view (`renderWpMonPeriodTable`) shows the same per-period buckets as the chart â€” Month/Quarter, Budget (BCB), Awarded (red), Cum. Budget, Cum. Awarded + Total row â€” respecting Monthly/Quarterly mode and active filters. Bucketing mirrors charts.js (budget by planned `awarding_date`; awarded by `actual_awarding_date||awarding_date` fallback, `total_awarded>0`). `renderWPMonAnalysis()` renders chart OR table per `_wpMonViewMode`; `#wpmon-chart-wrap`/`#wpmon-table-wrap` toggled in `setWpMonViewMode`.
  - **Month-to-month (From/To) range scope + bidirectional cascade** (`wpmon-from` + `wpmon-to`, state `_wpMonFrom`/`_wpMonTo`): two dropdowns in the Filtered Analysis header restrict the **KPIs + chart + table** to WPs whose **planned award month/quarter (`awarding_date`)** falls within the inclusive `[From, To]` range (`_wpMonPeriodMatch` on `_wpMonKey(d)` — `YYYY-MM` monthly / `YYYY-Qn` quarterly, string-comparable). Either end "all" = unbounded; both "all" keeps null-date rows. **Bidirectional faceting:** `populateWpMonAnalysisFilters()` (called at the top of `renderWPMonAnalysis`) rebuilds the From/To option lists from the **Trade/Works/Vendor/Status-filtered** set (`_wpMonFiltered`) so the periods map to those filters; and `_recomputeFilterOptions()` scopes the **Trade + Works + Vendor** option lists to the selected period range (`periodOk`/`inRange`) so picking a range narrows them too (Trade value is preserved even if out-of-range so the main list isn't silently cleared; Works/Vendor invalid selections are dropped). `onWpMonAnalysisFilterChange` → `_recomputeFilterOptions()` then `filterWPTable()` (which re-renders + remaps From/To). `setWpMonPeriodMode` resets the range to "all" (keys change format) and re-renders. Scope applies ONLY to the analysis panel, not the main WP list below.
  - **Two-level grouping (Project â†’ Trade)**: rows group by `project_id` first (ordered by the `permitted` list), then by trade (ordered by `TRADE_ORDER`) within each project. The virtual `items[]` interleaves `projheader` (greater, red `#f0f0f0`/`#EE3124` header â€” `toggleProjectGroup(pid)`, state in `_projCollapseState`) â†’ `header` (trade subheader, indented, `toggleTradeGroup(key)` where **key = `${project_id}||${trade}`** so the same trade collapses independently per project, state in `_collapseState`) â†’ `row`. `collapseAllTrades`/`expandAllTrades` operate on the composite keys; expand-all also clears `_projCollapseState`. Mobile cards get a `.wpc-projgroup` header (dark-mode rule in dashboard.css "Mobile WP List cards"). The per-project dashboard (`project.html`) stays single-level trade grouping (one project only).
  - **WP No. display normalization**: `_normWpNo(v)` (index.html) / `normWpNo(v)` (project.html) strip a leading **`WP-`/`WP ` prefix** AND a trailing `.0`/`.00` from purely-numeric WP numbers at data-load — the WP No. is the **digit only** (agreed format). The Excel importer (`import-monitoring.html`) now stores just the number (was `'WP-<n>'`); existing rows are cleaned by `migrations/2026-07-09_update_wpno_strip_prefix.sql`; the CSV-import de-dup `_wpKey` also strips the prefix so legacy `WP-5` and new `5` match (e.g. SLN101 stores `"2.0"` â†’ shown as `"2"`); real decimals like `2.1` are preserved. Applied in the `.map()` right after `normTrade` on every load/reload path. The DB still stores the original; `UPDATE_wpno_normalize_SLN101.sql` (one-off) normalizes the stored values, and the award-date UPDATE scripts match `wp_no` via `regexp_replace(wp_no,'\.0+$','')` so they work regardless.

**Mobile WP List (both pages)**: On â‰¤767px the `.wp-table-wrap` is hidden and `.wp-card-list` shown instead. Cards render from the same paged `items[]` â€” trade group header + one card per WP. Each card: WP No (red, tappable â†’ detail panel) + status badge, description, trade â€º works, budget/awarded/variance (non-viewer), target award + delivery dates, vendors, View Details + Edit. CSS classes: `.wp-card-list`, `.wp-card`, `.wp-card-*`, `.wp-table-wrap` â€” defined in each page's inline `<style>` block.

**Collapse pattern**: items[] built from trade groups (header always + rows only if expanded). `toggleTradeGroup` / `collapseAllTrades` / `expandAllTrades`. Works tab uses DOM-only toggle (`toggleWkTrade`, `toggleWkBudget`). Schedule tab uses `toggleSchRow`.

**Project filter**: `_activeIds` Set; `toggleProjectPill` calls `renderAll()` immediately. Empty Set = "No projects selected" state (red label, empty charts).

**READ-ONLY badge**: never add `display:inline-flex` as inline style â€” media query sets `display:none` and inline overrides it.

---

## Per-Project Dashboard (project.html)

Tabs: Overview → Backlog → WP List (**Dashboard tab removed** — its period chart + Top 5 panels moved into Overview)

**Header project switcher:** the topbar page title (`#page-title`) is a clickable searchable dropdown (`toggleHdrSwitcher`/`renderHdrSwitcher`, popover `#hdr-switcher` + search `#hdr-sw-search` + list `#hdr-sw-list`) that filters the user's permitted projects by id/name and links to `project.html?id=`. Reuses `window._sidebarProjects` (permitted, non-archived, non-DEMO). The title text lives in `#page-title-text` (a span) so the runtime `.textContent` update doesn't wipe the caret `#hdr-sw-caret`; the caret + click affordance are hidden when the user has ≤1 project. Mirrors the sidebar Projects search. **Styled as a visible pill button, not plain text (2026-08-03)** â€” the switcher used to be inert-looking heading text with a tiny low-opacity chevron, so users didn't realize it opened a project picker. `.proj-switcher`/`.proj-switcher-caret` (dashboard.css) give it a bordered `--surface-2` pill background and a circular caret badge that highlights red on hover/focus; markup also gained `role="button" tabindex="0"` + Enter/Space handling for keyboard access.

**Popover row redesign (2026-08-03, same day):** the dropdown list itself was a plain single-line-ellipsis row per project (generic building icon + `"ID — Name"` string truncated to one line), so on a full project name the browser's native `title` tooltip popped up on hover and visually overlapped the highlighted (active) row — read as a rendering glitch. Redesigned via new `dashboard.css` classes (`.proj-switcher-pop`, `.proj-switcher-pop-search`, `.proj-switcher-pop-list`, `.proj-switcher-item`, `.psi-pid`, `.psi-name`, `.psi-check`, `.proj-switcher-empty`) replacing the old giant inline-style blob: each row is now a small red **ID pill** (`.psi-pid`, inverts to white-bg/red-text on the active row) + the project name in a `-webkit-line-clamp:2` block (wraps instead of truncating to one line, so the native `title` tooltip is no longer needed and was dropped) + a trailing check icon (`.psi-check`) on the active row. Popover widened 300px→340px. Hover uses `--mw-red-light` (same tint used for row-hover elsewhere in the app, e.g. the Excel grid). `renderHdrSwitcher()` now escapes `p.id`/`p.name` through `esc()` before interpolating into `innerHTML` (the old code only escaped the `title` attribute, not the visible text — a real gap versus the app's Known Issues #23 "escape every `${...}` sink" rule; `renderSidebarProjects()`'s identical sidebar-list interpolation was fixed the same way). **Don't reuse `.nav-item` for this popover** — that class is tuned for the dark sidebar (near-white text/hover) and only looked right here because the row markup was overriding it with an inline `color:inherit`; the new dedicated classes are correct for a light popover surface in both themes via the standard `--surface`/`--border`/`--text-primary` var set.

**`--line` was a phantom CSS variable — never fix by reusing it (found 2026-08-03):** the original `.proj-switcher` pill (added earlier the same day) referenced `border: 1.5px solid var(--line)`, but `--line` is never defined anywhere in `dashboard.css` — only `--border` (`rgba(0,0,0,.08)`/dark `rgba(255,255,255,.09)`) and `--border-md` (`.14`/`.16`) exist. An undefined custom property with no fallback makes the property invalid at computed-value time, so `border-color` silently resolved to `currentcolor` instead of a visible gray — the pill's border was effectively invisible/wrong-tinted in both themes. Fixed by switching to `var(--border-md)` (and `var(--border)` for the popover's lighter internal dividers). **When adding a border/divider, use `--border`/`--border-md` — `--line` doesn't exist**, despite reading like a plausible token name.

- **Charging Scope switcher** (`#ov-charging-switch`): a 3-way segmented toggle **Combined / Main Contract / Change Order** (`setOvCharging('all'|'main'|'co')`, state `_ovCharging`, filter `ovWPs()`, `_isCO(w)` = `/change\s*order/i` on `charging_type`). A **blank `charging_type` counts as Main Contract** (the Excel importer defaults every WP to Main Contract). `_syncOvCharging()` replaces the old `buildMetrics(allWPs)` call sites (initial load + `__wpmReloadFn`): it re-applies the current scope and, via `_syncOvChargingVisibility()`, **hides the whole switcher when the project has no Change Orders** (resetting to Combined). Button CSS `.ov-ch-btn`/`.ov-ch-btn.active` in the page's inline `<style>`. A count hint (`#ov-ch-count`) shows `N WPs of M` when scoped.
  - **Pinned to the right end of the view-tabs row (2026-08-07), not its own row inside the Overview tab.** `#ov-charging-switch` now lives inside a new `#view-tabs-row` flex wrapper alongside `#view-tabs` itself (`view-tabs` gets `flex:1;min-width:0`, the switch gets `flex-shrink:0`) — both sit at the top level of `#main-content`, ahead of the per-view `#view-overview`/`#view-backlog`/`#view-table` containers, so it reads as one seamless row with the Overview/Backlog/WP List tabs instead of a second row underneath them. Because the switch now lives OUTSIDE the per-view containers that `switchView()` show/hides wholesale, its **visibility can no longer piggyback on that show/hide loop** — it's driven by **`_syncOvChargingVisibility()`** (`display = _ovHasCO && (onScopedTab || window.__isViewer) ? 'flex' : 'none'`, where `onScopedTab` = Overview/Backlog/WP List), called from both `switchView()` (on every tab change) and `_syncOvCharging()` (on data load/reload, which also recomputes `_ovHasCO`). The `window.__isViewer` clause exists because viewers get the single-page stacked layout (`_viewerSinglePage()` pins `currentView='table'` permanently to keep the WP-List filter handlers live) where every section is always on-screen — without that clause the switch would never show for viewers even when the project has COs.
  - **Mobile (≤767px):** as a wrapped flex item with no width constraint of its own, `#ov-charging-switch` was sizing to its ~447px max-content width and overflowing the 375px viewport (the page grew a horizontal scrollbar) — `#view-tabs-row #ov-charging-switch{flex-basis:100%;width:100%}` in `dashboard.css` forces it to the row's own width so its internal `flex-wrap` actually engages. **The "Charging Scope" label (`.ov-ch-label` class on that span) and the `#ov-ch-count` "N WPs of M" text are hidden on mobile** (`display:none` in the same `≤767px` block), per user request, so only the 3 buttons show.
  - **Now scopes ALL THREE tabs — Overview, Backlog, and WP List (2026-08-07, was Overview-only), Status Tracker is unaffected** (it edits individual WPs by id regardless of scope). `ovWPs()` is now the upstream base for: `renderBacklog()`'s not-awarded set (and its `c-backlog-submittal` donut), `_blRenderPeriod()`'s `countpct`/`budgetpct` denominators (now "% of the current scope" rather than "% of the whole project"), and the WP List's `getFiltered()`/`getFilteredSorted()`/`_pwaFilteredWPs()` trio (**must stay in sync — same convention as the existing `#f-charging` filter documented below**) plus `_recomputeFilterOptions()` (the Works/Vendor cascade). **The WP List's own local "Charging Type" filter (`#f-charging`) is UNCHANGED and stacks on top of this page-wide scope** (same AND-combination as Trade/Works/Vendor) — it is not replaced by the shared switcher, so setting the switch to "Main Contract" and the local filter to "Change Order" correctly yields zero rows rather than one silently overriding the other.
  - **`setOvCharging()` re-renders the currently active tab, not every tab eagerly.** Backlog is lazily rendered and caches its last render across tab switches (`_rendered.backlog`) — the switch handler resets that flag (same pattern as `setBcbBaseline`) so Backlog recomputes next time it's opened, and additionally calls `renderBacklog()`/`buildTable()`+`_recomputeFilterOptions()`+`renderPwaAnalysis()` immediately **only when `currentView` is already `'backlog'`/`'table'`** — both guarded so the very first `_syncOvCharging()` call at data load (before `switchView()` has ever run, `currentView` still defaults to `'overview'`) is a no-op rather than calling into WP-List/Backlog render functions before their surrounding page state (filter dropdown population, `_msfSel`, etc.) is ready.
- **Overview**: Left/right split. Left: Cost Overview 6 KPIs (BCB, Actual Award, Cost to Complete, Estimate at Completion, Variance, %Variance) via `buildMetrics(wps)`. Right: two WP Status cards â€” battery chart card (`#ov-battery-charts`, renders via `renderOvBattery(wps)` â€” two vertical CSS battery bars for % by WP and % by Value, segments: green=Awarded, red=Due Not Awarded, gray=Not Due Yet; no toggle, no Canvas) + stats table card (`#metrics-wp`). CSS: `.ov-top-row`, `.ov-cost-grid` (2-col), `.ov-wp-split`, `.ov-donut-card`, `.ov-stats-card`. Below the top row: **Procurement S-Curve** (`#c-ov-scurve`, `Charts.sCurve(id, wps)`, project view only) â€” cumulative WP count by month, Planned line from `awarding_date` (Planned Award) vs Actual line from `actual_awarding_date` of `award_status==='Awarded'` WPs; the actual line stops at the current month (no future projection). Mirrors the Excel "S-Curve" sheet (Cumulative Planned WP vs Cumulative Actual WP) and updates automatically as WPs are awarded. Rendered from `buildMetrics()`. **Date parsing (`sCurve` in charts.js) must be format-agnostic**: the internal `pd()` helper extracts the `YYYY-MM-DD` prefix via regex from ANY value (date-only string, full ISO timestamp `â€¦T00:00:00+00:00` / `â€¦Z`, space-separated timestamp, or a `Date` object) and builds a **local-midnight** Date. This fixes two bugs at once â€” (1) `new Date('2026-06-30')` is UTC midnight = June 29 evening in UTC+8, which pushed month-end comparisons into the wrong bucket; (2) a naive `split('-')` parser returned `null` for timestamp-typed columns (the live DB predates the current schema and may return timestamps), which made `valid` empty and hid the curve for EVERY project behind the `.scurve-nodata` placeholder. When there are genuinely no parseable award dates, a `.scurve-nodata` message is shown and the canvas hidden. **Call-site guard gotcha (was the real "no S-curve on any project" bug):** `buildMetrics()` in `project.html` must guard the call with `if (typeof Charts !== 'undefined' && Charts.sCurve)` â€” NOT `if (window.Charts && â€¦)`. `Charts` is a top-level `const` in charts.js, which does **not** create a `window.Charts` property, so the old `window.Charts` guard was always falsy and the S-curve was never even called (blank panel, no chart, no placeholder). **Actual-award-date fallback:** both the S-curve "Actual" line and the period charts' "Awarded"/"Cumulative Awarded" series fall back to the **planned `awarding_date`** when `actual_awarding_date` is null â€” the WP-Monitoring imports populate `award_status` + `total_awarded` (award flag from Remarks col P) but never an actual award *date*, so without the fallback every awarded series was flat at â‚±0 / had zero points. Awarded series filter on `total_awarded>0`; the month/quarter key set includes awarded WPs' fallback dates so their buckets exist. **No future awards (clamp-to-today):** an awarded WP can never be plotted in the future â€” the effective award date is **clamped to today** (`_awDate`/`_actAwDate` in charts.js; inline `awd()` in `sCurve`; clamped `awDate` in index.html `renderWpMonPeriodTable`). So when the actual date is missing and the planned `awarding_date` is in the **future**, the award is pulled back to the current day (it lands in the current period instead of a future month). Budget (BCB) bars still use the raw planned `awarding_date`, so future **budget** is shown but future **awarded** never is. Applies to `budgetAwardedByPeriod(Monthly)`, `wpCountByPeriod(Monthly)`, `wpByPeriodQuarterly`, `sCurve`, and the WP-List period table. **Cumulative Awarded line stops at today:** in `budgetAwardedByPeriod(Monthly)` the `Cumulative Awarded` series is nulled for periods after the current month/quarter (`cumADisp`, `spanGaps:false`) so the red cumulative line **ends at today** rather than running flat across the future budget months to the last planned quarter (the awarded bars are already 0 there, but the line previously extended to e.g. Q3 2028). Cumulative Budget still extends (future planned budget is legitimate).
- **Dashboard**: Period chart + **WP Status split** (donut card `c-dash-status` with `setDashStatusMode('wp'|'val')` toggle + stats table `#dash-status-table`) + WP by Trade + **Top 5 panels (now ABOVE the backlog)** + **Work Package Backlog â€” Not Awarded** table. `renderDashBacklog()` now also applies a **Trade filter** (`#dash-bl-trade`, populated once from the not-awarded set) and **Min/Max â‚±M budget filter** (`#dash-bl-bmin`/`#dash-bl-bmax`) in addition to the existing sort dropdown (single project â†’ no project grouping)
- **Backlog**: Filter bar (Trade, Sort, Search, Budget min/max) + backlog table + aging/status/period/submittal charts. `renderBacklog()` applies filters. Collapsible trade groups.
- **WP List**: filter bar = Status / **OSM (`f-osm`: All/Yes/No)** / Trade / **Works (multi)** / **Vendor (multi)** / Search. (**Zone filter removed** — to match the Portfolio WP List, which has none. The `f-zone` `<select>` was earlier dropped from the markup but JS references lingered; `document.getElementById('f-zone').innerHTML=…` in the data-load path then threw a TypeError that **aborted the rest of `loadData` → project views appeared to not load / load slowly**. All `f-zone` reads removed from `getFilteredSorted`, `_pwaFilteredWPs`, the filter-populate block, and the change-listener array.) Works + Vendor are the same `.msf` checkbox-dropdown multiselects as the portfolio (CSS now in `dashboard.css`, shared), cascading **Trade â†’ Works â†’ Vendor** via `_recomputeFilterOptions()` (state `_msfSel={works:Set,vendor:Set}`; `applyWpListFilters()` re-renders table + Filtered Analysis). Vendor options from `_projVendors(w)` (`contractor||proposed_vendors` split on `/[\r\n;|]+/` â€” newline/semicolon/pipe only, never comma/space). Applied in `getFiltered`, `getFilteredSorted`, and `_pwaFilteredWPs` (empty Set = all). **Source data must delimit multiple vendors with a newline (Alt+Enter) or `;`** â€” AVR101's legacy data is space-joined with no delimiter (can't be auto-split reliably), so re-import with a delimiter is required to get per-vendor options. 5 view tabs (Overview/Award/Schedule/Submittals/All). `WP_TABLE_VIEWS` + `getActiveCols()`. `setWPTableView(view)`. `buildTable()` renders colgroup + thead + tbody via `innerHTML` exactly like `renderWPMonTable()` in `index.html` â€” uses `_stickyLeft` dict (never mutates col objects), `renderCell` switch, `white-space:normal` on `<th>`. Trade group header splits into sticky + non-sticky cells. Also populates `#proj-wp-cards` (mobile card view â€” same paged items, CSS toggles visibility). `openWPDetail(w)` slide-in panel.
  - **WP List — user-configurable frozen columns (2026-07-31):** mirrors `review.html`'s Excel-grid pin (see its entry above). Each header in `buildTable()`'s thead carries a hover-reveal **pin** (`.wp-pin`, Tabler `ti-pin`/`ti-pinned-filled`) — click it to freeze columns up to and including that one (contiguous from the left, matching Known Issue #12); click the current boundary again to reset to the view's default (Overview: cost_code+wp_no+works; other views: cost_code+wp_no+description). State is **`_wpFreezeKey`** (the KEY of the last frozen column, `null`=default), applied as an override pass at the end of `getActiveCols()` (`cols.map((c,idx)=>({...c, sticky: idx<=i}))`) — falls back to the view's default sticky set if the pinned key isn't present in the currently active view's columns (e.g. pinned in Overview then switched to Schedule). Persisted per user in `localStorage['wpm_wplist_freeze_<uid>']` (`_wpLoadFreeze`/`_wpSaveFreeze`, loaded in `requireLogin` right after `userProfile` is set). `window.wpSetFreeze(key)` toggles + calls `buildTable()`. Scoped to `project.html` only — `index.html`'s WP List (`renderWPMonTable`) still uses its hardcoded sticky set; ask if you want it added there too.
    - **Header/body desync fix (2026-07-31, same day):** the first cut of this feature hardcoded sticky positioning inline into only 4 of ~35 `_wpRenderCell` case branches (`cost_code`/`wp_no`/`works`/`description` — the only columns the OLD fixed sticky set ever used). Freezing any OTHER column marked it `.sticky=true` in `getActiveCols()`'s override (correctly, for the thead + `_stickyLeft` offset math) but that column's body `<td>` still fell through to a case with no sticky styling at all — the header floated in place while the body cell scrolled normally underneath, which is what read as "table contents are clashing". Fixed by making `_wpRenderCell` apply stickiness **generically** via a `td(inner, extraStyle, extraAttrs)` helper wrapping every case (same principle as `review.html`'s per-index `fz` check — sticky-ness is a property of the COLUMN POSITION, never opted into per case), and moving `position`/`background`/`z-index` out of inline styles into a **`.wp-freeze`** CSS class (`background:var(--surface)`/`var(--surface-2)`, dark-mode-safe by construction — the class replaces the old hardcoded `background:#fff` inline, one instance of the "reserialised inline style" dark-mode trap called out elsewhere in Known Issues). Only the per-column `left:Xpx` offset stays inline (genuinely per-render data). The thead `<th>` sticky styling was updated the same way. **When adding a new WP-List column, don't hand-roll sticky support for it — it's automatic now; just make sure any case you write returns via the `td(...)` helper, not a raw `` `<td>...</td>` `` string.**
    - **Freeze semantics settled on "pin = the ONE frozen column" (2026-07-31, iteration 2 of 3):** the first cut copied `review.html`'s literal "freeze everything up to index i", which blanket-froze most of the table for a distant pin (that grid rarely shows more than 1-2 columns ahead of a pin, since most WP-form-section groups start collapsed — the WP List has no such collapsing). A second cut tried "default sticky columns + reorder the pin next to them," but explicit user feedback ruled that out too: pinning **Work Package Description** still left Cost Code No. and WP No. frozen, which wasn't wanted. That iteration made the pinned column the ONLY sticky column, moved to index 0.
    - **Extended to MULTIPLE simultaneous pins (2026-07-31, iteration 3, per user request):** the single-column model above couldn't do what the user actually wanted next — Description **and** Procurement Status frozen at the same time; pinning the second column just replaced the first. `_wpFreezeKey` (one key or `null`) became **`_wpFreezeKeys`** (a `Set`), persisted comma-joined in the same `localStorage['wpm_wplist_freeze_<uid>']` key. **`window.wpToggleFreeze(key)`** replaces `wpSetFreeze` — it adds/removes that one key from the set (doesn't replace the whole set), then `buildTable()`. `getActiveCols()`'s override now pulls out every column whose key is in `_wpFreezeKeys` and moves ALL of them to the front — **in their original relative column order, not click order** (so pinning Procurement Status then Description still renders Description first, since it comes first in `COLS`) — a sticky column still has to be part of the single leftmost contiguous run to render correctly (Known Issue #12), which is why scattered pins can't stay in their natural positions. Empty set ⇒ view's default sticky set (unchanged); non-empty set ⇒ ONLY the pinned columns are frozen, defaults dropped (same "no unrelated columns stay frozen" rule as iteration 2). **The pin's "on"/filled state now reflects an EXPLICIT entry in `_wpFreezeKeys`, not mere `c.sticky`** — a column that's sticky only because it's part of the default set shows the outline (unpinned) icon, so a default-sticky column doesn't look "already pinned" when it isn't yet a deliberate choice. Verified the multi-key reorder with a standalone simulation (pinning in either click order produces the same column order) before shipping.
  - **WP List restructure (2026-07-23):** the standalone **Filtered Analysis** panel and the separate **Status Tracker** tile were REMOVED from the project WP List — status is now edited **inline in the table's Overview view** (Procurement Status and Delivery Status are `_stSelect` dropdowns, Award Status is the derived `_stAwardBadge` refreshed in place via `_stRefreshAwardBadge`; `delivery_status` was added to `COLS` + the overview/all view keys). `updateWpStatus`→`_stSaveFields` preserves the `.wp-table-wrap` scroll position across the inline-edit re-render. `renderPwaAnalysis`/`renderStatusTracker` are now guarded no-ops (their panels are gone). The Filtered Analysis stays on the **Portfolio** WP List (`index.html`), but was **moved BELOW the table**. The `#st-award-modal` (cost+vendor prompt on →Awarded) is unaffected.
  - **Per-trade SUBTOTALS on the trade group headers (2026-08-17):** `buildTable()`'s virtual `items[]` builder now stamps `budgetSum` (Σ `approved_budget_bcb`) and `awardedSum` (Σ `window.effectiveAwardedCost(w)` — **NOT raw `total_awarded`**, per Known Issue #28, so a `not_to_be_awarded`/buyback WP contributes its effective cost) onto every `{type:'header'}` item. The desktop trade group row's right-hand cell now reads `N WPs · Budget ₱X · Awarded ₱Y`, and the mobile card view's `.wpc-trade-count` reads `N WPs · ₱X / ₱Y`. **Both are gated on `!window.__hideBudget`** (the cost-hiding flag, NOT `__isViewer` — so `viewer_budget` still sees them) and omitted entirely when both sums are 0. Sums are computed over the group's FILTERED rows (`getFilteredSorted()`'s output), so they follow the Status/Trade/Works/Vendor/Charging/Buyback filters and the Charging-Scope switcher. Portfolio (`index.html`) WP List was NOT changed — its grouping is two-level Project→Trade, so ask before mirroring this there.
    - **The subtotal cell is LEFT-aligned, and that is load-bearing (fixed 2026-08-17, same day).** The trade header row is two cells — a sticky one spanning the frozen columns, and `tdR` spanning **every remaining column**. `tdR` was `text-align:right` (from when it held only a short "N WPs"), which on this very wide, horizontally-scrolling table parks its content hundreds of pixels off the right edge — so neither the WP count nor the new subtotal was ever visible at the default scroll position (reported as "I don't see the subtotals"). Left-aligning puts it immediately after the frozen columns, always on screen. **Don't right-align anything in a full-width colspan cell of a horizontally-scrolling table.**
  - **`#wplist-edit-hint` wording clarified (2026-07-31):** was *"Set Procurement & Delivery status inline in the Overview columns — Award Status follows Procurement."* — "Overview columns" was ambiguous (it means the WP List's own **Overview** sub-tab, one of the 5 view tabs: Overview/Award/Schedule/Submittals/All — NOT the Overview dashboard tab), and "Award Status follows Procurement" didn't say *which* Procurement value triggers it. Now: *"Switch to the **Overview** view below to set Procurement & Delivery status inline — Award Status updates automatically once Procurement Status is set to Awarded."* The hint (`project.html` line ~400) sits ABOVE `#proj-view-tabs`, so "view below" is positionally accurate.

**Work Package Status Tracker** (`#status-tracker`, between `#pwa-analysis` and the WP table): a scrollable inline-edit table letting user/manager/specialist/admin update **Award Status / Procurement Status / Delivery Status** per WP without opening the form. `renderStatusTracker()` builds it from `getFilteredSorted()` (respects the WP-List filters, trade-grouped). Each cell is a `<select>` (`_stSelect`/`_stOptsHtml`; option sets `ST_AWARD_OPTS`/`ST_PROC_OPTS`/`ST_DELIV_OPTS`; a stored value outside the set is prepended so legacy data shows pre-migration). `updateWpStatus(id,field,value,sel)` **respects the approval model** (`_stAutoApprove()` = `AppAuth.isAutoApprove({role})`): auto-approve roles (admin/super_admin/specialist/manager) → `WPDb.updateWPDirect` (live, keeps approved status, toast "Saved"); non-auto-approve `user` → `WPDb.updateWP` (sets `review_status='pending_review'`, toast "Submitted for approval") so the change goes to the manager/WP approver — same as editing via the form. Either way it updates the local `allWPs` object, busts the `wpm_wps_idx*` cache, then re-renders `renderPwaAnalysis()`+`buildTable()` (NOT the tracker, to avoid scroll jump). The header hint text (`#status-tracker-hint`) switches to reflect whether edits are live or sent for approval. Wired into `switchView('table')` and `applyWpListFilters()`. **Hidden for viewers** (display:none in the `__isViewer` block). **Award column is a READ-ONLY badge** (`_stAwardBadge`, id `st-aw-<id>`) — Award Status is derived from Procurement Status, not edited directly. Changing **Procurement Status** drives it: →`Awarded` sets award=Awarded and, if the WP lacks a cost/vendor, opens the `#st-award-modal` popup (`_stOpenAwardModal`/`stAwardConfirm`/`stAwardCancel`) to collect **Awarded Cost (Net)** + **Awarded Vendor** and commits `{procurement_status, award_status, awarded_cost, contractor}` together via `_stSaveFields`; any earlier stage sets award=Not Yet Awarded. `_stRefreshAwardBadge(id)` updates the badge after save (tracker isn't fully re-rendered, to avoid scroll jump); Cancel reverts the Procurement dropdown. **"Partially Awarded" was removed everywhere** (tracker `ST_AWARD_OPTS=['Not Yet Awarded','Awarded']`, `awardDonut`, `statusByZone`, CSV import `pAward` maps "partial"→Not Yet Awarded; existing rows migrated by `migrations/2026-07-02_update_remove_partially_awarded.sql` — **ALL** Partially Awarded → Not Yet Awarded with the partial `awarded_cost` **cleared** so it can't be read as fully awarded or inflate savings). **Final straggler cleanup (2026-07-21):** the last inert `award_status==='Partially Awarded'` color-branches (detail-panel `sc`, WP-list cell colors, `_sc`/`_bc` status-donut helpers, the `admin.html` status color, the `project.html` `{...}` color map) were collapsed to the 2-state `Awarded ? green : red`, and the dead `partial` count was dropped from `computeStats()` (it was returned but never read). `pAward()` in `db.js` (maps legacy "partial" text → Not Yet Awarded on import) and the unrelated **Claims** `Partially Approved` status are intentionally retained.
  - **Filtered Analysis â€” Budget & Awarded by Period** (`#pwa-analysis`, ported from the index.html WP-List panel): sits above the WP table, scoped to the project's `f-status`/`f-trade`/`f-zone`/`f-search`-filtered set (`_pwaFilteredWPs()`). Same controls as the portfolio version â€” **From/To month-to-month range** (`pwa-from`/`pwa-to`, state `_pwaFrom`/`_pwaTo`, `_pwaPeriodMatch`), Monthly/Quarterly (`setPwaPeriodMode`), Chart/Table (`setPwaViewMode`) â€” plus the 4 KPI cards (Work Packages / Total Budget / Total Awarded / Total to be Awarded) and the period chart (`c-pwa-period`, `{tidyLabels:true}`) or `renderPwaPeriodTable`. From/To options come from the filtered set (`_populatePwaFilters`). `renderPwaAnalysis()` is called from `switchView('table')` and the WP-List filter/search listeners (NOT pagination). **Hidden for viewers** (`#pwa-analysis` display:none in the `__isViewer` block). All ids/functions use a `pwa`/`_pwa` prefix to avoid collision with the per-project chart code.
- Claims tab exists in HTML but hidden (`display:none`)

`_rendered = { overview, dashboard, backlog, table }` â€” reset on filter change or data reload.

---

## WP Form (wp-form.html)

### Sticky Section Navigator
The form is a single scroll (NOT tabs — tabs would hide fields from `save()`'s cross-section validation + `showErr` "scroll-to-offending-field"). A **sticky section navigator** (`#form-nav`, `.form-layout` flex wrapper around the nav + `.panel`) gives fast jumping without hiding anything. Each of the 8 `.form-section`s has an id (`sec-identity`/`sec-approval`/`sec-bonds`/`sec-submittals`/`sec-schedule`/`sec-budget`/`sec-payment`/`sec-status`) with `scroll-margin-top` for the sticky-topbar offset. Desktop: a 210px left rail sticky at `top:72px`. Mobile (≤900px): a horizontal sticky scroll-spy strip under the topbar (`top:52px`). A vanilla IntersectionObserver (`rootMargin:'-80px 0px -55% 0px'`) highlights the section nearest the top; clicking a link smooth-scrolls (a 700ms `clickLock` suppresses spy flicker mid-animation). All inline in wp-form.html — no new deps. When you ADD or REORDER a form section, add its `id` + a matching `#form-nav` link.

### Sections & Key Fields
1. **Identity & Classification**: Cost Code, Trade â†’ Works â†’ Type (cascade), WP No., Description, Scope, Project, Zone, **OSM (Yes/No, `f-osm` → `osm` column, filterable in the WP List)**, Detailed Description, Type of Service, Type of Procurement, Type of Contract, Proposed Vendors, No. of PO/JO, PO/JO Numbers
2. **Approval Matrix**: Responsible (multi-select), Approver (single select, admin/super_admin only), Support (multi-select). All from `WPDb.getAllUsers()`. Uses `u.name` for display. Values stored comma-separated in `responsible_team` / `approver` / `support_team`.
3. **Insurance Bonds**: Surety Bond, Performance Bond, Warranty Bond
4. **Material/Subcon Submittals**: Requires Approval, Type of Submittal, Name of Approver, Date of Approval. **When Requires Approval = "No" the other three fields are Not Applicable** — `onRequiresApprovalChange()` (wired to the `f-requires-approval` `onchange`) sets Type of Submittal + Name of Approver to **`N/A`** and disables them, and clears + disables Date of Approval (a date can't hold "N/A"). Switching to "Yes" re-enables all three and clears the "N/A" placeholders. Called on fresh load (new WP defaults to "No" → fields start N/A) and after edit-fill (`fill('f-requires-approval', …)` then `onRequiresApprovalChange()`) so stored values sync to the flag. Disabled fields still return their `.value` via `getVal`, so save persists `submittal_document_type='N/A'` / `approver_name='N/A'` / `approval_date=null`.
5. **Procurement Schedule**: Lead Time (â†’ `lead_time`), Awarding Date, Actual Awarding Date, Target Delivery, Actual Delivery, Target Installation, Target Completion
6. **Budget & Contract**: **Budget (BCB) (Net)** (label; â†’ `approved_budget_bcb`), **Awarded Cost (Net)** (label; â†’ `awarded_cost`), Award Status, **Awarded Vendor** (label; â†’ `contractor`), **No-Cost Award** (checkbox → `free_of_charge` — see Known Issues #28c: a real award at ₱0 because the vendor provides the scope free; zeroes + locks Awarded Cost but keeps Awarded Vendor REQUIRED), **Not to be Awarded** (checkbox â†’ `not_to_be_awarded` â€” see Known Issues #28: waives the Awarded Cost/Vendor requirement and tells the dashboard to book this WP's full budget as savings at an effective â‚±0 cost, for a WP Ops has permanently pulled from the award pipeline), **Buyback** (checkbox â†’ `buyback`) + **Depreciation %** (required when ticked â†’ `buyback_depreciation_percent`, stored as a decimal; the recovered **Buyback Value** is shown live and counts towards savings; the buyback can be entered as a **Depreciation %** OR as an **exact amount** via the `Buyback entered as` picker — see Known Issues #28b)` is shown live and counts towards savings â€” see Known Issues #28b)
7. **Payment Terms**: Terms (Days) (**free numeric input** â€” any value, not a fixed dropdown), Down Payment % (free numeric input â€” user enters percentage e.g. 20, stored as 0.20), DP Terms, DP Amount, Date of DP Release, Payment Notes, **Retention %** (**free numeric input** â€” user enters percentage e.g. 7, stored as 0.07; edit mode multiplies by 100; was a fixed 5/10/15% dropdown), Retention Amount, Retention Period
8. **Procurement Status**: Procurement Status, Submittal Status, Delivery Status, Remarks, Charging (required: Main Contract â†’ Contract Package No. | Change Order â†’ CO Description)

### WP No. Auto-Numbering
Both add-WP surfaces pre-fill the next WP No. from the project's existing WPs — **always editable**.
- **`wp-form.html`** (new WP only, never in edit mode): `suggestWpNo()` fetches the target project's WPs via `WPDb.getAllWPs(pid)` (cached per project in `_wpNoCache`) and sets `#f-wp-no` to `floor(max whole number) + 1` (so `2.1` → next is `3`; empty project → `1`). Wired to load **and** to the `#f-project` `change` event, so switching project re-suggests. `_wpNoTouched` (set by `onWpNoInput`) locks the field once the user types their own value; **clearing the field re-enables** auto-suggest. A hint under the input (`#wp-no-hint`) says it's auto-numbered and editable.
