- **`project.html` quick-add modal**: `_wqmSuggestWpNo()` computes the next number from the already-loaded `allWPs` (no extra query) on modal open and again after each save so **"Add Another" keeps counting up** — `_wqmExtra[]` holds numbers saved during the session since the `__wpmReloadFn` refresh is async.
- **Duplicate guard (both paths):** the DB has a `(project_id, wp_no)` unique constraint, so each save first checks the typed number against the project's existing WP Nos. (normalized: strip `WP-` prefix + trailing `.0`, lowercase) and shows a plain "WP No. X already exists in <PID>" error instead of a raw Postgres constraint failure. `wp-form.html` skips this in edit mode.

### Trade â†’ Works â†’ Type Cascade
**Trade dropdowns list exactly the 10 canonical trades** (see Known Issues #7) — the WP-form `#f-trade` and the project.html quick-add `#wqm-trade` were updated so **Electrical Works + Auxiliary Works are a single `Electrical and Auxiliary Works` option** (their `TRADE_WORKS` works-lists were merged, duplicates removed) and the quick-add's stale list (Civil Works / MEPF & Allied Services / General Requirement / Site Development) was replaced with the canonical 10. `TRADE_WORKS` object maps Trade â†’ array of `[works, type]`. `onTradeChange()` repopulates Works; `onWorksChange()` sets Type. The Type dropdown (`_setTypeOptions`) offers **`Materials` / `Labor`** for material works (the combined **"Materials & Labor" option was removed** per user request) and **`Service`** for service works; no value is auto-selected for material works. Legacy stored values (incl. old "Materials & Labor" records) are preserved via the `unshift` fallback.

### Approval Matrix Multi-Select
`toggleMs(field)`, `msClearAll(field)`, `getMsValues(field)`, `_setMsFromText(field, text)`, `_msPillRemove(field, uid)`. Mobile: dropdown uses `position:fixed` full-width.

### Budget Input Formatting
`f-budget` uses `type="text" inputmode="numeric"` with comma formatting on blur, stripped on focus. All reads use `.replace(/,/g,'')` before `parseFloat`.

### Unsaved Changes Guard
`formDirty` flag. `markDirty()` on input/change events. `markClean()` called on successful save. `beforeunload` fires only if `formDirty`.

### Local Draft Auto-Save (2026-08-06, `wp-form.html` + `review.html` Grid)
**Local recovery only — nothing is written to the database until the user clicks Save/Save changes.** This periodically snapshots in-progress, unsaved edits to `localStorage` so a crashed tab, accidental back-navigation, or browser close doesn't lose typing. It does NOT bypass any existing validation (required fields, Awarded-cost/vendor safeguard, PO/JO consistency, duplicate WP No. guard) — those still only run at actual Save time.
- **`wp-form.html`:** `markDirty()` (already fired on every input/change) also calls `scheduleAutosave()`, a 1200ms debounce that snapshots every `[id^="f-"]` field inside `.panel` (value or `.checked`) plus the Approval-Matrix multi-select state (`_msState.responsible`/`.support`, stored as user-id arrays) into `localStorage['wpm_wpdraft_<uid>_<edit_<id>|new_<projId>>']`. A `_formLoaded` flag (set true only after the initial fill/defaults finish, with an explicit `markClean()` right before it) suppresses autosave from firing during the programmatic load itself — several `on*Change()` handlers call `markDirty()` as part of syncing dependent UI even when nothing was actually typed. On load, `_maybeOfferDraft()` shows a dismissible banner ("Restore draft from **HH:MM**?") above the form if a draft exists for that key; **Restore** (`restoreDraft()`) replays the values back in cascade order (Trade → `onTradeChange()` → Works → `onWorksChange()` → Type, so the dependent dropdowns are populated before their value is set) then re-runs the dependent-UI functions (`onRequiresApprovalChange`/`onNotToBeAwardedChange`/`onProcStatusChange`/`onChargingChange`/`_updateBcbDeltas`/`updateVariance`); **Discard** just clears the key. The draft is cleared on successful save (`discardDraft()` in `submitForm()`) and on WP delete. A small `#autosave-indicator` next to Cancel shows "Draft saved HH:MM" after each snapshot.
- **`review.html` Grid (Excel) view:** the grid's existing in-memory staged state (`_gridEdits`, `_newRows` — see "Excel-like Grid View" below) is snapshotted the same way, via `_xlUpdateBar()` (already called after every cell edit / new row / undo) calling `_xlScheduleAutosave()` → `localStorage['wpm_griddraft_<uid>_<currentProjId|ALL>']`. **Race-condition guard:** a `_xlDraftChecked` flag stays `false` from page load until the user clicks Restore or Discard on the offered banner (or there was nothing to offer) — without it, the grid's own initial (empty) render would call `_xlUpdateBar()` → autosave → see no staged edits yet → silently `localStorage.removeItem()` the very draft the banner is about to offer, before the user could click Restore. `xlRestoreDraft()`/`xlDiscardDraftBanner()` set the flag true. `gridSave()` calls `_xlSaveDraftNow()` directly (not the debounced scheduler) right after the save loop so a successful save clears the draft immediately rather than waiting out the 1200ms debounce.
- **When adding a new field to either surface:** the WP-form autosave is fully generic (any `id^="f-"` element is picked up automatically — no per-field registration needed, except the Trade→Works→Type cascade order which is hardcoded in `restoreDraft()`). The grid autosave needs no per-column changes at all — it just mirrors whatever's in `_gridEdits`/`_newRows`.

### Save Validation
Required fields (in `save()`): WP No., Work Package Description, Project, Trade, Awarding Date, Target Delivery Date, Target Completion Date, Budget (BCB), Charging type. **Cost Code No. is NOT required** (label has no `*`, no validation check). **Award-status safeguards:** when Award Status = `Awarded`, both **Awarded Cost (Net)** (`f-contract`) and **Awarded Vendor** (`f-contractor`) are required. **PO/JO consistency:** `No. of PO/JO` (`f-po-jo-count`) must equal the number of entries in `PO/JO Numbers` (`f-po-jo-numbers`, split on `/[\s,;]+/`); mismatch aborts the save. **Actual Awarding Date cannot be in the future** — if `f-actual-awarding-date` parses to a date after today (local midnight), `save()` aborts with "Actual Awarding Date cannot be in the future." (a WP can't be awarded on a date that hasn't happened yet). The **CSV import** path enforces the same rules (future-date + the Awarded-cost/vendor + PO/JO safeguards): both `importWPsFromCSV()` (in `project.html` and `wp-form.html`) validate the whole batch up front and `throw` (naming the offending WP No(s)) before any DB write, so any violation aborts the entire import with no partial rows. The **quick-add modal** (`saveQuickWP()` in `project.html` — now has `wqm-contract` Awarded Cost + `wqm-contractor` Awarded Vendor inputs) enforces the Award-status safeguard too (it has no PO/JO fields, so that check is N/A there).

### Cost Overview KPIs â€” abbreviated headline figures
The 6 Cost Overview KPIs (`#metrics-cost`, `.ov-cost-grid`) show **`Fmt.moneyShort`** (auto-scales: `â‚±16.52B` / `â‚±2.40M` / full < 1M; sign dropped, callers prepend +/-) with the exact value in a `title` tooltip (`Fmt.moneyFull` â†’ `â‚±16,521,423,165`). This replaced the earlier "shrink-to-fit" approach for billion-scale portfolios. The `card()` helper in both `index.html` and `project.html` takes a 4th `full` arg for the title. `.ov-cost-grid .metric-value` stays `white-space:nowrap;overflow:hidden` and `fitMetricValues()` still runs as a harmless safety net. Scoped to `.ov-cost-grid` only.

### Forecast Diagnostic (`?diag=1`)
`project.html?id=<PID>&diag=1` runs `runForecastDiag()` after data load (non-viewers only): a dismissible panel (prepended to `#view-overview`) + `console.table` output that explains when the dashed Forecast line sits above the Cumulative Budget. The chart's red lines are **row-derived** (sum of per-WP `total_awarded` vs `approved_budget_bcb`), while the Cost-Overview "Savings" KPI is **Helping-Sheet-derived** (`HELPING_FIGURES`/`helpingMetrics`). The diag shows the row-based forecast end vs budget, the row-vs-Helping-Sheet reconciliation gap, and lists: WPs awarded over their own BCB, cost-bearing rows still flagged "Not Awarded" (`total_awarded>0` but `award_status!=='Awarded'`), and awarded rows with no BCB. Use it to find which WPs push the forecast over budget.

### Delete WP
A WP can be deleted from three places (non-viewers only): the **WP detail slide-in panel** on `index.html` and `project.html` (`deleteWPFromDetail(id, wpNo)` â€” Edit + Delete buttons in the panel footer), and **`wp-form.html` edit mode** (`#btn-delete` â†’ `deleteCurrentWP()`, shown only when `editId` is set). All confirm first, call `WPDb.deleteWP(id)`, bust the `wpm_wps_idx*` sessionStorage cache, then reload (index: `location.reload()`; project: `loadData()`; form: redirect to `project.html?id=`). Requires DELETE granted on `work_packages` (already present â€” `deleteProject` uses it).

---

## Excel Monitoring-Sheet Importer (`import-monitoring.html`)

A **self-contained** tool (open to any **approved** signed-in user — the admin-only gate was removed). **Contributors (specialist/manager/user) can now run a FULL Replace import on their assigned projects** — `migrations/2026-07-14_contributor_wp_delete.sql` widened the `wp_delete` RLS policy from super_admin-only to the SAME scope as insert/update (super_admin/admin anywhere; specialist/manager/user on `assigned` projects; viewer denied), so the delete-then-insert Replace succeeds for the projects they own (also lets them delete individual WPs on assigned projects via the detail panel). Admins/super_admins can Replace any project. The delete safety-net still aborts before insert if the delete removed nothing (no access to that project). The "open the Excel importer →" links in `wp-form.html` + the `project.html` CSV modal are labelled *"(imports to projects you're assigned to)"*. **Target-project picker is a searchable combobox** (`#proj-search` text input + `#proj-options` popover + hidden `#project` holding the id; `_projs` list, `renderProjOptions`/`selectProj`/`initProjCombo`) — filters by project id or name as you type; nothing is pre-selected so `parse()`/`doImport()` guard on an empty `#project` and prompt to pick one that reads a raw **Work-Package Monitoring `.xlsx` directly in the browser** (SheetJS via CDN) — no Python extractor, no CSV step. It **replaces** the old `gen_import.py` + generated `avr101-import.html` workflow (both git-ignored — `avr101-import.html` embeds real data and must never be committed). Loads supabase-js + SheetJS via CDN, reuses the existing localStorage session. Stores **digit-only `wp_no`** (no `WP-` prefix). **Delete-permission safety net:** after the "Replace" delete it re-counts the project's WPs and aborts before inserting if any remain (RLS restricts WP delete to `super_admin`, so a blocked delete can't silently create duplicates).

- **Header-name AUTO-DETECTION (no fixed column maps)** — the monitoring sheets differ per project (AVR, GRP, SLN, SLT, UTM, OPW each have different column positions, header rows, and even templates), so the parser locates every field by matching its **header text** instead of hardcoded indices. `detectLayout(rows)` finds the header row (the one containing `WORK PACKAGE NO.`), then `findCol(band, incl, excl)` scans a 4-row header band for each field via regex (e.g. budget = `/budget.*(bcb|vat ?ex|net)/`, awarded = `/awarded cost/`, awarding date = prefer `/^awarding date/` then `/awarding date/` excluding `/actual|baseline|revised/` — some sheets (e.g. **CCM302**) have a decoy **"BASELINE AWARDING DATE"** column (col Q, mostly blank) to the *left* of the real planned **"AWARDING DATE"** (col DR); since `findCol` returns the leftmost match, without the `baseline`/`revised` exclusion the parser picked the blank baseline column and every planned award date came in empty (fixed 2026-07), stage cols `^sourcing$`/`^rfq$`/…). This adapts to all current sheets **and future ones**. Replaced the earlier fixed `LAYOUTS` profiles. **Verified against all 5 real files** (GRP 80 / OPW 104 / SLN 87 / SLT 87 / UTM 84 WPs) — correct budgets, awarding dates, and trades.
  - **Trade is canonicalised at import** — `rowToWP` runs the tracked `curTrade` (after `mepfSplit`) through a local **`normTrade()`** (same map+keyword logic as the dashboard's) so the **stored + previewed** trade is one of the 10 official trades with proper capitalization (e.g. `Structural Materials and Consumables` / `Structural Steel` / `SITE WORKS` → `Structural Works` / `Site Works`; `Landscapes and Amenities` → `Architectural Works`). Previously the importer stored the raw group-header text and relied on the dashboard to normalize on load; now both agree. Keep this `normTrade()` in sync with the two in `index.html`/`project.html`.
  - **Hierarchical trade** (all these templates): the discipline is written on group-header rows (no WP No.) above the WPs it covers — `parse()` tracks `curTrade` when a non-WP row matches `TRADE_RE` (general requirement / site works / structural / architectural / mechanical / electrical / auxiliary / plumbing / fire protection / mepf / allied…), ignoring SUBTOTAL/GRANDTOTAL. **The trade header lives in a DIFFERENT column per template**, so `detectLayout` computes **`tradeCols` — the whole column window between WP No. and the WP-Description column** (`[wp_no+1 .. wp_name]`) and `parse()` scans ALL of them (first TRADE_RE match wins). Most sheets (AVR/GRP/SLN/SLT/UTM/CCM) put the trade in the **Description** column, but **QHL706 writes it in the "WORK PACKAGE NAME" column** (one left of Description) — with the old single-`descCol` scan its trades never updated and every WP fell back to the garbage `type_of_service` value (the reported bug, fixed 2026-07-23; QHL706 now parses 170 WPs with correct trades). Col 0 is **deliberately excluded** from the window because some sheets (e.g. CCM302) use it as a per-row helper that can carry a stray/wrong trade on a header row. The `NON-PO/JO` break scans the same `tradeCols`. `normTrade()` in the app canonicalises the result to the 10 trades. Any residual `MEPF WORKS` header is split into Mechanical / Electrical&Auxiliary / Plumbing / Fire by keyword on the description (`mepfSplit`). Falls back to the type-of-service column if no group header is found.
  - `rowToWP(r, pid, today, C, curTrade)` takes the detected column map `C` with a null-safe getter; award = `awarded_cost>0` (+ the future-date rule), reconciled with proc status.
- **Sheet + project pickers:** the workbook's sheet list (defaults to `/monitoring/i`) and a target-project dropdown (**any project**). A green **"Auto-detected layout"** readout shows the header row + key column numbers so the user can sanity-check before importing.
- **Award status comes from the sheet's own Remarks/Status column, NOT from the awarded cost** (`pAwardText()`, mirroring `pAward()` in `db.js` which the CSV path always used). Precedence: (1) actual award date in the **future** → Not Yet Awarded (advance-date rule); (2) actual award date in the past → Awarded; (3) the **Remarks flag** ("Awarded" / "Not yet Awarded"); (4) only then infer from `awarded_cost>0`. **This was a real over-count (fixed 2026-07-23):** the monitoring sheets carry a **provisional cost on work packages that are still "Not yet Awarded"**, and the old `awarded_cost>0` inference imported them as Awarded — OPW101 reported **70 awarded against the sheet's own 64** (6 rows, ₱19.3M of provisional cost, e.g. WP 19 Vertical Equipment Consumables ₱6.8M). Same pattern on **GRP101 (50→38)** and **SLN101 (43→32)**, matching Known Issues #17. The provisional cost is still imported, but the WP stays Not Yet Awarded so it can't inflate the awarded total or the savings. The preview KPI row shows a **"Provisional cost, not awarded"** tile with that count, and `proc` is reconciled back to `Evaluated` when award is not Awarded. Preview-only keys (`_future`, `_heldByFlag`) are stripped from every row before insert (the strip is now `_`-prefix-wide, not just `_future`).
- **"Awarded, no cost" preview tile:** a WP flagged Awarded in the sheet but with **no awarded cost** imports as `award_status='Awarded'`, yet the dashboard KPIs exclude it — they require `award_status==='Awarded' && total_awarded>0` so the awarded count/value/budget reconcile (Known Issues #17). That is why **OPW101 imports 64 awarded but the Overview reads 62**: WP 29 (Supply of Gravel Bedding) and WP 30 (Chemical Treatment and Soil Poisoning) carry `awarded_cost = 0`. The importer preview now shows an **"Awarded, no cost"** KPI tile and logs the offending WP Nos., so the gap is visible before importing. Fix is at source — enter the awarded cost (in the sheet or via the app) and the dashboard count matches.
- **Deploy-order safety for the BCB columns (2026-07-23):** `budget_bcb0..2` only exist after `migrations/2026-07-23_bcb_baselines.sql`. Until then Supabase rejects the whole insert with *"Could not find the 'budget_bcb0' column … in the schema cache"* — and because **Replace deletes the project's WPs first**, MSJ101 was left EMPTY (deleted 32, inserted 0). Three guards now: (1) `db.js` `_isMissingBcbCol`/`_stripBcb` retry every `submitWP`/`updateWP`/`updateWPDirect` without the columns, exactly like the audit-trail guard; (2) the importer runs **`probeColumns()` BEFORE the delete** — one throwaway row is inserted and immediately removed, and if it is rejected the import either drops the optional columns or **aborts with nothing changed**; (3) the per-batch insert still strips the columns and retries. A genuinely unknown column is NOT swallowed — it aborts. When the columns are absent the budget still imports via `approved_budget_bcb`, so only the per-baseline split is lost.
- **BCB baselines from the sheet (2026-07-23):** the importer records which baseline the **main budget column** represents — `detectLayout` reads a digit out of that column's own header (`/bcb ?\(?([0-2])\)?/`), so **QHL706's `BUDGET 1 - BCB1 (VAT EX)` maps to `budget_bcb1`** while every other file's `BUDGET (BCB) (NET)` / `BUDGET (VAT EX)` maps to `budget_bcb0`. **DANGER — the `BCB (0)` / `BCB (1)` columns in these sheets are QUANTITIES, not budgets:** they sit in the `AVE. QTY | UoM` block and their totals are nothing like the budget (CCM302 ₱2.6M vs ₱885.9M; LCR102 ₱175K vs ₱406.7M; SLT101 ₱21.5M vs ₱4.24B). A separate per-baseline money column is therefore accepted ONLY when its header is exactly `BCB (n)`, its header stack has no `qty|quantity|average|per tower|uom|%`, **and its median is ≥ 1% of the budget column's median**. Across all nine current files that guard correctly finds **no** extra money-BCB column — do not relax it. **Manual override (2026-07):** an **"Import budget into"** picker (`#bcb-level`: Auto-detect / BCB0 / BCB1 / BCB2) lets the user force the main budget column into a chosen baseline instead of the header-detected one. `parse()` reads it and sets `L.col.budget_level` (which drives `bcbLv(n)` → `budget_bcb{n}`) unless left on `auto`; changing it re-runs the preview so the choice shows before import.
- **Import Excel Sheet is a first-class action** — a green button in the `wp-form.html` bulk-import banner and a **Tools → Import Excel Sheet** item in the `project.html` / `wp-form.html` sidebars (it used to be reachable only from inside the CSV-import modal). Both carry the project through as `import-monitoring.html?project=<id>`, which the importer pre-selects in its combobox.
- **Merged-header rescue for the WP Description (LCR templates, fixed 2026-07-23):** LCR102/LCR352 use `WORK PACKAGE DESCRIPTION` as a **merged PARENT header at col 7**, whose real sub-columns sit further right (col 9 = the WP name, col 10 = the long description under a `DESCRIPTION` sub-header). `findCol` returned the parent, which is **empty on every data row**, so every WP imported with the `WP-<n>` fallback name (the user's screenshot: Description read "WP-1", "WP-2"…). `detectLayout` now measures the **fill rate of the detected name column over real data rows** and, when it is under 30%, adopts the best-filled text column in the span between WP No. and the next known field (Type of Service / Type of Procurement / Proposed Vendors / Cost Code), taking the next filled column as `detailed_description`. Verified: LCR352/LCR102 go from 0% → 88%/96% description fill (and gain detailed descriptions); CCM302/SLT101 etc. keep the column they already detected (97–98% fill), so no other template is affected. **Note the trade-scan window (`tradeCols`) widens with it** — still correct, since the group headers sit at col 7 inside the span.
- **LCR field audit (2026-07-23):** with the two fixes above, every other mapped field resolves for both LCR files — only `cost_code`, `loa` and `sourcing` come back empty and `purchase_request` sparse, and those are genuinely blank in the source (cost code is written on group-header rows, not per WP; the stage columns are unused because award status comes from Remarks).
- **Awarded-cost header variants:** most sheets head the column `AWARDED COST (NET|VAT EX)`, but the **LCR templates (LCR352 / LCR102) use just `AWARDED (VAT EX)`** — the old `/awarded cost/` regex matched nothing there, so **no awarded cost was imported at all** (fixed 2026-07-23). Detection is now `/awarded cost/` → `/^awarded\s*\((vat|net)/` → `/awarded amount|contract amount/`. The middle pattern deliberately requires the parenthesised qualifier so it can **not** match a bare `AWARDED` column, which some sheets (e.g. SLT101 col U) use as an unrelated flag/quantity helper.
- **The per-project "helping sheet" UNDER-counts awarded WPs — do not treat it as ground truth.** Its `Total number of awarded Packages` is `AGGREGATE(4,…)` over a month-bucketed `SUMIFS` that sums a **counter column (col B)** gated by actual-award date. Two failure modes seen in real files: (a) a genuinely awarded row with a **blank counter cell** is skipped — **AVR102-107** reports 15 vs the true **17** (WP 44 Wiring Devices, WP 58 BI & cPVC Pipes), and **SLT101** reports 31 vs the true **35** (WP 71, 74, 75 blank counters + WP 83 which has no actual award date); (b) the hardcoded row range (`$22:$154`, `$14:$109`) truncates later rows (the AVR101 case in Known Issues #17). The importer counts every row flagged Awarded in Remarks, which is why it legitimately reports MORE than the helping sheet. SLT101 on the app basis: budget-for-awarded ₱1,938,623,384 − awarded ₱1,831,143,321 = **savings ₱107,480,063 (+5.5%)**, vs the helping sheet's ₱99,326,457 computed over only 31 WPs.
- **Three import lookouts (per user):** (1) **advance award dates** — a WP whose actual award date is in the **future** is imported as **Not Yet Awarded** with `actual_awarding_date`/`awarded_cost` cleared (the advance date is ignored until it arrives); (2) **OSM** — `osm='No'` for every WP unless its **Remarks** match `/osm/i`; (3) **charging** — every WP `charging_type='Main Contract'`. Also reconciles the proc⇄award invariant.
- **Scope-shrink guard (`checkScope`/`renderScopeWarning`/`scopeConfirmText`, 2026-07-23):** "Replace" deletes the whole project and inserts whatever the chosen sheet holds, so importing a **narrower or older working file silently shrinks the project**. This happened on **CCM302**: a workbook named with a LATER date (`2026 07 07`) held only WP 1–68 with 31 awarded, while the stored data came from `2025 08 28` with WP 1–78 and 40 awarded — the re-import dropped 10 work packages and un-awarded eleven ₱-bearing ones (Precast ₱223M, Logistics ₱47M, Ready Mix ₱39M…). After parsing, the importer now fetches the target project's stored `wp_no/award_status/total_awarded` and compares: fewer WPs, fewer awarded, stored WP Nos. **missing from the sheet**, or stored-Awarded rows arriving **Not Yet Awarded**. Any of those renders a red banner in the preview naming the affected WP Nos., and **Replace demands an extra confirmation** listing the losses before the delete. A same-scope or growing re-import shows nothing.
- **Duplicate WP No. guard (`doImport`):** the DB has a `(project_id, wp_no)` unique constraint, so a monitoring sheet with two rows sharing a WP No. (e.g. **SLN101** had two *different* Architectural door WPs both numbered **53** — "Wooden Doors - Supply" and "PVC Doors - Supply" — a source-sheet data-entry error) previously deleted the project's WPs then failed mid-batch (`duplicate key value violates unique constraint "work_packages_project_wp_unique"`), leaving a **partial import** (e.g. only 40 of 89 rows). `doImport` now scans `_parsed` for duplicate `wp_no` **before** the delete/insert and aborts with a clear alert naming the offending numbers — nothing is changed. Fix is at source: give each row a distinct WP No. in the .xlsx, then re-parse and import.
- **Numeric-overflow-safe percents/money (`pPct`/`pMoney`, 2026-07):** the `% of DP` and `Retention %` columns map to DB `numeric(5,4)` (max ±9.9999, stored as a decimal), but source sheets sometimes carry **garbage in those cells** — **LCR352 WP 45** had `% of DP = 60` and `Retention % = 17,258,238` (an amount mis-entered as the percent). The old raw `Math.round(dpPct*10000)/10000` stored those verbatim → Postgres **`numeric field overflow`** aborted batch 3, and because Replace deletes first, LCR352 was left with only 40 of 49 rows. `pPct(v)` now mirrors the CSV rule (**`>1 ⇒ /100`**, so `60 → 0.6`) **and clamps** — anything still `|v|>9.9999` is dropped to `null` (garbage), so it can't overflow. `pMoney(v)` guards money columns the same way (drops `|v|>1e15`). Applied to `dp_percent`/`retention_percent` and every money field (`approved_budget_bcb`, `awarded_cost`, `additionals`, `budget_bcb0..2`, `dp_amount`, `retention_amount`) in `rowToWP`. Fix the source cell to keep the real value; the importer just refuses to abort on it.
- **Preview before write:** parses → shows KPI tiles (count, awarded, advance-held, OSM=Yes, **No-trade count**, total budget) + a per-WP table, THEN a **Replace** button deletes ALL of the target project's WPs and batch-inserts (20/batch) with `review_status='approved'`. Strong confirm first. `parse()` throws a clear error if the WP-No or Budget header can't be found (wrong sheet).
- Dates handled via a robust `pDate` (JS Date from `cellDates:true`, Excel serial fallback, `YYYY-MM-DD`/`MM/DD/YYYY` strings) using **local** components (no UTC off-by-one). Generated columns are never inserted (the detected map has no `total_awarded`/`variance`/`awarding_lead_time`).
- Linked from the `project.html` CSV-import modal ("open the Excel importer →").

## CSV Import (Work Packages)

`downloadCSVTemplate()` generates `WPM_Import_Template.csv`. Both `project.html` and `wp-form.html` share the same 54-column template. The two sample rows are clearly marked **EXAMPLE ROW - edit or delete before importing** in the Description and are self-consistent valid samples (one Not-Yet-Awarded minimal row; one Awarded row with cost+vendor+matching PO/JO and no future dates) so they pass the import safeguards; officers edit or delete them freely. `importWPsFromCSV()` calls `WPDb.submitWP()` per row (throws on error), then `WPDb.approveWP()` if auto-approve role.

**Import is de-duplicating (upsert by WP No.)** â€” re-importing a CSV that already contains existing WPs plus a few new ones will **UPDATE the matching rows in place and only INSERT the genuinely new ones**, so no duplicate work packages are created. Both import paths (`project.html` and `wp-form.html` `importWPsFromCSV()`) first load the project's existing WPs, build a map keyed by **normalized WP No.** (`String(wp_no).trim().replace(/\.0+$/,'').toLowerCase()` â€” so `"5"`â†”`"5.0"` match), then per row: if the key exists â†’ `WPDb.updateWPDirect(existingId, wpData)` (keeps approved status), else `WPDb.submitWP()` (+`approveWP` for auto-approve roles). The map is updated after each insert so repeated WP Nos. within the same CSV also don't duplicate. Status message reports `N new, M updated (no duplicates)`.

**`WPCsv` shared module (`db.js`, `window.WPCsv`)** â€” both pages now route import through it:
- **`WPCsv.parse(text)`** â€” RFC-4180 tokenizer. Respects quoted fields containing embedded newlines (e.g. a header like `"BUDGET (BCB)\n(Net)"`). The old `text.split('\n')` broke on these â†’ counted 705 "rows" for an 87-row file. Returns array-of-row-arrays, drops blank rows, strips BOM.
- **`WPCsv.isHeaderMode(table)`** â€” true when the header row contains a recognizable WP No. column. When true, `toWPData` maps **by normalized header name** (not position), so the existing **Work Package Monitoring** layout imports directly â€” no need to reorder columns into the native template. `HMAP` aliases cover both the native template headers and the WP-Monitoring headers; `norm()` = lowercase + strip non-alphanumerics.
- **`WPCsv.toWPData(table, pid)`** â€” header-name mapping; falls back to position-based (native 54-col / legacy <40-col) when no header match. Handles: **Excel serial-number dates** (`pDate` converts e.g. `46050` â†’ `2026-01-28` via the 1899-12-30 epoch; range 20000â€“80000); **procurement_status** from the explicit `Procurement Status` column, else the right-most marked stage column; `pPct` treats values >1 as percentages (Ã·100) and â‰¤1 as already-decimal. Arbitrary `type_of_works` and combined trades (e.g. "Electrical & Auxiliary") pass through unchanged â€” dashboards group by exact trade string.
- **Award status comes from the Remarks/AWARDED text, NOT the stage**: `award_status = pAward(AWARDED col) || pAward(REMARKS col) || (awarded_cost>0 ? Awarded : Not Yet Awarded)`. In the WP-Monitoring files the award flag lives in **Column P â€“ Remarks** ("Not yet Awarded" / "Awarded"). Procurement stage columns are NOT a reliable award signal (a WP can be flagged stage=Mob/Del yet still "Not yet Awarded"), so the old "Contract/Mob-Del â‡’ Awarded" inference was **removed**. Award Status (under Budget & Contract) is the single source of truth for whether a WP is awarded; dashboard KPIs read `award_status`.
- **`WPCsv.dataRowCount(table)`** â€” counts rows with a WP No. for the "Ready to import N" message.

> **Status option sets (updated 2026-07)** — the three WP-form status dropdowns are:
> - **Procurement Status** (`f-proc-status`): **Not Started / Sourced / Solicited / Evaluated / Awarded** (renamed from Sourcing/Solicitation/Evaluation & Negotiation).
> - **Submittal Status** (`f-submittal`): **Not Required / Submitted / Approved w/ Comments / Approved** (added "Approved w/ Comments").
> - **Delivery Status** (`f-delivery-status`): **(blank "— Not started —") / N/A / DP Paid / Accomplished Detailed Drawings / Manufactured/Produced / Delivered** (replaced Not Awarded/DP Processing/Detailed Drawings/Production/Installed; **N/A added 2026-07** for WPs with no delivery phase). Delivery has a blank default so a not-yet-awarded WP isn't forced to a delivery state. Mirrored in `project.html` `ST_DELIV_OPTS` and `review.html` `XL_DELIV`.
>
> These same sets are mirrored in: the **Status Tracker** (`project.html` `ST_PROC_OPTS`/`ST_DELIV_OPTS`) and its badge colour maps (`procStatusBadge`/`submittalBadge`/`deliveryBadge`), the **CSV import** `STAGES` map + delivery defaults in `db.js` (sourcing→Sourced, rfq/bidopen→Solicited, bidclosed→Evaluated, loa/contract/mobdel→Awarded; delivery default now blank), the **Excel importer** (`import-monitoring.html` stage→proc derivation), and both CSV-template example rows. **Award Status is DERIVED from Procurement Status** (`onProcStatusChange()`): `proc==='Awarded'` → Award Status = `Awarded`, any earlier stage → `Not Yet Awarded`. The `f-award-status` select is **disabled** (auto-set). Edit mode uses `fillSelectLegacy()` to append any stored value not in the current list as a temporary "(legacy)" option, so **old data (Sourcing/Solicitation/DP Processing/Installed/etc.) still displays** — no migration is strictly required; run a one-off `UPDATE` in Supabase only if you want stored values realigned to the new labels. When you change any of these sets, update ALL the mirrors above.

**Importing the WP-Monitoring `.xlsx` files** â€” they are NOT in importable shape (hierarchical: trade in Column A, works-subgroup rows, WP leaf rows; cost/award/schedule columns spread across cols Aâ€“EB; dates as real datetimes). A one-off Python extractor (openpyxl) flattens each monitoring sheet into the adopted CSV format: trade from **Column A** (with "MEPF" split into Mechanical/Electrical&Auxiliary/Plumbing/Fire by keyword), Works from the nearest sub-header row, award from **Remarks (Col P)**, budgets/dates/vendors by header-name lookup. Generated CSVs (87/87/80/84/104 WPs for SLN101/SLT101/GRP101/UPTM/OPW101) live next to the source files as `EPC. PMO. Import WP. <PID>. 2026 06 11.csv` and import directly via the dashboard. OPW101 uses a different sheet (`OPW WP Monitoring`) and VAT-EX cost headers â€” the extractor detects both by header name.

The native template (`downloadCSVTemplate`) remains 54-column and imports via header mode (its headers all map through `HMAP`). Legacy files (<40 cols, no header match) still parse via position fallback.

**54-column mapping (position-based) â€” WP No. at col 4 is required (row skipped if blank):**

| # | Header | DB Column | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Identity & Classification** |
| 0 | Cost Code No. | `cost_code` | |
| 1 | Trade | `trade` | |
| 2 | Works | `works` | |

| 11 | Type of Contract | `type_of_contract` | |
| 12 | Charging Type | `charging_type` | |

| **Submittals** |

| 27 | Date of Approval (MM/DD/YYYY) | `approval_date` | |

Dates accept MM/DD/YYYY or YYYY-MM-DD. `dp_percent` and `retention_percent` are entered as plain percentages (e.g. 20) and divided by 100 before storing.

## Vendor Management Dashboard — Phase 2a (2026-08-10)

**Standalone vendor directory + vendor self-service portal, built on the SAME Supabase project (transferred to PMODepartment's Org, Pro tier, 2026-08-10 — needed for Storage/cert uploads) and the SAME repo.** Deliberately scoped to the directory only — `work_packages.vendor_id`, `vendor_bids` (per-WP competitive bidding with original/negotiated/final offers), and the WP-form vendor combobox are **Phase 2b, not built yet**.

**Migration:** `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_management.sql` (run once) — adds `vendors`, `vendor_products`, `vendor_certifications`, `vendor_personnel`, `vendor_rates`, `users.vendor_id` + `role='vendor'`, a private `vendor-certs` Storage bucket, and all RLS.

### Vendor Code (external SAP/ERP ID) — `migrations/2026-08-13_vendor_code.sql` (2026-08-12)
Source: **`Conso PO List. 2026 05 11.xlsx`** (a SAP-style PO/JO export the user provided from `Downloads`) — its `Raw` sheet has 135,567 PO/JO line items across 71 projects, 1,461 distinct `Vendor Code`s (format `V-00XXX`) and 18,171 PO/JO numbers, spanning 2013–2026. **Vendor Code is a far more reliable vendor identity key than free-text name matching** — of the 1,461 codes, only 97 have more than one associated name string, and nearly all of those are pure case/spacing variants (`Jm2 Builders Incorporated` vs `JM2 Builders Incorporated`), not real duplicates. This is the exact problem the Split/Merge duplicate-cleanup tools in `vendors.html` exist to fight after the fact — Vendor Code lets new/matched records avoid it in the first place. The export also carries a `U_WPNo` column populated on **85% of rows (115,129 of 135,567)** — a ready-made link from historical POs to `work_packages.wp_no`, and two example per-vendor sheets (`RP-RA`, `JCAP`) showing a **Contract Amount / POC% / Accomplishment / Balance of Works** view Megawide already builds manually — a contract-performance dimension not modeled anywhere in the app yet (noted for a future phase, not built).
- **`vendors.vendor_code text`** (nullable, indexed, not unique-constrained at the DB level — dedup stays an application concern) added via `alter table ... add column if not exists`.
- **`VendorDb.createVendor`/`updateVendor`** (`db.js`) gained the same deploy-safe missing-column strip-and-retry guard as every other optional vendor column (`_isMissingCol(error, /vendor_code/)`) — safe to ship before the migration runs.
- **UI**: a "Vendor Code" input in the staff detail Overview form (`vendors.html`, `#dov-vendor-code`, saved via `saveDetailOverview`'s `fields.vendor_code`), shown as a small monospace line under the company name in the directory table and folded into the card subtitle (`vendor_code · contact`); added to the search predicate in `_vendorFiltered()` so staff can search by code.
- **Backfill — DONE (2026-08-14)**: the 1,461 code→name pairs were matched against the live `vendors` directory by **normalized name** (trim + collapse whitespace + lowercase) → **326 matches, 0 ambiguous** (no normalized name resolved to two different live vendor rows); deduped to **321** `(vendor_id, vendor_code)` pairs after dropping 5 vendor ids that matched more than one code. All 321 stamped successfully. The ~1,135 unmatched codes are expected and were deliberately **not** auto-created as vendor stubs — they're overwhelmingly non-construction PO vendors (IT, insurance, utilities) that were never in this construction-trade directory.

### `vendor_bids` backfill from historical PO/JO data (2026-08-14) — DONE

Second half of the Conso PO List work: **1,518 `vendor_bids` rows written**, spanning **14 projects, 242 vendors, 561 work packages, ₱11.06B of PO value**, split **646 `awarded` / 872 `shortlisted`**. Verified live post-write: `vendor_bids` = **1,884 rows total** (1,518 from this backfill + 366 pre-existing from the earlier `backfillVendorDataFromWPs` run and manual entry), with **0 duplicate `(vendor_id, wp_id)` pairs**.

- **Resolution chain**: Excel `Vendor Code` → the freshly-stamped `vendors.vendor_code` → `vendor_id`; Excel `U_WPNo` → `work_packages.wp_no` (normalized the app's usual way — strip `WP-`/`WP ` prefix and trailing `.0`, lowercase) scoped to the mapped project → `wp_id`.
- **Two explicit user decisions**, both baked into the dataset before any write: PO lines whose `U_WPNo` lists **multiple WP numbers** (e.g. `"9,15"`) are **skipped entirely** (no arbitrary attribution); **Closed + Open** PO statuses count as bid history, **Cancelled** is excluded.
- **Aggregation**: rows grouped to one bid per `(vendor_id, wp_id)`, `final_amount` = summed PO value, `bid_date`/`award_date` from the group's date range, `status` = **`awarded` if any contributing PO line is Closed, else `shortlisted`**. `original_offer`/`negotiated_offer` stay null — the PO export has no pre-negotiation figures.
- **`PROJECT_MAP` is deliberately conservative — 14 of the Excel's 71 projects.** Only confidently-matchable live projects were mapped (AVR101, AVR102, BAU101, CCM302, CDP101, CSF101, GPR101, OPW101, QHL706, SLN101, SLT101, STM101, UTM101, WCB358). **Ambiguous names were skipped, not guessed** — e.g. Excel's "4PH Lancaster" could be LCR102 or LCR352 with nothing in the export to disambiguate, so both were left out; LCR102/LCR352/MSJ101/MST347/NIA101 got no backfill.
- **Funnel from 135,567 raw PO lines → 19,994 usable → 1,518 bids**: `status_excluded(cancelled)` 12,457 · `no_vendor_code_match` 39,935 · `no_wp_no` 10,216 · `multi_wp_skipped` 5,357 · `project_unmapped` 42,826 · `wp_not_found_in_live_db` 4,782.
- **Written via `upsert` on the existing `UNIQUE (vendor_id, wp_id)` constraint**, so the operation is idempotent and safe to re-run. Every row carries `notes: 'Backfilled from Conso PO List (<PO nos>)'`, which is the marker to use if these ever need to be identified or reversed in bulk.
- **Execution gotcha worth remembering:** the payload was staged as 11 JSON chunk files and pushed through the authenticated browser session (the `javascript_tool` result limit forces ~150 rows per call). A mid-run file merge caused **chunk 4 to be silently skipped** — every call still reported `ok:150, fail:0`, so the per-call results looked perfectly clean. It was caught only by a **post-write reconciliation** (counting rows carrying the backfill `notes` marker and comparing against the 1,518 staged) and then re-run. **When pushing a batch through many separate calls, reconcile the final DB count against the source count — per-call success totals do not prove the whole set landed.**
- **NOT done: `vendor_rates`.** Only bids were backfilled. Rates would need a per-unit basis the PO export doesn't cleanly provide, and the earlier `backfillVendorDataFromWPs` already populates rates from awarded WPs.

### Login-time dashboard chooser (`login.html`, 2026-08-10)
After sign-in, internal (non-vendor) roles now land on a **"Select Dashboard"** chooser — two cards, **Work Package Management** vs **Vendor Management** — before the existing project picker, treating Vendor Management as a co-equal dashboard rather than a WPM sub-page. `showDashboardChooser(profile)` replaces the two former call sites of `showProjectPicker(profile)` (session auto-restore + `handleSignIn`); clicking **Work Package Management** calls the original `showProjectPicker(profile)` unchanged (still handles the viewer skip-picker and single-project skip-picker shortcuts), clicking **Vendor Management** goes straight to `vendors.html`. **`vendor`-role logins bypass the chooser entirely** — `showDashboardChooser` redirects them to `vendor-portal.html` immediately, same as before, they never see WPM at all. `window._chooserProfile` bridges the profile from the chooser to the WPM-card click handler (kept as a plain global rather than threading it through DOM data attributes, matching this page's existing script-level state style e.g. `allItems`/`selectedValue`).

### Onboarding flow
Staff creates a **vendor skeleton** (company name + `invite_email`, `status='pending_review'`) in `vendors.html` → gets a link `vendor-register.html?vendor=<id>` to send the vendor (no email automation yet — copy/paste). Vendor opens the link, sets a password (`vendor-register.html`, standalone/public like `register.html`) → Supabase Auth `signUp` + insert into `users` with `role:'vendor', status:'approved', vendor_id:<id>`. That insert is gated by the `users_insert_vendor` RLS policy, which checks the submitted email matches `vendors.invite_email` (case-insensitive) and the invite hasn't already been claimed (`invite_claimed_at is null`) — an `AFTER INSERT` trigger (`internal.claim_vendor_invite`, `SECURITY DEFINER`) then stamps `invite_claimed_at` so the same link can't mint a second login. Vendor lands on **`vendor-portal.html`**.

### Roles & data ownership
- **New role `vendor`** — never sees WPM project data. `login.html`'s `showProjectPicker` redirects `role==='vendor'` straight to `vendor-portal.html` (same pattern as the existing viewer skip-picker redirect).
- `users.vendor_id` links a vendor login to exactly one `vendors` row (not `projects[]` like internal roles). `internal.get_my_vendor_id()` is the new RLS helper.
- **`users_select` was tightened**: a `vendor` role now sees only their own row (previously `using(true)` let everyone see everyone — fine for internal staff, not for an external vendor login). Internal roles are unaffected.
- **Vendor-editable**: `vendors` (own row only), `vendor_products`, `vendor_certifications`, `vendor_personnel` — self-service via `vendor-portal.html`.
- **Staff-only, vendor has zero access**: `vendor_rates` (money — mirrors the `viewer` budget-hiding convention: readable by `super_admin/admin/specialist/manager/user/viewer_budget`, not `viewer`).
- **Approval workflow**: any UPDATE to a `vendors` row made by that vendor's own login is forced back to `status='pending_review'` server-side by the `internal.vendor_edit_guard` `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger — regardless of what the client sends — so the live directory only ever reflects staff-reviewed data, and a vendor can never self-approve. Staff updates (any other role) pass through untouched. `vendor-portal.html`'s `flagEdited()` calls `VendorDb.updateVendor(id, {})` after every child-table (products/certs/personnel) add/remove too, since those tables don't carry their own status — touching the parent row is what fires the trigger.

### Pages
| File | Role | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `vendor-register.html` | public | Claims a staff-issued invite, creates the `vendor` login. Doesn't pre-validate the invite client-side (RLS blocks anonymous reads of `vendors` by design) — a bad/reused link just fails the insert with a plain error. |
| `vendor-portal.html` | vendor only | Self-service editor — Company Info (contact, trade categories via chip-select), Products/Services, Certifications (with file upload to the private `vendor-certs` bucket + signed-URL viewing), Personnel. Status banner explains what `pending_review`/`approved`/`rejected`/`inactive` means. No sidebar/WPM chrome — a lightweight standalone shell (topbar + status pill + sign out only). |
| `vendors.html` | internal staff (all non-vendor roles) | Directory — KPI cards, search + status filter, table with inline Approve/Reject for `pending_review` rows, **Invite Vendor** modal (creates the skeleton + generates the `vendor-register.html?vendor=<id>` link to copy/send), and a detail modal (Overview/Products/Certifications/Personnel/**Rates** tabs — Rates is staff-only manual entry in Phase 2a, no auto-insert from WP awards yet since that's Phase 2b). Reuses the standard sidebar/topbar shell (`dashboard.css`, `AppAuth.requireLogin`), redirects `role==='vendor'` to `vendor-portal.html`. |

Sidebar link **"Vendor Management"** (`ti-building-store`) added to `index.html`, `project.html`, `admin.html` — visible to all internal roles including `viewer` (mirrors the "mirror WP roles" access decision: viewer can read the directory, just like they can read WPs).

### `VendorDb` API (`db.js`, appended after `WPCsv`)
`getVendors/getVendor/createVendor/updateVendor/approveVendor/rejectVendor/setVendorStatus/deleteVendor`, `products/certifications/personnel` (each `{list,add,update,remove}` over the respective child table), `getVendorRates/addVendorRate/deleteVendorRate`, `uploadCertFile/getCertFileUrl/deleteCertFile` (Storage — private bucket, `createSignedUrl` for viewing since it's not public). Follows the same audit-stamp + missing-column-retry-guard convention as `WPDb`.

### Fixes after live end-to-end testing (2026-08-10)
- **Trade Categories switched from chip-toggle to a checkbox list** (`.trade-checklist`, both `vendors.html`'s invite modal and `vendor-portal.html`'s Company Info tab) — the chips already supported multi-select functionally (verified live: a vendor was created with 3 trades at once), but per explicit request the control itself was swapped to a standard 2-column checkbox grid, which reads as multi-select without needing to discover it by clicking.
- **`vendors.html` was unreadable in dark mode** — the page's inline `<style>` block (KPI cards, `.v-table`, modal, badges, form fields) used hardcoded light colors instead of `dashboard.css`'s CSS custom properties, the exact "class rules vs attribute selectors" dark-mode pitfall already documented elsewhere in this file. Fixed with a `body.dark-mode` block using the existing `--surface`/`--surface-2`/`--text-primary`/`--text-hint`/`--border`/`--border-md`/`--mw-red` variables (same tokens `dashboard.css` itself uses), so it follows the site-wide dark-mode toggle correctly. **`vendor-portal.html` was NOT touched here** — it never loads `ui.js`, so `AppTheme` is undefined and `body.dark-mode` is never applied there in the first place (auth.js's `AppTheme.init()` call is guarded with `typeof AppTheme !== 'undefined'`); it stayed light-only at the time. **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-20** — the portal now carries its OWN theme (anti-flash script + `html.dark-mode` rules + a topbar toggle sharing the `wpm_theme_last` key), exactly like `login.html`. **Its rules are scoped to `html.dark-mode`, NOT `body.dark-mode`** — that class is never set on this page.

### Staff-side full edit of a vendor's directory data (2026-08-10)

`vendors.html`'s detail modal previously let staff Approve/Reject/Mark Inactive/Reactivate and manage Rates only — Overview/Products/Certifications/Personnel were pure read-only display, so staff had no way to fill in or fix a vendor's data on the vendor's behalf (e.g. a vendor that can't or won't use the self-service portal). All four tabs are now editable, mirroring `vendor-portal.html`'s own self-service form patterns exactly but scoped to the detail-modal's `dov-`/`dnp-`/`dnc-`/`dnp2-` prefixed element ids (to avoid colliding with the Invite-modal's `inv-*` ids and the Rates tab's `nr-*` ids already in this file):
- **`renderDetailOverview()`** now renders an editable form (Company Name, the same `TRADES` checkbox grid as the invite modal — `#dovTradeChips`/`buildDetailTradeChips()`/`toggleDetailTrade()`, state in the new module-level `detailTrades` Set — plus Contact Person/Number/Email, Address, Notes) with a **Save Changes** button wired to **`saveDetailOverview()`**. On success it calls `VendorDb.updateVendor(activeVendor.id, fields)`, patches the in-memory `allVendors` array in place (so the directory table/KPIs reflect the edit without a reload — `renderTable()`/`renderKpis()` called immediately after), refreshes the modal's title/status badge/footer (status buttons don't change since staff edits don't move `status`), toasts via `AppNotify.success`, and re-renders the form from the fresh row.
- **Products/Certifications/Personnel** each gained an `.add-form` (reusing the existing CSS class from the Rates tab, no new classes needed except the already-present `.trade-checklist`) + a per-row trash button, mirroring `vendor-portal.html`'s `add*()`/`remove*()` functions verbatim but calling `VendorDb.products/certifications/personnel.add/remove` directly (not through the vendor's own session) and re-rendering just that tab (`renderDetailProducts`/`renderDetailCerts`/`renderDetailPersonnel`, now also building their own add-forms rather than being pure list renders). Certifications carries the same file-upload sequencing as the portal (`certifications.add` first to get the row id, then `VendorDb.uploadCertFile` + `certifications.update({file_path})`) — reuses the existing `viewCertFileStaff()` viewer, unchanged.
- **Staff edits do NOT retrigger `pending_review`** — per the existing `vendor_edit_guard` `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger (documented above under "Approval workflow"), that trigger only forces `status='pending_review'` when the update comes from the vendor's OWN login; a staff-role `updateVendor` call passes through untouched, so approving a vendor and then staff-editing their info afterward does NOT knock them back out of the live directory. No new status-handling code was needed on the client for this reason.
- All new render functions run every user-entered value through `esc()` (matching this file's existing XSS convention — `esc()` also safely fills `value="..."` attributes since it escapes quotes).
- No `db.js`/`VendorDb` changes were needed — `updateVendor`/`products.add`/`products.remove`/`certifications.add`/`certifications.update`/`certifications.remove`/`personnel.add`/`personnel.remove`/`uploadCertFile` already existed and needed no new arguments.

### Vendor directory — scale redesign: pagination, compact cards, per-vendor invite link (2026-08-10)

After the WP import created **~900 vendors**, the directory was redesigned for scale (all in `vendors.html`):
- **Pagination** — `renderVendorList()` slices `_vendorFiltered()` into pages of **`_VENDOR_PAGE_SIZE` (60)** and renders only the current page (`_vendorPage`). `renderCards(rows)`/`renderTable(rows)` now take the page slice (no longer filter internally). A `#vendorCount` line ("N vendors · showing 1–60") sits above the grid and a `#vendorPager` (Prev / "Page X of Y" / Next → `gotoVendorPage`) below. Search + status changes call **`onVendorFilterChange()`** which resets `_vendorPage=0`. Kills the "900 tall cards render at once" perf/scroll problem.
- **Compact, content-sized cards** — smaller avatar/padding/font, 2-line name clamp, `minmax(270px)` grid. **Empty sections are omitted**: no trades → no chip row (was a noisy "No trades listed" pill); no contact data → no contact block (was "No contact details yet"). Imported rows with nothing show just avatar+name+badge+footer. Placeholder `…@no-invite.local` emails already hidden via `_displayEmail`.
- **Per-vendor invite link (self-service for pre-existing/imported vendors)** — the detail-modal Overview now has a **Login & Self-Service Access** block. If the vendor hasn't claimed a login (`!invite_claimed_at`): an **Invite Email** input (`#dov-invite-email`, blank when the stored value is a placeholder) + a copyable **Invite Link** (`_vendorInviteLink(id)` = `vendor-register.html?vendor=<id>`, `copyVendorInviteLink`). `saveDetailOverview` persists `invite_email` **only when non-blank** (never overwrites the NOT-NULL column with empty). Flow: staff sets the vendor's real email → Save → send the link → the vendor registers with that email (RLS `users_insert_vendor` matches `invite_email` + `invite_claimed_at is null`) and can then edit their own profile. If already claimed, the block just shows "Activated <date>". No `?v` bump — only `vendors.html` changed.

### Vendor directory — card grid as the default view (2026-08-10)

`vendors.html`'s main list was **a plain table**; per user request ("I don't like the table as the main page") it's now a **responsive vendor-CARD grid by default, with a Cards | Table toggle** (`.view-toggle` in the filter bar) so the dense table stays available — mirrors the Portfolio Overview's project cards/table pattern. `_vendorView` (`'cards'` default) drives `setVendorView()`, which shows `#vendorCards` (grid) or `#vendorTableWrap` (the old table, now wrapped + `display:none` by default) and calls **`renderVendorList()`** — the single dispatcher (`_vendorView==='table' ? renderTable() : renderCards()`) that ALL callers now use (search `oninput`, status `onchange`, `loadAll`, `saveDetailOverview`, `quickApprove/Reject`→`loadAll`). Search + status filtering were extracted into **`_vendorFiltered()`** so cards and table share one predicate (`renderTable` was refactored to consume it). Each `.vcard` (CSS in the page's inline `<style>`, dark-mode block added): an initials **avatar** (`_initials()`), company name + a subtitle (contact person/email), a **status badge** top-right, up-to-3 **trade chips** + `+N` overflow (or a muted "No trades listed"), a contact block (person/email/number with icons), and a footer that shows **Approve/Reject** for `pending_review` (with `event.stopPropagation()` so the row-click-to-open-detail still works) or a muted "View profile →" hint otherwise. Cards are keyboard-accessible (`role="button" tabindex="0"` + Enter/Space → `openDetailModal`). All user text runs through `esc()`. **No `?v=` bump** — only `vendors.html`'s own inline CSS/JS changed, not the shared `auth/db/ui/charts/css` assets (Fastly's ~10-min HTML TTL covers the page itself). **Data source unchanged:** cards use only `getVendors()` row fields (name/status/trade_categories/contact_*), NOT the on-demand child tables, so the list stays a single query.

### Vendor directory — import from WPs, merge duplicates, product search, delete, topbar fix (2026-08-10)

- **Topbar made consistent with the other pages:** besides the `renderUserBar` fix below (theme toggle + profile menu), `vendors.html` now has a **Refresh** button (`#btn-refresh-v` → `refreshVendors()`, re-runs `loadAll()` with a `.spin` on the icon) and (originally loaded `onboarding.js` for the "?" button — **now replaced by `vendor-guide.js`**, see the Vendor Management guide entry below) shows a **"?" guide button** (`#btn-guide`, self-injected before `#user-bar`) — matching `index.html`/`project.html`. Sidebar link + topbar title + `<title>` renamed **"Vendors" → "Vendor Management"** (across `index.html`/`project.html`/`admin.html`/`vendors.html`).
- **Topbar bug fix:** `vendors.html` called `renderUserBar(profile)` but the signature is **`renderUserBar(id, profile)`** — so `id` got the profile object, `getElementById` returned null, and the function bailed → **no dark-mode toggle, no profile menu** in the topbar (every other page passes `'user-bar'` first). Fixed to `renderUserBar('user-bar', profile)`.
- **Search now matches company + trade + PRODUCT** (per request "add the search/filter by product"). `loadAll()` fetches `VendorDb.getAllVendorProducts()` once and builds `_vendorProductText[vendorId]` (lowercased `category description …`); `_vendorFiltered()` matches the query against name + `trade_categories` + that product text. Placeholder updated to "Search by company, trade, or product…". Degrades gracefully (name+trade only) if the products query fails.
- **Import vendors from Work Packages** — toolbar **"Import from WPs"** → `importFromWPs()` → **`VendorDb.importVendorsFromWPs({includeProposed:true, linkWPs:true}, profile)`**. Scans `work_packages.contractor` + `proposed_vendors` (split on `/[\r\n;|]+/`, the established multi-vendor delimiter), dedups by normalized name (`trim`+collapse-ws+lowercase) against existing vendors, creates the missing ones **as `pending_review`** (via `createVendor`, which hardcodes that status), and **links each WP to its awarded vendor** (`work_packages.vendor_id`) where `contractor` matches EXACTLY (RLS limits WP updates to the caller's editable projects). Returns `{created, linked, distinct}` for the toast. Loops individual inserts/updates — fine for a one-time action at typical vendor counts. **Placeholder emails are hidden in the UI:** imported/quick-created vendors get a synthesized `…@no-invite.local` `invite_email` (the column is NOT NULL); `_displayEmail(v)` returns `contact_email` else `invite_email` UNLESS it matches `/@no-invite\.local$/i`, so the card subtitle/contact-block and the table Contact column show `—` instead of the placeholder. **Dedup is by NORMALIZED name** (`trim`+collapse-whitespace+lowercase), so raw WP vendor strings that differ only by case/spacing collapse into one vendor — the created count is therefore typically LOWER than the raw distinct-vendor count shown by the WP List Analysis filter (which lists un-normalized strings); true differently-spelled duplicates are consolidated with the Merge tool.
- **Merge duplicate vendors** — toolbar **"Merge"** opens `#mergeModal`: a **"keep this vendor" target `<select>`** + a checkbox list of every other vendor to fold in, → `doMerge()` → **`VendorDb.mergeVendors(targetId, sourceIds)`** (RPC). Handles typos / different names for the same company. Server side = **`merge_vendors(p_target, p_sources[])`** in **`migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_merge.sql`** (run once): SECURITY DEFINER, self-authorizes via `internal.get_my_role()` (blocks `vendor`/`viewer`), reassigns `vendor_products/certifications/personnel/rates` + `vendor_bids` (dropping a source bid when the target already has one for the same WP, per the `UNIQUE(vendor_id,wp_id)`) + `work_packages.vendor_id` + `users.vendor_id`, **unions `trade_categories`** into the target, then deletes the source rows — atomic. Same accepted-`security_definer` exception pattern as `admin_delete_user`.
- **Delete a vendor** — the detail-modal footer now has a **Delete** button (`deleteVendorFromDetail`) → **`VendorDb.deleteVendorCascade(id)`** → **`delete_vendor_cascade(p_id)`** RPC (same migration): nulls `work_packages.vendor_id` + `users.vendor_id`, deletes the vendor's child rows + bids/rates, then the vendor. Falls back to a plain `deleteVendor` if the RPC isn't deployed yet (which still FK-fails on a linked vendor — surfaced as a normal error). This is how the two **test vendors** (Multi Trade Vendor Inc., Test Vendor Co. — the only real rows; "Southern Steel"/"QuickBuild" were throwaway-harness fakes) get removed once the migration is run. Does NOT delete the vendor's `auth.users` login (use the admin Remove-user flow for that).

### Vendor detail modal — Work Packages section (2026-08-10)

A **"Work Packages"** section was added to the detail modal (2nd, after Overview — jump link `#dsec-wps`, render target `#dtab-wps`, rendered by **`renderDetailWps()`** in the `openDetailModal` `Promise.all`). Lists every WP this vendor is linked to, with a **Role badge per row — `Awarded` (green) and/or `Proposed` (blue)** — plus WP No., Project (name resolved via a cached `WPDb.getProjects()` map `_projName`), Description (+ works sub-line), Trade, Status, and BCB/Awarded (hidden for `role==='viewer'`). **`VendorDb.getWorkPackagesForVendor(vendorId, vendorName)`** (db.js) gathers candidate WPs via id-based `.or('vendor_id.eq.<id>,proposed_vendor_ids.cs.{<id>}')` + separate `.ilike()` queries on `contractor`/`proposed_vendors` (kept OUT of `.or()` so a vendor name with a comma can't break PostgREST's comma-delimited filter; deploy-safe fallback to vendor_id-only if `proposed_vendor_ids` is absent). The CALLER (`renderDetailWps`) computes the precise per-WP flags — awarded = `vendor_id` match OR normalized `contractor` equality; proposed = `proposed_vendor_ids` includes id OR a normalized-name match in split `proposed_vendors` — and **drops any candidate matching neither** (the ilike is a broad substring gather, not the final test). Sorted awarded-first then project/WP-no. Role-badge CSS `.wp-role-badge`/`.wp-role-awarded`/`.wp-role-proposed` (+ dark mode). db.js changed → shared `?v=` bumped `y → z`.

### Vendor directory — 18 duplicates merged by identity data, and a real Merge-tool bug fixed (2026-08-21)

Following the Analytics fix above, ran `vendors.html`'s own `_computeMergeGroups()` suggestion algorithm against the live directory (2,412 vendors) — it returned **56 candidate groups**. Rather than trust name similarity, **every group was cross-checked against `vendor_code` (SAP BP identity), TIN, address, phone, and email** before any merge ran.

- **18 merges executed**, each confirmed by real corroborating identity — either a matching `vendor_code`, a matching **TIN root** (the same taxpayer, different branch — PH TINs are `root-branch`, e.g. `000-313-856-006` vs `000-313-856-00000` is the same company's Lapu-Lapu vs Cebu City branch), or (for one pair, Foshan Becter Decorative Material) matching phone+address+contact despite different codes. **2 of the 18 were partial-group merges** — e.g. the "Abenson" group had 3 members, but only the two `Abenson Ventures Inc.` variants shared a TIN root; the third, `Abenson Inc.`, has a completely different TIN and was left alone as a genuinely separate legal entity. Same pattern for "Glory Lumber" (merged the two `Glory Lumber Construction Supplies Inc.` variants sharing a vendor_code, left bare `Glory Lumber` — different vendor_code and TIN — untouched).
- **15 of the 56 candidates were explicitly NOT merged** despite similar names, because the identity data contradicted or didn't support it — most tellingly **`Emerald` / `Emerald Vinyl Corporation`**: different city (Cebu vs Mandaluyong), different TIN, different vendor_code — a false positive the name-only algorithm would have merged. Also declined: **`Magcalas-Romero Construction Supplies`** (accreditation: **problematic** — blacklisted, zero identity data on file) vs **`...Trading`** (accredited, has a TIN) — no shared identity fields exist to confirm they're the same company, and merging either direction would misrepresent one of them (whitewash a blacklist flag, or wrongly blacklist a clean company). Left as two separate vendors pending real evidence.
- **Result: 2,412 → 2,394 vendors**, verified: exactly 18 dropped, 0 of the merged-away ids remain, 0 dangling references.
- **A real bug in `_mgTokenMatch` was found and fixed in the same pass.** The suggestion algorithm's edit-distance-1 rule (documented above under "Merge suggestions actually find the duplicates now") matched **`TRADE`/`TRANE`** — the HVAC brand — as a duplicate pair, because both are 5 chars and differ by one substitution (D→N), and the rule's only guard was a `>=5` length floor. This grouped **Trane with five unrelated trading companies** (Capacuan Trade, INGCOPH Traders Inc., LIVAN TRADE, Nitronne Trade, Traders Industrial Supply Company Inc). **Fix: the edit-distance-1 rule now additionally requires the two tokens to be DIFFERENT LENGTHS** (`a.length !== b.length`) — i.e. a true insertion/deletion typo, never a same-length substitution. A single-letter substitution between two equal-length tokens is far likelier to land on a genuinely different word than a dropped/added letter is. **Verified this doesn't regress either documented real duplicate the rule was built for** — `HARWARE`/`HARDWARE` (7 vs 8 chars) and `HAFELE`/`HAFFELE` (6 vs 7 chars) are both already different-length pairs, so they still match; only the same-length substitution case (like TRADE/TRANE) is now excluded. **Do not relax this back to allow same-length pairs** — no cited real-duplicate case needs it, and it's the exact shape of the false positive found here.
- vendors.html-only change (its own inline JS), so **no shared `?v=` bump**.

### Vendor Analytics — "(not in directory)" fixed by unambiguous core-name resolution (2026-08-21)

The Analytics tab reported **242 awarded vendors as "(not in directory)", carrying ₱6.39B across 330 awarded WPs — 70.9% of portfolio spend attributed to phantom vendors** rather than the real directory rows. `awardedOf()` resolved a WP's awarded vendor by `awarded_vendor_ids` → `vendor_id` FK → **exact** normalized-name match, and fell back to the raw name as the aggregation key. Most WPs carry only free text, and that text is very often the same company under a different spelling.

**Audited live against production before changing anything** — the 242 break down as: **45 case/punctuation variants** (₱1.62B — `Air Beyond Satisfaction Services Inc` vs `… Inc.`), **54 short forms/abbreviations** (₱1.10B — `Phelps Dodge` → `Phelps Dodge Philippines Energy Products Corporation`, `Amsteel` → `Amsteel Structures, Incorporated`), **92 garbled multi-company strings** (₱1.96B — the Split-tool backlog), **2 placeholders** (₱0.34B — `Various Supplier` on 11 WPs), and **49 genuinely absent**. So **99 of 242 (₱2.72B, 43%) already existed in the directory** — nothing was missing, the matcher just couldn't see them.

- **The fix is display-side only — NO database writes.** `byCore(raw)` was added to `renderVendorAnalytics()` and wired into both the awarded (`splitAw(w.contractor)`) and proposed (`splitV(w.proposed_vendors)`) fallbacks as `byName[nm(n)] || byCore(n)`. Result: **242 → 155 unresolved, ₱6.39B → ₱3.77B**. Chosen over stamping `vendor_id` onto the WPs (offered and declined) because it is reversible and cannot damage WP data.
- **⚠️ THE SAFETY RULE IS THE WHOLE POINT — a name resolves ONLY when it matches EXACTLY ONE directory vendor.** CLAUDE.md already warned that fuzzy matching in money attribution could mis-credit spend between distinct companies (which is why `VendorMatch.coreMatch` powered the vendor-detail WP list but deliberately NOT the analytics). Two or more candidates is a genuine ambiguity and stays "(not in directory)". **This is not theoretical: 100 names hit it**, and they are exactly the dangerous ones — garbled multi-company strings (`Rainbow Commercial Glory Lumber Construction Supplies Inc.`) and real duplicate directory entries (`Affordaink OPC` vs `Affordaink Store`). Crediting an arbitrary one would be worse than showing it unresolved. **Do not relax this to "first match wins".**
- **A ≥5-char core floor** applies (same floor as the accreditation seed's core matching) — below that a core is too collision-prone.
- **A TOKEN INDEX culls candidates before the real test.** Scanning all 2,412 vendor cores per name took **~5s** (2,412 × ~500 names, each compiling a regex inside `wordContains`). Culling is **provably safe**: every branch of `coreMatch` implies the two cores share ≥1 token (equality shares all; a whole-word run means one core's tokens are a subset of the other's), so a vendor sharing no token can never match and dropping it cannot change either the match or the ambiguity count. **Verified, not assumed — the token-index and full-scan implementations were run against ALL 2,199 distinct free-text vendor names in production: 0 mismatches**, at 753ms vs ~5s.
- **NOT fixed by this, by design** (they are data problems, not matching problems): the 92 garbled multi-name strings are the **Split tool's** job; `Various Supplier`/`Various` are placeholders, not vendors, and should stay unresolved; the ~49 genuinely-absent vendors need creating. Directory-wide spend attribution will keep improving as those are worked down — re-run the audit after a Split pass.
- `vendors.html`-only change (its own inline JS), so **no shared `?v=` bump** — same precedent as the other vendors.html-scoped changes.

**Follow-up (2026-08-22): the tab now says how big the RESIDUAL gap is.** Core-name resolution recovers the spelling variants; what remains are companies with genuinely no vendor record — they appear as the awarded contractor on a WP and nowhere else. An amber banner under the KPI tiles counts them, their awarded spend and its share of the total, states that the view is built from work packages (id → name → unambiguous core name → contractor text), and points at **Data Tools → Import from WPs**. Asked after the not-accredited purge — deleting those rows nulled their `work_packages.vendor_id` links while leaving the `contractor` text intact (that is the procurement record), so the fallback went from an edge case to a large slice and read as a bug.

**The masterlist workbook itself is fully imported** — verified against all five sheets: 2,376 accredited + 32 non-accredited + 3 problematic all present, and of `Sheet1`'s 1,212 raw SAP vendor rows only **one** name is absent (`V-00800 R Marcos Construction Service`). `Sheet1` is a truncated SAP dump (names cut at ~31 chars) so match it by **BP code, never by name**; `Employee List 2` holds no vendor rows.

**Re-importing from WPs is a business decision, not an automatic fix** — it re-creates records for companies Megawide has genuinely spent money with (arguably the honest state) but also re-inflates the directory and reintroduces the garbled WP-derived names that Split/Merge exist to clean up.

### Vendor Analytics view (2026-08-10, feature 2 of 3)

A third **Analytics** mode on the `vendors.html` directory toggle (`vv-analytics`, alongside Cards/Table — `setVendorView('analytics')` shows `#vendorAnalytics`, hides the cards/table/pager/`.vendor-meta` bulk chrome). **Cost view — hidden for `role==='viewer'`** (button removed at init; `setVendorView` also refuses the mode for that role). **`renderVendorAnalytics()`** aggregates from `WPDb.getAllApprovedWPs()` (fetched once, cached in `_vaWPs`) + the directory (`allVendors`): per-vendor awarded spend / BCB / #WPs (wins) and participation (proposed OR awarded), keyed by directory vendor id when resolvable else the normalized free-text name (so un-imported contractors still tally, flagged "(not in directory)"). Uses shared `window.isMoneyAwarded`/`effectiveAwardedCost` (#28). Sections: 4 KPI tiles (Total Vendors/approved, Awarded Spend, Awarded WPs, Portfolio Savings ₱+%), **Spend Concentration** (top-1 % + top-5 % of awarded spend), **Top Vendors by Awarded Spend**, **Top Vendors by Savings Delivered**, **Bid Win Rate** (vendors on ≥3 WPs, wins/participated), **Vendor Coverage by Trade** (directory count per canonical trade — relies on the trade normalization above). Ranked-table rows open the vendor's detail modal when the row resolves to a directory vendor. Tables-only (no canvas). vendors.html-only change (no shared-asset `?v=` bump). **Still to come: feature 3 — Cycle-Time & Efficiency.**

### Vendor detail modal — tabs → single scrolling page (2026-08-10)

The `vendors.html` detail modal's five tabs (Overview/Products/Certifications/Personnel/Rates) were **converted from a tab switcher to one continuous scrolling page** with a **sticky jump-nav + scroll-spy** — now that every tab is editable (see above), tabbing hid the fact that there was more to fill in, and staff had to click each tab to discover empty sections. All five sections now render at once, stacked, each under a `.detail-section-title` inside a `<section class="detail-section" id="dsec-<tab>">` (the original `#dtab-<tab>` render-target divs are preserved, just nested inside these sections — the five `renderDetail*()` functions were untouched, they still `getElementById('dtab-<tab>').innerHTML = …`). The old `.detail-tabs`/`.detail-tab` button row became `.detail-jump` (a `position:sticky;top:0` `<nav>` of `<a href="#dsec-<tab>">` links); `switchDetailTab()` was **removed** and replaced by **`_wireDetailScrollSpy()`** — an `IntersectionObserver` rooted at `#detailModalBody` (`rootMargin:'-10% 0px -70% 0px'`) that toggles `.active` on the jump link of whichever section is nearest the top, plus click handlers that `scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth'})`. `openDetailModal()` renders all sections, resets `#detailModalBody` scrollTop to 0, then wires the spy; `closeDetailModal()` **disconnects the observer** (`_detailScrollSpy`) to avoid a leak across opens. Dark-mode rules updated (`.detail-jump`/`.detail-section`/`.detail-section-title`). Same self-contained inline-`<style>`+`esc()` conventions as the rest of the file.

### Vendor data backfill — trades, products, bid history, rates from WPs (2026-08-10)

`importVendorsFromWPs` only creates vendor IDENTITY rows (name + placeholder email) and links `work_packages.vendor_id` — it never touched `trade_categories`, `vendor_products`, `vendor_bids`, or `vendor_rates`, so an imported vendor showed an otherwise-empty profile. A new **`VendorDb.backfillVendorDataFromWPs(profile)`** (db.js) fills those in from the existing WP data, re-runnable without duplicating anything:
- **Trade Categories** — unioned from BOTH awarded (`contractor`/`vendor_id` match) AND proposed (`proposed_vendors`, split on the same `/[\r\n;|]+/` delimiter used elsewhere) WPs, for every vendor the WP resolves to. Merges with whatever's already on the vendor (never removes existing values); a vendor whose union is unchanged from its current `trade_categories` is skipped (no no-op `updateVendor` call).
- **Products / Bid History / Rates** — ONLY from genuinely-awarded WPs, using `award_status==='Awarded' && awarded_cost>0` (the `isMoneyAwarded` predicate's shape, using `awarded_cost` since `total_awarded` is a generated column not selected here) and only for the ONE vendor that WP was actually awarded to (`vendor_id` else normalized `contractor` match) — not every vendor merely listed in `proposed_vendors`.
  - **Products** dedupe by normalized (`trim`+collapse-ws+lowercase) `description` **per vendor** — the same vendor awarded the same description across multiple WPs yields exactly one `vendor_products` row. Dedup checks against `getAllVendorProducts()` fetched up front, so a re-run never creates a second row for a description already backfilled (or already entered by staff/the vendor). `category` = that WP's `trade`.
  - **Bids** upsert via the existing `VendorDb.upsertBid` (`(vendor_id,wp_id)` unique constraint) — one row per awarded WP. `final_amount: awarded_cost`, `status:'awarded'`, `bid_date: awarding_date||actual_awarding_date`, `award_date: actual_awarding_date||awarding_date`, `notes:'Backfilled from WP <no>'`. **`original_offer`/`negotiated_offer` are always null** — `work_packages` has no such data, only the final awarded cost.
  - **Rates** upsert via a new **`VendorDb.upsertRate(wpId, vendorId, projectId, fields, profile)`** — mirrors `upsertBid`'s shape but targets `vendor_rates`. Required a new migration **`migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_rates_wp_link.sql`** (run once): `vendor_rates` had no column tying a row back to its source WP, so a re-run would have duplicated rate rows every time. Adds `vendor_rates.wp_id uuid references work_packages(id) on delete cascade` (nullable/additive — manual staff-entered rates keep `wp_id NULL`) plus a **partial unique index** `(vendor_id, wp_id) where wp_id is not null`, so only auto-imported rows are constrained to one-per-WP; manual entries are untouched. `upsertRate` falls back to a plain insert if `wp_id` doesn't exist yet (pre-migration — loses the de-dup guarantee until the migration runs, same deploy-safe pattern as the rest of this file). No RLS change needed — `vendor_rates`' existing row-level policies apply regardless of which columns are touched.
- Returns `{tradesUpdated, productsAdded, bidsUpserted, ratesUpserted, skippedNoVendor}` for the toast. Loops individual writes sequentially (same "fine for a one-time action" tradeoff as `importVendorsFromWPs`).
- **UI:** `vendors.html` toolbar gained a second button, **"Backfill Trade/Bid Data"** (`#btn-backfill-vendor-data` → `backfillFromWPs()`), right next to "Import from WPs" — same confirm-then-call-then-reload-then-toast shape, with a disabled/spinner label while running.

### Vendor directory cleanup — Split garbled names + Merge auto-suggest (2026-08-10)

After the WP-derived bulk import, `vendors.html` had ~1,000 vendor rows with two distinct data-quality problems, so two separate tools were added (deliberately not conflated into one "cleanup" feature — a garbled multi-name entry and a true duplicate need opposite fixes: create N rows vs delete 1):

- **Split tool** (`#splitModal`, opened via a toolbar badge button **"Needs Splitting (N)"** that's hidden when N=0): detects garbled multi-name vendor records via the shared **`_splitSegments(name)`** helper, used identically for candidacy (`_splitCandidates()` requires `_splitSegments(name).length >= 2`) and the modal's default segment chips, so detection and default-split can never disagree. **Two detection passes, tuned against a live-DB audit of the real ~1,000-row import (2026-08-10), not guessed:**
  1. **Delimiter split**: `/[,/;:|\r\n]+/` (comma, slash, semicolon, colon, pipe, newline) — widened twice from an initial comma-only cut after live data showed slash-joined runs (`"A / B, C / D"` — comma-only detection left slash segments merged, an 11-name entry only broke into 2) and a colon-delimited case (`"J VAZCO COMPANY: TRADETEK: CONCRETE SOLUTION"`).
  2. **Suffix-run fallback** (only tried when pass 1 finds no delimiter at all): some garbled entries have ZERO punctuation between company names — e.g. `"Meralco Energy Inc. Maxrate Construction Inc. Top One Construction... AlternativePower Solution Inc"` — separated only by a repeating legal-suffix word. `_countSuffixTokens(name)` counts occurrences of `Inc/Incorporated/Corp/Corporation/Ltd/Limited/LLC/Co/Company` (case-insensitive, whole-word) anywhere in the string; **2 or more** triggers `_splitOnSuffixRuns()`, which inserts a split point right after every such occurrence that has real alphanumeric content following it (checked via `/[A-Za-z0-9]/.test(rest)`, NOT a bare truthiness check on trailing text — a lone trailing period is not "more content" and must not trigger a split there, a real bug caught by testing against `"...Arcline Builders Inc."` before shipping). A genuine single company name has its suffix word ONCE, at the end, so count=1 never triggers this pass — verified safe against `"Megawide Construction Corporation - BU Formworks"` (real live-DB row, correctly never flagged despite "Corporation" being followed by more text, since it only occurs once).
