  - **Regex gotcha hit twice while building this, both worth remembering:** (1) `\b` immediately after an optional trailing period (`\.?\b`) silently fails and backtracks away from the period whenever that period is followed by whitespace — a period and a space are BOTH non-word characters, so no `\b` boundary exists between them, forcing the engine to drop the period from the match and corrupting the next segment with a stray leading `.`. Fixed by moving the `\b` to bind only the bare suffix word, then consuming `\.?` unconditionally afterward outside the boundary check. (2) A Python heredoc escaping mistake once collapsed `\\b` down to a literal backspace byte (`\x08`) instead of the two literal characters `\b` — caught only by re-extracting and eval'ing the ACTUAL committed file content against the real test strings before pushing, not by trusting the edit diff at face value.
  - **False-positive suppression:** a bare legal-suffix fragment left as its own segment after either pass (`CORP_SUFFIX_RE`, whole-segment match) is merged back onto the PRECEDING segment instead of counted as its own vendor — `"Abenson Ventures, Inc."` → 1 segment (not a candidate at all), not two.
  - Each remaining candidate renders as editable segment chips (`_splitSegEdit`/`_splitSegRemove`/`_splitSegAdd`) so a heuristic's imperfect guess (e.g. a run-on segment the suffix-pass couldn't further divide, like `"JCAP Construction AP Electromechanical & Construction Corporation"` staying as one chip) can be manually corrected before confirming — this tool never auto-executes.
  - **"Split into N vendors" links to existing vendors instead of always creating new ones (2026-08-10 fix):** the first cut of `doSplit` created a brand-new `VendorDb.createVendor(...)` for EVERY segment unconditionally — if a garbled entry contained a name that already existed as its own clean vendor elsewhere (common, since the same real company can appear both cleanly imported from one WP and garbled-together with others from a different WP), splitting guaranteed a duplicate. Fixed via **`_findExistingVendorByName(name, excludeId)`**, which normalizes with the SAME `_mgNormalize()` Merge's duplicate-suggestion already uses and matches against `allVendors` (excluding the vendor being split). Each segment chip now shows a live **"🔗 links to existing"** badge (`.seg-match`) as you type/edit it. `doSplit` builds a plan per segment: a match unions the original's `trade_categories` into the EXISTING vendor via `updateVendor` (no new row, no duplicate); no match still `createVendor`s as before. The confirm dialog lists each segment's new-vs-linked fate before committing, and the success toast reports both counts — the outcome is visible before AND after, not just assumed.
  - **"Split into N vendors"** (`doSplit`) creates one `VendorDb.createVendor({name:segment, trade_categories:<original's>, invite_email:<placeholder>}, profile)` per segment with NO existing match (reuses the original's trade categories since there's no way to know which segment belongs to which trade; `createVendor` always inserts `pending_review` internally regardless of what's passed, so these stay pending for staff to individually confirm, unlike the auto-approved "Add Vendor" flow, since these are algorithmically inferred — **`invite_email` must be supplied explicitly since `createVendor` has no default and the column is `NOT NULL`; the first cut of this tool omitted it and every split failed**, fixed via the same `_addVendorPlaceholderEmail()` placeholder generator the Add Vendor flow uses), then `VendorDb.deleteVendorCascade(originalId)`. **"Skip"** (`skipSplit`) just removes the row from the current session's candidate list (`_splitSkipped` Set, not persisted — reopening the modal re-offers it) without touching data. Confirm-before-execute names the WP-relink limitation explicitly. **Explicit limitation, by design, not a bug: this tool does NOT redistribute the original vendor's WP links across the new/linked split vendors** — any WP still pointing at the deleted original's `vendor_id` needs manual re-linking afterward (the confirm dialog says so). Known remaining gap, deliberately NOT covered (would need heavy false-positive-prone heuristics like flagging on `&`/"and"/weak business-type words — rejected as too risky across ~1,000 legitimate single-company names): entries with no delimiter AND no repeated legal suffix, e.g. `"Comanchesteel Corporation Rheymar Trading"` or exact-duplicate-prefix garbles like `"Toyota Pasig Toyota Pasig Evolander Motor Corporation"` — these need manual discovery via the directory search. All user text via `esc()`.
- **Merge tool** rebuilt into two sections in one modal: **"Suggested Duplicate Groups"** (auto-computed on open, `_computeMergeGroups()`) plus a de-emphasized **`<details>`-collapsed "Manual Merge (advanced)"** disclosure (target `<select>` + duplicate checklist, name-filter `<input id="mergeSearch">` above the checklist since scrolling ~1,000 raw entries was impractical — the target `<select>` itself stays unfiltered so a search text can't hide the vendor currently picked as canonical). Collapsed by default so the modal reads as ONE primary flow (review suggested groups); manual merge is the fallback path, not an equally-weighted second UI. The modal subtitle explicitly tells the user garbled multi-name entries are handled by the separate Split tool instead, since fix (below) means they never show up here.
  - **Real incident that drove a rewrite (2026-08-10, live ~1,000-vendor data):** opening Merge produced a group of **431 similar names** — nearly half the directory chained into one group — with the auto-picked canonical "Keep" being a garbled slash-joined entry (`"Emerald Vinyl Corp./Starbright Sales Enterprises Inc./Glory Lumber Construction Supplies Inc./..."`). Root causes, all fixed the same day:
    1. **Transitive over-chaining via union-find** — any pair scoring above threshold gets unioned, so A~B~C become one group even when A and C share almost nothing; generic industry words (Construction/Corp/Services/Supplies/Trading/Enterprises) made this snowball across the whole directory.
    2. **Garbled multi-name entries acted as chaining hubs** — a slash/comma-joined entry contains substrings from many unrelated real companies, so it superficially "matched" dozens of them.
    3. **Canonical auto-pick preferred the LONGEST name**, which on this data was disproportionately the garbled ones — actively picking the worst possible "keep" record.
  - **The real fix is in the similarity math, not segment-count exclusion — a first draft got this backwards and was caught by testing before shipping.** The first attempt excluded any vendor with `_splitSegments(name).length >= 2` (later loosened to `>= 3`) from Merge's pool entirely, on the theory that Split candidates shouldn't be Merge candidates. That broke the flagship `"A.L Design, BCS"` ⊂ `"A.L Design, BCS, JCDC"` case this tool has used as its canonical example since it was built — the 3-segment comma variant IS the correct merge partner for its 2-segment sibling (they score 0.85 via the subset rule, well above threshold), but excluding it by segment count removed it from the comparison pool before it could ever be scored, and the harness returned **zero groups** for a dataset built specifically to contain it. Re-tracing the real incident's actual numbers showed the exclusion was never the necessary fix in the first place (see below) — it was rolled back to a generous `>= 6`-segment backstop that only ever fires on genuinely extreme multi-way garbles, not the common 2–4 segment ambiguous names Split and Merge both have a legitimate claim to.
  - **Fix 1 — size-ratio-gated subset-boost is what actually stops a garbled entry from chaining to unrelated vendors.** The pre-existing subset-boost rule (one name's token set is a subset of the other's → floor score at 0.85) is what let the real incident's garbled 15-token name superficially "match" short real vendor names like `"BCS Construction Corp"` (1 distinctive token after stripping) — a giant token bag trivially contains almost any short name's 1–2 words. Traced by hand against the exact real garbled string: `sim("BCS Construction Corp", <garbled>)` scored **0.85** (a false match) before this fix, **0.067** (correctly far below threshold) after. Fix: the subset-boost now additionally requires `smaller.size / bigger.size >= 0.5` — the smaller set must be at least half the size of the bigger one. This still lets close-in-size genuine variants boost normally (`"A.L Design, BCS"` 4 tokens vs `"...BCS, JCDC"` 5 tokens → ratio 0.8 → boosts to 0.85 as intended) while refusing to boost a tiny real name against a huge garbled bag (ratio ~0.07).
  - **Fix 2 — stopword-stripped, stricter similarity.** `_mgTokens(name)` strips a fixed **`MERGE_STOPWORDS`** set (`INC, INCORPORATED, CORP, CORPORATION, LTD, LIMITED, LLC, CO, COMPANY, CONSTRUCTION, SERVICES, SERVICE, SUPPLY, SUPPLIES, TRADING, ENTERPRISES, ENTERPRISE, INDUSTRIES, INDUSTRIAL, PHILIPPINES, PHILS, AND, OF, THE`, whole-token case-insensitive) before computing Jaccard — two names are compared only on their REMAINING distinctive tokens. A vendor whose stripped token set is EMPTY (pure boilerplate name) is skipped from comparison altogether (`_computeMergeGroups()`'s inner loop `continue`s on an empty set) rather than let two boilerplate-only names falsely score 1.0. **`MERGE_SIM_THRESHOLD` raised `0.6` → `0.75`.**
  - **Fix 3 — group-size safety valve.** `MERGE_GROUP_SIZE_CAP = 8`: any union-find group larger than 8 members after grouping is treated as a likely over-chaining artifact (real duplicate-name clusters for one company run 2–6 spelling variants, not dozens) and is **excluded from the rendered list entirely** — never shown, never auto-mergeable via Merge All. The suppressed count is surfaced, not silent: `renderMergeGroups()` shows `"N large low-confidence cluster(s) hidden — review manually via search below"` whenever `_computeMergeGroups()`'s `suppressed` count is nonzero (verified by test: 12 identically-named synthetic vendors form one 12-member cluster that is suppressed with `suppressed:1`, not shown).
  - **Fix 4 — canonical auto-pick tie-break changed.** Order is now: `status==='approved'` → `invite_claimed_at` set (claimed login) → **has `trade_categories` populated** (cheap client-side proxy for "more complete / less likely garbled," avoids an extra child-table fetch per vendor) → **shortest name** (final tie-break, replacing the old "longest name" — on this dataset the longest names are disproportionately the garbled ones, so longest was actively picking the worst record).
  - **Grouping** is otherwise unchanged: a union-find/disjoint-set (`_mgFind`/`_mgUnion`) over every (post-`>=6`-segment-exclusion) vendor PAIR scoring ≥ `MERGE_SIM_THRESHOLD` — a plain O(n²) nested-loop comparison, deliberately left unoptimized as a one-time client-side computation (flagged in-code as needing real indexing well before a few-thousand-row scale). Singleton components are discarded; remaining (non-suppressed) groups sorted by size descending and capped at **150** (`MERGE_GROUPS_MAX_SHOWN`) with a "N more small groups not shown" note when truncated.
  - **"Keep this one" UI**: the old single-line `<select>` (which truncated/overflowed a long vendor name illegibly) is now a **radio-button list** (`.mg-keep-list`/`.mg-keep-opt`) matching the visual style of the checkbox rows below it — a long name wraps and reads in full. `_mgChangeCanonical(gi, newId)` re-renders just that one card's HTML from live state — does NOT recompute the groups, so a manual canonical change / unchecked box survives independently of the rest of the list.
  - Each group card's **"Merge Group"** (`mergeGroup(gi)`) calls the same `VendorDb.mergeVendors(canonicalId, sourceIds)` RPC as the manual section, then reloads + re-renders both the group list and the manual checklist so a merged-away vendor disappears from both immediately.
  - **Modal restructured for "not intuitive"/"clashing UI" feedback**: Suggested Groups is the primary flow; the old equally-weighted manual target-`<select>`+checklist is now collapsed behind a `<details>` disclosure labeled "Manual Merge (advanced)", de-emphasized as the fallback path rather than a second competing UI. The subtitle now explicitly states garbled multi-name entries are Split's job and never appear here as a suggestion, so their absence from Merge isn't confusing.
  - **Verified by a standalone Node harness** (evals the actual extracted `_mgNormalize`/`_mgTokens`/`_mgSimilarity`/`_mgFind`/`_mgUnion`/`_computeMergeGroups`/`_splitSegments` source from the shipped file, not a reimplementation, re-run after the segment-exclusion regression was found and fixed) against a synthetic dataset built from the real incident's exact garbled string plus the app's own flagship duplicate example: `"A.L Design, BCS"` + `"A.L Design, BCS, JCDC"` correctly group into one 2-member cluster with `"A.L Design, BCS"` as canonical; 8 distinct vendors sharing only generic boilerplate words (Construction/Trading/Services/Corp/Enterprises) do NOT get falsely grouped; the real incident's garbled name AND a second garbled example (11 companies run together via repeating "Inc./Corp.") never appear in any suggested group AND are never picked as canonical; a forced 12-member identical-name cluster is correctly suppressed (`suppressed:1`, zero groups shown) rather than displayed. All checks passed against the actual shipped file content, not a hand-copied reimplementation.
- Both tools live entirely in `vendors.html` (no `db.js` changes — reuse the existing `createVendor`/`deleteVendorCascade`/`mergeVendors` API surface). New CSS classes (`.split-row`, `.seg-chip`, `.mg-card`, `.mg-keep-row`, `.mg-keep-list`, `.mg-keep-opt`, `.section-divider`, etc.) follow this file's existing `body.dark-mode .classname{...}` pattern.

### Split/Merge follow-up fixes (2026-08-10) — leading-suffix reattach, Merge data-safety, modal scroll

Four fixes to the Split/Merge tools + modal chrome in `vendors.html`, all `esc()`/inline-CSS conventions unchanged:
- **Split — a stranded leading legal suffix now reattaches to the company it belongs to.** A delimiter split of `"Affordaink … Philippines, Inc. MCC - EPC …"` breaks on the comma into `["… Philippines", "Inc. MCC - EPC …"]`, leaving `Inc.` (Affordaink's) glued to the FRONT of the next segment (reported: "the Inc. for each vendor is mistakenly at the start of other vendors"). `_splitSegments` now, after the delimiter split + suffix-run fallback, checks each segment against **`LEADING_SUFFIX_RE`** (`^(inc|corp|ltd|llc|co|…)\.? + more content`) and, when the remainder starts with an uppercase letter/digit (checked in JS since `/i` would otherwise let it grab a lowercase continuation like `"Co. of Manila"`), moves the suffix onto the PREVIOUS segment and keeps the remainder as this segment's real name. Complements the existing bare-suffix merge-back (`CORP_SUFFIX_RE`, `"Abenson Ventures, Inc."` → 1 segment). Verified against the real garbled sample: each company keeps its own trailing `Inc.`, `"Megawide Construction Corporation - BU Formworks"` (single suffix occurrence) is untouched.
- **Merge — multi-company records that would lose a company name are excluded from suggestions (the "`Cebu Oversea, Felport`" report).** Root cause: the old segment-count exclusion in `_computeMergeGroups` only removed **6+**-segment garbles from the pool, so a 2-name entry sharing ONE segment with a real cluster (e.g. `"Cebu Oversea"` matching the "Cebu Oversea Hardware Co." variants) still got suggested — merging it would DELETE the record and discard `"Felport"` (no vendor by that name exists = data loss). New helper **`_mgUnsafeMultiName(member, group)`**: a member that is a genuine multi-name Split candidate (`_splitSegments().length >= 2`) is unsafe when at least one of its SEGMENTS is foreign to the rest of the group (its distinctive tokens, stopword-stripped, appear in no other member). `_computeMergeGroups` strips unsafe members per group (groups dropping below 2 are discarded) and returns a **`garbledExcluded`** count, surfaced by `renderMergeGroups` as a red scissors note pointing to the Split tool. **A single-segment typo/abbreviation variant** (`"Cebu Oversea Harware Co."`) has one segment → never flagged → still merges (Merge's core job is intact). **Deliberate trade-off per the data-safety decision:** the subset-extension pattern `"A.L Design, BCS" ⊂ "A.L Design, BCS, JCDC"` is **no longer auto-suggested** — `…JCDC` carries a token absent from its sibling, reads as possibly-distinct, and is routed to Manual Merge rather than risk silently dropping it. This reverses the earlier "keep the flagship subset case auto-mergeable" priority documented above; **when tuning this, data-loss avoidance wins over auto-merging ambiguous extensions.** Both behaviors verified with a Node harness against the real Cebu group + the A.L Design pair.
- **Split modal — killed the horizontal scrollbar.** The chip `<input>` inline width was `s.length*7px` uncapped, so one long segment forced the modal wider than itself and (since `.modal-body` sets only `overflow-y:auto`, making `overflow-x` compute to `auto`) grew a side scrollbar. Width is now `min(s.length*7, 440)` + `max-width:100%`; added `max-width:100%` to `.seg-chip`/`.seg-chip input`, `min-width:0` to `.split-segs`, and `#splitModalBody{overflow-x:hidden}`.
- **Both modals — background page no longer scrolls behind an open modal.** New **`_syncModalScrollLock()`** toggles `body.modal-open{overflow:hidden}` based on whether ANY `.modal-overlay` is visible (so closing one while another is open keeps the lock); called from every open/close (`openMergeModal`/`closeMergeModal`, `openSplitModal`/`closeSplitModal`, `openInviteModal`/`closeInviteModal`, `openDetailModal`/`closeDetailModal`).

### Split — manual add for no-delimiter garbles + on-demand split-point suggester (2026-08-10)

The Split tool auto-detected only records with a delimiter or a repeated legal suffix (`_splitSegments`). Records that are several companies **run together with no punctuation and no repeated suffix** — e.g. `"Comanchesteel Corporation Rheymar Trading"` — were undetectable and stranded (flagged in the earlier fix as "still manual"). Rather than auto-flag them (rejected: too many false positives across ~1,000 legitimate single-company names — it *would* mis-split `"First Balfour Construction Group"`), the Split modal now supports a **manual, opt-in path**:
- **Search box in the Split modal** (`#split-vendor-search` + `#split-vendor-results` popover, `_splitVendorSearch`/`_splitHideSearch`/`_splitAddVendor`) pulls ANY vendor into the tool by name. State: **`_splitManual`** Set (reset on modal open). **`_splitRows()`** = auto-detected `_splitCandidates()` ∪ manual adds (deduped, minus skipped) — `renderSplitList` and `splitAll` both iterate it now (was `_splitCandidates()`). A `manually added` blue tag marks manual rows; segments seed lazily from `_splitSegments(name)` (usually 1 chip for these).
- **"Suggest split points" DRILLS DOWN into the row's CURRENT segments**, not just the original name (`_splitSuggest` flatMaps `_suggestSplitPoints` over each current segment) — so on a record with SOME delimiters whose resulting segments still contain several companies joined only by spaces + mid-name `Inc.`/`Corp.` words (e.g. the "Affordaink … MCC … Corp. Microphase Corporation Northgate …" monster: delimiter split gives 4, one click → 10), it further-breaks each segment on its internal boundary words and preserves prior edits. Residual repeated-prefix garbles (`"MCC - EPC Central Warehouse MCC - EPC …"`) and boundary-word-less pairs still need a manual chip edit — no safe automatic signal. NOTE `"services"` is deliberately NOT a boundary word (too common in single names: "IT Services", "Allied Services").
- **"Suggest split points" button per row** (`_splitSuggest` → **`_suggestSplitPoints(name)`**) — a deliberately aggressive, MANUAL-ONLY guesser (never wired into `_splitCandidates()`/the badge, so it can't pollute the auto count): splits after a company-boundary word (`SPLIT_HINT_RE` = inc/corp/ltd/…/trading/enterprises/industries/ventures/construction/builders/resources/manufacturing/…) **only when the next word starts with a capital** (a new company name begins), then runs the shared coalesce pass so a suffix continuation (`"… Trading Corporation"`) rejoins. Falls back to the whole name as one segment. Every suggested segment is editable before committing. Verified: `"Comanchesteel Corporation Rheymar Trading"` → 2; `"ABC Construction and Development Corp"` / `"Megawide Construction Corporation"` → 1; `"First Balfour Construction Group"` → 2 (accepted false positive — staff edits/removes). **Still not covered:** exact-duplicate-prefix garbles (`"Toyota Pasig Toyota Pasig Evolander Motor Corporation"`) — needs manual chip editing.
- **"Split" shortcut in the vendor detail-modal footer** (`splitVendorFromDetail(id)`, write-roles only via `_canManage`): closes the detail modal, opens the Split modal, `_splitAddVendor(id)`s that vendor, and auto-runs `_splitSuggest` if its name is a single segment — so staff can jump straight from a profile they're viewing into splitting that record.
- The **shared segment-cleanup loop was extracted to `_coalesceSegments(raw)`** (bare-suffix merge-back + leading-suffix reattach) so `_splitSegments` and `_suggestSplitPoints` can't drift. **Both `\u0001` sentinels (`_splitOnSuffixRuns`, `_suggestSplitPoints`) use the ASCII ESCAPE `'\u0001'`, never a raw 0x01 byte** — a raw byte slipped into `_suggestSplitPoints` during authoring and was caught by `grep -c $'\x01'` (it makes the file register as binary; same trap as the earlier NUL-byte incident). If you add another sentinel, write `'\u0001'` in source, never paste the control char.

### Vendor guide — stable "Next" button (2026-08-10)

`assets/js/vendor-guide.js` `.vg-body` used `min-height:230px`, but the bulleted slides (Finding vendors / Data Tools) exceed it, so the footer + **Next** button shifted down between slides ("moving per click, hard to click"). Changed to a fixed **`height:300px;overflow-y:auto`** so every slide occupies the same vertical space and the footer never moves; taller content scrolls inside the body. Bumped `vendor-guide.js?v=20260810w → x` in `vendors.html` (versioned independently of the 5-file shared bump).

### Vendor trade categories normalized to the 10 canonical trades (2026-08-10)

Vendor `trade_categories` were seeded from **raw** work-package `trade` strings (`importVendorsFromWPs`/`backfillVendorDataFromWPs`), and WP trades are only normalized at DISPLAY time on the dashboards (the inline `normTrade` in index/project), never in the DB — so casing/label variants piled up on vendors: `GENERAL REQUIREMENT` vs `General Requirements`, `ARCHITECTURAL WORKS` vs `Architectural works`, `OTHER ALLIED SERVICES` vs `Allied Services`, etc.
- **New shared normalizer in `db.js`: `window.CanonTrade(t)` / `window.CanonTrades(arr)`** (map + keyword fallback mirroring index/project's inline `normTrade` — **keep in sync with those + `import-monitoring.html`**; `CanonTrades` also de-dups case-insensitively and preserves first-seen order). Defined just before `VendorDb`.
- **Recurrence prevented:** `backfillVendorDataFromWPs` now `CanonTrade`s each WP trade before unioning AND canonicalizes the merged set against the vendor's existing values (so a backfill also cleans any raw variants already stored on a touched vendor). `importVendorsFromWPs` was unaffected (it creates vendors with `trade_categories: []`; trades come from backfill).
- **`vendors.html` display:** `loadAll()` runs every vendor's `trade_categories` through `window.CanonTrades` in memory (non-destructive — DB untouched until a save), so cards/table/detail/search all show canonical trades immediately.
- **One-time DB cleanup:** Data Tools → **"Normalize Trades"** (`normalizeVendorTrades()`) re-fetches the RAW stored values (the in-memory copy is already normalized, so it can't detect drift), updates only vendors whose stored array differs from canonical via `VendorDb.updateVendor`, then reloads. Re-entrancy-guarded (`_normTradesRunning`), per-item try/catch with a first-error sample in the toast, safe to re-run. **Staff edits don't retrigger pending_review** (the `vendor_edit_guard` trigger only fires for the vendor's own login), so this is safe on approved vendors.
- The invite/detail trade CHECKLISTS already build from the canonical `TRADES` const, so a normalized `trade_categories` now pre-selects the right boxes. **db.js changed → shared `?v=` bumped `20260810w → y`** across all pages (vendor-guide.js keeps its independent `x`).

### Toolbar cleanup — "Data Tools" dropdown (2026-08-10)

The 4 admin/cleanup actions (Import from WPs, Backfill Trade/Bid Data, Merge, Needs Splitting) were separate `.btn-cancel` buttons flat in the main toolbar alongside search/status-filter/view-toggle/Add Vendor — cluttered, and per user feedback the row would wrap into an uneven two-line split at moderate widths (the same flat-flex-wrap anti-pattern documented elsewhere in this app: a long flat list of siblings wraps unpredictably at the individual-item level rather than as coherent groups). Fixed two ways:
- **Collapsed into one "Data Tools ▾" dropdown** (`#toolsMenuWrap`/`#toolsMenu`, `toggleToolsMenu()`/`closeToolsMenu()`/`_toolsMenuOutsideClick()` — same click-outside-to-close pattern as the WP List's `.msf` multiselect dropdowns elsewhere in this app). Each item keeps its icon + a one-line description. The Split tool's live count moves from a whole conditionally-hidden button (`#btn-split-needed`, now removed) to a small red pill inside the "Needs Splitting" menu item (`#split-count-badge`) plus a red dot indicator on the "Data Tools" button itself (`.has-alert::after`) — `updateSplitBadge()` now drives both instead of toggling a button's visibility.
- **Toolbar regrouped into explicit flex children** — `.vendor-toolbar-filters` (search + status + view-toggle) and `.vendor-toolbar-actions` (Data Tools + Add Vendor) — rather than one flat `flex-wrap` list, so the row can only ever wrap as two coherent groups, never split mid-group (the same "group into an explicit flex child" fix pattern used for `review.html`'s Excel-grid toolbar, documented earlier in this file's Known Issues).

### Vendor Management — second audit + duplicate cleanup (2026-08-12)

A full re-audit (DB/RLS/migrations, `VendorDb`, `vendors.html`, portal/register, Phase 2b cross-page) verified live as super_admin. **Verified clean:** RLS scoping on vendors/child tables/rates/bids (vendor role sees only its own row; `viewer`/`viewer_budget` blocked from all writes; `viewer` denied rates); the 3 SECURITY DEFINER RPCs use an allow-list; every vendor-authored string passes through `esc()` and inline `onclick` args are directory-guarded UUIDs; 0 dangling `work_packages.vendor_id`; `vendor-portal.html` enforces `role==='vendor' && vendor_id`. Findings and fixes:

- **~1,396 duplicate vendor rows (553 names, up to 5× each) — root-caused to the 1000-row cap.** `getVendors()` was capped at PostgREST's default, so `importVendorsFromWPs` compared new names against only the first 1,000 **name-ordered** existing vendors and re-created everything sorting past `METRO DYNABUILD INC.` on every run. Proven by position: rows in a duplicate group were 0/250, 3/250, 0/250, 5/250 for name positions 0–999, then **231/250, 231/250, 235/250, 248/250…** from 1000 on. The cap itself was fixed 2026-08-11 (`_pagedSelect`), so no NEW duplicates — this was the historical damage.
- **`importVendorsFromWPs` + `backfillVendorDataFromWPs` still had the cap on their OWN `work_packages` read** (plain `.select()`), so both silently processed **1,000 of 1,653 WPs (~60%)**. Both now use `_pagedSelect` (the backfill keeps its missing-column fallback, now also paged). **When adding any "load everything" read, use `_pagedSelect` — this is the third time this cap has bitten.**
- **`merge_vendors` / `delete_vendor_cascade` / `delete_vendors_cascade` now scrub `awarded_vendor_ids` + `awarded_vendor_amounts`** (they previously handled only `proposed_vendor_ids`, predating the awarded arrays). **The two arrays are INDEX-ALIGNED, so they are rebuilt TOGETHER** — merge substitutes the id and **SUMS** amounts when the target is already co-awarded on the same WP (keeping the earliest position); delete drops the position from **both** arrays via `unnest … with ordinality` + a `filter (where …)` aggregate. **Never use `array_remove` on the ids alone** — that shifts the arrays against each other and reattributes every later vendor's money. Each block is guarded by an `information_schema` column check like the existing ones.
- **`merge_vendors` aborted on `vendor_rates_vendor_wp_uidx` (23505) — found by actually running the cleanup, not by reading.** The function reassigns `vendor_rates` with a plain UPDATE, but `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_rates_wp_link_fix.sql` added `UNIQUE(vendor_id, wp_id)` to that table **after** the function was written, so merging two vendors that both hold a rate for the same WP failed — **which also breaks the normal Merge Duplicates tool**, not just the cleanup. Fixed with the same delete-collisions-then-reassign shape the function already used for `vendor_bids` (`wp_id IS NULL` manual rows are unconstrained and always move). **Re-run `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_merge.sql`.**
- **Vendor analytics: `awarded_vendor_ids` is now AUTHORITATIVE when it resolves.** `awardedOf()` also merged in the free-text `contractor` split on `/` — and **424 directory vendors have a slash in their name**, so such a name shredded into unresolvable fragments, added phantom vendor keys, flipped `haveAmts` false and silently replaced each vendor's real negotiated amount with an **even split**. It now falls through to the FK/free-text path only when the ids resolve to nothing (legacy rows, or all ids dangling).
- **Detail-modal editors are gated on `_canManage`** (Overview Save, the Products/Certifications/Personnel/Rates add-forms, and every row delete). RLS already rejected those writes for `viewer`/`viewer_budget`, so this only removes controls that could exclusively produce permission errors. Helpers `_lockDetailInputs(id)` / `_stripRowDeletes(id)` run after the relevant renders — **call them from any new detail section you add.**
- **New admin-only Data Tool "Remove Exact Duplicates"** (`openDupModal`/`renderDupList`/`runDupCleanup`, backed by `VendorDb.findExactDuplicateGroups(vendors, activeIds)` + `mergeExactDuplicates(groups, onProgress)`). Groups by **exact normalized name** (trim + collapse whitespace + lowercase) so a suggestion is never a judgement call — unlike the fuzzy Merge tool, which **cannot** clear damage at this scale (it caps at 150 shown groups, suppresses large clusters and excludes garbled records). Canonical row per name: `approved > claimed login > has trade_categories > has activity > oldest > lowest id`. Folds duplicates in via the `merge_vendors` RPC so children/WP links/trades are reassigned, never dropped. Admin-gated because that RPC rejects everyone else. Menu badge via `updateDupBadge()`.
- **Cleanup RUN against production 2026-08-12:** 553 groups previewed → **471 merged, 1,175 vendor rows removed (3,021 → 1,846)** in ~2 min, with **bids 574→574, rates 573→573, products 569→569, WP vendor links 324→324 and zero dangling references** — nothing lost. The remaining **82 groups (221 rows)** are exactly those blocked by the `vendor_rates` constraint bug above; re-run the migration, then run the tool again to finish. Side effect: the Merge suggestion pass went from ~1,493ms to ~219ms.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-19** (was: every vendor picker in the app returns nothing) — `searchApprovedVendors` no longer filters on `status='approved'`, and new vendors are written `approved`. Original finding follows.
- **(historical) every vendor picker in the app currently returns nothing.** All 1,846 vendors are `pending_review` (**0 approved**), and `searchApprovedVendors()` filters `status='approved'` — so the WP-form Awarded Vendor + Proposed Vendors comboboxes, the Status-Tracker award modal and the BidWidget vendor search all come back empty, pushing officers into "+ Add as a new vendor" and **re-creating the duplicates that were just cleaned up**. Fix is a business decision (bulk-approve the imported directory via Select all matching → Approve), so it was left for the user.
- **Merge collisions are TWO-DIRECTIONAL — source-vs-source, not just source-vs-target (2026-08-12).** After the `vendor_rates` fix above, one 5-copy group still failed with 23505: the cleanup only deleted source rows colliding with the TARGET, but when several sources are folded in at once, **two SOURCES can each hold a rate/bid for the same WP and collide with each other** the moment both move onto the target. `merge_vendors` now drops a source row if the target has one for that WP **or another source does** (keeping the lowest id, so the pick is stable) — applied to `vendor_rates` AND `vendor_bids`, which had the same blind spot since it was written. Belt-and-braces, **`mergeExactDuplicates` also folds ONE source per RPC call**, so each source de-duplicates against a target that already absorbed the previous one and the clash cannot arise even on a database where the hardened function hasn'''t been re-run. **When adding a child table with a UNIQUE(vendor_id, …) constraint, handle both collision directions in merge_vendors.**
- **CLEANUP COMPLETE (2026-08-12): 3,021 → 1,625 vendors, 0 exact-duplicate groups left, 0 dangling references** (`vendor_id`, `proposed_vendor_ids`, `awarded_vendor_ids` all clean), WP vendor links 324 and products 569 unchanged throughout. `vendor_bids` 574→394 and `vendor_rates` 573→393: those are the bid/rate rows that duplicate vendor copies had duplicated for the SAME (vendor, WP) — by construction a row is only ever deleted when another row for that WP survives, so per-WP coverage is preserved (284 WPs with bids / 283 with rates).
- **DONE — `migrations/2026-08-10_wp_proposed_vendor_ids.sql` has now been run** (2026-08-12, verified by a live round-trip on the DEMO sandbox: the array writes and reads back). The WP form'''s Proposed Vendors chips now persist real vendor ids, not just the denormalized text.
- **Split was SILENTLY LOSSY — fixed 2026-08-12.** `_executeSplit` created the segment vendors and then called `deleteVendorCascade` on the original, which **deletes that vendor'''s bids, rates, products, certifications and personnel and nulls its `work_packages.vendor_id` links** — and **104 of the 394 remaining garbled records carry exactly that data** (the confirm dialog even promised the deletion). The original is now folded into the **FIRST segment'''s vendor via `mergeVendors`**, which reassigns every child row, WP link and trade category and still removes the garbled row; the confirm text says where the data goes instead. Staff move anything that actually belonged to a different segment from that vendor'''s profile afterward — no heuristic can know which segment owned it.
- **Post-migration verification (2026-08-12), all live against production:** the hardened `merge_vendors` was proven with a synthetic **source-vs-source** collision (two sources holding a rate on the same WP merged into one target) — previously 23505, now collapses to a single rate on the target. The **index-aligned awarded-array scrub** was proven both ways on the DEMO sandbox: deleting the MIDDLE vendor of a 3-way co-award `[100,200,300]` leaves `[100,300]` correctly aligned (a naive `array_remove` would have left 3 amounts against 2 ids), and merging a co-awarded vendor into another already on the same WP **collapses to one entry with the amounts SUMMED (400)**. `proposed_vendor_ids` round-trips. The `lower(invite_email)` unique index **is now enforced** (case-insensitively) — it applied on this migration run because the duplicate rows it previously choked on were gone, so that audit item self-resolved via the cleanup.
- **`merge_vendors` now DEDUPS the child rows it reassigns (2026-08-12).** Reassigning `vendor_products`/`vendor_personnel`/`vendor_certifications` left the target holding the same row once per merged copy — the "doesn'''t dedup merged child rows" limitation noted in the first audit, which the 1,396-row cleanup then actually triggered: **178 duplicate product rows across 82 vendors**. All three child tables are now deduped on the target after the reassign (same trim+collapse-whitespace+lowercase key used elsewhere, lowest id wins). The 178 existing rows were cleaned client-side: **products 569 → 391, 0 duplicate groups left, 282 vendors still hold products.** Rates/bids were already covered by their unique constraint.
- **Read-only gating had a hole — `_lockDetailInputs` ran BEFORE `buildDetailTradeChips()`** injected the 10 trade checkboxes, so a `viewer`/`viewer_budget` could still toggle them (nothing persisted — Save was already hidden — but the affordance was live and it mutated `detailTrades`). Lock moved after the chips are built. **Any control injected AFTER a render must be locked after it too.** Verified live by simulating `_canManage=false`: 17/17 overview inputs disabled, 10/10 trade boxes disabled, no Save, 0 of 4 add-forms — and unchanged for staff.
- **Analytics attribution fix verified** against the shipped source: a slash-named directory vendor awarded via `awarded_vendor_ids` + amount now receives the **full amount as a single entry** (previously the free-text `contractor` split added phantom keys and forced an even split). The legacy free-text path still splits a garbled `A / B / C` contractor into its constituent companies — that is **intended** (it credits the real companies and is why the Split tool exists), not a regression.
- **Buyback exact-amount mode confirmed end-to-end (2026-08-12)** now that `migrations/2026-08-11_wp_buyback.sql` has been run: `buyback_amount` persists, `buybackValue`/`effectiveAwardedCost` return ₱4.0M/₱7.5M on an ₱11.5M award, and that WP'''s savings move ₱500K → ₱4.5M.
- **Test data removed:** the last test vendor (`Test Vendor Co.`) was deleted after confirming it carried no products/certs/personnel/rates/bids, no WP links and no login. **Directory is now 1,624 vendors, and every remaining `invite_email` is a `@no-invite.local` placeholder** — so the invite/portal flow has never been exercised against a real address.
- **Deploy guards now WARN instead of stripping silently (2026-08-12) — and that was chosen over "hardening" them.** The ~10 `_isMissing*Col` guards decide from free-text Postgres errors that a column is absent, drop it, and let the write report success. **The silence is the hazard, not the string matching:** both bugs these guards have actually caused were OVER-matches that discarded columns the DB really had (`/vendor_id/` also matching a `proposed_vendor_ids` error; `_stripBuyback` dropping all three buyback fields when only `buyback_amount` was missing) — rewriting the regexes would not have caught either, but seeing the drop would. **`_warnDropped()`** names the dropped columns, states they were NOT saved, flags that a fire for an already-migrated column means the GUARD is wrong rather than the schema, and echoes the Postgres text; wired into `_stripOptional` (all three WP write paths) and the three insert-path fallbacks that bypass it. No behavioural change. **Deliberately NOT done:** rewriting the matching logic (every guarded migration is now applied, so all these paths are dormant — editing working write paths would add more risk than it removes) and per-instance `BidWidget` state (only one WP-detail panel or vendor-detail modal can be open at a time and the two mount points live on different pages, so the shared state is unreachable). **If you add another optional column, the guard is fine — just make sure its strip flows through `_warnDropped`.**
- **OPEN — was: `migrations/2026-08-10_wp_proposed_vendor_ids.sql` never run** (verified: the column does not exist), so the WP form's Proposed Vendors chip picker persists only the free text — the deploy guard strips the column and the save reports success. That ID-linked feature is inert until the migration runs. Same for `buyback_amount` (`migrations/2026-08-11_wp_buyback.sql`).
- **Also noted, not actioned:** the `unique index on lower(invite_email)` never applied (its migration only `NOTICE`s when duplicates pre-exist, and two "Test Vendor Co." rows share `test@vendor.com`), so which vendor row an invite claim stamps is ambiguous; 2 test vendors still live; 424 garbled slash-named records await the Split tool.

### Vendor Management — end-to-end audit fixes (2026-08-10)

A 3-surface audit (DB/migrations, `vendors.html`, Phase 2b cross-page) produced these fixes:
- **HIGH — `merge_vendors`/`delete_vendor_cascade` authorization was a deny-list** (`r in ('vendor','viewer')`) that let read-only `viewer_budget` + all write roles bypass the admin-only `vendors_delete` RLS policy via the SECURITY DEFINER RPCs. Changed both to an **allow-list `r not in ('super_admin','admin')` → reject** (matches `vendors_delete`). In `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_merge.sql` — **re-run it**.
- **MED — merge/delete left dangling ids in `work_packages.proposed_vendor_ids uuid[]`** (no FK). Both RPCs now scrub the array (merge substitutes source→target with dedup; delete `array_remove`s), each guarded by an `information_schema.columns` existence check so the RPC still works pre-`migrations/2026-08-10_wp_proposed_vendor_ids.sql`.
- **MED — `vendor_bids.vendor_id` had no `ON DELETE`** (every sibling child table cascades). `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_merge.sql` now `drop/add`s the FK with `on delete cascade`.
- **MED — literal NUL byte** in `vendors.html` `_splitOnSuffixRuns` (`const marker = '\x00'` written as a raw 0x00) made the whole file register as **binary** (broke `grep`/diff). Replaced with the plain-text escape `'\u0001'` (still a non-space sentinel; functionally identical).
- **LOW — `db.js upsertRate`** fallback only caught a missing `wp_id` COLUMN; now also catches a missing/mismatched ON CONFLICT **constraint** (`/on conflict|unique or exclusion constraint/i`) — the partial-deploy state where `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_rates_wp_link.sql` ran but not the `_fix`.
- **LOW — backfill button feedback**: `backfillFromWPs()` referenced a dead id `#btn-backfill-vendor-data` (the trigger moved into the id-less Data Tools dropdown, which closes on click). Removed the dead spinner code; feedback is the `AppNotify` toast + a new **`_backfillRunning`** re-entrancy guard so it can't be double-triggered.
- **LOW — `wp-form.html` reconcile** now guards `typeof VendorDb !== 'undefined'` (matches `project.html`) so a db.js load failure can't throw before the `.catch` attaches and make a good save look failed.
- **LOW — WP-detail Edit/Delete** were gated on `!isViewer` (`__hideBudget`), so read-only `viewer_budget` saw them (server denied anyway). Now `!window.__isViewer` on both `index.html`/`project.html` — the bid-mount stays on `__hideBudget` (bids are cost, `viewer_budget` may see them).
- **LOW — `vendors.invite_email` uniqueness**: `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_merge.sql` adds a `unique index on lower(invite_email)`, wrapped in a `DO`/`exception` block so pre-existing duplicates only `NOTICE` instead of aborting the re-run.
- **Left as documented fragility (not changed):** deploy-guards parse free-text PG error strings; `merge_vendors` doesn't dedup merged child rows (products/personnel/certs); `BidWidget` single-instance assumption; `openDetailModal` async double-click race.

### Vendor Management guide — `assets/js/vendor-guide.js` (2026-08-10)

A dedicated **staff-facing slide-deck guide** for `vendors.html`, replacing the generic WPM `onboarding.js` tour on that page (the "?" button there previously opened the portfolio/project tour, which was irrelevant to Vendor Management). Self-contained in the same pattern as `onboarding.js`/`patch-notice.js`: injects its own `#vg-css` (theme-aware via `--surface`/`--text-*`/`--mw-red`), builds its own modal DOM, keyboard nav (←/→/Esc), progress dots, and **self-injects the topbar `#btn-guide` "?" button** before `#user-bar` (idempotent — only this file loads it on vendors.html, so no clash with onboarding's identical injector; it reuses onboarding's `.btn-guide` CSS when present, else adds a minimal copy). 8 slides: overview → finding vendors (cards/table/search/pagination) → approving → adding (Add Vendor + invite link) → Data Tools (import/backfill/merge/split) → bulk actions → the profile tabs (Products&Services with Materials/Labor/Service, certs, personnel, rates, bid history) → done. `window.VendorGuide.{open, close, maybeAutoOpen}`. **Wiring (`vendors.html`):** the `onboarding.js` `<script>` was swapped for `vendor-guide.js?v=…`, and `requireLogin` calls `VendorGuide.maybeAutoOpen(profile.id)` (auto-opens once per user, `localStorage['wpm_vendorguide_<uid>']`, ~800ms). **GOTCHA fixed during build:** a header comment containing `--text-*/--mw-red` prematurely closed the `/* */` block comment (the `*/`) → SyntaxError; reworded to avoid `*/` inside comments. The profile-menu "Restart tour" still falls back to `onboarding.html` on vendors.html (Onboarding isn't loaded there) — acceptable; the "?" button is the primary entry.

### Vendor directory — bulk select (2026-08-10)

Triage the ~900 imported/pending vendors at scale. **Checkboxes on every card + table row** (gated on `_canManage` = write roles only, i.e. NOT `viewer`/`viewer_budget`) feed a persistent **`_selectedVendors` Set** that survives pagination, filtering, and card/table view switches (selection is by id, not by rendered element). A **bulk bar** (`#vendorBulkBar`) appears in the count row whenever ≥1 is selected: **N selected · Select all matching · Approve · Reject · Delete · Clear**.
- **Select-all master checkbox** (`#vendorSelAll`, next to the count) selects/deselects the current PAGE (`toggleSelectPage` over `_pageIds`, which `renderVendorList` refreshes each render); **"Select all matching"** (`bulkSelectAllFiltered`) selects the entire current `_vendorFiltered()` set across all pages. The master checkbox shows `indeterminate` when the page is partially selected. `_syncBulkUI()` (called at the end of `renderVendorList` + on every toggle) drives the bar visibility, count text, and master-checkbox state; a single row toggle updates just that card/row's `.selected`/`.row-selected` highlight without a full re-render.
- **Actions**: `bulkApprove`/`bulkReject` → **`VendorDb.bulkSetVendorStatus(ids, status)`** (chunked `.in()` update, 200/batch, audit-stamped); `bulkDelete` → **`VendorDb.bulkDeleteVendors(ids)`** → **`delete_vendors_cascade(uuid[])`** RPC (added to `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_merge.sql` — **re-run it**; admin-only allow-list + the same cascade + `proposed_vendor_ids` scrub as the single-delete RPC, chunked 200/batch, falls back to looping the single RPC if the bulk one isn't deployed). All three confirm first, clear the selection, and `loadAll()`.
- **Permission gating**: checkboxes + bar only render for `_canManage`; the **Delete button is removed for non-admins** (`_canBulkDelete = AppAuth.isAdmin`) since the RPC rejects them anyway. `_initBulkUI()` (once, in `requireLogin`) also strips the table's leading checkbox `<th class="vth-check">` when `!_canManage` so the header stays 5 columns (rows omit the leading `<td>` too); with manage rights it's 6 columns and the empty-state colspan is 6.

### Vendor offering type — Materials / Labor / Service (2026-08-10)

Vendors supply materials, labor, or services — but `vendor_products` modeled every offering as a "product". Added a nullable **`item_type`** column (`migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_product_type.sql`, run once — additive, no default/CHECK) using the **SAME three values the WP form's Type field uses: `Materials` / `Labor` / `Service`** (per user: match the WP form, not a Product/Service binary).
- **`db.js`**: `products.add`/`products.update` wrapped to **strip `item_type` and retry** on a missing-column error (deploy-safe, same pattern as the other guards); `getAllVendorProducts()` switched to `select('*')` so `item_type` flows through when present but still works pre-migration; **`backfillVendorDataFromWPs`** now selects `type_of_works` and copies it straight into the product's `item_type` (only when it's one of the three) — the WP already stores exactly this value.
- **UI**: a **Type `<select>`** (Materials/Labor/Service, defaults Materials) added to the offering add-form in BOTH `vendor-portal.html` (`#np-type`, `addProduct`) and `vendors.html`'s staff detail (`#dnp-type`, `addDetailProduct`); each offering row shows the type after the category (`category · Type`); the directory product-search text (`_vendorProductText`) now includes `item_type` so searching "labor"/"service" matches. `vendors.html` detail section title stays "Products & Services".

### Action Center moved out of the main tab bar — super_admin-only via the profile menu (2026-08-10, `index.html`)

Per user request, the Action Center tab button (`switchTab('actions')`) was **removed from `#view-tabs`** entirely — it's now reachable ONLY from the topbar profile dropdown (`renderUserBar()` in `db.js`), gated on `profile.role === 'super_admin'` (not `admin` or any other role). The menu item (icon + label matching the removed button's style) calls `window.switchTab('actions')` directly — no button, no tab highlight, just opens the same `#tab-actions` content. `renderActionsTab`/`ALL_TABS`/`_rendered.actions`/the `#tab-actions` div are all **unchanged** — `'actions'` stays a valid key in `ALL_TABS` so the tab-content show/hide loop (string-keyed, not position-dependent) still works when invoked this way; only its clickable entry point moved.

### Fuzzy core-name matching for vendor↔WP linkage (2026-08-11)

The vendor detail **Work Packages** section links a vendor to a WP when they're the awarded OR proposed vendor — for historical WPs, by **name-matching the directory name against the WP's free-text `contractor`/`proposed_vendors`**. A directory name often carries extras the WP text doesn't (a leading `*`, a `(LOCAL)`/`(IMPORTED)` tag, trailing legal/generic words), so `"*JANGHO (LOCAL)"` never matched a WP that says `"JANGHO"`. New **`window.VendorMatch`** (defined in `db.js`, in the `VendorDb` closure): `core(s)` strips parentheticals + markers + a `_CORE_STOP` word set (Inc/Corp/Construction/Trading/Services/Philippines/…) to a comparable core; `wordContains(hay,needle)` is a **whole-word** run test guarded to **≥4-char** needles (so a tiny fragment can't match half the portfolio); `coreMatch(vendorCore, wpName)` = core-equal OR either is a whole-word run of the other. `getWorkPackagesForVendor` adds a **core-name `ilike` gather** (alongside the exact-name one) so variant WPs surface as candidates, and `vendors.html`'s `renderDetailWps` precise filter uses `VendorMatch.coreMatch` as the fallback after exact `nm()` equality. **Scoped to the WP-linkage display only** — the analytics money aggregation still uses exact normalized-name resolution (fuzzy matching there could mis-credit spend across distinct vendors). **Create vs. link:** Import from WPs (and Split, and manual add) CREATE a directory row for every distinct awarded/proposed name it sees — that's how `"*JANGHO (LOCAL)"` got INTO the directory — but the stored `vendor_id` back-link is only stamped on an exact single-award match, and proposed-only vendors get no stored link at all; so a directory row existing ≠ a stored WP link. This fuzzy matcher recovers the *display* linkage without stamping anything.

### 1000-row cap fix (paginated reads) + sticky progress notifications (2026-08-11)

- **`getVendors()`, `getAllApprovedWPs`/`getApprovedWPs`/`getApprovedWPsForProjects`/`getAllWPs`/`getAllWPsForAdmin`/`getAllWPsForProjects`, and `getAllVendorProducts()` were silently capped at PostgREST's default 1000 rows** (plain `.select()` with no `.range()`) — the "vendor count shows exactly 1000" symptom, and (if the portfolio exceeds 1000 approved WPs) a silent under-count across every dashboard + the vendor analytics. New **`_pagedSelect(makeQuery)`** helper in `db.js` loops `.range(from, from+999)` until a short page, concatenating all rows; `makeQuery` MUST return a FRESH builder each call (a PostgREST builder is single-use once awaited). All the above reads now go through it. When you add a new "load everything" read of a table that can exceed 1000 rows, use `_pagedSelect`.
- **`AppNotify.progress(msg, opts)`** (new, `ui.js`) — a **sticky** "working…" toast (spinner via `.spin` on a `ti-loader-2` icon, `ttl:0` so it never auto-dismisses) returning a handle: `.update(msg)` (change text mid-run), `.done(msg,opts)` → **sticky** green success (stays until dismissed), `.fail(msg,opts)` → red error, `.close()`. Backed by a new `progress` KIND + an `opts.sticky` flag in `show()` (suppresses the auto-close timeout). Use it for long Data-Tools actions so the user gets an unmistakable running→finished signal instead of an auto-dismissing info toast that's easy to miss. Wired into `vendors.html`'s **Import from WPs / Backfill / Normalize Trades** (the long loops) — spinner while running, then a persistent ✓/✗ summary. Import/Backfill also `.update('Refreshing directory…')` before `loadAll()`; Normalize updates a `N/total` counter every 25 rows.

### Awarded Vendor — multi-vendor chip field + per-vendor amounts (2026-08-11)

Follow-up to the co-award fixes below: the WP-form **Awarded Vendor** field is now a **multi-select chip combobox** (a WP can be co-awarded to several vendors), each vendor carries their **own negotiated amount**, and awarded vendors get precise ID linkage via new array columns — so analytics resolves them by ID (and their real per-vendor spend) regardless of how the free-text `contractor` reads.

**Per-vendor negotiated amounts (2026-08-11, before the migration was run):** a co-award is NOT an even split — each vendor is awarded their **own negotiated amount**, and the WP's **Awarded Cost is the SUM**. So the model stores per-vendor amounts, not just ids.

- **Two new columns** (`migrations/2026-08-11_wp_awarded_vendor_ids.sql`, run once): `work_packages.awarded_vendor_ids uuid[]` (the co-awarded vendors, mirrors `proposed_vendor_ids`) + **`awarded_vendor_amounts numeric[]`** (each vendor's negotiated amount, **index-aligned** with the ids). Additive; `vendor_id` = the **first/primary** awarded vendor and `contractor` (newline-joined names) are both still written for back-compat; `awarded_cost`/`total_awarded` remain the WP total (= sum of the amounts). **db.js deploy-safe guard** `_isMissingAwardedVendorIdsCol` matches `/awarded_vendor_(ids|amounts)/` and `_stripAwardedVendorIds` strips BOTH; folded into `_stripOptional` + the combined retry condition of `submitWP`/`updateWP`/`updateWPDirect` (precise match — can't cross-trigger with `_isMissingVendorCol`'s `/vendor_id\b/` or the proposed guard). `mapWP`/`unmap` pass both through (spread; not generated). `getWorkPackagesForVendor` selects both and adds `awarded_vendor_ids.cs.{id}` to its `.or()` (deploy-safe fallback strips both new cols).
- **WP form (`wp-form.html`)** — the single-select combobox was **replaced by a chip combobox mirroring Proposed Vendors** (`_avChips` = `[{id,name,amount}]`, `#av-wrap`/`#av-chips`/`#av-input`/`#av-pop`; `_avAdd`/`_avRemoveAt`/`_avRenderResults`/`_avAddNew`/`_avLoadFromWP`; `initVendorCombo()` rewired). `#f-contractor`/`#f-vendor-id` are **hidden mirrors** (`_avSyncHidden`: names `\n`-joined / first linked id) so existing readers keep working.
  - **Per-vendor amount UX** (`_avIsMulti`/`_avTotal`/`_avSyncCost`): with **≥2 vendors** each chip renders an **amount input** (`.av-row`/`.av-amt`) and **Awarded Cost (`#f-contract`) becomes the read-only auto-sum** (`.av-cost-locked`); with **≤1 vendor** the editable Awarded Cost field is authoritative and the single vendor's amount is taken from it at save. Transition 1→2 seeds the first chip's amount from the current Awarded Cost; 2→1 writes the remaining chip's amount back into the (re-enabled) field. Amount edits update the total + Awarded Cost **without re-rendering** (would drop focus) and call `updateVariance`/`_updateBcbDeltas`. Save writes `awarded_vendor_ids`, `awarded_vendor_amounts` (multi → each chip's amount; single → `[getNum('f-contract')]`), and `awarded_cost` (already the sum via `#f-contract`). A co-award save **requires every awarded vendor to have a positive amount** (validation, scroll-focuses `av-input`). Edit-load (`_avLoadFromWP`) rehydrates chips + amounts from the aligned arrays. Autosave draft snapshots/restores `av:_avChips`.
- **`reconcileBidsOnAward(wpId, awards, totalAmount)`** now accepts **`[{vendorId, amount}]`** (per-vendor negotiated amounts → each bid's `final_amount`) OR a single id / id-array (splits `totalAmount` evenly — the Status-Tracker fallback). Marks each winner `awarded`, everything else on the WP `lost`. wp-form passes the `[{vendorId,amount}]` form.
- **`backfillVendorDataFromWPs`** now **prefers `awarded_vendor_amounts`** (real per-vendor amounts) over the even split when present — so a re-run never clobbers a correctly-authored co-award's bids/rates with an even split; legacy contractor-text co-awards still even-split (no breakdown exists). Its select gained both columns (deploy-safe retry without them).
- **Status Tracker award modal (`project.html`)** stays a single-vendor quick-award (only opens for a WP with no vendor yet, so it can't clobber a co-award), now writes `awarded_vendor_ids:[id]` + `awarded_vendor_amounts:[cost]`. Full co-award authoring is via the WP form.
- **Analytics + detail modal (`vendors.html`)** — `awardedOf(w)` returns `{key,name,amount}` and the money block credits each vendor their **own amount** when all present (Σ = WP cost), else even-splits; both resolve `awarded_vendor_ids` (precise) before FK + slash-aware name split. The detail Work Packages list shows **this vendor's own share** (labeled "(share)") for a co-award, not the WP total.
- **Not scrubbed on vendor delete/merge yet:** the `delete_vendor_cascade`/`merge_vendors` RPCs scrub `proposed_vendor_ids` but not `awarded_vendor_ids`/`awarded_vendor_amounts` — low-traffic edge case, flag if it matters.

### Co-awarded vendors — analytics "(not in directory)" fix + multi-award splitting (2026-08-11)

Two related complaints: (1) the Vendor Management **Analytics** tab listed vendors as **"(not in directory)"** even after running **Import from WPs**; (2) some work packages are **awarded to more than one vendor** (co-award), which wasn't considered anywhere. Both stem from the awarded `contractor` field: a co-award holds several names, and — per the live data (screenshot) — they're joined with a **slash** (`Vendor A / Vendor B`), a delimiter `_splitVendors` (`/[\r\n;|]+/`, comma/space/slash all excluded to protect "Company, Inc.") never split.

- **Root cause of the "(not in directory)" label:** `renderVendorAnalytics()` (`vendors.html`) resolved the awarded vendor **only via the `vendor_id` FK** and keyed everything else by the raw `nm(contractor)` string — so a vendor that DOES exist in the directory (created by the import) but whose WP was never FK-linked always read "(not in directory)", and a slash-joined co-award became one bogus combined entry. **Fix:** new `awardedOf(w)` resolves each awarded name **by FK first, then BY NAME** (`byName`), falling back to the normalized name only when truly absent; spend/BCB are **split evenly across co-awarded vendors** so portfolio totals still reconcile (Σ splits = the WP's full awarded cost), each co-awarded vendor getting one win. `awardedWpTotal` now counts distinct money-awarded WPs (once), not the sum of per-vendor wins.
- **New shared splitter `window._splitAwarded(s)` in `db.js`** — like `_splitVendors` but **adds `/` to the delimiter set** (`/[\r\n;|/]+/`). Used ONLY for the awarded `contractor` field (proposed vendors keep `_splitVendors`). Wired into `importVendorsFromWPs` (creates a vendor identity row per co-awarded name; FK-links `vendor_id` only when the WP has exactly ONE awarded name, since the FK can't hold a co-award) and `backfillVendorDataFromWPs` (`resolveVendors` + the awarded products/bids/rates loop, which now credits EACH co-awarded vendor and splits bid `final_amount`/rate evenly). `vendors.html` has a local `splitAw` mirror in both the analytics and the detail-modal Work Packages section (`renderDetailWps`'s `isAwd` was full-string `===`, so a co-award never showed for either constituent — now `splitAw(contractor).some(...)`).
- **Comma-joined / run-on garbles stay unresolved by design** (`rp-ra, builders, jcap…`, `first wintaf international inc. goldstrong corporation…`, `various supplier`) — comma/space can't be safely split (legit names contain them) and generic placeholders aren't real vendors; those remain the **Split tool**'s job in the directory, not the analytics'.
- **Follow-up (done 2026-08-11):** the WP-form **Awarded Vendor** field was subsequently converted to a multi-vendor chip selector with an `awarded_vendor_ids` column and multi-winner `reconcileBidsOnAward` — see the "Awarded Vendor — multi-vendor chip field" section above. The fixes in THIS section still handle READING existing slash-delimited co-award strings for historical rows.

**Real pre-existing bug found and fixed in the same pass:** the `.view-tab` active-highlight toggle (`switchTab()`) matched buttons to `ALL_TABS` **by DOM position** (`ALL_TABS[i] === tab`) — but the `#view-tabs` bar has a **"Cycle Time" button with no corresponding `ALL_TABS` entry** (that feature isn't built yet, per the Vendor Analytics roadmap), so every button from "Cycle Time" onward was ALREADY silently mis-highlighting before this change (clicking Backlog, for instance, was testing `ALL_TABS[3]==='backlog'` against whatever button actually sat at DOM index 3). Removing the Action Center button would have shifted every subsequent button up one more slot and made this worse. Fixed at the root instead of patched around: the toggle now extracts the tab key straight from each button's own `onclick="switchTab('key')"` attribute via regex and matches on that — **DOM position no longer matters at all**, so any future button add/remove/reorder in `#view-tabs` can't misalign the highlight again. `ALL_TABS` itself is untouched (still needed for the tab-content show/hide + `_rendered` reset loops, both already string-keyed and unaffected by this). **The "Cycle Time" button was later REMOVED entirely (2026-08-11, `index.html`)** — it was an unwired placeholder (no `ALL_TABS` entry, no `#tab-cycle` content div, no handler) for a never-built feature; deleting it is safe precisely because of the regex-based highlight matching above. The comment in `switchTab()` that referenced it as the example was updated to cite Action Center instead.

### All Work Packages grid — Awarded Vendor + Proposed Vendors are searchable multi-vendor pickers (`review.html`, 2026-09-01)

Both vendor fields in the Excel grid were plain `type:'text'` cells, so an officer typed a vendor name
by hand — no directory search, and nothing linked the row to a `vendors` record. They are now
**`type:'vendors'`**, a new column type whose editor is a **searchable multi-select popover over the
vendor directory**, matching what `wp-form.html`'s Awarded Vendor / Proposed Vendors chip comboboxes
already do. Both support **any number of vendors** (a WP can be co-awarded, and proposed vendors are
plural by nature).

- **Column defs** carry the side-columns they own, so nothing is hardcoded per field:
  `proposed_vendors` → `{type:'vendors', idsKey:'proposed_vendor_ids'}`; `contractor` →
  `{type:'vendors', idsKey:'awarded_vendor_ids', amtsKey:'awarded_vendor_amounts',
  primaryKey:'vendor_id', reqIfAwarded:true}`. Both widened to 190px.
- **Editor** — `_xlBeginEdit` dispatches `type:'vendors'` to **`_xlOpenVendorPop`** before the normal
  input/select branch (after the `_xlEditable` scope check, so a locked row still can't be edited).
  Chips for the current picks, a debounced `VendorDb.searchApprovedVendors`, **accreditation badges**
  on every result plus a **⚠ on a problematic awarded chip** (same guardrail as the WP form), and an
  inline **"Add … as a new vendor"** (`quickCreateVendor`) for a vendor not in the directory yet.
  ↑/↓ + Enter pick, Esc / outside-click / Done close. Typing a character on a selected cell opens the
  popover with that character seeded, like every other editor here.
- **⚠️ Every add/remove stages IMMEDIATELY, but the whole popover session is ONE undo batch** —
  a single Ctrl+Z takes back the entire vendor edit rather than one chip at a time.
- **⚠️ THE INVARIANT: `awarded_vendor_ids` and `awarded_vendor_amounts` are INDEX-ALIGNED and are
  always rebuilt TOGETHER** (`_xlSetVendorChips`), over the **linked** chips only — an unlinked
  free-text chip goes into the names but never into the arrays, or the two would desync and every
  later vendor's money would be reattributed. An amount already on file for a vendor that STAYS in
  the list is preserved (matched by id, so it survives a reorder); a newly added vendor gets `null`,
  which the analytics reads as an even split. **Per-vendor negotiated amounts are AUTHORED in the WP
  form** — the grid deliberately doesn't collect them in a cell, and the popover footer says so.
- **⚠️ A vendor NAME arriving through a text path carries no id, so the stored ids would keep pointing
  at the vendor that USED to be there** — which the dashboards and vendor analytics treat as
  authoritative. **`_xlRelinkVendorIds`** (called from `_xlSet` for any `type:'vendors'` column)
  re-derives them from the name cache on every paste / Ctrl+D fill / header-▾ set-all. **Exact
  normalized-name match only, and a name two vendors share resolves to `false` and links to NOTHING**
  — the same refuse-to-guess rule the vendor analytics uses, because a wrong link mis-credits award
  money to another company. Never make this fuzzy.
- **`_xlVendorName` / `_xlVendorByName` / `_xlVendorAccred`** are a lazily-grown cache, never the whole
  ~1,600-row directory: `_xlLoadVendorNames()` resolves (chunked 150, `getVendorsByIds`) just the ids
  the loaded WPs reference so a linked cell shows a real name, and every search result is cached too,
  which is what later lets a pasted name resolve back to its id. `_xlWarmVendorName` looks a name up
  once before a header-▾ set-all, or a bulk apply would leave every row unlinked purely because nobody
  had searched for that vendor yet this session.
- **`xlFmt` renders a vendors cell on ONE line (`' ; '`-joined) and `xlCoerce` splits it back to
  newline-joined** — a newline inside a cell would break the tab/newline TSV that Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V and
  Excel interop are built on. **Splitting is newline/semicolon/pipe only, NEVER comma or space**, so
  `"XYZ Builders, Inc."` stays one vendor (the app-wide rule).
- **The linkage now survives the save and the cross-project copy**: `_xlDraftPayload` writes the
  side-columns for a grid-added WP (they have no cell, so without this a new row saved the NAMES and
  silently dropped the ids); `_xlSerializeRows` carries them through Copy WPs → Paste WPs (a vendor is
  global, so its id stays valid in the target project); the whole-WP TSV paste resolves what the cache
  knows. `gridSave` needed no change — it already sends `_gridEdits[id]` wholesale, and `db.js` has the
  deploy-safe strip-and-retry guards for all three columns.
- **⚠️ The popover holds an (r,c) coordinate and a live `<td>`, so it must never outlive a re-render** —
  a pick could otherwise land on a different work package. `renderGrid()` closes it on entry, and
  `_xlLoadVendorNames` skips its re-render while one is open.
- **New generic helpers worth reusing**: **`_xlStage(w, k, val, undoBatch)`** stages against a raw
  FIELD KEY rather than a grid coordinate (for columns with no cell), and **`_xlSameVal`** compares
  arrays element-wise so re-picking the same vendors stages nothing and raises no dirty flag. Both
  keep `_xlSet`'s contract: a value back at the stored one deletes the override instead of writing a
  no-op, and no empty edit record is created (which would send a blank patch).
- **`review.html` contained TWO literal NUL bytes** (the draft-group ``'\u0000new'`` sentinel written as a
  raw byte rather than an escape), which made the whole file register as **binary** to `grep`/diff — the
  same trap CLAUDE.md already records for `vendors.html`'s `_splitOnSuffixRuns`. Both are now the
  plain-text six-character escape sequence `\u0000`. **Write the escape in source; never paste the control character.**
- **Verified**: 75 assertions in a Node harness against the **verbatim extracted** shipped functions
  (splitting incl. the comma rule, the xlFmt/xlCoerce TSV round-trip, cache ambiguity, chip reads for
  ids-authoritative / legacy-text / legacy-`vendor_id` / unresolvable-id rows, the amount-alignment
  cases above, clearing, no-op re-picks, proposed-vs-awarded differences, all three relink outcomes,
  and a locked cell) — plus the popover rendered against the real `dashboard.css` in a throwaway
  repo-root `_preview_*` harness (deleted before commit): 360px wide, no overflow, correct in light AND
  dark (`--surface`/`--text-*` resolve; the unlinked chip stays dashed). **Not yet exercised against a
  live authenticated session** — the search/quick-create round-trip needs the user's own login; flag
  anything that misbehaves on first real use.
- **`review.html`-only change, so no shared `?v=` bump** (same precedent as the `vendors.html`-scoped
  work). **Not done, flag if wanted**: the grid still collects no per-vendor awarded amounts, and
  `index.html` / `project.html`'s WP-detail panels are untouched.

### Vendor Grid — Excel-style editing model (`vendors.html`, 2026-08-14)

> **Superseded in scope by the 2026-09-03 entry below**, which added the column/row/layout
> features this one left out. This section still describes the editing model correctly.

The vendor directory's view toggle is **Cards | Grid | Analytics** — the plain **Table** view was removed the same day, per explicit request, once Grid existed: it was a strict subset of what Grid does (same rows, same fields, no editing) so keeping both was pure repetition. `renderTable()`, `#vendorTableWrap`, the `#vv-table` button, and the now-dead `.v-table`/`.table-wrapper`/`.vth-check` CSS were deleted outright rather than hidden; `quickApprove`/`quickReject` (the row-level buttons Table used to expose) survive unchanged since Cards already renders them for `pending_review` vendors, and the Grid's Status cell can set `approved`/`rejected` directly.

**Grid shows every filtered vendor, unpaginated — Cards stays paginated.** `renderVendorList()` branches on `_vendorView`: for `'grid'` it passes the FULL `_vendorFiltered()` array straight to `renderVendorGrid()` (no `_VENDOR_PAGE_SIZE`/`_vendorPage` slicing) and blanks the pager, matching `review.html`'s WP grid — a plain `<table>` with a sticky header scrolls fine at thousands of rows. Cards keeps the existing 60-per-page slicing (`_vendorPage`/`_VENDOR_PAGE_SIZE`) unchanged — it was added specifically because ~900 heavy per-card DOM blocks rendering at once was the actual perf/scroll problem (see the "Vendor directory — scale redesign" entry above), a constraint a plain grid `<td>` row doesn't share. `_pageIds` (used by the page-level "select all" checkbox) is set to every filtered vendor's id in grid view, so that checkbox now means "select everyone currently shown," consistent with there being no pages left to select across.

**Grid** (`#vv-grid`, `#vendorGridPanel`) is a full spreadsheet over the current page of `_vendorFiltered()` vendors, matching `review.html`'s WP-grid feature-for-feature (built after an initial lighter cut was explicitly asked to be brought up to parity):

- **Columns (`VXL_COLS`)**: Company Name (required), Vendor Code, Status (select), Trade Categories, Contact Person/Number/Email, Address, Notes — the same fields `saveDetailOverview()`'s form already edits one vendor at a time; the grid is a bulk-edit surface over the same field set, not a new data model. **Trade Categories is a click-to-open checkbox popover** (`_vxlOpenTradePop`, reuses the page's `TRADES` roster) rather than free text, to avoid typos against the canonical trade list; copy/paste/fill still treat it as a plain comma-joined string, matching how the popover itself stages its value.
- **Full mouse model**: click a cell to select it; **drag to select a range**; **Shift+click** extends from the current cell; a **corner fill-handle** (`#vxl-fill-handle`, synced via `_vxlSyncFill()`) drag-fills down, repeating the source block Excel-style (`_vxlFillValue`/`_vxlCommitFill`), with a live preview (`.vxl-fillprev`) while dragging.
- **Full keyboard model** (`_vxlKeydown`, wired once at module scope, not per-render): Arrows move; **Ctrl+Arrow jumps to the edge of the data block** (`_vxlJumpEdge`, same walk-through-empty/walk-through-filled logic as the WP grid); Shift+Arrow extends the range; Tab/Enter/F2 edit or advance; **Delete/Backspace clears the whole selected range**; **Ctrl+D fills down** from the top row of the range; **Ctrl+A selects the whole grid**; **Ctrl+Z undoes** the last batch.
- **TSV copy/paste** (`_vxlCopy`/`_vxlPaste`, `document` `copy`/`paste` listeners): copying a range serializes tab/newline-delimited text (Excel interop both ways); pasting places the clipboard block at the current selection, clamped to the grid's bounds, and reports how many cells were rejected (blank Company Name, or a Status value that doesn't normalize to one of the 4 valid states).
- **Undo stack** (`_vxlUndo`, an array of edit-batches `[{id,k,had,prev}]`, mirroring `review.html`'s `_xlUndo`): every staging path (single edit, fill, paste, column-range delete) pushes one batch; `vxlUndo()` pops and reverses it, re-deriving the affected cells' display/dirty/missing state via `_vxlRefreshRow`. Cleared on Save and Discard.
- **A range-selection readout** (`#vxl-sel-info`, shown only for multi-cell ranges — "`N cells selected (R rows × C cols)`") gives an unambiguous confirmation independent of the `.vxl-range` highlight, same rationale as the equivalent readout in `review.html`.
- **Staged edits, not live writes**: `_vxlEdits = {vendorId: {field: value}}`, painted amber (`.vxl-dirty`); nothing hits the DB until **Save changes** (`vxlSave()`, loops `VendorDb.updateVendor(id, patch)` per dirty vendor, `trade_categories` patch re-split from the comma string back to an array). **Discard** drops all staged edits + the undo stack; switching away from Grid view with unsaved edits confirms first (`_vxlDirty()` guard in `setVendorView`).
- **Required-empty highlighting**: only Company Name is `req:true` (an empty name is nonsensical for a vendor row); painted `.vxl-missing` (red inset bar), counted in a toolbar `#vxl-missing-count` readout.
- **Read-only for `viewer`/`viewer_budget`**: every write path (`_vxlSet`, `_vxlBeginEdit`, `_vxlPaste`) checks `_canManage` and no-ops otherwise, so those roles can open Grid to scan data but nothing they do stages an edit.
- **`_vxlSet(r,c,raw,undoBatch)` is the single choke point every staging path goes through** (click-edit, trade popover, fill, paste, range-delete, Ctrl+D) — it normalizes Status text (`"Approved"` → `approved`, rejects anything that isn't one of the 4 values), refuses to blank Company Name, and only pushes an undo entry when the value actually changes versus the *current effective* value (staged-if-present, else the stored one) — mirrors `review.html`'s `_xlSet` exactly, including the "no-op writes don't pollute undo" rule.
- **Not a literal copy-paste of `review.html`'s `_xl*` engine** — same function shapes and names (with a `_vxl` prefix) so the two are easy to compare, but reimplemented against `VXL_COLS`/`_vxlRows` since the WP grid's version carries WP-specific concerns (per-project edit-scope via `_xlEditable`, generated-column stripping, procurement↔award status derivation) that don't apply to a flat vendor list.
- **Bug caught and fixed before shipping (first draft, single-cell-only version)**: the cell editor's own `keydown` handler for Enter/Tab/Escape called into a commit function that reset the "currently editing" flag, but didn't `stopPropagation()` — the same keystroke then bubbled to the wrap's own `keydown` listener, which no longer saw "still editing" and reprocessed the identical Enter/Tab as a **second** navigation (double-move / instant re-open of the editor on the cell just moved to). Fixed in the rewrite by keeping `finish()` (the editor's commit/cancel closure) entirely local — it doesn't rely on a bubbling re-check at all — and still adding `e.stopPropagation()` defensively. **General lesson: when an outer handler keys off state an inner handler just cleared, either stop the bubble or don't depend on the outer handler re-evaluating that state at all.**
- Scoped entirely to `vendors.html`'s own inline `<style>`/`<script>` (CSS class prefix `vxl-`, JS function prefix `_vxl`/`vxl`) — no `db.js`/shared-asset changes, so no `?v=` bump was needed.
- **Not yet verified against a live authenticated session** (built and pushed without a logged-in preview — static-parse / no-console-error checked only, since verifying needs the user's own login). Flag anything — especially the drag/fill/copy-paste interactions, which are the highest-risk part of this build — that doesn't behave as expected on first real use.

### Vendor Grid — the REST of the parity with `review.html`'s WP grid (`vendors.html`, 2026-09-03)

The 2026-08-14 entry above was titled "full parity" but delivered the editing MODEL only
(select / edit / nav / range / fill / copy-paste / undo / staged-save). Everything that makes
the WP grid workable on a wide, dense table was still missing. Asked for by pointing at the
All Work Packages grid: *"I want the grid to follow the features already available."* Added:

- **Collapsible column SECTIONS.** `VXL_GROUPS` (Identity / Accreditation / Classification /
  Contact / Address / Notes) render as a clickable header band above the column row, exactly
  like `XL_GROUPS`. **`VXL_GROUPS_DEFAULT_COLLAPSED = ['contact','address','other']`** on a
  first visit so the grid opens at **~1,700px instead of ~3,180px** (measured); the user's own
  choice persists per user thereafter.
- **⚠️ `VVC` IS THE COORDINATE SPACE, exactly as `VC` is in `review.html`.** Every r/c
  coordinate — selection, nav bounds, copy/paste, fill, editors, dirty repaint — indexes into
  `VVC`, **never `VXL_COLS`**. Only the required-field tally (`_vxlRowMissing`) and the
  save-time draft scan read `VXL_COLS`, so **a gap hidden behind a collapsed section is still
  counted** (tested). If you add a coordinate-based lookup, use `VVC`.
- **A collapsed section keeps its `keep:true` column or, failing that, a narrow hatched
  placeholder** (`ph:true`) — without it that section's header would leave the screen and could
  never be clicked again. Verified: collapse-all still leaves all six sections clickable.
- **Row-number gutter** (`VXL_GUTTER = 42`), reddening on row hover so a row can be tracked
  across the horizontal scroll; on a draft row it becomes the `×` discard button.
- **Drag-to-resize columns** (`_vxlWidths`, persisted per user; double-click a grip to reset a
