  in when `signUp` reports the address as taken), just hygiene.

**Still open:** no email anywhere (a claimant must revisit the link to see their status); the
`?code=` QR is per-vendor but nothing generates them in bulk for a PO mail-merge; and
`users.vendor_id` is 1:1, so several people at one company each need their own approved claim.

### Vendor child data — archive, not delete (2026-09-01)

**`migrations/2026-09-01_vendor_child_soft_delete.sql`** (run once, after the other vendor migrations).
Step 1 of the plan in “Vendor self-service at scale” above — but it stands on its own, because
this was a **live exposure regardless of how anyone authenticates.**

**⚠️ THE BUG: `for all` INCLUDES DELETE.** `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_management.sql` created the
child-table write policy as `for all`, which in Postgres RLS covers SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE **and
DELETE**; `migrations/2026-08-20_vendor_accreditation_requests.sql` copied the same shape for
`vendor_documents`. So any vendor login could hard-delete every row under its own `vendor_id` —
every offering, certification, personnel record and accreditation document (the BIR 2303 and
business permit their accreditation was granted on) — plus the files behind them in the
`vendor-certs` bucket. None of those tables carry `updated_by`/`updated_at`, none had a
soft-delete, and this app has no undo anywhere, so the loss was **silent, untraceable and
total.** A compromised password, a shared login, a departing employee at the vendor or a
mis-click did exactly the same damage as a stranger. **Hardening the login gate does not fix
this; only removing the capability does.**

- **The policy split is the fix.** `%s_write` is dropped and replaced by `%s_insert` /
  `%s_update` / `%s_delete` on all four tables. Insert and update keep the
  `or vendor_id = internal.get_my_vendor_id()` own-row clause; **the DELETE policy deliberately
  has NO such clause** — staff only. `vendor_certs_storage_delete` lost the vendor branch for
  the same reason. **Do not “simplify” these back into one `for all`** — that silently restores
  the exposure, and it is exactly how it got here.
- **`archived_at` / `archived_by` / `archived_by_name`** on `vendor_products`,
  `vendor_certifications`, `vendor_personnel`, `vendor_documents`, plus a partial
  `(vendor_id) where archived_at is null` index (every list filters on it). Nullable and
  additive — NULL means live, so nothing vanished from any list the moment it ran.
- **`internal.stamp_archived()` sets `archived_by` SERVER-SIDE from `auth.uid()`**, so the
  attribution cannot be forged by the client, and clears it on restore — the same reasoning as
  `internal.vendor_edit_guard`. Definer because a vendor login can only see its OWN `users` row.
- **`db.js` `_child(table, orderCol)` gained `archive()` / `restore()`**, and `list(vendorId,
  opts)` now filters `archived_at is null` unless `opts.includeArchived`. `vendorDocuments` was
  rebased onto `_child` (it keeps its own missing-table guard and `uploaded_by` stamp).
- **⚠️ `archive()` THROWS when the column is missing — it must NEVER fall back to `remove()`.**
  A guard that quietly performs the destructive act it exists to prevent is worse than a loud
  failure (the `bulkSetAccreditation` precedent). `list()` does degrade gracefully pre-migration,
  which is safe: if the column does not exist, nothing can have been archived.
- **`remove()` is still a real hard DELETE and is retained for STAFF** — they are the recovery
  mechanism. `delete_vendor_cascade` / `merge_vendors` are SECURITY DEFINER and bypass RLS, so
  they are unaffected.
- **`vendor-portal.html`**: all four “Remove” actions call `archive()`; the page now contains
  **zero** child-table `remove()` calls and **no `deleteCertFile()`** — the uploaded file is
  deliberately retained, or a restore would hand back a row pointing at a missing file. Wording
  says staff can restore it, and `removeProduct`/`removePersonnel` gained the try/catch they
  never had.
- **`vendors.html`**: the Products / Certifications / Personnel sections load with
  `{includeArchived:true}`, split live from archived, and render archived rows in a struck-through
  `.arch-block` with who removed them, when, and a **Restore** button (gated on `_canManage`).
  **Without this the archive would just be a quieter delete** — the staff-visible restore path is
  what makes it a safety net.

**Verified:** the migration parses with libpg_query/`pglast` (8 statements, the PL/pgSQL body, and
every `format()` policy fragment with its placeholders substituted), with text assertions that the
DELETE policy has no `get_my_vendor_id` escape hatch and UPDATE still does; every inline `<script>`
block in both edited pages passes `node --check`; and a Node harness over the **verbatim extracted
shipped `_child` source** asserts 16 behaviours — including the one that matters, that a failed
archive leaves the row intact and issues no DELETE.

**⚠️ NOT RUN YET, and NOT exercised by a real vendor** (no vendor has ever logged in — see the
guard-consolidation section). The migration must be run in Supabase; until then `archive()` throws
by design and the portal reports “Cannot archive — … has not been run”.

**Still open, deliberately out of scope here:** a vendor keeps UPDATE, so it can still overwrite a
row’s contents with rubbish — soft-delete does not cover that, the **audit trail does, and that is
the next step** (`updated_at`/`updated_by` on the child tables). Staff hard-delete also remains
un-undoable.

### Vendor child data — audit trail (2026-09-01)

**`migrations/2026-09-01_vendor_child_audit_trail.sql`** (run once, **after** the soft-delete
migration — it sorts BEFORE it alphabetically, so this is the second place filename order is not
run order; see `migrations/README.md`). Step 3, the follow-on the archive change explicitly left
open.

**WHY:** soft-delete stopped a vendor DESTROYING its own child rows, but a vendor keeps UPDATE and
can overwrite any row with rubbish. **Authorization cannot fix that** — editing their own profile
is the entire point of the portal — so the answer is not another restriction but being able to see
WHAT changed and WHO changed it. `vendor_products` / `vendor_certifications` / `vendor_personnel`
carried **only `created_at`**: no `updated_at`, no actor on any operation, so "what did this
account do?" was unanswerable. `vendors` itself has had `created_by`/`updated_by`/`updated_by_name`
since Phase 2a; this brings its four child tables to the same standard.

- Adds `created_by` / `created_by_name` / `updated_at` / `updated_by` / `updated_by_name` to all
  four child tables, stamped **SERVER-SIDE from `auth.uid()`** so the actor cannot be forged — same
  reasoning as `internal.vendor_edit_guard`. `created_*` is **immutable on UPDATE** (a client that
  sends its own value is overruled), and `archived_by` is preserved on a plain update rather than
  cleared.
- **⚠️ A NEW FUNCTION NAME (`internal.stamp_child_audit`), NOT a `create or replace` of
  `stamp_archived`.** Replacing it in place would set up the exact trap that hit
  `vendor_edit_guard` five times: re-running the soft-delete migration afterwards would silently
  revert this file's stamping. With a distinct name, a stray re-run merely re-adds that migration's
  own narrower trigger alongside this one — both stamp `archived_by` identically, so the result is
  **redundant but harmless, never silent loss.** The migration drops `trg_stamp_archived` and
  `internal.stamp_archived()` itself.
- **Nothing is back-filled.** A NULL actor honestly means "changed before this migration, or by a
  service-role / SQL-editor process where `auth.uid()` is NULL". Inventing an actor would be worse
  than admitting we do not know. `vendor_documents.uploaded_by` (client-set, predates this) is left
  alone so nothing reading it breaks — but **prefer `created_by`, which is server-stamped and
  therefore unforgeable.**
- **`vendors.html` surfaces it**: a muted `.row-audit` line on every product / certification /
  personnel row — `Last edited by X · date`, or `Added by X · date` when never edited. A stored
  audit trail nobody can see is not one. `db.js` needed **no change** — `list()` already does
  `select('*')`, and the stamping is entirely server-side.
- **⚠️ `_fmtAuditDate()` range-checks the timestamp, and that is load-bearing.** `new Date('rubbish')`
  does **not** throw — it returns an Invalid Date whose `toLocaleDateString()` is the literal string
  `"Invalid Date"`, so a `try/catch` alone renders that straight to the user. **Caught by the test,
  not by review** — the first cut of both this and `_archWhen` had the bug. Any new date formatter
  here must use `isNaN(d.getTime())`, not just a try/catch.

**Verified:** migration parses under `pglast` (6 statements, the PL/pgSQL body, all 17 trigger
assignments as SQL) with text assertions that it supersedes `stamp_archived`, keeps `created_by`
immutable and preserves `archived_by`; every inline `<script>` passes `node --check`; and 15
assertions against the **verbatim extracted shipped `_auditLine`/`_archWhen`/`_fmtAuditDate`** —
pre-migration rows rendering nothing, add-vs-edit wording, actor-name escaping (a vendor controls
their own display name), and the Invalid-Date case above.

**⚠️ NOT RUN YET** — run it in Supabase; its section 4 verification `SELECT` must read true on all
six columns. `vendors.html`-only client change, so **no shared `?v=` bump** (db.js untouched).

**Still open:** staff hard-delete remains un-undoable, and the audit trail records WHO and WHEN but
not the OLD VALUE — a full before/after history would need a separate `*_history` table, which was
not built.

### Vendor change history — version history and restore (2026-09-01)

**`migrations/2026-09-01_vendor_history_restore.sql`** (run once, after the three hardening
migrations). The follow-on the audit trail explicitly left open: it recorded WHO changed a row and
WHEN, but not WHAT it used to say, so a bad edit had no way back.

**Covers the one destructive act a vendor login can still legitimately perform** — overwriting its
own rows. Soft-delete covers removal; locking covers approved evidence; this covers content.

- **`vendor_history`** takes a full `old_data`/`new_data` jsonb snapshot on every INSERT / UPDATE /
  DELETE across all five vendor tables (`vendors` + the four children), stamped with the actor from
  `auth.uid()`.
- **⚠️⚠️ THE RLS ON THIS TABLE IS THE WHOLE SECURITY STORY.** A snapshot of a `vendors` row
  contains **`notes` and `accreditation_notes`** — precisely the staff-only columns
  `2026-08-20_vendor_self_view.sql` exists to keep from vendors. If a vendor could read this table
  that entire lockdown would be undone through the back door. It is therefore **staff
  write-roles only — no vendor, no viewer.** **Do not add a “vendors can see their own history”
  policy without first stripping those columns from the snapshot.** Nothing from it is rendered in
  `vendor-portal.html`.
- **⚠️ The log is APPEND-ONLY: there is no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy at all.** It is written by
  trigger (running as the table owner) and cannot be forged or edited through the API — a tamperable
  audit log is worse than none, because it still looks authoritative.
- **`restore_vendor_row(history_id)`** puts one row back to that entry’s `old_data` (staff).
  **An INSERT entry is refused** — it has no “before”, and undoing it would mean deleting the row,
  which is not a restore.
- **`preview_vendor_restore(vendor, when)` → `restore_vendor_to(vendor, when)`** is the answer to
  extensive damage: roll a whole vendor and all its children back to a moment. **ALWAYS SHOW THE
  PREVIEW FIRST** — a blind bulk revert is exactly the irreversible action this body of work exists
  to prevent, and the UI wires Preview before the apply button even appears.
  - State at T = the `new_data` of the newest entry at or before T, **else the `old_data` of the
    oldest entry after T**. That second branch is what makes rows that predate the migration work:
    their state at T is whatever they were before their first later change.
  - **A row that did not exist at T is ARCHIVED, never deleted**, and the `vendors` row itself is
    never archived.
  - **ADMIN ONLY**, unlike the single-row restore — its blast radius matches `merge_vendors` and
    `delete_vendor_cascade`, which are admin-gated for the same reason.
- **⚠️ `internal.apply_snapshot` UPDATES, it never delete-and-reinserts.** Deleting a `vendors` row
  cascades to every product, certification, personnel record, document, bid and rate it owns — a
  “restore” that destroyed the children would be catastrophic. It builds the column list at run time
  (excluding `id` and any generated/identity column) so a table gaining a column needs no change
  here, and refuses any table outside the five-table allow-list.
- **A restore is itself an UPDATE, so it is recorded in the history too** — every rollback is
  reversible.
- **UI:** a **Change History** section in the vendor detail modal — per-entry old→new field diffs,
  who and when, a per-row **Put this back**, and (admins) a datetime picker with Preview → Restore.
  Audit columns are filtered out of the diff (`_HIST_NOISE`) or every entry would read as a change
  to `updated_at`.

**⚠️ History starts the day the migration runs.** Edits made before that were never captured, so a
point-in-time restore can only reach back to the first recorded change for a given row. Say so when
anyone asks to “restore to how it was last month”.

**Verified:** migration parses under `pglast` (21 statements, 5 functions) with assertions that the
policy is staff-only with no vendor path, that the log has no write policy, that `apply_snapshot`
never deletes and refuses unknown tables, that an INSERT entry cannot be “restored”, that
point-in-time is admin-only and archives rather than deletes, and that the `vendors` row is never
archived. 19 assertions against the **verbatim extracted shipped** diff helpers, including that an
audit-column-only write shows no changes, that `null` vs a missing key is not a change, and that
`0`/`false` are not rendered as “(empty)”.

**⚠️ NOT RUN YET.** Its section 7 verification `SELECT` must read true on all six columns. **Storage
note:** every write stores a full row snapshot, so a bulk action (e.g. `bulkSetAccreditation` over
2,000 vendors) writes 2,000 history rows. Fine at this scale, but it is unbounded — there is no
retention policy, deliberately, since pruning an audit log needs a decision nobody has made yet.

### Vendor documents — freeze approved evidence (2026-09-01)

**`migrations/2026-09-01_vendor_doc_lock.sql`** (run once, **after** the soft-delete and audit-trail
migrations). Step 2 of the vendor-data hardening, and the last of the three.

**WHY:** a vendor holds UPDATE on their own `vendor_documents` rows, so after being accredited they
could overwrite or archive the very BIR 2303 / business permit / invoice the approval was granted
on — leaving the accreditation standing on nothing. Worse, **nothing recorded which documents an
approval relied on**: `vendor_documents` had no request link and `vendor_accreditation_requests` had
no document list. So this does two things that only make sense together — it **snapshots** the
evidence at approval, then **freezes** it.

- **⚠️ SCOPE IS `vendor_documents` ONLY, and that is deliberate.** `window.accredReadiness()` builds
  the checklist from `ACCRED_DOC_TYPES` over that table plus vendor profile fields;
  **`vendor_certifications` is NOT part of accreditation** (an earlier framing of this step said
  “certification or document” — that was wrong). Profile fields are also untouched: contact details
  and address are current-state a vendor should keep updating, not evidence.
- **Lock columns** `locked_at` / `locked_by` / `locked_by_name` / `locked_request_id`. The FK is
  **`on delete set null`**: if a request row is ever deleted the document is STILL evidence and must
  stay locked — it just loses the pointer.
- **`internal.lock_vendor_evidence()` locks only LIVE, not-yet-locked rows.** `locked_at is null`
  makes it idempotent AND makes **first lock win**, so on a renewal each document still traces to
  the approval it originally supported while newly-uploaded ones are picked up by the new approval.
- **Fired from BOTH routes to “accredited”** — `trg_lock_evidence_on_request` (a request approved)
  and `trg_lock_evidence_on_accredit` (staff setting the standing directly via the detail form, the
  grid, or `bulkSetAccreditation`). **The second is the commoner path today and a request-only
  trigger would have locked nothing.** Both fire on a TRANSITION, so section 5 **back-fills** the
  ~500 vendors stamped by the one-time SQL seeds, with a NULL actor — honest, since nobody clicked
  approve.
- **⚠️ `internal.vendor_doc_lock_guard` RAISES rather than silently pinning, unlike
  `vendor_edit_guard`.** That guard covers a MIXED row (some columns vendor-owned, some staff-owned),
  so pinning is the only sensible outcome there. Here the WHOLE ROW is frozen, so a silent no-op
  would leave the vendor believing they had replaced a document when they had not. **Archiving is an
  UPDATE, so this blocks that too** — which is the point.
- **⚠️ ENFORCED BY TRIGGER, NOT THE UI.** RLS grants a vendor UPDATE on their own rows and cannot
  restrict WHICH rows or columns within that grant, so a vendor could PATCH a locked document
  straight at the REST API whatever the portal renders. The portal hiding its Remove button is
  presentation; the trigger is the enforcement. Its `caller_role is distinct from 'vendor'`
  early-return lets staff, the service role and the SQL editor (`auth.uid()` NULL → no users row
  → NULL role) pass through, same shape as `vendor_edit_guard`.
- **UI:** `vendor-portal.html` shows a **Locked** badge in place of the Remove button (offering a
  button the database will reject only produces a confusing error) and surfaces the trigger's `hint`,
  which says to upload a replacement. `vendors.html` shows a lock icon naming who locked it and when,
  plus an **unlock** link for `_canManage` roles — the escape hatch for a document locked in error,
  backed by the new **`VendorDb.documents.setLocked(id, locked)`** (which throws loudly, never a
  silent no-op, if the migration has not run).

**Verified:** migration parses under `pglast` (21 statements, all four PL/pgSQL bodies) with
assertions that the guard raises for vendors and passes a NULL role, that the snapshot is idempotent
and skips archived rows, that all three triggers are drop-then-create, and that the FK is
`on delete set null`; every inline `<script>` passes `node --check`; and 13 assertions against the
**verbatim extracted shipped `_docLockMarker`** — including that a read-only role gets no unlock
link and that a locker name containing `"><img onerror=...>` cannot break out of the `title`
attribute.

**Still open:** staff hard-delete remains un-undoable, and the audit trail records who/when but not
the OLD VALUE — a full before/after history would need a separate `*_history` table.

### Vendor detail modal — Save moved to the persistent footer (2026-09-01)

**Reported as "there is no save changes."** The detail modal is ONE LONG SCROLL (Overview → Work
Packages → Schedule → Products → Certifications → Personnel → Rates → Bid History → Change
History) with a jump-nav, and `Save Changes` sat at the END OF THE FIRST SECTION. From anywhere
below it — Change History, say — there was no Save on screen at all, and the modal footer offered
only Close / Split / Delete. A TIN was typed, `Close` was pressed, and the edit was silently lost.

- **The button moved into `renderDetailFooter()`**, which is `flex-shrink:0` and therefore always
  visible — the same shape as the Planning app's Edit Activity dialog, which is what the user asked
  to follow. It keeps the id **`btnSaveDetailOverview`**, so `saveDetailOverview()` needed no change.
- **`margin-left:auto` pins Save alone on the right**, which also puts the entire toolbar between it
  and **Delete** — a deliberate misclick guard now that a destructive and a primary action share one
  row. `.modal-footer` gained `flex-wrap:wrap` so four buttons cannot overflow a narrow modal.
- **Unsaved-change tracking**: `_detailDirty` + `_wireDetailDirty()` (ONE delegated listener on
  `#dtab-overview`, which survives the `innerHTML` rewrites its children go through, guarded by a
  `dataset.dirtyWired` flag so repeated renders cannot stack duplicates). A dirty form gives the
  button a red halo and a bullet (`.has-changes`, mirroring `review.html`'s grid Save), and
  **`closeDetailModal()` now confirms before discarding** — without autosave, closing is exactly
  where work gets lost.
- **⚠️ NOT AUTOSAVE, deliberately, even though the Planning app has it.** Every vendor save now
  writes a full before/after snapshot into `vendor_history`, so an autosave tick per keystroke would
  bury the audit trail under noise; and a VENDOR's own save flips them back to `pending_review`
  each time, so autosave would spam the staff review flag. Explicit save is correct here.
- The flag clears on open and on a successful save (`renderDetailOverview()` resets it, and the
  success path already calls that). The two `btn` references in `saveDetailOverview()` were made
  null-safe since the button is now conditionally rendered elsewhere.
- `vendors.html`-only change, so **no shared `?v=` bump** (`db.js` untouched).

### The "still open" backlog — three of six items turned out already shipped (2026-09-02)

Asked to close six previously-flagged gaps. Investigation before writing anything found three
were **already fully built** in earlier sessions and the backlog note was simply stale:

- **Reclassifying an existing offering** — `openProductEditor`/`openStaffProductEditor` (the
  catalogue rewrite) already re-file an item in place via the taxonomy picker + `update()`; the
  "remove and re-add" limitation no longer exists. Only cleanup remained: the pre-catalogue
  `reclassifyOwnProduct()` + its `#reclHost` div in `vendor-portal.html` had no call site any
  more (superseded, same as `reclassifyProduct()` was already removed from `vendors.html`) —
  removed as dead code.
- **Taxonomy rollups in Vendor Analytics** — the "Vendors by Product Category" table
  (`renderVendorAnalytics`'s `taxTbl`) already rolls every offering up to its L1 trade via
  `path[0]` and was already committed. Verified the rollup logic (deep node → L2 → L1, unknown id)
  against the shipped source rather than trust it blind — all four cases correct.
- **`findVendorsForWP` wiring** — `wp-form.html`'s Proposed Vendors section already has a
  **"Suggest vendors for this Trade / Works"** button calling `VendorDb.findVendorsForWP` exactly
  as designed, with accreditation badges and one-click Add. Already shipped.

**⚠️ Found in passing, not asked for, and fixed because it was live and broken:**
`_vendorInviteLink()` still built `vendor-register.html?vendor=<id>` — the URL scheme
`vendor-register.html` **stopped reading** when it was rewritten to the generic claim flow
(`migrations/2026-09-01_vendor_self_registration.sql`). **Three separate call sites** were handing
vendors a dead link: the detail-modal "copy invite link" button, the `exportInviteList()` CSV, and
a THIRD, independent inline copy of the same dead URL in the post-create "Add Vendor" callback that
never went through `_vendorInviteLink` at all. All three now go through **one shared builder,
`_regLinkFor(vendorCode)`** — `?code=` only prefills the vendor-code field (see `openQrModal`), so
a vendor with no code yet correctly falls back to the plain generic link rather than a broken one.
`_vendorById(id)` resolves via `allVendors`, falling back to `activeVendor` for a just-created
vendor not in that array yet. The detail modal's "Login & Self-Service Access" panel — whose copy
described the OLD single-use, email-gated link ("the link works once, until the account is
claimed") — was rewritten to describe the actual mechanism, and gained a **View QR** button
(`viewVendorQrFromDetail`, reusing `openQrModal` — its FIRST per-vendor UI entry point; until now
it was reachable only as the generic Data Tools QR).

**Verified**: 18 assertions on `bulkGenerateQr` (below) plus 5 on the link builder, all against the
verbatim shipped source — correct `?code=` construction, a bare-fallback for no/empty code, and
URL-encoding of special characters in a code.

**Flagged, not fixed here** (`spawn_task task_5662eb6a`): "Enable self-service" (bulk bar) only
sets `vendors.invite_email` — a column `vendor-register.html` no longer reads at all under the new
claim flow, so the button runs, writes a real value, and reports success while having zero effect
on whether anyone can actually register. Deliberately not ripped out inline — retiring a working
bulk-bar button is a decision worth its own look, not a side effect of an unrelated fix.

### Vendor guide brought current with the self-registration + catalogue rewrites (2026-09-02)

`assets/js/vendor-guide.js`'s "Adding vendors" slide still described the fully-retired per-vendor
invite-email flow as the primary path. Rewritten to lead with the QR/generic-link flow, keeping the
old bulk CSV path mentioned as a still-functional alternative (pending the `spawn_task` decision
above). Added a new **"Vendor Registrations"** slide covering the claim queue, match confidence,
and the duplicate-refusing "Create new vendor" action — an entire nav item + feature that had no
mention anywhere in the guide. **"Inside a vendor profile"** now names **Change History** (was
missing) and explains document locking on approved accreditation evidence. Versioned independently
of the 5-file shared bump, per its own convention: `?v=20260820a → 20260902a`.

### Vendor Registrations — a top-level sidebar item (2026-09-01)

The registration queue was a **Data Tools** menu entry — an approval queue filed among
data-cleanup tools, two clicks deep. It is now a sidebar nav item on **all four pages that carry
the sidebar** (`index.html`, `project.html`, `admin.html`, `vendors.html`), directly under Vendor
Management.

- **Visually sits under User Management (2026-09-02, per request), but is NOT inside the container
  that gates on `AppAuth.isAdmin`.** A claim can be approved by **specialist, manager and user**
  too — the RPCs' allow-list is the same `_canManage` audience — so nesting the `<a>` inside
  `#sidebar-admin`/`#nav-admin-section` (both revealed on `AppAuth.isAdmin` alone) silently hid the
  queue from every contributor role for one day, until fixed the same week (2026-09-02, "all
  contributors should be able to see it").
- **⚠️ THE FIX IS EXTRACTION, NOT WIDENING THE ADMIN CONTAINER.** `#sidebar-admin` also gates
  **User Management**, **New Project** (index.html), and — on `project.html` — the admin project
  list and edit/delete-project buttons; `#nav-admin-section` also gates User Management on
  `vendors.html`. Widening any of those containers to contributor roles would have exposed all of
  that alongside the queue. Instead, on `index.html`/`project.html`/`vendors.html` the `<a>` now
  lives in its **own sibling wrapper `#sidebar-vendor-claims`**, positioned immediately after the
  admin block so it still reads as adjacent to User Management, but toggled independently by
  `initVendorClaimsNav()`. `admin.html`'s copy needed no change — nothing there hides its ancestor
  (the whole page is already restricted to admin/super_admin/manager), so the bare `<a>` was never
  affected; `initVendorClaimsNav()` falls back to toggling it directly when `#sidebar-vendor-claims`
  is absent.
- **One shared helper in `db.js`, not four copies**: `window.initVendorClaimsNav(profile)` reveals
  the item and fills the count; `window.setVendorClaimsNavCount(n)` is the setter `vendors.html`'s
  own `updateClaimsBadge()` calls after an approve/decline so the badge follows without a reload.
  Hidden for `viewer`/`viewer_budget`/`vendor`, matching both the queue's own buttons and the
  Vendor Management gate (Known Issues #37/#41 — **those guards must keep agreeing**).
- **The count fetch is fire-and-forget** (`.then(…).catch(…)`, never awaited) so it can never sit
  on a dashboard's render path, and `claims.pending()` already returns `[]` when the migration has
  not run. Reuses the existing `.nav-item .nbadge` style rather than inventing another badge.
- **`vendors.html` links straight to `openClaimsModal()`**; the other three link to
  **`vendors.html?claims=1`**, which that page opens after `loadAll()` — the queue needs
  `allVendors` populated to resolve each claim's matched vendor name.
- Removed from Data Tools, and **`claim-count` dropped from `_syncToolsAlert()`** so the red dot
  there no longer counts claims — the sidebar badge carries that signal now.

**⚠️ A `?v=` BUMP WAS MISSED AND NEARLY SHIPPED BROKEN.** The bump was written as a
`c → d` sed, but the pages were ALREADY on `d` (claimed by the change-history feature), so it
matched nothing: `db.js` changed while the cache-buster stood still. A browser holding
`db.js?v=20260901d` would have kept serving the old file, `initVendorClaimsNav` would have been
undefined, and — because every call site guards with `typeof … === 'function'` — the nav item
would have silently never appeared, with no error to notice. **Read the CURRENT `?v=` out of the
files before writing a sed for it; do not assume the letter you last set is still there.**

### Vendor portal — catalogue, profile cards, capability (2026-09-02)

Phase 1 of the portal rework. The offering list was an inline three-field row (type / description /
unit) rendering flat `.row-item` lines — a spreadsheet. It is now a **photo-led, grouped card
catalogue**, and the field set closes the gap against what a work package actually asks for.

- **Patterned on the Planning app's Stakeholder Map**, at the user's direction: `.cat-group` →
  `.cat-ghead` (pill + count) → `.cat-grid` → `.cat-card`, mirroring that module's
  `.sm-cgroup`/`.sm-chead`/`.sm-cgrid`/`.sm-card`. Cards lead with a photo that falls back to an
  icon; **Personnel** uses the same shape as profile cards with an initials-fallback avatar.
  Grouping is by **trade** — the same axis work packages group by.
- **The editor is grouped, not one flat row**: *What it is* / *How you supply it* / *Indicative
  price* / *Photo & spec sheet*, replacing the four inline inputs.
- **⚠️ Indicative price is NOT a quotation** and carries its own `price_valid_until`, because an
  indicative price with no expiry silently becomes a lie. A real price still comes from
  `vendor_bids` against a specific work package.
- **Files reuse the existing private `vendor-certs` bucket** via the new
  **`VendorDb.uploadVendorFile(vendorId, kind, file)`** — no new bucket, no new policy. **The
  `<vendorId>/` path prefix is load-bearing**: that bucket's Storage policies authorize on the
  first path segment. Photos are private objects, so each needs its own signed URL; they are
  fetched AFTER render (`_catLoadPhotos`) so the grid paints immediately, and a missing file is
  swallowed rather than breaking the grid.
- **Company capability** (`can_surety_bond` / `can_performance_bond` / `can_warranty_bond` /
  `can_provide_submittals` / `payment_terms_days`) answers the bond and submittal requirements a WP
  carries. Kept in a separate **`CAPABILITY_FIELDS`** map rather than `OVERVIEW_FIELDS` because they
  are tri-state booleans and a number, not plain strings. **`''` means "not answered" and stays
  NULL** — writing `false` would assert the vendor *cannot* provide a bond when they simply have
  not said. Wired into all three loops: render, collect-for-save, and draft-restore.
- **Real empty states** (`.pf-empty`). An empty section previously showed only a bare add-row.
- Portal topbar now uses the red **`megawide-mark.png`** rather than the wide logo.

**Follow-up fixes after the first live look (2026-09-02, same day):**
- **⚠️ BUG — `.save-bar` was hardcoded `background:#fff` with NO dark-mode rule**, so the Save
  button sat on a white band. It is `position:sticky` with an opaque background by design (content
  scrolls under it), which is why the colour could not simply be dropped.
- **The editor was a RED card nested inside the panel card** and read as a second surface floating
  inside the first. Now the same neutral surface as the panel with a **red left accent bar** — it
  reads as part of the panel rather than a thing on top of it.
- **⚠️ Uneven fields**: a label that wrapped to two lines pushed ITS OWN control down while its
  neighbours stayed put (visible on “Material / subcon submittals” in the capability row).
  `.itm-grid` is now `align-items:end` with `min-height:2.5em` reserved on every label, so a
  wrapping label cannot knock its control out of line. **Any new grid field inherits this.**
- **Personnel cards rebuilt to the Stakeholder Universe shape** — avatar LEFT with the details
  stacked beside it (was centred), a green presence dot on the primary contact, and the name
  ellipsised so a long one cannot blow out the card.
- **Tabs reordered basics-first**: Company Info → Products / Services → Personnel → Certifications
  → Accreditation. Accreditation is the OUTCOME of filling the rest in, so leading with it asked for
  a submission before there was anything to submit. **⚠️ The panel `display` defaults had to flip
  with it** — `#panel-accreditation`/`#panel-documents` were the only ones visible at load, so
  moving the `active` class alone would have highlighted Company Info while showing Accreditation.
- **NOT reproduced**: an empty red editor box. The real `_catEditorHtml` returns 3,088 characters
  and renders in full against the real CSS. `openProductEditor` sets `display` BEFORE `innerHTML`,
  so a throw in between would leave exactly that empty box — but nothing in it throws, and there
  are no duplicate declarations of `_f`/`Q`/`sel`. Most likely a stale cached build. Re-check.

**Verified**: every inline `<script>` passes `node --check`; the card grid, group headers, profile
cards and empty state were rendered against the REAL portal CSS in a throwaway harness (deleted)
and read back with `get_page_text` — the blank region in the screenshot was a static-snapshot
artifact, not a layout fault.

**Staff side (2026-09-02, same day):** `vendors.html`'s detail modal now mirrors the portal — the
same grouped card grid (`.sp-*`), the same grouped editor (`.itm-*`, copied into this file since
those classes lived only in the portal), photos via signed URLs, a spec-sheet link, and an initials
avatar on personnel rows. **Staff get the FULL field set**, not a subset: the old four-field
add-form could only set type/description/unit/category, so a staff member filling a profile in for
a vendor could not record a lead time. `reclassifyProduct()` was removed as dead code — the editor
covers the category, and nothing called it any more.

**The bid board is BUILT (2026-09-03)** — the invitation-vs-open decision it was waiting on was
made (invitation-only). See "Vendor Bid Board" below.

### ⚠️ Vendor portal — the profile never loaded for ANY vendor (2026-09-02)

**Reported as “Could not load your vendor profile: Cannot coerce the result to a single JSON
object”.** That message is PostgREST’s `.single()` failing on **zero rows**, and every other broken
thing on that page — blank accreditation checklist, empty products list, empty editor — was
downstream of it.

**Root cause:** `db.js`’s `_vendorRel()` routes a vendor to `vendor_self_view` only when
`window.__wpmRole === 'vendor'`, otherwise returning the base `vendors` table.
**`vendor-portal.html` was the one page that never set that global** — `index.html`,
`project.html`, `admin.html`, `review.html`, `wp-form.html`, `claim-form.html` and
`project-selector.html` all do it in their auth callback. So a vendor queried `vendors`, which
`migrations/2026-08-20_vendor_self_view.sql` had deliberately removed their SELECT on, and got zero
rows.

**⚠️ This is exactly the hazard recorded under the guard-consolidation section: the entire vendor
self-service path was verified by construction only and had never been exercised, because no vendor
had ever logged in.** The first real login found it immediately.

- **Fix**: the portal now sets `window.__wpmRole` and `window.__profile` in its auth callback,
  **before any `VendorDb` call**, like every other page.
- **Hardening**: `_vendorRel()` reads the role from **either** `window.__wpmRole` or
  `window.__profile.role` via a new `_myRole()`. Depending on a single global that one page forgot
  is what shipped this broken; one page setting only one of the two must not reroute a vendor to a
  relation they cannot read.
- **`renderProducts`/`renderPersonnel` return early when `vendor` is null**, so a failed profile
  load degrades instead of throwing again inside each tab.
- **The catalogue editor can no longer render an empty box.** `openProductEditor` sets
  `host.style.display` BEFORE assigning `innerHTML`, so any throw in between left a blank panel with
  no clue why; the assignment is now wrapped and renders the error text instead.

**Accreditation documents finalised** to the four Megawide actually requires — Company Profile, BIR
2303, Sample Invoice, Mayor’s Business Permit — reordered to match how they are asked for.
**⚠️ Only the LABELS changed: `ACCRED_DOC_TYPES` keys are stored in `vendor_documents.doc_type` on
every uploaded row, so a key rename would orphan existing uploads.**

**The capability block was re-worded, not removed.** Bond and submittal requirements differ per work
package, so “what your company can commit to” over-promised: it is a **pre-filter for shortlisting**,
not a commitment. It now says so explicitly, and the binding commitment belongs to the bid.

### Vendor Portal — a staff account no longer bounces to the login page (2026-09-02)

**Reported as “opening `vendor-portal.html` redirects me to the procurement login page”.** The page
guarded with `if (profile.role !== 'vendor' || !profile.vendor_id) { window.location.href =
'login.html'; }` — but `AppAuth.requireLogin` has ALREADY established a valid session by that point,
so a signed-in staff member was sent to a login page that re-authenticated them and dropped them on
the dashboard chooser. A loop that reads as a broken page.

- **A vendor with NO session is still sent to `login.html`, and that is correct** — that is where
  vendors sign in, and `login.html` routes `role==='vendor'` straight back to the portal.
- **A signed-in NON-vendor now gets `showWrongAccount(profile)`**: the tabs and panels are hidden and
  a panel explains whose account they are on, with links to Vendor Management and the Portfolio.
  Telling someone which account they are using is more useful than silently re-routing them.

### Masterlist re-import — `SEED_vendor_masterlist.sql` (2026-08-20)

The chosen cleanup is **delete every Not-Accredited vendor, then re-import the whole masterlist**, so the directory becomes the masterlist. The import is a **one-time SQL seed**, not an app feature — the user's position on that is settled (see the importer note above). **NEITHER the seed NOR its generator is committed** — both are git-ignored, for the same reason as the accreditation seeds: the `.sql` embeds ~2,400 real vendor names, TINs, contacts and addresses, and **GitHub Pages serves this whole repo publicly**. Regenerate locally with `gen_master_seed.py <workbook.xlsx>`; the generator itself is data-free but is ignored too, per the existing convention.

- **2,410 distinct companies** from three sheets — 2,387 accredited + 32 non-accredited + 3 problematic raw, with 12 duplicate names collapsed (strongest standing wins: `problematic > accredited > none`; contact fields merge). The **non-accredited sheet imports with NO standing** — "Not Accredited" is the absence of a value now, not a word.
- **The new profile columns finally have a source**: `BP Code → vendor_code`, `Federal Tax ID → tin`, `Telephone`, `Mobile Phone → contact_number`, `E-Mail → contact_email`, `Bill-to Street/City → address/city`, `Contact Person`, `Position → contact_position`, `Vendor Category`, `Vendor Group`, `Payment Terms`. `trade_categories` is deliberately left empty — `Vendor Group` is free text ("Ready Mix Concrete", "Equipments & Earth Works") and mapping it onto the 10 canonical trades would be guesswork.
- **`invite_email` is always a deterministic placeholder** — `masterdata+<md5(normalized name)>@no-invite.local` — with the real address going to `contact_email`. That sidesteps the `unique index on lower(invite_email)` entirely (1,347 rows carry an email and some repeat), and doubles as the marker for "this row came from the import".
- **Three match tiers, in order: vendor code → exact normalized name → core name.** Per company: UPDATE the matching row filling only BLANK fields and **never downgrading a standing**, else INSERT. A **re-run is a true no-op** (verified: 10 × `INSERT 0 0`, totals unchanged, 0 duplicate names).
- **Tier 3 only matches rows that predate the import** (`invite_email not like 'masterdata+%'`). Without that clause, whether two similar names in the FILE collapsed into one row depended on whether they landed in the same batch — a CTE cannot see rows its own statement inserted. The masterlist is the source of truth: if it lists `Genus Rigging Supplies` and `Genus Rigging Supplies Inc.` separately, both import and the **Merge tool** (which now scores that pair 0.85) suggests joining them, with a person deciding.

**Three bugs worth remembering, each caught only by RUNNING it against a real Postgres 16:**
1. **`min(uuid)` does not exist** — the identical trap the pass-2 seed hit. `(array_agg(id order by id))[1]` is the substitute, valid because the `having count(*) = 1` already guarantees one row.
2. **A single `regexp_replace(' (inc|builders|...) ', ' ', 'g')` cannot strip consecutive stopwords.** The match consumes the space on BOTH sides, so in `eiros builders inc` the leading space of ` inc ` is already eaten: **`EIROS BUILDERS INC.` reduced to `eiros inc` while `Eiros Builders` reduced to `eiros`**, and they failed to match — silently importing a duplicate of a problematic vendor. `_vcore` now tokenises with `string_to_array` + `unnest … where w not in (…)`, which is also what the app's JS `core()` does. **The pass-2 seed already run in production has this same flaw** — it under-matched rather than mis-matched, so it is not worth re-litigating, but do not copy that regex again.
3. **A correlated `count(*)` uniqueness subquery per source row took >2 minutes and blew the timeout.** Hoisting the three match keys into CTEs computed once per statement (plus temporary expression indexes on `_vcore(name)`, the normalized name and the vendor code, all dropped at the end) took the whole run to **~16 seconds across 14 separate psql sessions**.

**Verified end-to-end against Postgres 16**, each statement in its OWN session (stricter than the SQL Editor): against a fixture modelling the post-delete state, the survivors MATCH and are enriched rather than duplicated (`*ACERSTEEL INDUSTRIAL SALES (LOCAL)` picks up `V-00010` + TIN by core name; `EIROS BUILDERS INC.` picks up `V-00125`; a vendor absent from the masterlist is left untouched) — **2,411 rows = 2,410 + the one survivor not in the file**; into an EMPTY directory it produces exactly **2,410 / 2,376 accredited / 3 problematic / 31 not accredited**; and a second pass changes nothing.

**The deleted vendors' WP links, bids and rates do not come back** — the re-imported rows are new ids. That was accepted when the delete-first plan was chosen.

### Bulk delete: real impact numbers + a CSV backup (2026-08-20)

Bulk delete asked `"Permanently DELETE N selected vendor(s) and all their records?"` and gave no idea what those records were. For the decision it is actually used for — clearing the not-accredited backlog, hundreds of rows at once — that is not enough to consent to.

- **`VendorDb.getDeletionImpact(ids)`** counts, **for the exact rows selected**, the work-package links, bids, rates, products, documents, how many vendors carry any of it, and how many have a **claimed login** (a person losing portal access, not just a row). Chunked `.in()` reads (150) so a long id list can't blow the PostgREST URL; a missing table is warned and skipped, never thrown — it is a pre-flight, not the operation.
- **Scope it to the SELECTION, never directory-wide.** Directory-wide totals (324 links / 394 bids / 393 rates) are the numbers everyone reaches for and they **overstate the loss badly** — they include every vendor you are keeping.
- **`bulkDelete()` now runs three gates**: the impact summary; an offer to **download a CSV of the exact rows** (`downloadVendorCsv`, 23 columns incl. accreditation, vendor_code, contacts and trades, UTF-8 BOM so Excel opens it cleanly, RFC-4180 quoting) — there is no undo anywhere in this app, so that file is the only way back; then a final confirm. It also **warns when any selected vendor carries an accreditation standing**, which normally means it should be kept.
- CSV quoting/BOM/array-flattening unit-tested against the shipped source.

### Merge suggestions actually find the duplicates now (2026-08-20)

Reported live: the Merge modal showed **no suggested groups at all** while obvious duplicates sat in the Manual Merge list right below it. Two separate causes, both fixed.

**1. Token-set equality could not see the commonest real duplicates.** `_mgTokens` strips boilerplate and `_mgSimilarity` compared the remaining sets exactly, so a plural, a typo or an abbreviation scored below the 0.75 threshold. **Measured against a labelled sample of real pairs from the live directory, the old matcher found 6 of 10.** The misses were all the same shapes:

| pair | old score |
|---|---|
| `Abenson Inc.` / `ABENSON VENTURES INC.` | 0.50 |
| `10k Concrete Mix Specialist` / `10K Concrete Mix Specialists, Inc.` | 0.60 |
| `Cebu Oversea Hardware Co.` / `Cebu Oversea Harware Co.` | 0.50 |
| `Adcons Signage & Billboard` / `Adcons Signage and Billboards` | 0.50 |

Two narrow additions, and **both are deliberately narrow — widening either re-opens the over-chaining hazard**:
- **`_mgTokenMatch`** — tokens match when equal, when one is a **prefix** of the other and the shorter is ≥4 chars with a ≤2-char difference (plurals/truncations: `SPECIALIST`/`SPECIALISTS`, `PHIL`/`PHILS`), or at **edit distance 1** when the shorter is ≥5 chars (`HARWARE`/`HARDWARE`). `_mgLev` early-returns 2 when the lengths differ by more than 1, since only ≤1 matters. `_mgSoftInter` matches **greedily one-to-one**, so a repeated token can't inflate the score.
- **The subset-boost now accepts a SINGLE distinctive token** (≥4 chars) as well as 2+. That one relaxation is what surfaces `Abenson` ⊂ `Abenson Ventures` — by far the commonest duplicate shape here and invisible to the old `smaller.size >= 2` rule. **Short acronyms stay out**: `ABS` (3 chars) never boosts against `ABS Builders`. **The ≥0.5 size-ratio gate is untouched** — that is the thing that actually prevented the 431-vendor over-chaining incident, and the garbled name from that incident still scores **0.22** against short real names.

**2. Large clusters were HIDDEN, which is what made the modal look empty.** A group over `MERGE_GROUP_SIZE_CAP` (8) was dropped with a "review manually via search below" note. `_computeMergeGroups` now returns them as **`large`** alongside `groups`, and `renderMergeGroups` shows them under an amber **"Large clusters — check these by hand"** header with **nothing pre-checked** — which also keeps **Merge All** off them for free, since it filters on a non-empty checked set. Confident groups still arrive fully pre-checked (one click merges). `_mgSuppressed` is now just the large-cluster count; the old hidden-cluster note is gone. The empty state reads as success rather than as a dead end.

**Verified with a Node harness over the SHIPPED file's own script** (stubbed DOM, real `_computeMergeGroups`), on a 288-vendor synthetic directory: all 8 planted duplicate pairs surface as 2-member groups; **zero false positives** across deliberately-similar decoys (`AAC Lightstruct`/`AAC Cement`, `Megawide`/`Megatec`, `Steel Asia`/`Steel Craft`, `Pacific Timber`/`Pacific Paint`, `ABS`/`ABSI`, `First Balfour`/`First Metro`); 250 filler names never chain; the garbled hub stays out; the accredited row is picked canonical; and a deliberately-built 10-member cluster lands in `large` with a canonical rather than being dropped. **Re-run that harness before touching this matcher** — every past regression here was caught by it and by nothing else.

### Merging can no longer lose an accreditation (2026-08-20)

**The dedupe tools predate accreditation, and folding a duplicate could silently discard a standing.** `merge_vendors` DELETES the source rows; it reassigns child rows, WP links and trade categories server-side, but it knows nothing about `accreditation` / `vendor_code` / the profile columns. A duplicate pair is very often *the row the masterdata seed stamped* + *the row the WP import created*, so whichever way the canonical pick fell, one of them was going to be deleted. Worst case: **a PROBLEMATIC flag disappearing** because the accredited-looking twin was kept.

Two changes, both in `db.js`:

- **`findExactDuplicateGroups`'s canonical score now leads with the standing** (`accredKey(v.accreditation) !== ACCRED_NONE ? 32 : 0`), so the stamped row wins outright and the merge usually has nothing to carry. `status === 'approved'` was dropped from the score — it is no longer a user-facing concept and every legacy row shares one value, so it was noise.
- **`_preserveOnMerge(targetId, sourceIds)` runs inside `mergeVendors()` itself**, before the RPC. It reads the target + sources, and (a) carries the **strongest** standing onto the target — ranked `problematic (3) > accredited (2) > off-roster (1) > none (0)`, so a blacklist flag survives even when the accredited row is the one being kept — along with its notes and date; (b) fills only the target's **BLANK** identity/profile fields (`vendor_code`, `tin`, contacts, address, payment terms, category, group) from the sources, never overwriting curated data. A failure here is logged, never thrown — it is a safety net, not the operation.
- **It lives inside `mergeVendors` deliberately**: every path goes through that one function — the exact-duplicate cleanup, the fuzzy Merge tool, Merge All, and Split's fold of the garbled original into its first segment. **If you add another merge path, do not re-implement this; call `mergeVendors`.**
- Unit-tested against the shipped source: the canonical score, the standing rank (including the legacy `'unaccredited'` fold and off-roster placement), and the patch the preserve pass produces for each case — carry-up, problematic-overrides-accredited, never-downgrade, never-clobber, and no-op on identical rows.

**Known and accepted:** Split still spreads a garbled record's standing onto its FIRST segment only (via that fold) — nothing can know which of several companies in one name was the accredited one; the other segments are created with no standing.

### One not-accredited state + a UI pass on the directory (2026-08-20)

**"Unaccredited" and "Not Assessed" were two names for the same fact** — the company is not on the accredited list — and having both meant a vendor could sit in either bucket for no reason anyone could explain. There is now **ONE not-accredited state, and it is the ABSENCE of a value (NULL)**, so it cannot drift.

- **`window.ACCREDITATIONS` is now `['accredited','problematic']`**; `ACCRED_NONE` (`'none'`) labels as **"Not Accredited"** with a `ti-circle-dashed` icon. **`accredKey()` folds a legacy stored `'unaccredited'` / `'non-accredited'` / `'not accredited'` onto `'none'`** (regex `_UNACCRED_RE`) so **no data migration was needed** — and none would have found anything anyway: production had 0 rows carrying the value. An off-roster value (e.g. `'Provisional'`) is still returned as-is so it displays rather than vanishing.
- **Every write path sends NULL, never the word:** `accredFromText()` returns `ACCRED_NONE` for the not-accredited patterns, and `requests.decide()` on a decline now clears the standing instead of writing `'unaccredited'`.
- **Data Tools → "Mark rest Unaccredited" was REMOVED** along with `markRestUnaccredited()` and `updateUnassessedBadge()` — it existed only to move NULL → `'unaccredited'`, which is now a distinction without a difference. **Don't reintroduce it.**
- Unit-tested against the shipped `db.js` source: the fold, both real standings, off-roster preservation, and the free-text mapper.

**UI pass on `vendors.html`, same day:**
- **A page header** (`.vm-head`) — the directory used to open straight onto a row of bare numbers with no title or context.
- **The KPI tiles ARE the standing filter now.** They were unlabelled counters you had to *know* were clickable (only the Requests one was). All five are `<button>`s — All / Accredited / Not accredited / Problematic / Requests to review — each `icon + count + label`, driving `setAccredFilter()` and carrying a live **`aria-pressed`** state that `_syncKpiPressed()` keeps in step with the `<select>` (either control can change the filter). Row count went 6 → 5, and the grid is `repeat(5,1fr)` → 3 → 2 columns down the breakpoints.
- **The toolbar is sticky.** `position:sticky; top:60px` — **not `top:0`**: `.topbar` is itself sticky at `top:0` with `z-index:50` and is 60px tall (52px under 767px, hence the media query), so `top:0` slides the toolbar underneath it and out of sight. Its `z-index:20` deliberately stays below the topbar, and it paints `var(--bg)` (the token `.content` actually sits on, so one rule covers both themes).
- **Card avatar is tinted by standing** (`av-accredited` / `av-problematic` / `av-none`), so a card reads from its left edge as well as its right-hand badge.
- **A real empty state** — icon, a sentence saying whether a *filter* emptied the list, and a **Clear search & filter** button (`clearVendorFilters()`). A bare "No vendors found." reads as a broken page.
- **Measured with a Chromium harness** over the real `dashboard.css` + the real markup, not eyeballed: KPI row stays 1 row from 1000–1900px and never overflows (3 rows at 390px, no horizontal scroll); the toolbar stays one row of two groups down to 1000px, below which the *filters group* wraps internally rather than the row splitting mid-group; and when scrolled the toolbar pins at exactly y=60, flush under the topbar with no overlap. **Group toolbar rows by vertical CENTRE with a ~12px tolerance** — `align-items:center` gives items in the same row different `top` values, and a naive top comparison reports phantom extra rows (it did here first time).

### Vendor self-service + accreditation requests (2026-08-20)

**`migrations/2026-08-20_vendor_accreditation_requests.sql`** (run once). Retiring `vendors.status` from the UI closed the one channel a vendor had for saying "I'm ready, please assess me" — a vendor self-edit still flips `status` back to `pending_review` server-side, but nothing reads that column any more, so the signal went nowhere. This replaces it with an **explicit request**, rather than resurrecting the old workflow.

**The checklist is the procurement team's own, not invented.** It is taken verbatim from the **"KC Steps"** sheet of the *EPC. PROC. Vendor Masterdata* workbook — required documents **BIR 2303 · Company Profile · Business Permit · scanned copy of an Invoice**, required information **Terms of Payment · Contact Person · Position · Contact Number · Email Address** — plus TIN and address, without which the masterdata/SAP encoding step that follows accreditation cannot be done.

- **Migration adds:** eight vendor profile columns (`tin`, `contact_position`, `telephone`, `city`, `website`, `payment_terms`, `vendor_category`, `vendor_group` — the SAP Business-Partner fields the accredited sheet actually carries); **`vendor_documents`** (doc_type / file_path / file_name, RLS the same shape as `vendor_products`); and **`vendor_accreditation_requests`** (kind / status / message / submitted_* / decided_*).
- **One request in flight per vendor** — a **partial** unique index `on (vendor_id) where status='pending'`, so decided rows stay as history and a withdrawn request doesn't block a new one.
- **`internal.vendor_request_guard`** pins the decision fields the same way `vendor_edit_guard` pins the staff-owned vendor columns: RLS grants row access but **cannot restrict which columns move**, so without it a vendor could `PATCH` themselves `approved` through the REST API. A vendor's INSERT is forced to `pending` with the decision fields nulled, and the only transition they may make is `pending → withdrawn`. **Verified against a real Postgres 16**: a vendor's self-approve attempt left the row `pending` with empty decision fields, withdraw worked, a second pending request was refused by the index, and a staff decision applied normally.
- **Documents reuse the existing private `vendor-certs` bucket.** Its Storage policies key off the **first path segment being the vendor id**, so `uploadVendorDoc` writes `<vendorId>/doc_<type>_<ts>_<name>` — **that prefix is load-bearing**; no new bucket or policy was needed.
- **`db.js`**: `VendorDb.documents.{list,add,update,remove}`, `uploadVendorDoc`, and `VendorDb.requests.{forVendor,pending,create,decide,withdraw}`. **`requests.decide()` writes BOTH the request row and the vendor's accreditation** so a decision can never leave the queue and the standing out of step. A missing table returns `[]` rather than throwing, so both pages still render pre-migration. `_stripProfile` guards the eight new columns, stripping **precisely the column the error names** (the `_stripBuyback` lesson, Known Issues #28b).
- **ONE readiness definition, in `db.js`: `window.accredReadiness(vendor, docs)`** (+ `ACCRED_DOC_TYPES` / `accredDocLabel`). The vendor portal uses it to decide what to ask for and whether to enable the Request button; the staff modal uses it to show the reviewer the same list. **Do not reimplement it in either page** or the two will disagree about whether a vendor is ready. Unit-tested against the shipped source: a document row with no `file_path` doesn't count, a whitespace-only field doesn't count, an empty `trade_categories` doesn't count, and a null `docs` argument is tolerated (the portal renders before the fetch lands).
- **`vendor-portal.html`** leads with an **Accreditation** tab: a % readiness bar, the checklist split into Required information / Required documents, per-document upload rows (15 MB cap, signed-URL viewing), and a **Request Accreditation** button that stays disabled until the checklist is complete and names exactly what is still missing. A declined request shows the reviewer's reason with a "request again" path. **The pill and banner now report the ACCREDITATION standing**, not `vendors.status` — this page was the last place that column still surfaced, and it would have contradicted the standing shown everywhere else. Company Info gained the eight new fields.
- **`vendors.html`**: a **"Requests to review"** KPI tile (click → filters the directory to them), an **"Awaiting my decision"** option in the accreditation filter, a blue **Requested** chip on those cards, and a review panel at the top of the vendor's Overview with the vendor's message, the same checklist, the uploaded documents as signed-URL links, and **Approve / Decline**. Declining **requires** a reason — the vendor sees it. The panel is deliberately hidden for a vendor with no request, no documents and no claimed login, otherwise all ~1,600 WP-derived legacy records would carry a large "incomplete" panel nobody asked for. The eight new fields are in the detail form and as grid columns.

**Not done, flag if wanted**: no email/notification when a request is raised or decided (staff must look at the tile — the app has no mail transport of its own); no expiry/renewal reminder on `accreditation_date`; `vendor-guide.js` doesn't mention the request queue yet. (`vendor-portal.html`'s own dark mode landed 2026-08-20.)

### Schedule Performance detail — S-Curve + Monthly Accomplishment (2026-08-26, `vendors.html`)

`renderDetailSched()` (the "Schedule Performance" section built on `planners_vendor_performance`, see the section above) rendered the four scorecard tiles and the text notes but never drew the `months` jsonb column it was already fetching — `[{key,pd,pc,ad,ac}]`, duration-weighted 0..1 fractions, `p`=planned/`a`=actual, `d`=this-period/`c`=cumulative. Two new render helpers, called per project card between the tiles and the notes:
- **`_scurveSvg(months, statusColor)`** — a plain inline `<svg>` (dashed gray Planned-cumulative line vs solid Actual-cumulative line in the card's own status colour, gridlines at 25/75/50/100%, thinned x-axis labels via `Math.ceil(n/8)` so a long programme doesn't overlap them, native `<title>` tooltips on the last point). **Not Chart.js** — `vendors.html` loads neither `Chart.js` nor `charts.js` anywhere else, so a whole chart stack for one section wasn't worth it; this is self-contained like the rest of the file (`vendor-guide.js`, `TaxonomyPicker`, etc.).
- **`_accompTable(months)`** — the period-by-period breakdown behind the curve (Period / Planned / Actual / Cum. Planned / Cum. Actual / Variance in percentage points, green/red by sign) — the number a buyer actually reads against a vendor's own monthly accomplishment report, rather than only the cumulative %.
- Both degrade to `''` (not an empty box) when `months` is missing/empty — covers a snapshot pushed before the Planners side started including the curve, or a vendor with no `planners_vendor_performance` row at all (the pre-existing "No schedule performance yet…" message still handles that case upstream).
- CSS (`.scurve-*`, `.accomp-table`) added beside the existing `.sc-tile`/`.scorecard` block, same `var(--surface-2)`/`var(--border-md)` dark-mode tokens.
- **No `db.js`/migration change** — `VendorDb.getVendorSchedulePerf()` already selected `*`, so `months` was already arriving on every row; this only started rendering it. vendors.html-only change, no shared `?v=` bump.
- **Not done, flag if wanted**: no per-project collapse for a vendor with many projects' curves stacked one after another; the SVG has no interactive hover crosshair (only the last-point `<title>`s); no CSV/export of the monthly table.

### ⚠️ The vendor portal was dead from ONE line of infinite recursion (2026-09-03)

**Every panel was empty and nothing errored on screen.** Trade Categories rendered blank, Products /
Services blank, Accreditation readiness stuck on "Checking…", personnel photos never appeared, the
status pill stayed a placeholder em-dash. Cause:

```js
function esc(v) { return window.esc ? window.esc(v) : /* inline escaping */ }
```

**A top-level `function esc` in a classic script BECOMES `window.esc`**, and the portal's inline
block is parsed AFTER `db.js`, so it overwrote db.js's global — and then `window.esc(v)` called
**itself**. `RangeError` on the first escaped string. Every render escapes something, so one line
took out the whole page. It also broke the error handler added to report it (that `catch` called
`esc` too), which is why the screen stayed silent.

- **`esc()` in `vendor-portal.html` is now self-contained and must never delegate to `window.esc`
  again** — there is a comment saying so at the definition.
- **⚠️ THREE ROUNDS OF READING THE CODE FOUND NOTHING.** What found it in seconds was **running the
  real page**: `scratchpad/mk_portal_harness.py` rebuilds `vendor-portal.html` verbatim with only
  the network layer stubbed (fake `window.supabase` + `AppAuth`, then `window.VendorDb`'s data
  methods monkey-patched — it is assigned to `window`, so they can be swapped after load), and
  records every `error` / `unhandledrejection` / `console.error` into `window.__errs`. **Reach for
  that harness on the second failed hypothesis, not the fourth.**
- **⚠️ The harness MUST cache-bust `db.js`.** It loads `assets/js/db.js` without the app's `?v=`,
  so the browser served a stale copy and the harness silently tested yesterday's code — a new
  `window.COUNTRIES` read as `undefined` for a while because of exactly this.
- `loadAll()`'s render steps are now isolated behind **`_step(label, fn, hostId)`**, which reports a
  failure INSIDE the panel it belongs to. They used to run in sequence, so the first throw silently
  took every later one with it.

### Vendor portal + staff page, one review pass (2026-09-03)

`migrations/2026-09-03_vendor_owner_and_logo.sql`, `_vendor_country.sql`, `_vendor_claim_tin_only.sql`
(all **run**), plus a re-run of `2026-09-01_vendor_edit_guard_consolidated.sql`.

**⚠️ A VENDOR MAY NOT OPEN AN INTERNAL PAGE, and the guard lives in `auth.js`.** None of the nine
internal pages had a vendor check — not index, project, admin, review, wp-form, my-wps, claim-form,
project-selector or import-monitoring. RLS starved the data (the dashboard rendered zeros) but the
page still exposed the tab set, the KPIs, the reporting structure and every feature name.
`requireLogin` now redirects `role==='vendor'` to the portal, so **a page added later is covered by
default**. Vendor surfaces opt IN with `window.__vendorSurface`, set before `auth.js` runs; only
`vendor-portal.html` does. **Never gate this per page again.**

**Separate front doors.** `auth.js` reads **`window.__loginPage`** for all three redirects it owns
(no session, unapproved profile, `logout()`), defaulting to `login.html`. `vendor-portal.html` sets
it to the new **`vendor-login.html`**, which routes a vendor to the portal, a still-pending claimant
back to `vendor-register.html` (they have **no `users` row** by design, so they must never land in
the staff approval queue), and a Megawide account to its own entrance.
**⚠️ Take the anon key from an existing public page, never from memory** — the first draft had a
wrong one.

**Vendor detail and Vendor Registrations are PAGES, not modals** — `vendors.html?vendor=<id>` and
`?claims=1`. Three `.page-content` siblings; **`_vdShow('directory'|'detail'|'claims')`** decides
which is visible (it still accepts its old boolean).
- **⚠️ THE SECTION/BODY IDS ARE UNCHANGED** (`#dsec-*`, `#dtab-*`, `#claimsModalBody`) — every
  `renderDetail*()` and `renderClaims()` writes into them, so the markup moved with no change to any
  of them. `openDetailModal` / `closeDetailModal` / `openClaimsModal` are kept as aliases.
- **⚠️ THE BLANK-PAGE TRAP, hit again:** `_vdShow` revealed a page with `style.display = ''`, which
  clears the inline value and hands control to the stylesheet's own `#vendorDetailPage{display:none}`
  — directory hid, profile never appeared. **The initial hide is INLINE and both branches set an
  explicit value; no stylesheet rule may claim `display` for these.** Same trap as Known Issues #30.
- Sidebar: **its own LABELLED section ("Approvals") immediately after the Admin block**, so it reads
  directly below User Management. It cannot go INSIDE that block — the queue is open to every
  contributor while `#sidebar-admin` is revealed on `isAdmin` alone, which would also expose User
  Management / New Project / the project tools. **The label is what stops it being an unlabelled
  orphan** for a contributor, which is how it read before.

**Accreditation requirements = the email Procurement sends**: BIR 2303, Company Profile, Business
Permits, Scanned Copy of Invoice. **⚠️ ONLY LABELS AND ORDER CHANGED — `ACCRED_DOC_TYPES` keys are
stored in `vendor_documents.doc_type` on every uploaded row, so renaming one orphans every existing
upload.** Added **Owner** and **Contact # of Owner**; they were on the checklist for a day before
either form offered the inputs, so those two items were unsatisfiable.
- **Staff reach the documents on the vendor page's Accreditation section** (`renderDetailAccred`) —
  readiness bar, a row per document with on-file state, filed date, a signed-URL **View**, the lock
  marker, and **Upload/Replace**. Staff upload is deliberate: no vendor has ever logged in, so these
  arrive by email to an officer. Previously reachable only as a one-line strip inside the
  request-review panel, which is suppressed entirely for a vendor with no request/documents/login.

**⚠️ Upload guards are PER DOCUMENT TYPE** (`accredDocRule` / `accredDocAccept` / `checkAccredDoc`
in `db.js` — one definition, so the portal and the staff uploader cannot disagree): BIR 2303 and
the invoice 5 MB, permits 10 MB, company profile 20 MB with DOC/PPT allowed because it is a deck.
Each row states its allowance BEFORE the file is picked. **Enforced on submit, not just via
`accept`** — that attribute is a picker hint and is trivially bypassed. **Checks the EXTENSION, not
the MIME type**: browsers report `file.type` inconsistently, so a MIME check would reject
legitimate uploads. This is a usability guard, not a security boundary. `split('.').pop()` on a name
with no dot returns the whole name, so "scan" once reported itself as type SCAN — check for the dot.

**⚠️ `payment_terms` is VENDOR-OWNED and no longer pinned in `internal.vendor_edit_guard`.** There
were TWO fields for one concept (a staff-locked text one and a vendor-owned number one), and the
text one was **required AND locked**, so a vendor was blocked on a checklist item they could not
fill. Payment terms are commercial: the vendor proposes their usual terms and the binding terms are
agreed per work package at bid time. One field, free text (real terms are richer than a day count).
The canonical guard file was edited and **re-run**; its verification now asserts the ABSENCE of that
pin (`payment_terms_left_to_vendor`), so the earlier 17-field superset check now expects 16.

**Contact Person has ONE editor: the Personnel tab.** `vendors.contact_*` is now a mirror of
whoever is flagged primary there, re-synced on every personnel change. **⚠️ That column is NOT
abandoned** — the checklist, the staff cards, the analytics and the WP vendor pickers all read it;
it just stops being separately editable. A lone contact counts as primary without the box ticked.
**⚠️ With NO personnel on file the four fields stay EDITABLE** with a prompt: they are required
checklist items and a vendor must never be blocked on a field they cannot fill.

**Personnel cards** (both sides) use the portal's `.prof-*` shape. The name **wraps to two lines**
instead of truncating — it was cutting real names short, the one thing a contact card must not do.
**⚠️ `_perLoadPhotos()` REPLACES `.prof-av`'s innerHTML with the `<img>`, so anything nested inside
the avatar is destroyed when a photo loads** — that had been silently deleting the primary-contact
dot. The dot and the camera button now live in a `.prof-avwrap` BESIDE the avatar.

**Country + dial code.** `vendors.country` (ISO alpha-2, defaulted `PH`) replaces the stopgap of
typing "City, Country" into City. Roster and dial codes are `window.COUNTRIES` in `db.js` so adding
one needs no migration; ~21 relevant markets plus Other, because a long list is worse to use than a
short right one. The phone fields carry the dial code and **only ever replace a RECOGNISED prefix or
fill an empty field** — a number typed with another prefix (`00 44 …`) is left alone.
**`dialPrefixOf` matches longest-first**, or `+65` would be read as `+6`.

Also: the **company logo** is the vendor's to set in Company Info (private bucket → signed URL,
initials fallback) and shows on the staff page; a **personnel photo can be swapped in place**;
**"Other supporting document" takes several files** (uploaded one at a time so a mid-way failure
names the file and keeps what landed); change history **restores from an ENTRY, not a calendar**
(passing that entry's own `changed_at`, so the version you are looking at is the version you get
back — the MS model); and `.field-hint` now exists in the portal at 11px (it is defined in
`vendor-register.html`, and using it here before defining it rendered hints at full body size).

**Shared `?v=` reached `20260903g` across this batch.**

## Vendor Management — Phase 2b (2026-08-10)

Links a work package to the vendor directory and adds per-WP competitive-bidding history. Migration: **`migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_phase2b.sql`** (run once, requires Phase 2a's migration already applied) — adds `work_packages.vendor_id uuid references vendors(id)` (nullable, additive; the existing free-text `contractor` column is untouched and still populated in parallel for display/legacy filters) and a new **`vendor_bids`** table (`vendor_id`, `wp_id` on delete cascade, `project_id`, `original_offer`/`negotiated_offer`/`final_amount`, `status` check `participated|shortlisted|awarded|lost|withdrawn` default `participated`, `bid_date`, `award_date`, `notes`, plus the standard audit columns). **UNIQUE (vendor_id, wp_id)** — a vendor bidding twice on the same WP updates in place rather than duplicating. **RLS mirrors `vendor_rates` exactly** (staff read/write for `super_admin/admin/specialist/manager/user`, `viewer` excluded, `viewer_budget` can read since that role is allowed cost data, `vendor` role has **zero** access — this is staff's internal bidding data about a vendor, not the vendor's own record, unlike `vendor_products`/`certifications`/`personnel` which a vendor edits about themselves).

- **`assets/js/db.js`**: `_isMissingVendorCol`/`_stripVendor` follow the exact `_isMissingBcbCol`/`_stripBcb` deploy-order-retry pattern, folded into `_stripOptional` and the retry chains in `submitWP`/`updateWP`/`updateWPDirect` — a WP save on an un-migrated environment silently drops `vendor_id` and succeeds rather than failing outright. `VendorDb` gained: `getBidsForWP(wpId)` (joins vendor name via a manual second query + merge, since no Supabase-join select syntax was already in use elsewhere in this file), `getBidsForVendor(vendorId)`, `upsertBid(wpId,vendorId,projectId,fields,profile)` (upsert on `vendor_id,wp_id`), `deleteBid(id)`, `searchApprovedVendors(query)` (status='approved', case-insensitive `ilike`, limit 20 — feeds the combobox typeahead), `quickCreateVendor(name,profile)`, and **`reconcileBidsOnAward(wpId,vendorId,finalAmount)`** — upserts the winning vendor's bid to `status='awarded'` + `final_amount` + today's date, and sets every OTHER bid on that WP to `status='lost'` (skips `withdrawn` ones — a vendor that pulled out isn't "lost"). No-ops (never throws) when `vendorId` is empty, and swallows a missing-`vendor_bids`-table error the same deploy-safe way as the column guards, so it can never fail the WP save it's attached to.
- **`invite_email` is `NOT NULL` on `vendors`** (Phase 2a schema) — `quickCreateVendor` does NOT relax that constraint (a staff-side quick-add from the WP form has no invite email to give, that's only collected via the vendors.html "Invite Vendor" flow). Instead it synthesizes a placeholder (`pending+<timestamp>.<rand>@no-invite.local`) that can never collide with a real address a vendor could self-register with (`vendor-register.html` only matches on `invite_email` + `invite_claimed_at is null`) — staff can overwrite it later when formally inviting the vendor through the normal flow.
- **Awarded Vendor combobox, `wp-form.html`**: `#f-contractor` stays the visible text field (still saves as plain text either way) alongside a new hidden `#f-vendor-id` and popover `#vc-pop`, structurally mirroring `import-monitoring.html`'s searchable project combobox (`#proj-search`/`#proj-options` pattern) but built fresh per this codebase's no-shared-JS-module convention. Typing debounces (~250ms) into `VendorDb.searchApprovedVendors`; picking a result sets both fields; typing a name with no exact match shows an inline **"+ Add '<name>' as a new vendor"** option that calls `quickCreateVendor` and selects the result immediately — so an officer isn't blocked waiting on the vendors.html invite flow for a vendor that isn't in the directory yet. Edit-mode load seeds `_selectedVendorId` from `existingWP.vendor_id` (the text already arrives via the existing `contractor` fill). Clearing the text input clears the selected id. Save payload includes `vendor_id: _selectedVendorId||null` alongside `contractor`. **After a successful save**, if `award_status==='Awarded'` and both `vendor_id` and `awarded_cost` are present, `VendorDb.reconcileBidsOnAward(savedWpId, vendor_id, awarded_cost)` fires with a `.catch(console.error)` — deliberately fire-and-forget so a bid-reconciliation failure can never block the WP save or its success toast.
- **Same combobox, `project.html`'s Status Tracker award modal (`#st-award-modal`)**: `#st-award-vendor` + hidden `#st-award-vendor-id` + `#st-vc-pop`, duplicated (not shared) per the same convention — `_stVcRender`/`_stVcSelect`/`_stVcAddNew`/`_stInitVendorCombo`, prefixed `_st` to avoid colliding with wp-form.html's identically-shaped functions (they're different pages, but keep the prefix if this logic is ever consolidated). `_stOpenAwardModal` seeds the vendor id from the WP's stored `vendor_id`; `stAwardConfirm()` adds `vendor_id` to the fields object passed to `_stSaveFields` and, after a successful save, fires `VendorDb.reconcileBidsOnAward` the same fire-and-forget way as wp-form.html.
- **Not built yet**: switching the WP List Filtered Analysis vendor filter (`_msfSel`/`_recomputeFilterOptions`/`_wpVendors` in `index.html`/`project.html`) from free-text `contractor` parsing to the `vendor_id` FK — left on `contractor` text for now since WPs aren't broadly linked yet.

### Log Bid UI + Bid History — `window.BidWidget` (2026-08-10)

The two deferred Phase 2b fast-follows are now built as **one shared self-contained widget** in `assets/js/ui.js` (`window.BidWidget`, appended after `AppNotify`) — chosen over duplicating ~120 lines of combobox+form logic across the two WP detail panels. Same self-contained convention as `AppNotify`/`Tooltip`: injects its own `#bidwidget-css` (dark-mode via CSS vars), one document-level outside-click listener to close the vendor popover, single-instance module state (only one WP-detail / one vendor-detail is open at a time). Two entry points:
- **`BidWidget.mount(container, {wpId, projectId, canEdit, onChange})`** — the **Log Bid** UI, mounted into the WP detail slide-in panel on **both `index.html` and `project.html`** (`openWPDetail` appends a `<div id="wp-bid-mount">` and calls `mount()` right after setting the panel's `innerHTML`, since `openWPDetail` is synchronous but bids load async). Lists every bid on the WP (`VendorDb.getBidsForWP`, which already joins vendor name), each row showing the vendor, a status pill (participated/shortlisted/awarded/lost/withdrawn), the offer chain (`Orig → Neg → Final`) and bid/award dates. When `canEdit`, a **Log Bid** button opens an inline add form with a **searchable approved-vendor combobox** (`searchApprovedVendors`, debounced 250ms) + inline **quick-create** for an unknown vendor (`quickCreateVendor` → pending_review) — the SAME pattern as wp-form.html's Awarded Vendor combobox, but generalized. Add/edit both go through `VendorDb.upsertBid` (unique `(vendor_id,wp_id)` means editing = re-upserting the same vendor; the combobox is `readonly` while editing so the vendor can't be changed out from under the row). Delete via `deleteBid`. **The combobox picks vendors by ID from a cached `_vcResults` array — it never interpolates a vendor NAME into an inline `onclick`** (avoids the HTML-attr/JS-arg double-encoding XSS trap called out in Known Issues #23).
