- **`BidWidget.mountForVendor(container, {vendorId, canEdit})`** — the **Bid History** tab, a new 6th section in `vendors.html`'s (already-restructured, scroll-spy) detail modal (`#dsec-bids`/`#dtab-bids`, jump link "Bid History", rendered in `openDetailModal`'s `Promise.all` via `renderDetailBids()`). Read-only per-vendor list (+ staff delete) — bids are LOGGED per-WP, not added here. Uses the enhanced **`getBidsForVendor`** (now merges `wp_no`/`wp_description` from `work_packages`, mirroring `getBidsForWP`'s vendor-name merge) so each row reads `WP <no> · <description> · <project> · Bid <date>`.
- **Money gating**: bids carry offer amounts, so callers must NOT mount for the plain `viewer` role — both panels gate the mount on `!isViewer` (`isViewer = window.__hideBudget`), so `viewer_budget` and all staff see bids but plain `viewer` never does, matching `vendor_bids` RLS (which denies `viewer` server-side too). **Write** (`canEdit`) is stricter: `project.html` uses `window.__canEdit && !window.__isViewer && !window.__archived`; `index.html` uses `AppAuth.canEditProject(window.__profile, w.project_id) && !window.__isViewer`; `vendors.html` uses `userProfile.role !== 'viewer'`.
- **Award reconciliation is unchanged** — logging a bid does NOT change WP award status; `reconcileBidsOnAward` (wired into the wp-form save + Status Tracker award modal, Phase 2b) still fires at award time and flips the winner→awarded / others→lost. `BidWidget.mount`'s `onChange` is available but currently unused (a bid edit is metadata, not a WP mutation).
- **No `db.js` API changes beyond the `getBidsForVendor` WP-context merge** — `getBidsForWP`/`upsertBid`/`deleteBid`/`searchApprovedVendors`/`quickCreateVendor` already existed from Phase 2b. When adding a bid field, it flows through generically (the form reads fixed ids `bw-orig`/`bw-neg`/`bw-final`/`bw-status`/`bw-bid-date`/`bw-notes` — extend `formHtml()` + `_save()`'s `fields` object together).

### Proposed Vendors — multi-select chip combobox, `wp-form.html` (2026-08-10)

**Proposed Vendors** (Identity & Classification section) was pure free text (`work_packages.proposed_vendors`, a textarea) — the only remaining vendor field on the WP form not linked to the directory (Awarded Vendor was linked in Phase 2b). It's now a **multi-select tag/chip combobox**, mirroring the Awarded Vendor combobox's visual style and search/quick-create behavior but allowing several vendors per WP instead of one.

- **New column**: `work_packages.proposed_vendor_ids uuid[] DEFAULT '{}'` (`migrations/2026-08-10_wp_proposed_vendor_ids.sql`, run once). **Additive only** — the existing `proposed_vendors` text column is untouched and keeps being written in parallel (denormalized names, newline-joined), so every existing reader (the free-text vendor filter `_wpVendors()`/`_projVendors()` in `index.html`/`project.html`, CSV/Excel import+export) keeps working unmodified. No FK constraint on the array elements (Postgres can't constrain array elements natively; vendor delete/merge via `delete_vendor_cascade`/`merge_vendors` doesn't scrub this array yet — low-traffic edge case, flag if it matters).
- **`assets/js/db.js` deploy-safe guard**: `_isMissingProposedVendorIdsCol`/`_stripProposedVendorIds`, folded into `_stripOptional` and the three `submitWP`/`updateWP`/`updateWPDirect` retry chains — same pattern as `_isMissingVendorCol`/`_stripVendor`. **The existing `_isMissingVendorCol` regex was tightened from `/vendor_id/` to `/vendor_id\b/`** in the same pass — `vendor_id` is a literal substring of `proposed_vendor_ids`, so the old loose pattern would have matched a "`proposed_vendor_ids` column not found" error too (false cross-trigger, stripping the wrong column). `vendor_id\b` requires a non-word character (or end of string) right after `vendor_id`; inside `proposed_vendor_ids` the next character is `s` (still a word character, no boundary), so it correctly fails to match there while still matching a real `'vendor_id'` error (always followed by a quote or other punctuation). `_isMissingProposedVendorIdsCol` matches the literal `proposed_vendor_ids` substring, which likewise never appears in a plain `vendor_id` error — so the two guards can't cross-trigger in either direction. New helper **`VendorDb.getVendorsByIds(ids)`** (`db.js`) resolves a set of ids to `{id,name,status}` rows — used to populate chip labels on edit-mode load without pulling the whole ~900-row directory (`VendorDb.getVendors()`).
- **UI (`wp-form.html`)**: the textarea was replaced with `#pv-wrap` (chips row `#pv-chips` + search input `#pv-input` + popover `#pv-pop`, reusing the `.vc-pop`/`.vc-opt`/`.vc-add`/`.vc-empty` CSS from the Awarded Vendor combobox) plus a **hidden** `#f-proposed-vendors` that auto-regenerates (newline-joined chip names) on every chip add/remove via `_pvSyncHidden()` — so the legacy text column always saves a coherent value with zero user typing into it directly. State: `_pvChips` (`[{id, name}]`, single ordered array — `id` is a real `vendors.id` for a linked chip, `null` for a legacy/unlinked one). Typing debounces into `VendorDb.searchApprovedVendors` (excluding already-added vendors); picking a result ADDS a chip and clears the input for the next pick (does not close/replace, unlike the single-select); an inline "+ Add '<name>' as a new vendor" option (`VendorDb.quickCreateVendor`) covers a vendor not yet in the directory. Each chip has a small × (event-delegated via `data-pv-remove`, not a per-chip inline `onclick` with an interpolated name — avoids the HTML-attr/JS-arg double-encoding trap called out elsewhere in this doc for vendor/company names that might contain quotes). Chip text is `esc()`-escaped.
- **Legacy rows**: `_pvLoadFromWP(wp)` on edit-mode load prefers `proposed_vendor_ids` (resolved via `getVendorsByIds`) when present; when empty/null it parses the legacy `proposed_vendors` text (same `/[\r\n;|]+/` delimiter as `_wpVendors()`) into **unlinked "legacy" chips** (muted/dashed style, an "unlinked" tag) so nothing already on file is silently dropped. A legacy chip's name still flows into the regenerated text column; only linked chips (`id` set) populate `proposed_vendor_ids` on save. Staff can leave a legacy chip as-is or delete it and re-add the same vendor via search/quick-add to link it.
- **Save payload**: `proposed_vendor_ids: _pvChips.filter(c=>c.id).map(c=>c.id)` alongside the existing `proposed_vendors` (now read from the auto-synced hidden field).
- **Autosave/draft**: `_pvChips` is snapshotted into the draft (`draft.pv`, same treatment as the Approval Matrix's `draft.ms`) and restored explicitly in `restoreDraft()` (chips restored first, then the hidden text field is regenerated from them — `f-proposed-vendors` is added to `restoreDraft`'s generic-restore `skip` list so the plain value-copy pass can't leave it holding stale text that disagrees with the restored chips).
- **This closes the loop for NEW work packages** — a WP created or edited through this form from now on always has an accurate `proposed_vendor_ids`, so the one-time `backfillVendorDataFromWPs`-style re-derivation from free text (used to retrofit historical rows onto the vendor directory) is only ever needed again for pre-existing historical WPs, never for new ones.

### `vendor_rates` backfill ON CONFLICT bug + resilience, "Invite Vendor" → "Add Vendor" (2026-08-10)

- **Bug — backfill failed with "no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification."** `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_rates_wp_link.sql` created a **PARTIAL** unique index (`vendor_rates_wp_uidx ON vendor_rates(vendor_id, wp_id) WHERE wp_id IS NOT NULL`), but `VendorDb.upsertRate()` (`db.js`) calls `.upsert(payload, {onConflict:'vendor_id,wp_id'})`, which PostgREST translates into a plain `ON CONFLICT (vendor_id, wp_id)` with **no WHERE predicate** — Postgres requires the ON CONFLICT target to exactly match an existing constraint, and a partial index never satisfies a non-partial arbiter. **Fix:** `migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_rates_wp_link_fix.sql` (run once, idempotent — `DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS` before `ADD CONSTRAINT`) drops the partial index and replaces it with a FULL unique constraint `vendor_rates_vendor_wp_uidx UNIQUE(vendor_id, wp_id)`. This still works correctly for manually-entered rates with `wp_id IS NULL` — standard SQL unique constraints never treat NULL as equal to NULL, so any number of manual rate rows with a null `wp_id` remain unconstrained, exactly as before. `vendor_bids` was NOT affected — its `vendor_bids_vendor_wp_unique` constraint (`migrations/2026-08-10_vendor_phase2b.sql`) was already a full (non-partial) unique constraint.
- **Resilience — `backfillVendorDataFromWPs()` (`db.js`) had no per-item error handling.** Its four execution loops (trade `updateVendor`, `products.add`, `upsertBid`, `upsertRate`) each `await`ed directly with no try/catch, so a single failure anywhere aborted the whole function with none of the remaining work run and no partial-progress reporting. Each loop body is now individually try/caught, tallying successes AND failures separately (`tradesUpdated`/`tradesFailed`, `productsAdded`/`productsFailed`, `bidsUpserted`/`bidsFailed`, `ratesUpserted`/`ratesFailed`) and logging failures to `console.error` with vendor/WP id context, then continuing. Since the function's writes are already upsert/dedup-by-description (safely re-runnable), a partial-failure run followed by a second run cleanly finishes whatever didn't complete the first time. `vendors.html`'s `backfillFromWPs()` toast now reports a failure count (`AppNotify.warn`, "N failure(s) — see console; safe to re-run.") instead of unconditionally showing success when any loop had failures.
- **"Invite Vendor" → "Add Vendor" (`vendors.html`).** The toolbar button and modal now read **"Add Vendor"**; the modal collects the FULL field set at creation — Company Name (required), Trade Categories, Contact Person/Number/Email, Address, Notes — mirroring the detail modal's `renderDetailOverview()` field set, instead of only Company Name + Trade Categories + a required Invite Email. **Invite Email is now OPTIONAL**: when left blank, `createInvite()` synthesizes the same placeholder format `quickCreateVendor()` uses (`pending+<timestamp>.<rand>@no-invite.local`, via a new local `_addVendorPlaceholderEmail()`) since `vendors.invite_email` is `NOT NULL`; when an email IS provided, the copyable invite link still generates exactly as before, now framed as optional/secondary copy ("An invite link was also generated — send it if you'd like this vendor to self-manage their own profile"). **New vendors created via this form are set to `status:'approved'` immediately** (`createInvite()` calls `VendorDb.createVendor()` then `VendorDb.approveVendor(v.id)`) — per explicit decision, staff have already vetted the data they just typed in, unlike vendor self-registration (`vendor-register.html`, stays `pending_review`) or the bulk `importVendorsFromWPs`/`quickCreateVendor` paths (unchanged, stay `pending_review` — those are lower-confidence auto-created records with no human review at creation time). `createVendor()` itself still always inserts as `pending_review` (unchanged, still correct default for every OTHER caller) — the approve-immediately behavior lives only in `vendors.html`'s `createInvite()`, not in `db.js`.

To re-enable: remove `style="display:none"` from sidebar section + tab button in `project.html`; Claims tab button in `index.html`; restore Claims/CO cards in template picker modals.

`claim-form.html`: `?section=change-order` for CO mode, `?project=ID` pre-selects project, `?id=UUID` for edit. Claims and COs share the `claims` table distinguished by `claim_type`.

## CSS / Styling

**Design system**: `--mw-red: #EE3124`, `--mw-black: #231F20`, `--mw-dark: #282C28`. Font: Montserrat via `<link>` (not `@import`). Icons: Tabler Icons v2.44 (`ti ti-*`).

**Breakpoints**: â‰¥1024px desktop sidebar (240px) | â‰¤1024px tablet (220px) | â‰¤767px mobile slide-in drawer | â‰¤399px single-column.

**View tabs** (`.view-tabs` / `.view-tab`): defined globally in `dashboard.css` â€” do NOT redefine inline. On mobile: `position:sticky; top:52px`, horizontally scrollable.

**Mobile topbar** (52px): `.topbar` must have `overflow:visible` â€” `hidden` clips the user profile dropdown.

**Critical â€” sticky tabs + overflow**: Never set `overflow-x:hidden` or `overflow-x:clip` on `.main`, `.content`, `html`, or `body` on pages with sticky tabs â€” kills `position:sticky` in Safari iOS. Clamp overflow at element level:
```css
canvas { max-width:100% !important; }
.panel, .grid-2 > *, .grid-3 > * { min-width:0; }
.data-table, .budget-table { display:block; overflow-x:auto; }
```
Standalone pages (login, register, pending, forgot-password) without sticky tabs can use `overflow-x:hidden` safely.

**Logo**: styled globally in `dashboard.css` â€” do NOT add inline `.sidebar-logo img` CSS to individual HTML files.

**PWA / Add-to-Home-Screen icon**: `manifest.webmanifest` (repo root) + `assets/icons/` (generated from `../procurement app-white-logo.png` via Pillow â€” **white** Megawide mark + "PROCUREMENT APP" text on a full-bleed **red `#EE2E24`** square; the source tile has transparent corners so the canvas is filled with the sampled tile-red to avoid black corners on iOS). Icons: `apple-touch-icon.png` (180, iOS), `icon-192/512.png` (`purpose:any`), `icon-192/512-maskable.png` (18% padding for Android adaptive safe-zone), `favicon-32.png`. **Every** `*.html` `<head>` carries the same block (`rel=manifest`, `apple-touch-icon`, favicon, `apple-mobile-web-app-*` + `mobile-web-app-capable` metas, title "Procurement") â€” inserted idempotently (skip if `apple-touch-icon` already present). All paths are relative (pages + assets are at the `/prc-app/` root). To regenerate icons: re-run the Pillow snippet (sample tile-red bg, center, pad 4% regular / 14% maskable).

**One visual language (2026-07-23).** A second, Tailwind-ish blue palette (`#EFF6FF` / `#BFDBFE` / `#1e40af` / `#2563EB` / `#dbeafe`) ran through the WP form, importer, claim form and info panels alongside the Megawide red/black — **75 occurrences**. All are now mapped onto brand-derived tints: info surface → `#FDECEA` (`--mw-red-light`), chip surface → `#FBD9D5`, border → `#F5B8B3`, ink → `#C42127` (`--mw-red-dark`), action fill → `--mw-red`. The matching **dark-mode** panel surfaces moved from blue-grey (`#1B2533`/`#2A3F5F`) to red-tinted (`#33211F`/`#5A2E2A`) so both themes tell the same story. Contrast verified: 5.11:1 (light panel), 7.91:1 (dark panel), 6.23:1 (chip — its ink was darkened to `#9B1B1B` because `#C42127` landed at 4.45:1, just under AA). `onboarding.html`'s role-tier accents keep their own hues deliberately — they encode admin/contributor/viewer, not brand.

**Root font-size respects the browser preference (2026-07-23).** `html { font-size: 14px }` hard-overrode a user's chosen default, silently shrinking the UI for anyone who had set a larger one. It is now **`87.5%`** — identical at the 16px default, but it scales with the preference — and `body` pins `font-size:14px` so nothing shifted. **Font-sizes are now in `rem`** (2026-07-23): all **1,022 `font-size:Npx`** across `dashboard.css`, the 8 app pages and `db.js`/`ui.js`/`charts.js` were converted to rem at **1rem = 14px** (px ÷ 14, since the root is 87.5% of the 16px default). Pixel-identical at the default; text now scales with the user's browser font-size preference. **Only font-size was converted** — structural px (widths, positioning, borders, radii, shadows, and every JS-coupled dimension: grid sticky-column offsets, colgroup widths, chart canvas sizes) stay px on purpose. Chart.js numeric `font:{size:N}` configs are canvas, not CSS, and were untouched. Standalone pages with their own root (login/register/importer/onboarding) keep px. When you author a NEW font-size, use rem (`px/14`) on app pages.

**Topbar project count (2026-07-23).** `index.html`'s subtitle counted archived projects while the filter beside it said "N of M active" — the two disagreed (16 vs 15). It now reads `N active project(s)` and appends ` (+K archived)` only when archived projects exist.

**Errors use `AppNotify`, never `alert()` (2026-07-23).** The app raised **28 blocking `alert()`s**, 7 of which dumped a raw Postgres message at the user. `window.AppNotify` (end of `ui.js`) renders a dismissible toast top-right: `.error(msg,{title,detail})` (stays until dismissed, `role="alert"`), `.warn/.success/.info` (auto-dismiss). **`AppNotify.fromError(err, headline)`** maps common DB failures to plain language (duplicate key → "That value already exists", `42501`/RLS → "You don't have permission", missing column → "a migration is pending", network/timeout) and tucks the raw text behind a **"Show details"** disclosure so nothing is lost for bug reports. Styles `.ntf*` in `dashboard.css`. **Don't add new `alert()` calls** — all five app pages are now at zero. `confirm()` is retained for destructive actions (delete/replace), which genuinely need to block.

**Type scale (2026-07-23).** All **50 uses of `font-size:9px`** (uppercase micro-labels — the least legible combination) were bumped to 10px across the app. 9px should not reappear; 10px is the floor.

**Extracted components (2026-07-23).** Six repeated inline-style patterns became classes in `dashboard.css`, removing **94 inline declarations**: **`.seg-btn`/`.seg-btn.active`** (the Monthly|Quarterly, Budget|# WPs, Chart|Table toggles — 32 buttons), `.field-label`, `.sec-label`, `.hint-sm`, `.input-std`, `.empty-cell`. **The `.seg-btn` extraction also replaced ~15 JS setters that rewrote `el.style.cssText` on every toggle** with a shared `_segBtn(el, isActive)` that swaps a class and sets `aria-pressed`. That was the root of the "reserialised inline style escapes the dark-mode attribute selector" bug class (Known Issues, dark-mode section) — an inactive toggle now correctly follows dark mode (`--surface`) instead of staying hard-coded `#fff`. **Prefer these classes over new inline styles.**

**Status is never colour-alone (2026-07-23).** Award/procurement/submittal/delivery states were distinguished only by red vs green — invisible to ~8% of men and to a greyscale PDF print. **`window.statusLabel(text)` / `statusGlyph(text)` in `db.js`** prefix a glyph: **✔** done (Awarded/Delivered/Approved) · **◐** in progress (Sourced/Solicited/Evaluated/Submitted/Manufactured/DP Paid) · **○** open (Not Yet Awarded/Not Started/Not Required) · **⚠** attention (Overdue/Due-Not-Awarded/Rejected) · **⏱** pending. The glyph is wrapped in `aria-hidden` so screen readers read only the word. Wired into `procStatusBadge`/`submittalBadge`/`deliveryBadge`/`_stAwardBadge` (project.html) and every `award_status` cell + detail panel across `index.html`/`project.html`/`review.html`. The **battery "Due Not Awarded" segment is also striped** (`repeating-linear-gradient`) so it differs from Awarded by texture, and the legend carries the glyphs. **When you add a status badge, run its text through `statusLabel()`.**

**Tooltips — `data-tip`, not bare `title` (2026-07-23).** `title` never appears on touch, isn't keyboard reachable and is announced inconsistently. The `Tooltip` IIFE at the end of `ui.js` shows a real popover for any element with **`data-tip`** on click/tap, or Enter/Space when focused; Esc, blur, outside-click, scroll and resize all dismiss it. `kpiLabelAttrs()` now emits `data-tip` + `title` (desktop hover fallback) + `aria-label` + `tabindex=0` + `class="has-tip"`, and `.has-tip` gets a dotted underline so users can SEE that help exists. Styles: `.app-tip`, `.has-tip` in `dashboard.css`.

**Skeleton loading (2026-07-23).** Written while the project was on the free tier, whose 5–30s cold start left every page showing only a spinner and reading as broken. **The project is on Pro now and no longer cold-starts**, but the skeleton is kept — it still covers a slow network or a large WP fetch. `#loading` on `index.html`/`project.html` now renders a skeleton of the real layout (4 KPI cards → chart block → table lines) and `review.html` a table-shaped one, each with `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-busy="true"` and the spinner reduced to a small inline marker beside the text. Classes `.skel`, `.skel-card`, `.skel-line`, `.skel-grid`, `.skel-label` in `dashboard.css`; the shimmer is disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion`.

**Accessibility baseline (2026-07-23 UI audit)****Accessibility baseline (2026-07-23 UI audit)** — added at the end of `dashboard.css` under "ACCESSIBILITY & POLISH PASS":
- **Keyboard focus.** The app had **54 `outline:none` declarations and zero focus styles** — completely unusable by keyboard. A `:focus-visible` ring (2px `--mw-red`, offset 2px) now applies to every interactive element; it needs **`!important`** to beat the inline `outline:none`s (verified: an element with inline `outline:none` computes `solid` once the rule applies). The **dark sidebar gets a white ring** instead (red on `--mw-black` is too low-contrast). `:focus-visible` means mouse users see no change.
- **Skip link** (`.skip-link`, first tab stop on all 5 app pages) jumps past the sidebar to `#main-content-region`, which carries `role="main"`.
- **Contrast.** `--text-hint` was `#9B9999` = **2.83:1 (fails AA)**; now `#6E6C6C` = 5.22:1. The inline greys `#aaa` (106 uses), `#bbb` (41) and `#999` (25) are remapped to the same value **scoped to `.content`** — the dark sidebar is deliberately excluded so its greys stay light. Dark mode maps them to `#9D9B9B` (6.03:1 on `--surface`). `#888` (221 uses) is 3.54:1 — acceptable for the 10-11px bold labels it's mostly used on, left alone to avoid a sweeping visual change.
- **`prefers-reduced-motion`** disables animations/transitions (none existed before).
- **Touch targets**: `min-height:32px` on `.action-btn`/`.nav-item`/`.tab`/`.view-tab` and the icon buttons, applied only under `(hover:none) and (pointer:coarse)` so desktop tables keep their density.
- **Consistent `:disabled`** styling (opacity .5 + `not-allowed`) — previously ~20 disabled states had no shared treatment.
- `aria-label` on the icon-only topbar buttons (refresh / export PDF / export Excel) and `aria-hidden` on their decorative icons; `.sr-only` helper available for future icon-only controls.

**iOS pinch zoom****iOS pinch zoom**: handled in `ui.js` via `touchmove` + `gesturestart` listeners. `user-scalable=no` viewport is ignored by Safari iOS 10+.

**iOS PWA pull-to-refresh**: not available in standalone mode. A `.btn-refresh` button (Tabler `ti-refresh` icon, same style as `.btn-menu`) is added to `.topbar-right` on both `index.html` and `project.html`, left of the Export PDF button. On `project.html` it calls `loadData()` in-place; on `index.html` it busts `wpm_wps_idx_*` sessionStorage cache then reloads. Shows a CSS spin animation while loading; disabled during refresh to prevent double-taps. Style defined in `dashboard.css` (`.btn-refresh`, `.spin`, `@keyframes spin`).

## Script Loading (Dashboard Pages)

Scripts at **bottom of `<body>`** in this order â€” do NOT move to `<head>` without `defer`:
```html
<script src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@supabase/supabase-js@2/dist/umd/supabase.min.js"></script>
<script src="assets/js/auth.js"></script>
<script src="assets/js/db.js"></script>
<script src="assets/js/ui.js"></script>
<!-- index.html + project.html also load Chart.js + charts.js -->
<script>/* inline init */</script>
```

Public pages (login, register, pending, forgot-password) load UMD bundle inline and call `window.supabase.createClient()` directly â€” they do NOT load `auth.js`/`db.js`/`ui.js`.

Resource hints in `<head>`: `preconnect` for fonts.googleapis.com, fonts.gstatic.com, cdn.jsdelivr.net, cdnjs.cloudflare.com; `dns-prefetch` for Supabase URL; `preload as="script"` for all body scripts.

**Cache-busting (`?v=` query param)**: ALL five core asset includes (`auth.js`, `db.js`, `ui.js`, `charts.js`, `dashboard.css`) carry a shared `?v=YYYYMMDD<letter>` param in `index.html` and `project.html` â€” on both the `<link rel=preload>` and the `<script>`/`<link rel=stylesheet>` tags. GitHub Pages + browser caching can serve a **stale** asset for up to ~10 min after a push (symptom: a JS/CSS fix is confirmed live via `curl` but the user still sees old behavior â€” e.g. dark-mode chart bars still dark, or a `db.js` rank-table fix not applying, because the browser cached the previous file). **When you change ANY of those five files, bump the single `?v=` value in BOTH `index.html` and `project.html`** (use one replace for the old `?v=` string + ensure any newly-versioned file matches) so browsers refetch immediately. Current version: `20260903h` (`20260903h` = the Vendor Bid Board — `db.js` gained `VendorDb.bidBoard`, the portal gained a side panel + the Bid Board page, and the catalogue's Type/Supply-scope merge, searchable country of origin and the portal's missing design tokens; pairs with `migrations/2026-09-03_vendor_bid_board.sql`; `20260903g` = the vendor-portal review batch — vendors barred from every internal page in `auth.js`, a separate `vendor-login.html` front door via `window.__loginPage`, per-document upload rules + `window.COUNTRIES` + dial codes + the owner items in `accredReadiness` in `db.js`, and the `esc` recursion fix; pairs with `migrations/2026-09-03_vendor_owner_and_logo.sql`, `_vendor_claim_tin_only.sql`, `_vendor_country.sql` and a re-run of `2026-09-01_vendor_edit_guard_consolidated.sql`; `20260902c` = Vendor Registrations reachable by every contributor role, not just admin/super_admin — `db.js`'s `initVendorClaimsNav` now toggles a sibling wrapper instead of an ancestor gated on `AppAuth.isAdmin`, see "Vendor Registrations — a top-level sidebar item"; `20260902b` = the vendor portal could not load ANY vendor — `_vendorRel` hardening + the four accreditation documents, see “Vendor portal — the profile never loaded”; `20260902a` = `20260902a` = vendor catalogue — `db.js` gained `uploadVendorFile`, see “Vendor portal — catalogue, profile cards, capability”; pairs with `migrations/2026-09-02_vendor_catalogue.sql`; `20260901e` = `20260901e` = Vendor Registrations promoted to a top-level sidebar item — `db.js` gained `initVendorClaimsNav`/`setVendorClaimsNavCount`, see “Vendor Registrations — a top-level sidebar item”; `20260901d` = `20260901d` = vendor change history + restore — `db.js` gained `VendorDb.history`, see “Vendor change history — version history and restore”; pairs with `migrations/2026-09-01_vendor_history_restore.sql`; pairs with `migrations/2026-09-01_vendor_doc_lock.sql`; `20260901a` = `20260901a` = vendor child tables lost DELETE for the vendor role and gained archive/restore — `db.js` `_child()` rewritten, see “Vendor child data — archive, not delete”; pairs with `migrations/2026-09-01_vendor_child_soft_delete.sql`; `20260825g` = `20260825g` = the PMO ruling that an awarded WP counts whatever its cost, including ₱0 — `isMoneyAwarded` now delegates to `isResolved` (Known Issues #43); ## Onboarding / Guided Tour

Three deliverables share the same copy, organised by **capability tier** (not the 6 raw roles):
- **admin** = `super_admin`, `admin` · **contributor** = `specialist`, `manager`, `user` · **viewer** = `viewer`.
- `tierOf(role)` in `onboarding.js` does the mapping; content is personalised to the logged-in user's tier.

1. **In-app modal** (`assets/js/onboarding.js`, `window.Onboarding`) â€” a carousel of ~7 slides (Welcome → Two views → Reading the dashboard → Finding WPs → **your tier** → capability matrix → Tips). Self-contained: injects its own `#ob-css` styles (uses the `--surface`/`--text-*`/`--mw-*` CSS vars so it follows dark mode), builds its own DOM, keyboard nav (←/→/Esc), backdrop-click + **×** to close, progress dots. Viewer variant drops all cost content.
   - **Auto-open once per user:** `Onboarding.maybeAutoOpen(userId, role)` opens the tour ~650 ms after load unless `localStorage['wpm_onboarded_<userId>']` is set. The **"Don't show on login"** checkbox (checked by default) + finishing on the last slide set that flag.
   - **Re-open any time:** a **?** `.btn-guide` button is injected into `.topbar-right` (before `#user-bar`, idempotent) on every page that loads the script; `onclick → Onboarding.open()` reads `window.__wpmRole` at open time. A **Restart tour** item in the profile dropdown (`renderUserBar` in `db.js`, above Sign out) calls `Onboarding.open()` when available, else navigates to `onboarding.html` (fallback for pages that don't load onboarding.js). The **login page** also links to `onboarding.html` ("View the Training Guide", opens new tab) for pre-login training access.

**Coach-mark tour (`assets/js/coachmarks.js`, `window.CoachTour`):** an interactive spotlight tour that dims the screen and highlights REAL UI elements with a tooltip card (Back/Next/Done, progress, Esc/arrow keys, auto-skips missing/hidden elements). **The card is PINNED to a fixed corner (bottom-right), NOT anchored beside the moving spotlight** (2026-07-30) — the buttons stayed in one place so the user doesn't have to chase a travelling **Next** between steps; `reposition()` keeps the horizontal position constant and only flips the card vertically to the top when the spotlight would otherwise cover the bottom-right slot. Self-contained (injects `#ct-css`, dark-mode via CSS vars). `CoachTour.configure({key,steps})` where a step is `{sel,title,body,before?,pad?}` (`before()` can switch tabs to reveal the target); `start(force)`, `maybeAutoStart()` (once per user via `localStorage['wpm_<key>_<uid>']`, ~800ms delay), `available()`. **index.html** (`key:coach_portfolio`) and **project.html** (`key:coach_project`) build role-aware steps in their `requireLogin` callback (viewer/archived-only steps skipped) and call `maybeAutoStart()` — this REPLACED the slide-deck auto-open (`Onboarding.maybeAutoOpen`). The slide deck stays reachable via the topbar **?** button; the profile-menu **Restart tour** now prefers `CoachTour.start(true)` (falls back to `Onboarding.open()` / `onboarding.html`). Versioned independently: `?v=20260730a`. **`wp-form.html` also has a CoachTour** (`key:coach_wpform`, 13 steps: project → WP No. → description → **Trade→Works→Type cascade** → OSM → Approval Matrix → Submittals → Schedule → Budget → Procurement Status (drives Award) → Award Status → Charging → Save) configured at the end of its `requireLogin` callback + `maybeAutoStart()`; re-openable via a topbar **?** `#btn-form-guide` button. wp-form loads `coachmarks.js` (steps auto-skip fields hidden for the role).

**Patch notice — TEMPORARY, latest round 2026-08-10, delete after 2026-08-19 (`assets/js/patch-notice.js`, `window.PatchNotice`):** a one-off "What's New" modal, replaced each round with that period's content — the first round (Jul 27–Aug 3, headline: **Not to be Awarded**) expired 2026-08-08 and was overwritten in place (new `SEEN_PREFIX='wpm_patch_202608b_seen_'` so returning users see the new round even if they dismissed the old one). **Current round (Aug 4–10) headline: Buyback work packages** (depreciation % or exact-amount recovery counting toward savings); **"Also this week": BCB0/BCB1/BCB2 budget baselines, then Group Head tagging + filter** (in that order — content and order were iterated live via a throwaway `_preview_patch_notice.html` harness at the repo root, force-calling `PatchNotice.open()` outside the real expiry/localStorage gates for fast visual iteration, deleted before commit). Shown to contributor-tier (`specialist`/`manager`/`user`) **and** `admin`/`super_admin` — never `viewer`/`viewer_budget`, never `vendor` (vendor logins never load `index.html`/`project.html` at all) — gated by a hard-coded `EXPIRES = new Date('2026-08-19T00:00:00')` that both `maybeAutoOpen()` and `open()` check, so nothing can render it past that instant regardless of localStorage state. Auto-opens once per user 1.5s after login (deliberately later than onboarding.js's 650ms / coachmarks.js's 800ms, so it doesn't stack with either on a brand-new contributor's first login). Same self-contained pattern as `onboarding.js` (own injected CSS, `--surface`/`--text-*`/`--mw-red` vars, dark-mode automatic). Wired into `index.html` + `project.html` only (the two pages contributors actively work in) — not `admin.html`/`review.html`/`wp-form.html`. **On-demand access, not just the once-only auto-open**: `renderUserBar()` (`db.js`) now adds a **"What's New"** item to the profile dropdown, gated on `typeof window.PatchNotice !== 'undefined'` so it only appears on the two pages that load the script — calls `PatchNotice.open()` directly, bypassing both the expiry-adjacent 1.5s delay and the seen-flag, so any user can revisit the current round's notes anytime before it expires (mirrors the existing "Restart tour" item's shape). Versioned independently: `?v=20260810b`, NOT part of the 5-file shared bump — bump it whenever patch-notice.js's own content changes without needing to touch auth/db/ui/charts/dashboard.css. **Cleanup: once past 2026-08-19, delete `assets/js/patch-notice.js`, its `<link rel=preload>`/`<script>`/`maybeAutoOpen(...)` call on both pages, and the "What's New" dropdown item's `typeof window.PatchNotice` block in `renderUserBar()`** — the date gate makes the modal itself inert but the dropdown entry would otherwise silently vanish (harmless, since the guard just stops matching) rather than being intentionally removed.

**New-user aids (in-context help):** (1) **KPI tooltips** — `window.KPI_HELP` + `window.kpiLabelAttrs(lbl)` in `db.js` add a hover `title` to every Cost-Overview metric label (`card()` helpers in `index.html`/`project.html`). (2) **WP-form field tooltips** — small `ⓘ` info spans (native `title`) on Budget (BCB), Awarded Cost, No. of PO/JO and Charging. (3) **WP List empty state** — `_wpEmptyState()` in `project.html`: a brand-new project (no WPs) shows an **Add Work Package / Import from CSV** CTA (hidden for viewers/archived); a filtered-to-empty list shows a "no matches" hint. Used by `buildTable()` for both the table body and mobile cards.
   - **Wiring:** `index.html` + `project.html` each load `<script src="assets/js/onboarding.js?v=…">` (after ui.js/db.js) and call `Onboarding.maybeAutoOpen(profile.id, profile.role)` inside their `AppAuth.requireLogin` callback. To add to another page: include the script and call `maybeAutoOpen` (or rely on the injected **?** button for manual open). `onboarding.js` is versioned independently (`?v=20260708a`), NOT part of the 5-file shared `?v=` bump.
2. **Standalone deck** (`onboarding.html`, repo root) â€” 13-slide training deck, self-contained (Montserrat + Tabler via CDN, same as the app), keyboard/dot nav, `F` fullscreen, `P`/print CSS (one slide per page). Covers all three tiers in full (for group training). Not linked from the app.
3. **PowerPoint** (`WPM_Dashboard_Onboarding.pptx`, repo root) â€” editable pptxgenjs-generated version mirroring the deck (13 slides, Calibri, circle-motif, no icon-font dependency). Regenerate from `scratchpad/gen.js` if content changes.

**First-run checklist (`window.GettingStarted` in `onboarding.js`):** a small dismissible **"Getting started (n/4)"** card rendered into `#gs-checklist` (top of the Overview on both `index.html` and `project.html`). Four steps: **Pick a project → Add a WP → Set its status → Award it.** Progress persists per user in `localStorage['wpm_gs_<uid>']` (JSON of done-step keys); dismissed flag `wpm_gs_x_<uid>`. `GettingStarted.markStep(key)` sets a step + re-renders any visible card; `render(containerId,{projectId})`; `dismiss()`. **Steps are marked from real actions:** `pickProject` — project.html load (non-viewer, non-archived/demo); `addWP` — `saveQuickWP` (project.html) and `submitForm` new-WP (wp-form.html, which writes the same localStorage key directly via `_gsMark()` since it doesn't load onboarding.js); `setStatus` — `updateWpStatus` (Status Tracker); `awardIt` — `updateWpStatus` proc→Awarded, or a WP saved with `award_status='Awarded'`. Hidden for viewers and on the demo/archived (read-only) projects. When all 4 are done it shows a "You're all set" state until dismissed.

**Status Tracker "NEW" hint** (`project.html`): a one-time red **NEW** pill on the tracker title + a dismissible callout (`#st-new-badge`/`#st-new-callout`), shown until seen (per-user `localStorage['wpm_st_seen_<uid>']`). `_stMaybeShowNew()` (called from `renderStatusTracker`) reveals it; `dismissStNew()` (the "Got it" button) or the first actual status edit (`updateWpStatus`) retires it. Skipped for viewers and the demo.

**Role-aware landing** (`login.html` `showProjectPicker`): **viewers skip the picker and go straight to `index.html`** (Portfolio charts — they're read-only, the overview is their main value). Contributors with exactly one project still auto-open it; everyone else keeps the picker.

**Read-only DEMO sandbox project:** a project with id **`DEMO`** that everyone can open read-only to explore realistic sample data. Seeded by **`migrations/2026-07-08_seed_demo_project.sql`** (repo root — creates the DEMO project + 10 sample WPs across trades: 6 awarded incl. one over-budget "loss", 4 backlog incl. overdue/current/future; run in Supabase SQL Editor, idempotent). **RLS REQUIREMENT:** non-admin roles only see WPs for their *assigned* projects, so the demo needs the **`wp_select_demo`** RLS policy (in `supabase-schema.sql` + `migrations/2026-07-08_rls_hardening.sql`) or it appears empty for regular users/viewers — client-side `canAccessProject('DEMO')=true` cannot override server-side RLS. **Wiring:** `auth.js` `canAccessProject` returns `true` for `'DEMO'` (open to all roles); `login.html` surfaces DEMO as its own picker item for everyone (pulled out of the normal project list to avoid duplicates); `index.html` **excludes `DEMO` from the portfolio** `permitted` list so its sample data never skews consolidated KPIs (it's opened directly via `project.html?id=DEMO`); the **project.html sidebar project list excludes DEMO** (it stays reachable only via the login picker); `project.html` treats `id==='DEMO'` as **read-only for everyone** (`window.__demo`, sets `window.__archived=true` to reuse all read-only guards, shows a green "Demo Project — explore freely" banner instead of the archive one, and hides the archive button).

**Admin "Remove" user (COMPLETE delete):** the old soft "Revoke" (set `status='rejected'`) is now **"Remove"** — `admin.html` `revokeUser` calls `WPDb.deleteUser(id)` (strong confirm first). This now purges **BOTH** the `public.users` profile row **AND the `auth.users` login record** (Planning-App parity — previously only the profile was deleted, leaving an orphaned auth account that still occupied the person's email so they could never re-register). The auth schema isn't writable by the anon/authenticated client (no service-role key in a static front-end), so `deleteUser` calls the **`public.admin_delete_user(target_id uuid)`** `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC (**`migrations/2026-07-09_admin_delete_user.sql`**, run once in Supabase SQL Editor — owned by `postgres` which owns the auth schema). The function re-checks the caller is `admin`/`super_admin`, refuses self-deletion, then deletes both rows; `execute` is granted to `authenticated` only (revoked from `public`/`anon`). **The Supabase advisor flagging this as a SECURITY DEFINER function callable by `authenticated` is an ACCEPTED exception** (documented via `COMMENT ON FUNCTION` in the migration): it MUST be definer (writes `auth.users`, which the client can't), MUST be `authenticated`-executable and in the `public` schema (or PostgREST couldn't expose it for the admin UI), and it **self-authorizes** — `auth.uid()` returns the caller's id even inside a definer function, so a non-admin is rejected with `42501` before any row is touched (no escalation/enumeration). Don't switch it to `SECURITY INVOKER` (client can't write `auth.users`) or revoke EXECUTE (breaks the Remove button); dismiss the advisory. `deleteUser` **falls back to a profile-only delete** if the RPC isn't there yet (detects `PGRST202`/"schema cache" — safe to deploy before the migration runs). The user loses access immediately (no profile → `AppAuth.requireLogin` signs them out) and the freed email can register again. The admin KPI/badge label "Revoked" → **"Rejected"** (only denied *registrations* remain in `status='rejected'` now).

**Per-WP audit trail — "Last Change" log (2026-07-23):** now that the dashboard is cascaded to all procurement officers, every work package records **who last changed it and when**. Columns `updated_at` / `updated_by` (auth uid, no FK) / `updated_by_name` (name snapshot, survives user deletion) on `work_packages` — run **`migrations/2026-07-23_wp_audit_trail.sql`** once (adds the columns, backfills `updated_at` from `created_at`, and recreates `wp_view_public` to expose the three non-cost fields to viewers). **Stamped centrally in `db.js`**: `_auditStamp()` reads `window.__profile` and is spread into `submitWP` / `updateWP` / `updateWPDirect`, so ALL app write paths (WP form, quick-add, grid save, Status Tracker, CSV import) record it automatically; `unmap()` strips any stale read-back of the three fields first. The **Excel importer** (`import-monitoring.html`) stamps its inserted rows from the signed-in user (`_auditUser`). **Deploy-order-safe:** if the columns don't exist yet, every write path detects the `42703`/"column does not exist" error and **retries without the audit fields** (db.js `_isMissingAuditCol`/`_stripAudit`; the importer strips them from all remaining rows and retries the batch), so the code is safe to ship before the migration runs. **Displayed** in the WP detail slide-in panel (`openWPDetail` in both `index.html` and `project.html`) as a **"Last Change"** block (Updated By + Updated On, only shown when present). Read paths use `select('*')` so the fields flow through `mapWP` untouched.

**User-management approval (`admin.html`) — two fixes (2026-07-23):** (1) **Modal confirm buttons stayed disabled after the first success.** `confirmApprove`, `confirmEditUser` and `confirmRoleChange` set `btn.disabled=true` at the start but only re-enabled it in their `catch` — on success they closed the (reused, not recreated) modal without re-enabling, so `#btnConfirmApprove` / `#crm-save-btn` were permanently disabled and every subsequent approve/edit/role-change did nothing until a page refresh recreated the buttons (the "approve requires a refresh every time" bug). Fixed by resetting `btn.textContent` **and `btn.disabled=false`** in `openApproveModal`, `openProjectModal` and `openRoleModal` (i.e. every time a modal opens). If you add another reused modal with an async confirm handler, reset its button state on open, not just in the error path. (2) **Bulk approve didn't exist.** The bulk bar's `applyBulkActions` only wrote `role`/`projects` via `updateUser`, never `status:'approved'`, so selecting pending users and clicking Apply never approved them. Added `window.bulkApprove()` + a green **✓ Approve (N)** button in `#bulk-bar` (shown by `renderUsers` only when the selection contains pending users) that approves all selected pending users at once — keeps each user's existing projects, applies the chosen bulk role/project if set (role defaults to `user`), and (for `admin` callers) sets `assigned_admin` to self so they stay visible after reload. `applyBulkActions` now ALSO flips a still-pending user to `approved` when a bulk assign touches them. Both reload via `loadData()`.

**Actionable errors** (`wp-form.html` `save()`): `showErr(msg, fieldId)` now **scrolls to and focuses the offending field**, and each message says what to do (e.g. "This WP is marked Awarded — enter the Awarded Cost (Net), or set Award Status back to 'Not Yet Awarded'").

> `.claude/launch.json` (a `python -m http.server 8765` config) exists only to preview static pages locally â€” not a deploy dependency.

---

## Portfolio Overview + Project View — audit (2026-08-12)

Full audit of `index.html` + `project.html`, verified live against production as super_admin.

**Verified correct — no action needed:** `helpingMetrics()` reconciles to the peso with an independent recomputation over the raw rows (portfolio AND per project); the **Overview and Budget tabs agree exactly** (₱32.27B BCB / ₱20.99B awarded / +₱199.43M savings); the **Action Center is accurate** (its 403 over-budget WPs / +₱2.60B overrun matched an independent count exactly, and its 871 "data gaps" is precisely the union of 65 awarded-without-cost and 861 awarded-without-vendor); **project.html'''s three parallel filter predicates (`getFiltered`/`getFilteredSorted`/`_pwaFilteredWPs`) are byte-identical**; the **Charging Scope switcher** correctly scopes all three surfaces (BAU101: 96 Main + 23 CO = 119, KPIs re-scope with it); **viewers see zero money** (0 visible ₱ nodes across the whole single-page layout, with the Budget/Works tabs, value battery, period panel and PDF export all hidden); no new instances of the inline-`onclick`-reading-a-closure-variable trap; and 0 duplicate WP numbers, 0 missing trades, 0 missing planned award dates, 0 procurement⇄award status mismatches.

**FIXED — `normTrade` left 14 WPs in junk trade buckets.** `MEPF (Other Allied Services)` (MST347, 3 WPs) defeated BOTH paths: the exact map is keyed `other allied services` so the MEPF prefix misses it, and the keyword fallback'''s `/services$/` misses the **trailing bracket**. Such a trade is not in `TRADE_ORDER`, so `indexOf` returns -1 → sort index 999 → its own bucket dumped at the end of every trade-grouped chart, table and sort. Added an `/allied/` rule ahead of the generic services rule and relaxed it to `/services\W*$/`, in **all three copies** (`index.html`, `project.html`, `import-monitoring.html`). Verified it does not over-match (`Security Services` and `Housekeeping & Sanitation Services` still → General Requirements). **The other 11 (STM101, raw `MEPF WORKS`) are deliberately NOT mapped** — their descriptions are a mix of Electrical and Plumbing items and `normTrade` only sees the trade string, so collapsing them to one discipline would be wrong; they need correcting at source (which is exactly what the importer'''s `mepfSplit` does by reading the description).

**Savings/Loss is materially distorted by awarded WPs that have NO budget — the single most important finding.** An awarded WP with `approved_budget_bcb` = 0 contributes its cost to the numerator with nothing in the denominator, so it can only ever read as pure loss. Portfolio-wide: **41 such WPs carrying ₱204M**. On **BAU101 that is 27 WPs / ₱101.2M — 46% of its headline −₱221.47M "loss"**; the rest is genuine overrun (37 WPs, ₱145.6M). **Change Orders make this structural:** COs carry cost with no BCB by definition, so the CO scope on BAU101 reads BCB ₱0 / Awarded ₱32.26M / Savings **−₱32.26M — a 100% loss by construction**, and Combined (the default) folds that into the headline. This is a DATA/reporting-model issue, not a computation bug — the arithmetic is right. Do NOT "fix" it by excluding zero-BCB rows from the savings maths (that would hide genuinely committed spend); the useful change would be surfacing them, e.g. as a fifth Action Center signal beside Data Gaps.

**Other data-quality findings (not code defects):** **861 of 1,219 awarded WPs (71%) have no vendor recorded** — this is why the vendor directory and its analytics are thin; **106 not-yet-awarded WPs carry ₱3.76B of provisional cost** (the documented importer behaviour — correctly excluded from awarded money, but if any are in fact awarded the savings figures move a lot); 65 awarded WPs have no cost (₱84.5M BCB), which is the count-vs-money gap in #17.

**LOW — the admin fetch path ignores the DEMO exclusion.** `index.html` line ~843 uses `WPDb.getAllApprovedWPs()` for `_seesAllWPs` roles, bypassing the `filter(p => p.id !== '''DEMO''')` applied to `permitted` on line ~848 (the non-admin path at ~897 scopes correctly). So an admin loads DEMO'''s 10 sample rows into `_rawAll`/`_allWPs`/`window._wpById`. **Not currently a live defect** — every consumer filters by `_activeIds` (verified: DEMO'''s ₱344.5M BCB / ₱227.3M awarded are absent from the rendered KPIs, and the cache key is per-user) — but it means an admin and a regular user compute `BcbBaseline.resolve()` over different row sets, and anything added later that iterates `_allWPs` unscoped would silently include demo data for admins only.

### Vendor page, sidebar, accreditation requirements, history restore, vendor front door (2026-09-03)

Five changes from one review pass. `migrations/2026-09-03_vendor_owner_and_logo.sql` (**RUN — confirmed
by the user 2026-09-03, along with `_vendor_country.sql`**).

**Vendor Registrations moved in the sidebar.** It was its own `.sidebar-section` with **no
`.sidebar-section-label`**, so it rendered as an orphan item carrying section spacing, floating
between the Admin and Manager groups — and for a contributor, who never sees the Admin group at
all, as a lone unlabelled item above the footer. It now sits **beside Vendor Management** inside an
already-labelled section: same vendor surface, same contributor audience. **It keeps its own
wrapper** (`#sidebar-vendor-claims`) because its role gate differs from Vendor Management's —
`viewer_budget` may read the directory but **not** this queue — so `initVendorClaimsNav()` still
toggles it independently. All four sidebars; `admin.html`'s stale inline `display:none` on the link
is gone so the wrapper carries the toggle uniformly.

**⚠️ The vendor detail is now a PAGE, not a modal — `vendors.html?vendor=<id>`.** Nine sections deep
in a 760px modal meant a cramped scroll inside a scroll with the directory showing through.
`openVendorPage()` / `closeVendorPage()` swap `#vendorDirectoryPage` and `#vendorDetailPage` (both
`.page-content` siblings) and `pushState` the URL, so Back/Forward work and the URL is shareable;
`?vendor=` at boot opens it (captured into `_vdBootVendor`, opened off `loadAll().then` because
allVendors must be populated first). Measured 1177px of content width against the modal's 760px.
- **⚠️ THE SECTION IDS ARE UNCHANGED (`#dsec-*` / `#dtab-*`) ON PURPOSE.** Every `renderDetail*()`
  writes into `#dtab-<x>`, so all nine sections, their editors and the document locking moved with
  no change to any of them. **`openDetailModal` / `closeDetailModal` are kept as aliases** — the
  cards, the analytics tables, the claims queue, `splitVendorFromDetail` and
  `deleteVendorFromDetail` all call those names.
- The scroll-spy root moved from the modal body to the **viewport** (`root` omitted), and the
  action bar is `position:sticky;bottom:0` so **Save is never a scroll away** — the reason it was
  moved into the modal footer in the first place.
- **Company logo** in the page header. The `vendor-certs` bucket is PRIVATE, so it renders through
  a **signed URL** (`getCertFileUrl`) and falls back to initials; upload is `uploadVendorFile(id,
  'logo', file)` → `updateVendor({logo_path})`, write roles only. The `<vendorId>/` path prefix
  that `uploadVendorFile` adds is load-bearing — the bucket's Storage policies authorize on the
  first path segment.
- **Personnel now uses the portal's `.prof-*` profile cards** (photo, role, contact, primary dot +
  tag). Staff previously saw a flat one-line-per-person `.row-item` list, discarding the photo and
  primary flag the vendor had filled in; the staff add-form takes a photo too, via the same
  insert-then-attach sequence (`personnel.add` → `uploadVendorFile(id,'person_'+row.id)` →
  `personnel.update({photo_path})`).
- **⚠️ A syntax check cannot catch a missing identifier.** An over-greedy
  `function closeDetailModal\(\) \{.*?\n\}\n` regex during the conversion silently ate the alias,
  the `popstate` listener and `_vdInitials`, and all four inline blocks still "parsed". Caught by a
  **definition check** (`scratchpad/check_defs.js`: 34 helpers defined, 23 ids present exactly
  once) — run that shape of check after any structural edit here, not just `node --check`.

**Accreditation requirements now mirror the email Procurement sends**: BIR 2303, Company Profile,
Business Permits, Scanned Copy of Invoice, in that order. **⚠️ ONLY LABELS AND ORDER CHANGED —
`ACCRED_DOC_TYPES` keys are stored in `vendor_documents.doc_type` on every uploaded row, so
renaming one orphans every document already uploaded under it.** Vendors upload these in the
**Accreditation tab of `vendor-portal.html`** (a `.doc-row` per required type → `uploadVendorDoc`),
which also shows the readiness % and gates the Request Accreditation button.
- Added **Owner** and **Contact # of Owner** (`owner_name`, `owner_contact_number`) — that email
  asks for both under Required Information but the app stored neither, so the checklist could
  never match what staff were chasing. Both vendor-owned, in `accredReadiness`'s info group.
- **⚠️ The migration re-creates `vendor_self_view`** to add them plus `logo_path`: a `vendors`
  column absent from that view is invisible AND unwritable to a vendor, and the portal would render
  boxes that silently never save. Verified the new 31-column list is a **superset of every prior
  definition** — nothing dropped, staff-only `notes`/`accreditation_notes` still omitted. None of
  the three is pinned in `vendor_edit_guard`; they are the vendor's own information.

**Where staff reach the accreditation documents — a new Accreditation section on the vendor page.**
They were previously reachable from the employee side ONLY as a one-line `Documents on file: BIR
2303 · …` strip inside the request-review panel at the top of Overview, and **that panel is
suppressed entirely for a vendor with no request, no documents and no claimed login** — so there was
no register of what is on file, no view of WHICH of the four are missing, and no way for staff to
add one. The page's second section (`#dsec-accred`, `renderDetailAccred`) now shows the readiness
bar plus one row per required document: on-file/missing state, filed date, a **View** link (signed
URL via `viewVendorDoc`), the lock marker, and — for write roles — **Upload / Replace**
(`uploadDetailDoc` → `VendorDb.uploadVendorDoc`).
- **Staff upload is deliberate, not a convenience.** No vendor has ever logged in, so in practice
  these documents arrive by email to an officer, who needs somewhere to file them. The vendor's own
  route (portal → Accreditation tab) is unchanged and the note on the section says so.
- The checklist is `window.accredReadiness` — the SAME function the portal uses — so the two sides
  can never disagree about whether a vendor is ready. `_detailDocs` is still shared with
  `renderDetailRequest`, and a successful upload re-renders both.
- Documents filed under a type outside the four required ones are listed separately rather than
  dropped.

**Change history restores from an ENTRY, not a calendar.** A datetime picker meant knowing when
something happened before you could undo it, and nothing said which instants mattered. Each entry
now offers **"Restore everything to this version"** — the MS version-history model, where the list
IS the control. **Passing that entry's own `changed_at` is the correct reading**: `restore_vendor_to`
takes the newest entry at or before the instant, which is that entry, so the version you are
looking at is the version you get back. Preview still runs first, renders under that entry, and only
one stays open. The per-row **"Undo this change"** (one row, from its `old_data`) is kept beside it —
different question, and both are now labelled to say which. `_histWhenIso`,
`previewVendorRollback`, `applyVendorRollback` and `#histWhen` are gone; the patch asserts no
reference survives. Point-in-time restore stays **admin-only** (`_histCanRollback`), matching
`restore_vendor_to`'s own allow-list.

**⚠️ Vendors and Megawide staff now have SEPARATE FRONT DOORS.** Signing out of the vendor portal
dropped vendors on the employee login. **`auth.js` reads `window.__loginPage`** for all three
redirects it owns — no session, an unapproved profile, and `AppAuth.logout()` — defaulting to
`login.html`, so every other page is unchanged. **`vendor-portal.html` sets it to
`vendor-login.html` BEFORE loading auth.js** (order matters).
- **`vendor-login.html`** is new: public, self-contained (own Supabase client + own anti-flash
  theme on `html.dark-mode`, like `login.html`/`forgot-password.html` — it does not load `ui.js`).
  `route()` sends a vendor to the portal, a **still-pending claimant back to
  `vendor-register.html`** (they have **no `users` row** by design, so they must never land in the
  staff approval queue), and a **Megawide account to its own entrance** rather than into a portal
  that would only tell it it is on the wrong account. A quiet staff link sits at the bottom for a
  colleague who lands there by mistake.
- `vendor-register.html`'s two sign-in links now point at `vendor-login.html`.
- **⚠️ Take the anon key from an existing public page, never from memory** — the first draft of
  `vendor-login.html` carried a wrong one; it is now lifted from `vendor-register.html`'s
  `createClient` call.
- `login.html` still routes a `role==='vendor'` sign-in to the portal, deliberately: the doors are
  separated for signposting, not to lock a vendor out who uses the wrong one.

**Shared `?v=` bumped `20260902c → 20260903b`** (`db.js` for the accreditation checklist, then
`auth.js` for `__loginPage`).

**STILL OPEN from this review — the registration identity check.** The decision is **TIN only**:
Vendor Code has to come out of `vendor-register.html` because **it is internal SAP tagging a vendor
never sees** — confirmed against a real PO (`PO 376000333`, Switch Gear Phils.), which carries the
PO number, project code, supplier name and address but **no vendor code and no supplier TIN**. Not
yet built: dropping the Vendor Code field and the `?code=` prefill, replacing
`submit_vendor_claim`'s four tiers (code+TIN, code, TIN, name) with **TIN unique → high, exact name
unique → medium**, stopping `approve_vendor_claim`/`create_vendor_from_claim` writing `vendor_code`
from the claim, and retiring the per-vendor QR's `?code=` in favour of the generic link. Keep the
`vendors.vendor_code` column and its staff-side UI — it is still the best dedup key internally.

### Vendor self-registration identifies by TIN, not Vendor Code (2026-09-03)

`migrations/2026-09-03_vendor_claim_tin_only.sql` (**run me**).

**⚠️ VENDOR CODE IS INTERNAL SAP TAGGING AND A VENDOR NEVER SEES IT.** Verified against a real
purchase order (`PO 376000333`, Switch Gear Phils.): it carries the PO number, the project code,
the supplier name and address — and **no vendor code, and no supplier TIN**. The old registration
form asked for it anyway (and its help text claimed it was "printed on every Purchase Order",
which is simply false), so the matcher's strongest tier was dead weight and the field was pure
confusion on a public page.

- **Four tiers become two**: TIN unique → `high`, exact normalized company name unique → `medium`.
  TIN is now the strongest evidence a vendor can actually supply — it is on their own BIR 2303, a
  document they must upload for accreditation anyway, it is nationally unique, and the masterlist
  already gave us one for much of the directory. It is therefore rated `high`, not the `medium` it
  carried while the code tiers outranked it.
- **⚠️ The 5-argument `submit_vendor_claim` is DROPPED, not just replaced.** PostgREST resolves
  overloads by argument NAME, so leaving both signatures in place would make
  `rpc('submit_vendor_claim')` ambiguous and every registration would start failing. The migration
  drops it explicitly and the verification asserts `exactly_one_overload`.
- **⚠️ ANTI-ENUMERATION IS UNCHANGED AND LOAD-BEARING.** The RPC still returns ONLY a claim id, and
  the same answer whether or not it matched. Returning the match — even as a boolean — would make
  it an oracle for brute-forcing TINs to discover which companies Megawide works with. Never
  surface the match on the registration page.
- **⚠️ NO FUZZY MATCHING**, same refuse-to-guess rule as the vendor analytics: the directory holds
  real duplicates and garbled multi-company rows, so a fuzzy hit would hand one company's profile
  to another.
- **`vendors.vendor_code` and `vendor_claims.claimed_vendor_code` are both KEPT.** The column is
  still the best internal dedup key (of 1,461 codes only 97 mapped to more than one name string,
  nearly all case/spacing variants), it is what the PO-history backfill resolved against, and staff
  still set and search it. What changed is only that a VENDOR is never asked for it — and
  `create_vendor_from_claim` no longer stamps a claimed code onto a new vendor.
- **ONE registration URL for everyone.** `_regLinkFor()` no longer emits `?code=`, the per-vendor
  and bulk QRs both carry the generic link, and `vendor-register.html` reads `?code=` as a no-op so
  an already-printed QR cannot throw. The QR copy no longer describes a prefill.

## Known Issues / Gotchas

1. **Generated columns**: `total_awarded`, `awarding_lead_time`, `variance` â€” never INSERT into them. Use `awarded_cost` and `lead_time`. `unmap()` strips all three automatically.
2. **Sticky tabs + overflow**: `overflow:hidden/clip` on any ancestor of `.view-tabs` breaks `position:sticky` in Safari. See CSS section.
3. **READ-ONLY badge**: never set `display:inline-flex` as inline style on `.topbar-badge-readonly` â€” mobile media query can't override it.
3c. **Mobile filter bars — 2-column grid instead of one-control-per-row stack (2026-08-03).** The global mobile rule `.filter-bar { flex-direction: column }` stacked every filter control full-width on its own row — on the Portfolio WP List (`index.html`, Trade/Works/Vendor/Status/Search/Clear) and the Project WP List (`project.html`, Status/Trade/Works/Vendor/Search) that meant 5-6 rows of controls before any WP content appeared. `.filter-bar` now goes `display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr` on mobile (both pages share this one class, so the fix applies to both automatically), pairing controls two-per-row; the search box and any standalone button (e.g. `index.html`'s **Clear**) span the full width via `.filter-bar > div[style*="position:relative"]` (matches the search wrapper's inline style) and `.filter-bar > button`. Needed `!important` on `.filter-bar > * { width/min-width/max-width/flex }` because the controls carry inline sizing meant for a wide desktop row (`min-width:150px;max-width:190px` etc.) that would otherwise keep them narrower than their grid cell; `.filter-bar .msf-btn` (the Works/Vendor custom-dropdown button, one level deeper than `.filter-bar`'s direct children) needed its own override since `.filter-bar > *` only reaches direct children. **The Portfolio Backlog table's filter row** (`index.html`, Trade/Sort/Min₱M/Max₱M/Search next to the "Backlog — Not Awarded WPs" title) isn't built from the shared `.filter-bar` component — it's a bespoke `display:flex` row — so it got its own opt-in class **`.filter-row-2col`** (title spans full width via `.panel-title`, search spans full width via a new `.filter-row-2col-full` marker class, Trade/Sort/Min/Max pair up automatically). `.filter-row-2col`'s `display:grid` needed `!important` because the div carries an inline `style="display:flex"` (inline styles always beat a class selector unless `!important`) — a trap worth remembering for any other bespoke (non-`.filter-bar`) filter row this pattern gets applied to later. Verified geometrically (not just visually) via a throwaway harness loading the real `dashboard.css`, since the sandboxed preview pane doesn't reliably honor a forced mobile viewport width for computed-style checks.
3b. **`.topbar-left` must not have `overflow:hidden` on mobile (fixed 2026-08-03)** â€” the project-switcher popover (`#hdr-switcher` in `project.html`, `position:absolute` inside `.topbar-left`'s positioned wrapper) was being clipped by `.topbar-left{overflow:hidden}` in the `≤767px` media query in `dashboard.css`, so tapping the title on mobile showed only a sliver of the search box with the project list cut off — same bug class as the already-documented `.topbar` overflow:visible rule, just one level deeper. Removed `overflow:hidden` from `.topbar-left`; `.page-title` already has its own `overflow:hidden;text-overflow:ellipsis` so title truncation is unaffected.
4. **N+1 query**: never use `Promise.all(projects.map(p => WPDb.getApprovedWPs(p.id)))` â€” use `getAllApprovedWPs()` or `getApprovedWPsForProjects(ids)`.
5. **Role caching**: `window.__wpmRole` set once at login. Role/project changes require the user to log out and back in.
6. **Chart.js leaks**: always `chartInstance.destroy()` before re-rendering.
7. **Trade name consistency â€” 10 canonical trades only**: `normTrade(t)` in `index.html` and `project.html` (identical) collapses EVERY trade string into one of exactly 10: General Requirements, Site Works, Structural Works, Architectural Works, Mechanical Works, **Electrical and Auxiliary Works**, Plumbing Works, Fire Protection Works, Allied Services, Site Development Works. **Electrical Works and Auxiliary Works are combined into a single bucket named "Electrical and Auxiliary Works"** (per user request â€” the combined label keeps Auxiliary visible in the trade name): `electrical works` â†’ Electrical and Auxiliary Works; `auxiliary works` â†’ Electrical and Auxiliary Works; the combined `electrical and auxiliary works` / `electrical & auxiliary works` â†’ Electrical and Auxiliary Works. Other variants: OPW101 granular arch sub-trades â†’ Architectural (incl. **`Landscapes and Amenities` / `Laminates and Plastic Finishes`** — a `/landscap|amenit/` keyword rule sits **before** the architectural rule so Landscapes doesn't remain its own non-canonical trade); **`Structural Materials and Consumables` / `Structural Steel` â†’ Structural** (via `/structural/`); Structural Labor And Services â†’ Structural; Housekeeping & Sanitation / Drawing / Security Services â†’ General Requirements; Other Allied Services â†’ Allied Services. **NON-PO/JO items are NOT trades:** OPW101's monitoring sheet has a `NON-PO/JO` section (design/consultancy, bonds, permits, insurances, project details) below the last WP — those rows have **no WP No.** so they were never imported as WPs, and the Excel importer now **`break`s the parse loop at the `NON-PO/JO` header** (`/non[\s\-]?(po|jo)/i`) so nothing beneath it can ever be imported even if a future sheet stray-numbers a row there. Keyword-regex fallback: both `/electr/` AND `/auxiliary/` â†’ Electrical and Auxiliary Works. Applied at data load time. `IDX_TRADE_ORDER`/`TRADE_ORDER` list the same 10 (drives chart colour order; the combined label sits in the Electrical slot, "Auxiliary Works" removed). Keep the explicit map + keyword fallback in sync across both files. **`project.html`'s INITIAL data-load path skipped `normTrade` (fixed 2026-07-30):** `_rawWPs` was built with `trade: w.trade` (raw) on first load — only `__wpmReloadFn` (used after quick-add saves) applied `normTrade`. Raw variants like `General Requirement` (singular) or `Other Allied Services` never matched `TRADE_ORDER`'s canonical strings, so `TRADE_ORDER.indexOf` returned -1 → those groups fell back to sort index 999 and landed at the end of the WP List (alphabetically among themselves) instead of their intended TRADE_ORDER position. Both `_rawWPs` assignments now normalize identically.
8. **Supabase cold start — NO LONGER APPLIES (Pro since 2026-08-10).** Free-tier projects pause after 7 days → 5–30s delay (and a long-paused project can be deleted); **Pro projects do not pause.** Don’t cite cold start as the explanation for a slow load any more — look for a real cause. The **GitHub Action keep-alive** (`.github/workflows/keep-alive.yml`, every 3 days — needs repo secrets `SUPABASE_URL` + `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`) plus an optional external UptimeRobot ping.
9. **Dead-code cleanup (2026-07)**: the stale **root-level duplicate** scripts/styles (`auth.js`, `db.js`, `ui.js`, `charts.js`, `dashboard.css`, `dashboard-mobile-fixes.css`, `auth-guard.js`, `firebase-auth-shim.js`, `supabase-config.js`) and the unused **`assets/js/firebase-config.js`** (stale Firebase backend) were **deleted** — no page referenced them. The **duplicate `saveProject` in db.js** was also removed (one definition remains). Canonical assets live in `assets/js/` + `assets/css/`.
10. **dp_percent**: stored as decimal (0.20 = 20%). Form input accepts percentage (user types "20"), divided by 100 before storing; edit mode multiplies by 100 to display.
11. **Sticky columns require `table-layout:fixed` + colgroup**: Both `index.html` (`renderWPMonTable`) and `project.html` (`buildTable`) use `<table style="table-layout:fixed">` + a `<colgroup>` rebuilt on every render with explicit pixel widths matching the column defs. Without this, the browser auto-sizes columns narrower than the hardcoded sticky `left` offsets â€” causing sticky cells to physically overlap adjacent columns. Always keep colgroup in sync: `cg.innerHTML = cols.map(c => \`<col style="width:${c.w}px;min-width:${c.w}px">\`).join('')`.
12. **Sticky column chain must be contiguous**: All sticky columns must be consecutive from the left with no non-sticky column in between. A non-sticky column between two sticky ones causes the right sticky column to get a wrong `left` offset. In `index.html` overview: project(0)â†’cost_code(90)â†’wp_no(180); description NOT sticky. In `project.html` overview: cost_code(0)â†’wp_no(90)â†’works(180); description NOT sticky. In non-overview tabs (both pages): description is made sticky immediately after wp_no. In the **All tab** only `wp_no` and `description` are frozen â€” description is placed right after wp_no in the column order (before works) so the chain is unbroken.
13. **Period chart mode/button mismatch**: `_idxSchPeriodMode` (Schedule), `_idxDashPeriodMode` (Dashboard), `_idxBudPeriodMode` (Budget), `_idxBlPeriodMode` (Backlog) in `index.html` must match the button that is styled active in HTML. Schedule also has `_idxSchDataMode` (`'count'` default = # WPs; `'budget'` = BCB/Awarded). `_renderIdxSchPeriodChart()` branches on both. Mismatch = chart renders wrong on first load.
    - **4-mode data toggle collapsed into two 2-way toggles (2026-07-31):** the Overview/Dashboard period chart (`project.html` + `index.html`) and the Backlog period chart (`project.html`) used to show 4 flat buttons — `# WPs / Budget / Budget % / # WPs %` — for what is really two independent choices: **metric** (WP count vs Budget ₱) and **view** (raw # vs % of total). Per user request they're now **`WP | Budget` then `# | %`**, two segmented-button pairs separated by the same `|` divider used for Monthly/Quarterly. The underlying combined mode string (`'count'|'budget'|'countpct'|'budgetpct'`) is unchanged — `_dashDataMode`/`_blDataMode`/`_idxDashDataMode` and every render function that branches on them (`renderDashPeriod`, `_blRenderPeriod`, `_idxDashRenderPeriod`) were **not touched**. Only how that string gets SET changed: `_dashMetric`/`_dashView` (project Overview), `_blMetric`/`_blView` (project Backlog), `_idxDashMetric`/`_idxDashView` (portfolio Overview) are the two toggle states, combined by a small `_xComboMode()` helper (`wp+num→count`, `wp+pct→countpct`, `budget+num→budget`, `budget+pct→budgetpct`) and applied via the existing `setXDataMode(mode)` setter. **Scoped to exactly the 3 panels that had all 4 modes** — the Portfolio Budget tab (`bud-btn-*`, Budget/# WPs only, no % modes) and Schedule tab (`sch-btn-*`, # WPs/Budget/WP % — 3 buttons, no Budget %) and the WP-List Filtered Analysis (`wpmon-btn-*`/`pwa-btn-*`, Budget/# WPs only) were left as-is; they never had the full 4-mode pattern so there was nothing to collapse. **When adding a 4th mode to one of those 3-or-fewer-button panels, apply the same WP|Budget + #|% split rather than adding a 5th flat button.**
    - **The `#|%` pair is now a SINGLE toggle button, not two (2026-07-31, same day, per user request):** `dash-btn-view`/`bl-dm-view`/`idx-dash-btn-view` replace the `-num`/`-pct` button pairs — one button whose `onclick` flips the current `_xView` state (`onclick="setDashView(_dashView==='num'?'pct':'num')"`) and whose **label IS the current state** (`#` or `%`, defaults to `#` on load matching `_dashView='num'`). `setXView(v)` no longer toggles two buttons' `active` class — it just sets `btn.textContent`. Kept permanently `class="seg-btn active"` (red) since it's a switch control, not a pick-one-of-two choice like the `WP|Budget` pair beside it.
    - **Backlog period charts pass `{hideAwarded:true}`**: the Backlog tab (both pages) feeds `budgetAwardedByPeriod`/`Monthly` only not-awarded WPs, whose Awarded/Cumulative-Awarded series are â‚±0 by definition (red line stuck flat at 0M). All four backlog call sites pass `{hideAwarded:true}` so those two series are omitted â€” only Budget bars + Cumulative Budget line render. Don't remove the opts arg. The Dashboard tab instances get ALL WPs and keep all four series.
    - **Backlog "Work Package by Period" chart (project.html) has a 4-way data-mode toggle** (`#bl-dm-count`/`#bl-dm-budget`/`#bl-dm-countpct`/`#bl-dm-budgetpct` → `setBlDataMode`, state `_blDataMode`), mirroring the Overview period chart's `# WPs / Budget / WP % / Budget %` toggle, alongside the existing Quarterly/Monthly (`_blPeriodMode`). **The JS (`_blRenderPeriod`, `setBlDataMode`) already existed fully wired** (all 4 modes) but the HTML toggle buttons were missing (2026-07-30 fix) — only Quarterly/Monthly was reachable until then.
    - **`# WPs`/`WP %` backlog modes ALSO needed `hideAwarded` (fixed 2026-07-31):** the `{hideAwarded:true}` treatment above only ever reached the two Budget-mode calls — the default `# WPs` mode (and `WP %`) called `wpCountByPeriodMonthly`/`wpCountByPeriod` with no opts at all, so the Backlog tab's default chart always drew a **"Cumulative Actual" line pinned flat at 0** (backlog = not-yet-awarded WPs only, so the actual/awarded count can never be anything but 0) plus a dashed Forecast line. Per user feedback the flat line "doesn't bring value" — `wpCountByPeriodMonthly`/`wpCountByPeriod` (and the `_periodCountPct` helper they call for `%` mode) now accept the same `opts.hideAwarded` flag as the budget charts and drop the **Actual WPs bar + Cumulative Actual line + Forecast line** entirely when set (mirrors `budgetAwardedByPeriod`'s existing `hideAwd` spread pattern). `_blRenderPeriod`'s `count`/`countpct` branches now pass `{hideAwarded:true}` alongside the existing `budget`/`budgetpct` branches — all 4 backlog modes are consistent. The Dashboard/Schedule tab call sites (full WP set, not backlog-filtered) are untouched and keep all series.
    - **`Budget %` backlog mode had the SAME gap `_periodSharePct` never received `hideAwarded` (fixed 2026-07-31):** `budgetAwardedByPeriodMonthly`/`budgetAwardedByPeriod`'s `sharePct` branch called `_periodSharePct(...)` without forwarding `opts.hideAwarded`, so the Backlog tab's Budget % chart kept drawing the always-0% **Awarded %**, **Cumulative Awarded %** and **Forecast %** series even though its two count-mode siblings had already been fixed. `_periodSharePct` now takes a `hideAwd` 8th arg and drops those three the same way `_periodCountPct` does; both call sites forward `!!(opts&&opts.hideAwarded)`.
    - **Backlog Budget % is now weighted against the WHOLE project's budget, not the backlog's own total (2026-07-31):** `_periodSharePct` previously computed `totB` by summing the `budgetData` it was given — for the Backlog chart that's ONLY the not-awarded subset, so the cumulative line always climbed to 100% regardless of whether the backlog was 5% or 60% of the project (self-normalized, not informative). `_periodSharePct` now takes a 9th arg `totalOverride` (₱M) that, when present, replaces that self-sum as the % denominator; `budgetAwardedByPeriodMonthly`/`budgetAwardedByPeriod` forward it from `opts.totalBudget`. `project.html`'s `_blRenderPeriod` (`budgetpct` branch) computes `totalBudget = Σ allWPs.approved_budget_bcb / 1e6` (the full BCB-baseline-mapped project total, all WPs regardless of award status) and passes it in, so the backlog's Budget % bars/cumulative line now read as **share of total project budget** — a small backlog reads as a small share, a large one reads as a large share. The Dashboard/Overview tab's own `sharePct` calls are untouched (no `totalBudget` passed — they already include ALL WPs, so self-sum already equals the project total). **Only `project.html`'s Backlog tab has a Budget % toggle** — `index.html`'s Backlog tab has no % mode, so no change was needed there.
    - **Budget tab + Schedule tab period charts converted to the same split-toggle pattern (2026-08-03), per user request.** These two were the last two flat-button period charts left after the earlier collapse pass (which explicitly skipped them — "never had the full 4-mode pattern so there was nothing to collapse"). Both now have the metric pair **WP | Budget** plus a single **# | %** view toggle, same markup/JS shape as the Overview chart:
      - **Budget tab** (`bud-btn-monthly`/`bud-btn-quarterly` unchanged; `bud-btn-count` renamed to **`bud-btn-wp`**, now calls `setIdxBudMetric('wp')` instead of `setIdxBudDataMode('count')`; `bud-btn-budget` now calls `setIdxBudMetric('budget')`; new `bud-btn-view` toggles `#`/`%`). Previously this chart had ONLY Budget/#WPs — no % mode existed at all. New state `_idxBudMetric`/`_idxBudView` + `_idxBudComboMode()` (mirrors `_idxDashComboMode()`) drive the existing `_idxBudDataMode` variable, which gained two new branches in `_renderIdxBudPeriodChart()` — `countpct` (`Charts.wpCountByPeriodMonthly/Period(...,{countPct:true})`) and `budgetpct` (`Charts.budgetAwardedByPeriodMonthly/Period(...,{sharePct:true})`) — both calling helpers that already existed for the Overview chart, so no `charts.js` changes were needed. Default unchanged (Budget metric, `#` view → combo `'budget'`). No `body.budget-hidden` guard needed on the new buttons — the whole Budget tab is already hidden for budget-hidden viewers (`#tab-budget`/`#tab-budget-btn`).
      - **Schedule tab** (`sch-btn-monthly`/`sch-btn-quarterly` unchanged; the old 3-flat-button row — `sch-btn-count` ("# WPs"), `sch-btn-budget`, `sch-btn-wppct` ("WP %") — replaced with `sch-btn-wp`/`sch-btn-budget` (metric pair) + `sch-btn-view` (`#`/`%`). New state `_idxSchMetric`/`_idxSchView` + `_idxSchComboMode()` map to the SAME underlying `_idxSchDataMode` values as before (`'count'|'wppct'|'budget'|'budgetpct'` — kept the pre-existing `'wppct'` name rather than renaming to `'countpct'`, since it's purely internal to this chart's own switch statement). `_renderIdxSchPeriodChart()` gained one new `budgetpct` branch (Budget's own %-of-total mode never existed here before); `wppct`/`count`/`budget` branches are unchanged. `body.budget-hidden #sch-btn-budget{display:none!important}` still applies (id unchanged).
      - Both `setIdx{Bud,Sch}Metric`/`setIdx{Bud,Sch}View` follow the exact `_segBtn`-toggle + combo-mode-recompute shape as `setIdxDashMetric`/`setIdxDashView` — if a 4th such chart needs this treatment, copy this pattern rather than re-deriving it.
