      - **The #/% button was silently dead on ALL THREE of these `index.html` charts (Overview included — pre-existing, not something this pass introduced) until fixed the same day.** Its `onclick` read a bare closure variable directly — `onclick="setIdxDashView(_idxDashView==='num'?'pct':'num')"` — but `_idxDashView`/`_idxBudView`/`_idxSchView` are declared with `let` **inside the `AppAuth.requireLogin(async (user,profile)=>{...})` callback**, not at the top level of the `<script>` block. Inline `onclick` attributes always execute in GLOBAL scope regardless of where the element itself was created, so a bare reference to a closure-local variable throws a silent `ReferenceError` (inline-handler errors don't surface anywhere visible) and the click does nothing — button stays on `#`, chart never re-renders. **Fixed by adding a `toggleIdx{Dash,Bud,Sch}View()` wrapper function** defined (and exported via `window.`) in the SAME closure as the `let` declaration, so it can read the variable correctly; the `onclick` attribute now just calls it with no arguments (`onclick="toggleIdxDashView()"`) instead of embedding the ternary read inline. **`project.html`'s equivalent buttons (`dash-btn-view`, `bl-dm-view`) do NOT have this bug** — `_dashView`/`_blView` there are declared truly top-level (before that page's own `AppAuth.requireLogin` call), so the bare-variable-read pattern works fine there; only `index.html` needed the wrapper. **Rule going forward: never read a variable declared inside `AppAuth.requireLogin`'s callback directly inside an inline `onclick` attribute on `index.html` — always go through a `window.`-exposed function defined in that same closure**, even for something as small as a ternary toggle read.
    - **Backlog `# WPs %` (WP %) gets the SAME weighting fix (2026-07-31):** `_periodCountPct`'s `total` denominator was always `wps.length` — for the Backlog chart that's just the not-awarded subset, so Cumulative Planned % always climbed to 100% of the backlog itself regardless of how big the backlog is relative to the project. `wpCountByPeriodMonthly`/`wpCountByPeriod` now forward `opts.totalCount` as that denominator when present (`(opts&&opts.totalCount)||wps.length`); `project.html`'s `_blRenderPeriod` (`countpct` branch) passes `totalCount = allWPs.length` (all WPs regardless of award status), so the backlog's WP % bars/cumulative line now read as **share of the project's total WP count**, mirroring the Budget % fix above. Same scope note: only `project.html`'s Backlog tab has this toggle.
14. **Staging credential swap**: `git push origin main` (PMODepartment) and `git push dev staging:main` (fmlozano-pmo) use different GitHub accounts. After pushing to one, Windows Credential Manager caches that account and the next push to the other fails with 403. Fix: run `git credential reject` (protocol=https, host=github.com) before each cross-account push, then re-authenticate when prompted.
15. **Staging schema setup**: New Supabase staging project requires (1) run `supabase-schema.sql` in SQL Editor, (2) run all `ALTER TABLE` migrations from CLAUDE.md, (3) run GRANT statements: `GRANT SELECT,INSERT,UPDATE ON public.users TO authenticated; GRANT INSERT ON public.users TO anon; GRANT ALL ON public.users TO service_role;` (repeat for projects, work_packages, claims). Without GRANTs, all REST API calls return 403 even with valid JWT.
16. **WP List `buildTable`/`renderWPMonTable` rendering pattern**:
17. **⚠️ SUPERSEDED IN PART BY #43 (2026-08-25):** an Awarded WP now counts as money-awarded whatever its cost, including ₱0 — the `total_awarded > 0` requirement described below is gone. Read #43 first. **Awarded COUNT and awarded MONEY use different bases (changed 2026-07-23).** `helpingMetrics()` counts **`award_status==='Awarded'`** (every awarded WP, cost or no cost) but sums **`awardedCost` / `budgetAwarded` only over awarded WPs with `total_awarded>0`**. Reason: a WP that has genuinely been awarded but whose cost isn't encoded yet is still awarded, and excluding it made the Overview disagree with the WP List — **CCM302 read 39 against a list of 40**, **OPW101 64 against 62**. The money stays strict because counting a costless WP's budget in `budgetAwarded` would book its whole BCB as phantom savings. Provisional costs on Not-Yet-Awarded rows are still excluded — that is decided by `award_status`, which the Excel importer now takes from the sheet's own Remarks flag, not from the presence of a cost. Consequence: `awarded count ≥ the number of WPs behind the ₱ figures`; the gap is exactly the awarded-but-no-cost rows, which the review grid highlights as required-but-empty (Awarded Cost is `reqIfAwarded`).
17. **`actualAward` / awarded count come from `helpingMetrics(wps)` in `db.js` — FULLY ROW-DERIVED** (no Helping Sheet; `HELPING_FIGURES = {}`). **Stricter awarded definition:** a WP is awarded only when **`award_status==='Awarded'` AND `total_awarded>0`** ("if it's awarded it must have an awarded cost"). Awarded count, Awarded Cost (= "Actual Award") AND Budget-for-Awarded all use that SAME set so they reconcile with each other, with the chart/forecast, and with the source Helping Sheets. `totalBudget = Σ approved_budget_bcb` (all rows). Self-maintaining for every project incl. new ones. **All six projects (AVR/GRP/OPW/SLN/SLT/UTM) were reconciled against their WP-Monitoring files** and this basis reproduces their Helping Sheets — and CORRECTS AVR101 (its Helping Sheet under-counted ₱8.9M because its date-gated `SUMIFS` over a hardcoded row range silently dropped awarded WPs with a blank actual-award date / below the last row → AVR truly runs ~₱10M OVER budget). It also rejects the opposite failure mode (GRP/SLN/SLT/OPW had **provisional costs on "Not yet Awarded" WPs** that a naive `total_awarded>0` basis over-counted — the `award_status` filter excludes them). `HELPING_FIGURES` + the override branch are retained only as an escape hatch (currently empty); prefer fixing rows. The **chart/forecast budget series** (`budgetAwardedByPeriod`/`Monthly` `isAwd` + the `_wpForecastLine` `awardedFn`) use the SAME `award_status==='Awarded' && total_awarded>0` predicate so chart ↔ KPI agree. Use `project.html?id=<PID>&diag=1` for a per-project awarded reconciliation. Consumers: `buildMetrics()`/`renderOvBattery()`/`renderStatusTable()` (project.html); `renderAll()`/`renderIdxOvBattery()`/`computeStats()` (index.html). `Cost to Complete = totalBudget − budgetAwarded`; `Variance = totalBudget − (awardedCost + CTC)`. Due-Not-Awarded / Not-Due splits remain row+date derived. **Data-quality note:** awarded WPs with a blank cost, or costs on not-yet-awarded WPs, are excluded by design — fix them at source (the diag panel lists them).
18. **`award_status` is DERIVED from `procurement_status`**: `award_status` (`Not Yet Awarded | Awarded` — **"Partially Awarded" removed**, no partial state) follows `procurement_status` (`Not Started | Sourcing | Solicitation | Evaluation & Negotiation | Awarded`): **proc `Awarded` → award `Awarded`; any earlier stage → `Not Yet Awarded`.** One-way (proc drives award). Enforced in: **WP form** `onProcStatusChange()` (award select disabled/auto); **Status Tracker** (`updateWpStatus` — the Award column is a **read-only badge** `_stAwardBadge`, and changing Procurement Status sets award, opening the `#st-award-modal` for cost+vendor when →Awarded); **CSV import** sets award from the WP-Monitoring **Remarks (Col P)** text, then **reconciles proc⇄award in `WPCsv.toWPData` (P2, 2026-07)** — `proc==='Awarded' ⇒ award='Awarded'`, else `award==='Awarded' ⇒ proc='Awarded'` — so imported rows can't land with the two disagreeing (previously a "Not yet Awarded" Remark on a proc-Awarded row left the KPIs and the form inconsistent until a re-save). Dashboard KPIs (`buildMetrics`, `computeStats`, `helpingMetrics`) still read `award_status` (now kept consistent with proc). Because awarded requires an Awarded Cost + Vendor (safeguard), setting proc→Awarded triggers that requirement.
19. **Server-side authorization = Supabase RLS (`supabase-schema.sql`), NOT the client**: RLS is enabled on `projects`/`users`/`work_packages`/`claims` with role+status+project-scope policies via the `internal.get_my_role()/get_my_status()/get_my_projects()` SECURITY DEFINER helpers. Client checks (`canAccessProject`, `body.viewer-mode` CSS) are UX only — the DB enforces WP/claims read/write scope. **WP DELETE scope (`migrations/2026-07-14_contributor_wp_delete.sql`, 2026-07):** widened from super_admin-only to the same scope as insert/update — super_admin/admin any project; specialist/manager/user their `assigned` projects; viewer denied (so contributors can run a full Replace import on their projects). **Hardened 2026-07 (`migrations/2026-07-08_rls_hardening.sql`, run once):** `users_insert` now pins `role='user' AND status='pending'` and `users_update`'s `WITH CHECK` forbids self-changing `role`/`status`/`projects` — previously a user could `PATCH` their own row (or craft the registration insert) to become `super_admin`/`approved` (privilege escalation). Also added **`wp_select_demo`** (DEMO readable by all approved users). **Viewer cost is now enforced server-side (S4, `migrations/2026-07-09_specialist_scope_and_viewer_view.sql`):** `wp_select` now excludes viewers from the base `work_packages` table entirely; viewers instead read the **`wp_view_public`** security-definer view, which re-implements the row scope (incl. DEMO) and **OMITS every money column** (`approved_budget_bcb`, `awarded_cost`, `additionals`, `total_awarded`, `variance`, `dp_percent`, `dp_amount`, `retention_percent`, `retention_amount`). The client picks the relation by role via **`db.js _wpRel()`** (non-viewers → `work_packages`; viewers → `wp_view_public`, with a memoized probe that falls back to the table if the view doesn't exist yet, so it's safe to deploy before the migration runs). **MAINTENANCE:** when you ADD a `work_packages` column, add it to `wp_view_public` too — **non-cost columns only** (never add a money column, or viewers would see it). Export PDF is also viewer-guarded (`#btn-export` hidden + `if(window.__isViewer)return`). **Supabase "security_definer_view" advisory on `wp_view_public` is an ACCEPTED exception, not a defect** — the view MUST be definer-style (owned by a privileged role, not `security_invoker`) precisely because viewers are denied SELECT on `work_packages`: it bypasses the base-table RLS but re-derives the caller's OWN scope from their JWT via `internal.get_my_*()` in its WHERE clause and omits every cost column, so it leaks nothing. `security_invoker = on` is impossible here — all logged-in users share the single `authenticated` Postgres role (roles differ only via JWT claims), and RLS cannot hide COLUMNS, so per-viewer column hiding requires a separate definer relation. `migrations/2026-07-23_wp_audit_trail.sql` recreates the view **`with (security_barrier = true)`** (stops the planner pushing a leaky outer predicate ahead of the scope conditions) and adds a `COMMENT` documenting the exception. Don't "fix" the advisory by flipping to `security_invoker` — that would re-expose cost data. Dismiss it in the Supabase advisor UI.

**Specialist edit-scope is now enforced (P1, same migration):** specialists VIEW all projects (`canAccessProject`/`getPermittedProjects` unchanged) but EDIT only assigned ones. New **`AppAuth.canEditProject(profile, pid)`** (`super_admin`/`admin` → any; others → `profile.projects`; DEMO → false) gates `wp-form.html` edit-load + new-WP save; `project.html` puts a specialist on an **unassigned** project into a read-only **"View-only"** state (`window.__canEdit`, reuses the `__archived` guards + a blue view-only banner). Server-side, the `wp_insert`/`wp_update`/`claims_insert`/`claims_update` RLS policies dropped `specialist` from the all-projects branch (SELECT still allows specialist all).
20. **Never commit real procurement data**: `data/*.json` real WP dumps were removed (they were publicly served via GitHub Pages, bypassing auth/RLS) and `data/` is git-ignored. **Git history was scrubbed 2026-07** (`git filter-repo --path data/ --invert-paths`, force-pushed to `origin`) — the data is gone from all commits and the live Pages/raw `main` URLs 404. Residual: old commit SHAs may stay cached on GitHub until GC (open a Support ticket to purge), the `dev`/`old-origin` remotes still need scrubbing-or-deleting, and the data was public for months so treat it as already exposed. The legacy `assets/js/data.js` loader was deleted (unused).
21. **Performance / dead-code (medium audit batch, 2026-07)**: (a) **Debounced search** — `window.dbnc(key, fn, ms=180)` in `index.html` + `project.html` wraps the heavy per-keystroke handlers (`wp-mon-search`/`bl-search`/`proj-table-search` on index; `f-search` listener + `bl-search` + backlog min/max on project) so the WP table + analysis panel don't rebuild on every keystroke. (b) **`review.html` quick-add modal removed** — it was unwired dead code that could have created an Awarded WP with no cost/vendor (no safeguard). (c) **SW1 (leaner `select('*')`) deliberately NOT done**: the WP detail panel reads the cached row (`window._wpById`), and bulk views need `scope` (Works tab), `proposed_vendors`/`contractor` (vendor filter) — so trimming columns would break those for little gain. The right fix for very large portfolios is server-side pagination, not column-trimming; the current `sessionStorage` quota fallback (refetch on miss) is graceful, not a bug.
22. **Asset weights / infra (low audit batch, 2026-07)**: the brand logos `assets/img/logo-white.png` (330KBâ†’28KB) and `megawide-logo.png` (202KBâ†’24KB) were **8000Ã—4000 px** originals resized to 900Ã—450 (optimized PNG, transparency preserved) â€” they display â‰¤380px, so no visible change. When replacing a logo, downscale to â‰¤~900px wide first. `WPM_Dashboard_Onboarding.pptx` is intentionally kept in the repo (wanted training deliverable, not sensitive; GitHub Pages serves the whole repo so "moving" it would mean untracking). **Supabase-dashboard-only items (not code):** raise the Auth password policy (min length/complexity) and configure custom SMTP (Resend/Brevo) so password-reset emails aren't throttled by the ~3/hr free-tier cap.
23. **Stored-XSS remediation + CSP (security audit, 2026-07)**: user-entered WP fields (`description`, `remarks`, `proposed_vendors`, `contractor`, `wp_no`…) and **self-registered `name`/`email`** are attacker-controllable (a contributor can PATCH a WP via the REST API; a pending registrant sets their own name) and were being interpolated **raw into `innerHTML`** across every render path — a payload like `<img src=x onerror=…>` stored in a field ran in an **admin's** session when they opened the WP List / User Management, exfiltrating the Supabase JWT from `localStorage` (`sb-<ref>-auth-token`) → account takeover, bypassing all RLS. **Fix:** a single **`window.esc(v)`** helper in `db.js` (escapes `& < > " '`, returns `''` for null so `|| '—'` fallbacks still work) is now applied at **all** user-text sinks in `index.html`, `project.html`, `admin.html`, `review.html`, `my-wps.html`, and the Top-5 `buildRankTable` in `db.js`. **When adding ANY new `${...}` interpolation of a user-entered field into `innerHTML`, wrap it in `esc(...)`.** **Follow-up audit (2026-07-21) closed sinks the first pass MISSED:** the **WP detail slide-in `row()` helper** (`index.html` + `project.html`) interpolated `${val}` RAW — so `description`/`remarks`/`proposed_vendors`/`wp_no`/`po_jo_numbers`/`cost_code`/`works`/`contract_package_no`/`co_description` rendered unescaped when anyone opened a WP's detail panel (account-takeover vector: token exfil via top-level `location=` navigation, which `connect-src`/`img-src` do NOT block). Fixed by giving `row(label,val,isHtml)` a 3rd flag — `${isHtml?val:esc(val)}` — and passing `isHtml=true` only for the two pre-built-HTML calls (Award-status badge, Variance span, whose inner user value is itself `esc`'d). Also escaped: `admin.html` Performance-tab user rows (`d.user.name||d.user.email`), the `db.js` approval-matrix picker label (`u.name||u.email`), the `db.js` avatar name (title attr + dropdown), the `db.js` recent-project name, and assorted project-name/id sinks in `index.html`/`admin.html`/`review.html`. **If you add a `row()`-style helper that returns HTML, escape its text arg by default and opt specific call sites OUT — never the reverse.** Attribute values already escaped for `"` only are safe (can't break a quoted attr without a `"`). Trade option-text is left unescaped **because `normTrade()` normalizes it to one of 10 canonical safe strings at load** — don't rely on that for other fields. Two special cases: (a) **inline-handler JS-arg vectors** can't be safely double-encoded (HTML-attr decode happens before JS parse, so `&#39;`→`'` breaks out) — `admin.html` `openApproveModal` was refactored to take **only `userId`** and look up name/email from `allUsers` (rendered via `.textContent`, inherently safe); the WP-delete button's `safeNo` now strips `['"\\<>]` so a crafted `wp_no` can't break the `onclick`. (b) `esc` is a `window.` property (like the intent for globals), NOT a top-level `const` (those don't attach to `window` — the `Charts`/`Fmt` gotcha). **Defense-in-depth CSP:** every `*.html` `<head>` now carries a `Content-Security-Policy` meta (inserted after `<meta charset>`). `script-src`/`style-src` keep `'unsafe-inline'` (the app is built on inline handlers + init scripts, so CSP can't stop the inline-handler XSS — escaping does), so the real value is **`connect-src 'self' https://<supabase> wss://<supabase>`** (blocks a stolen-token beacon to an attacker host) + `img-src 'self' data: blob:` + `object-src 'none'`. Allowed script/style/font origins: `cdn.jsdelivr.net`, `cdnjs.cloudflare.com`, `fonts.googleapis.com`, `fonts.gstatic.com`. **`form-action 'self'` added to all 15 pages' CSP (2026-07-21)** as defense-in-depth — blocks a form-based token-exfil POST to an attacker host even if a future XSS sink is missed (complements `connect-src`/`img-src`, which don't govern form submissions). (`navigate-to` was intentionally NOT added — near-zero browser support; the real mitigation remains escaping every sink.) **CSP GOTCHA (broke icons on first deploy):** the **Tabler icons webfont loads from `cdn.jsdelivr.net`** as a *stylesheet* (`@tabler/icons-webfont/tabler-icons.min.css`) that then pulls `.woff2` font files from the same host — so `cdn.jsdelivr.net` MUST be in **`style-src` AND `font-src`**, not just `script-src`. The first CSP shipped with jsdelivr only in `script-src`, which blocked the Tabler CSS (→ all `ti ti-*` icons vanished across every page). `connect-src` governs `fetch`/XHR/websockets ONLY (NOT `@font-face` loads — those are `font-src`), so don't test font reachability with `fetch()`. Verify icon fonts via the **preview network panel** (look for the tabler CSS/woff2 requests **not** `[FAILED]`) or `document.fonts`. Current working set: `script-src` jsdelivr+cdnjs; `style-src` fonts.googleapis+jsdelivr+cdnjs; `font-src` gstatic+jsdelivr+cdnjs+data. **Verified via local preview:** Supabase (REST+realtime), Chart.js (cdnjs), datalabels (jsdelivr), Google Fonts, and Tabler icons all load with zero CSP violations. If you add a new external origin, update the CSP in every page (the Supabase URL is hardcoded to **prod**). **Housekeeping (same pass):** removed the dead root-level `logo-white.png` (unreferenced duplicate of `assets/img/logo-white.png`), untracked `gen_import.py` (`git rm --cached` — it was committed despite being in `.gitignore`), and deleted stray local artifacts (`{`, `{data/`, `(profile.projects`).
18. **Dashboard stale data / bfcache**: `project.html` has a `pageshow` handler that calls `__wpmReloadFn()` when restored from bfcache. `index.html` has a `pageshow` handler that busts the `wpm_wps_idx_*` sessionStorage cache and reloads. `wp-form.html` and `review.html` bust that cache on save/approve/reject. Never revert these handlers. Use `_stickyLeft = {}` dict (never mutate col objects with `c._left`). Build thead via `innerHTML` with `white-space:normal;overflow:hidden` on `<th>` â€” NOT `nowrap`, which overflows adjacent cells. Build tbody via `innerHTML` string concat with a `renderCell(key, wp)` switch. Trade group header: split into `<td colspan=nSticky>` (sticky, `left:0`) + `<td colspan=rest>` (non-sticky, WP count right-aligned) â€” a full-colspan cell has nothing to stick against horizontally.
24. **`.data-table td` has NO `overflow:hidden` by default** (`dashboard.css`, only `white-space:nowrap`) — with `table-layout:fixed`, the column width IS respected for layout but nowrap content that's WIDER than its column visually spills into the next column instead of clipping (fixed 2026-07-31: `project.html`'s WP List Award Status badge — `_stAwardBadge`'s "○ Not Yet Awarded" text at the old `w:'110px'` column overlapped the adjacent Delivery Status dropdown). Fix was two-part: widened the `award_status` COLS entry to `150px` (badge text + padding actually fits), and added `overflow:hidden` on that `<td>` as a safety net for any longer legacy/custom status string. **When adding a badge/pill (not a plain text cell) to a fixed-layout table column, size the column to the badge's actual rendered width (glyph + text + badge padding + td padding) and add `overflow:hidden` on the `<td>` — don't rely on the column `w` alone.**
25. **Vestigial empty elements still cost layout space** — `project.html` had a leftover `<div class="metrics-grid" id="metrics-grid"></div>` right above `#view-tabs`, from before the old Dashboard tab was merged into Overview (see the Dashboard-tab-removed entries above). JS explicitly kept it empty ("Keep legacy metrics-grid empty" — `.innerHTML=''` every render) rather than removing it, but `.metrics-grid`'s CSS `margin-bottom:20px` still applied to the empty element, showing as a blank gap between the topbar and the view tabs that the Portfolio Overview (`index.html`) didn't have. **`index.html` has the SAME element for the same historical reason but had already neutralized it with `style="display:none"`** (comment: "Hidden metrics-grid kept for JS compatibility") — `project.html`'s copy was missing that. Fixed 2026-07-31 by removing the div and its dead JS clear-block entirely from `project.html` (nothing else referenced it there, unlike `index.html`'s copy). **When a DOM node is kept "empty on purpose," either give it `display:none` or delete it — an empty block-level element with margin/padding still reserves visual space.**
26b. **The "% Awarded by Value" battery fills by BCB, NOT by awarded cost (2026-08-17).** Its awarded segment was `hm.awardedCost / hm.totalBudget` — actual spend over total budget — so an over-budget project filled **past 100%** (e.g. ₱400M spent against ₱150M of budget read 267%), which also pushed the Due-Not-Awarded and Not-Due segments off the battery entirely. It now uses **`hm.budgetAwarded`** (the BCB of the awarded WPs), so all three segments are BCB-based slices of the SAME total and can only sum to 100%. Belt-and-braces clamps: `valPct = min(100, …)` and `valDuePct = min(100 − valPct, …)` (the latter also absorbs rounding, so the two can't total 101%). The card text changed with it — line 1 is now `₱<budgetAwarded> of ₱<totalBudget> BCB` and line 2 `awarded at ₱<awardedCost> actual cost`, so the real spend is still on screen, just no longer the thing driving the fill. **Applied to BOTH `project.html` (`renderOvBattery`) and `index.html` (`renderIdxOvBattery`) — keep them in sync.** The `% Awarded by WP` battery was already count-based and is untouched. **Known consequence, not a bug:** an awarded WP with no BCB (there are ~41 live — see the Portfolio audit) contributes nothing to the awarded segment, because it has no budget to consume; it still counts in the WP battery. Verified against overrun / all-awarded / all-overdue / awarded-with-no-BCB / zero-budget cases — every one sums to ≤100.
26. **WP Status battery % label — fixed white text + outline instead of conditional dark/white (2026-07-31, both pages)** — `batteryCard()`'s `%` label sits at the battery's vertical center, which can land on the green (Awarded), red-striped (Due Not Awarded), or light-gray (Not Due Yet) fill depending on the split; the old logic picked white text when `pctAwd>=50` (label on green) else near-black `#231F20` (label on red/gray). Correct in isolation, but the **% Awarded by WP** and **% Awarded by Value** cards usually have different award rates, so their labels frequently landed on different fills and rendered in different colors side by side — reads as an inconsistency bug even though each card was individually contrast-correct. Fixed by dropping the conditional and always using **white text with a solid dark outline** (4-directional 1px `text-shadow` + a soft drop shadow, not just the old single soft blur) — reads clearly on green, red-stripe, AND the light-gray fill, so both cards always match. Applies to `project.html`'s `renderOvBattery`/`batteryCard` and `index.html`'s matching `batteryCard` (Portfolio Overview) — keep both in sync if this is touched again.

27. **Battery % label font + card misalignment (2026-08-03, both pages)** — the `%` digit's `text-shadow` used 4 separate hard-edged directional offsets, which rendered jagged/fuzzy at some zoom levels; replaced with a single soft glow stack (`0 0 3px / 0 0 6px / 0 1px 2px`, all `rgba(0,0,0,...)`) plus explicit `font-family:'Montserrat',sans-serif` and `-webkit-font-smoothing:antialiased`, so the digit reads crisp instead of outlined. Separately, the two battery cards' TITLE (`% Awarded by WP` / `% Awarded by Value`) wraps to a second line whenever the sidebar is expanded (narrower content column) — since the title's height was fixed-margin only, a 2-line title pushed that card's whole battery graphic lower than its 1-line sibling, so the two bars (and their `%`/line1/line2 rows below) went visibly out of alignment side-by-side. Fixed by giving the title a `min-height:2.4em;display:flex;align-items:flex-end` (bottom-aligned within a reserved 2-line slot) so both cards' battery boxes start at the same Y position regardless of whether either title wraps. Applies to both `batteryCard()` copies — keep in sync.

28. **"Not to be Awarded" flag — a WP that will NEVER get a real cost should count as SAVINGS, not be excluded (2026-08-03, decoupled from Award Status same day).** Known Issue #17's rule (a WP's money only counts when `award_status==='Awarded' && total_awarded>0`) was built to stop WPs whose cost is merely *pending data entry* from booking phantom savings. But some WPs are marked "Awarded" in the source Remarks with **no cost by design** — Ops decided they'll never go through a formal award at all (handled outside PRC: petty cash, direct arrangement, or removed from scope; found via a free-text note in the source monitoring sheet, e.g. "NOT TO BE AWARD PER OPS" / "NOT TO BE AWARDED"). Excluding those from BOTH sides of the ledger (old behavior) understates real savings — their budget genuinely will never be spent, so it should count as realized savings at an effective ₱0 cost. Example (CSF101): WP 10 (Transport Truck/Earthmoving, ₱3.25M budget) and WP 13 (Falsework, ₱0.64M budget) are flagged this way — Savings/Loss reads +₱27.80M with the flag vs +₱23.92M without (the ₱3.89M difference is exactly their combined budget).
    - **New column**: `work_packages.not_to_be_awarded boolean DEFAULT false` (`migrations/2026-08-03_not_to_be_awarded.sql`).
    - **Deliberately does NOT require `award_status==='Awarded'`.** The first cut did require it (so the flag only "activated" once Procurement Status was set to Awarded), but that reads as a direct contradiction in the WP form — "Award Status: Awarded" sitting right next to "Not to be Awarded: Yes". Fixed same day: the flag is now fully self-sufficient. A user can leave Procurement Status at whatever honestly reflects reality (e.g. "Not Started") and just check the box — no need to lie about the status to get the savings treatment.
    - **`window.isResolved(w)`** (db.js) — `award_status==='Awarded' || not_to_be_awarded` — "is this WP done, not sitting in the active backlog." **`window.isMoneyAwarded(w)`** — `not_to_be_awarded || (award_status==='Awarded' && total_awarded>0)`. **`window.effectiveAwardedCost(w)`** — `0` when `not_to_be_awarded`, else `total_awarded||0`. **Use these everywhere the app decides a WP's resolved/backlog state or its money — never re-inline `award_status==='Awarded'` (or its negation) or `total_awarded>0`,** or a flagged WP disagrees between tabs/charts again (the exact bug #17 was written to prevent, now generalized). Consumers, swept 2026-08-03 across `db.js`, `charts.js`, `index.html`, `project.html`, `admin.html`, `review.html`: `helpingMetrics()`'s awarded COUNT and MONEY bases (the shared basis for `buildMetrics()`/`computeStats()` — every Cost-Overview KPI card on both dashboards); the WP-status **batteries** (`renderOvBattery`/`renderIdxOvBattery`) and status-bars/status-table Due-Not-Awarded / Not-Due splits; the **Backlog** tables and their period-chart filters on both pages; `charts.js`'s `budgetAwardedByPeriod(Monthly)` `isAwd`/forecast, `wpStatusDonut(Value)`, `wpByTrade`, `awardRateByTrade`, and the WP-count period charts' Forecast predicate (`_actAwDate(w)||not_to_be_awarded`, additive — doesn't touch any other WP's forecast inclusion); Top-5 `withBoth`/`variance()` (already flowed through `isMoneyAwarded`, so this "just worked" once decoupled); the Budget-tab per-trade table and WP-List "Total to be Awarded" tiles (both pages); `admin.html`'s Performance-tab awarded/overdue counts; the PDF export's awarded count.
    - **WP-count battery/donut = Yes, ₱-value battery = savings, not spend.** By design the two lenses differ: the **WP-count** side (`helpingMetrics().awarded`, `wpStatusDonut`) counts a flagged WP as Awarded (green) — it's resolved. The **₱-value** side (`hm.awardedCost`, the battery's `awd`) stays real-dollars-spent only — a flagged WP contributes `effectiveAwardedCost=0` there, so its budget never inflates "Awarded ₱"; that budget's savings nature is captured by the Savings/Loss KPI card instead, not by the battery bucket. (`wpStatusDonutValue` is the one exception — it already summed *budget*, not real cost, for its "Awarded" bucket even before this feature, so it naturally treats a flagged WP's budget the same as any other awarded WP's, with no special-casing needed.)
    - **Status badges** (`_stAwardBadge` in `project.html`, the WP-detail panels in both pages, the WP List `case 'status'` cell in `index.html`, review.html's Cards view) show a distinct **"Not to be Awarded"** label (neutral gray, not red/green) instead of the literal (and now potentially inaccurate) `award_status` text, whenever the flag is set. **Not yet done** (minor, cosmetic, low-traffic corners): the mobile card-view badges on both pages (`_sc`/`_bc` helpers take a bare status string, not the WP), the Schedule tab's per-WP expandable row (`index.html`), and `admin.html`'s Performance-tab per-row status color — these still show the literal award_status color there. None of these affect any number, only a label/color in a rarely-viewed spot.
    - **UI:** a **"Not to be Awarded"** checkbox in `wp-form.html`'s Budget & Contract section (`#f-not-to-be-awarded`, `onNotToBeAwardedChange()`) disables + clears Awarded Cost/Vendor while checked and waives their `reqIfAwarded` requirement in `save()`. Same column added to the `review.html` grid (`XL_COLS`, `g:'budget'`, `type:'bool'`) — `_xlRequired()` waives `reqIfAwarded` the same way. Both reuse existing generic `bool`-column machinery — no new editor code needed.
    - **When to check it**: only for a WP that will *permanently* never have a cost (Ops removed it from the award pipeline). A WP whose cost simply hasn't been entered yet should stay unflagged — it'll correctly show as excluded (not as savings) until a real Awarded Cost lands, per #17.

28b. **Buyback work packages — the recovered value counts towards SAVINGS (2026-08-11).** Some WPs are procured under a **buyback** arrangement: the vendor takes the item back at the end of its use, so part of the awarded cost is recovered and is NOT real spend. Booking the full awarded cost understated savings. Modelled as a sibling of the `not_to_be_awarded` flag above (same "status modifier that changes the money basis" shape), routed through the SAME two central helpers so every KPI/chart/forecast/export agrees automatically.
    - **Two new columns** (`migrations/2026-08-11_wp_buyback.sql`, run once — additive, both `IF NOT EXISTS`): `work_packages.buyback boolean DEFAULT false` + `buyback_depreciation_percent numeric(5,4)`. The percent is stored as a **decimal fraction** (`0.30` = 30%), exactly like `dp_percent`/`retention_percent` — the WP form and the review grid both take a whole percent from the user and ÷100 before saving. **Deploy-order-safe:** `db.js` `_isMissingBuybackCol`/`_stripBuyback` (folded into `_stripOptional` + the `submitWP`/`updateWP`/`updateWPDirect` retry conditions) strip both columns and retry, so the code ships safely before the migration runs (buyback just won't persist until it does).
    - **Formula (chosen explicitly by the user):** the depreciation % is how much value is **CONSUMED**, so it is the part that stays as real cost; the remainder is bought back.
      `buybackValue(w) = total_awarded × (1 − dep%)` (recovered → savings) and `effectiveAwardedCost(w) = total_awarded × dep%`. e.g. ₱10M awarded at 30% depreciation → ₱7M buyback value, ₱3M effective cost. **Do NOT flip this to `awarded × dep%`** — that was the rejected alternative.
    - **`window.buybackDepFraction(w)`** (`db.js`) returns the clamped 0..1 fraction, or `null` when the WP isn't a buyback / has no depreciation % — so `buybackValue()` returns 0 and `effectiveAwardedCost()` falls back to the raw cost, i.e. **every non-buyback WP is bit-for-bit unaffected**. The clamp means a bad stored value can never produce a negative cost or a buyback larger than the award. A `not_to_be_awarded` WP short-circuits first (already booked at an effective ₱0), so the two flags can't compound.
    - **The whole ledger updated via ONE hook.** `window.effectiveAwardedCost` now nets off the buyback, and `helpingMetrics()` already summed `awardedCost` through it — so the Cost-Overview KPIs (Actual Award / CTC / EAC / Savings-Loss + %), the awarded bars + cumulative/forecast lines in `charts.js`, Top 5 Savings/Overbudget/High-Value, the Budget-tab per-trade table, the Action Center's over-budget list and the vendor analytics ALL picked this up with no per-consumer change. **This is exactly why #28's rule exists — never re-inline `total_awarded` for a WP's cost.** Note the **awarded COUNT and BCB-for-awarded bases are deliberately unchanged** (a buyback WP is still a normally-awarded WP with a real cost — only the *magnitude* of that cost changes), unlike `not_to_be_awarded` which changes the count basis too.
    - **WP form (`wp-form.html`, Budget & Contract):** a **Buyback** checkbox (`#f-buyback`, `onBuybackChange()`) reveals a required **Depreciation %** input (`#f-buyback-dep`) plus a read-only live **Buyback Value (auto)** readout (`#buyback-value-display`) that recomputes on every dep%/Awarded-Cost keystroke (`_updateBuybackValue()`, also called from `updateVariance()` since the value derives from the Awarded Cost) and spells out the effective cost booked. `save()` requires a 0–100 dep% when the box is ticked (scroll-focuses the field otherwise). Edit-load rehydrates ×100 then calls `onBuybackChange()`; the autosave draft's generic `f-*` snapshot covers both fields and `restoreDraft()` re-runs `onBuybackChange()` to re-reveal them. **Ticking "Not to be Awarded" hides + unchecks Buyback entirely** (`onNotToBeAwardedChange`) rather than leaving two contradictory money treatments on screen.
    - **Review grid (`review.html`):** three columns in the `budget` group — `buyback` (`type:'bool'`), `buyback_depreciation_percent` (`type:'pct'`, new **`reqIfBuyback`** rule in `_xlRequired` → required-empty highlighting + save gating whenever the flag is on and the WP isn't `not_to_be_awarded`), and a read-only derived **`_buyback_value`** recomputed live in `xlVal` from the STAGED flag/dep%/awarded cost (stripped from the write patch alongside `_variance`, and skipped by `_xlDraftPayload`'s `_`-prefix rule).
    - **WP List filter, both dashboards** (the user's explicit ask): a plain 3-option `<select>` beside the Charging Type filter — **`#f-buyback`** on `project.html` (`All (Buyback + Non-buyback)` / `Buyback only` / `Non-buyback only`, wired into ALL THREE parallel predicates `getFiltered`/`getFilteredSorted`/`_pwaFilteredWPs` — **keep in sync**, same convention as `#f-charging` — plus the `['f-status','f-charging','f-buyback']` change-listener array) and **`#wp-mon-buyback`** on `index.html` (wired into the single `filterWPTable()` predicate that feeds the table + cards + Filtered Analysis, and added to `resetWPMonFilters()`'s clear-list). Not added to `_recomputeFilterOptions()` — the Charging filter isn't either.
    - **WP detail slide-in panel (both pages):** the Financials block gains **Buyback** (`N% depreciated`), a green **Buyback Value**, and **Effective Cost** rows, and the panel's **Variance now uses the effective cost** (`bud − bbEff`) so it agrees with the dashboard KPIs instead of reading against the gross award. Non-buyback rows are unchanged (`bbEff === awd`, and `row()` omits any `—` value). All inside the existing `if (!isViewer)` (`__hideBudget`) guard, since these are cost figures.
    - **TWO INPUT MODES — depreciation % OR exact amount (2026-08-11, same day, per user request).** A third column **`buyback_amount numeric(18,2)`** (same `migrations/2026-08-11_wp_buyback.sql`, re-run it) holds an exact recovered figure. **Exactly ONE of `buyback_depreciation_percent` / `buyback_amount` is ever stored** — the WP form's `Buyback entered as` picker (`#f-buyback-mode`, `onBuybackModeChange()`) hides AND clears the input that no longer applies, and `save()` writes null for the unused one — so the two can never drift apart. **`window.buybackExactAmount(w)`** (db.js) returns the amount clamped to `[0, total_awarded]` (never recover more than was awarded, never negative) or `null`; **an exact amount WINS over the %** in `buybackValue()` wherever both somehow appear (e.g. a hand-edited row), and `effectiveAwardedCost()` is now `max(0, cost − buybackValue(w))` for any buyback — one formula covering both modes, so no consumer changed. In amount mode the form's auto readout relabels itself **`Effective Cost (auto)`** (echoing the typed buyback back as "auto" would be useless) and validates inline that the amount can't exceed the Awarded Cost. `review.html` gained a **`Buyback Amount`** money column, `_buyback_value` honours it, and **`reqIfBuyback` on Depreciation % is now either/or** — waived as soon as an amount is present. The WP-detail panel labels the row **`Exact amount`** vs **`N% depreciated`**.
    - **Live-test findings (2026-08-11), all fixed:** (1) the review grid's Depreciation % header had **no red required `*`** — the header's star/`xl-req` class checked only `req || reqIfAwarded`, so any future conditional-required flag needs adding there too (it now reads `req || reqIfAwarded || reqIfBuyback`). (2) **`_stripBuyback` was too coarse** — it dropped all three buyback fields on ANY buyback-named error, so on a DB that has `buyback` but not the newer `buyback_amount` (the real state between the two migrations) a save silently discarded the flag AND the % as well. It now strips only the column the error actually names, falling back to the whole set only when the base `buyback` column itself is missing. (3) **an emptied retry patch threw an opaque error** — when a patch's ONLY fields are columns the DB lacks (very reachable: stage just the Buyback cell in the grid pre-migration), the retry stripped it to `{}`, and PostgREST treats an empty update body as 0 rows, so `.single()` failed with *"Cannot coerce the result to a single JSON object"* — surfacing as a save failure when there was simply nothing persistable. All four **update** retry sites now route through **`_retryUpdate(sb,id,patch)`**, which skips the write, `console.warn`s, and returns the row unchanged; **insert** paths deliberately still fail loudly. **If you add another optional-column deploy guard, strip precisely and go through `_retryUpdate`.**
    - **NOT covered (flag if wanted):** the CSV import/template and the Excel monitoring-sheet importer have no buyback columns (source sheets don't carry the field), `exportProjectExcel`/the PDF export don't include it (it isn't in the dashboards' `COLS`), and the mobile card views / review Cards view show no buyback badge.

28c. **⚠️ SUPERSEDED IN PART BY #43:** `free_of_charge` no longer affects the money — a plain ₱0 cost now behaves identically. The flag is retained for INTENT + validation only. **No-Cost Awards — a WP can be genuinely awarded at ₱0 (2026-08-25).** Some WPs are awarded with the vendor providing the scope **free of charge**. That is a real, closed award whose entire BCB is realized savings — but `isMoneyAwarded`'s `total_awarded > 0` test (Known Issue #17) put it in the same bucket as "Awarded, cost not encoded yet": dropped from both sides of the ledger, booking no savings, its BCB stuck in Balance to Award forever.
    - **The UI label is "No-Cost Award"; the DB column is `free_of_charge`. That mismatch is deliberate** (2026-08-25, chosen over renaming the column: a rename needs a migration plus a backfill for zero behavioural gain). Grep for `free_of_charge` when tracing the data, and for "No-Cost Award" when tracing what the user sees. The shipped strings are the checkbox label + tooltip + hint in `wp-form.html`, the `No-Cost Award` grid column in `review.html`, and the green detail-panel row on both dashboards. **A one-off "Free of Charge"/"Free Issue" wording was considered and rejected** — "No-Cost Award" keeps the word *Award* in the name, which is the entire distinction from Not to be Awarded.
    - **New column**: `work_packages.free_of_charge boolean DEFAULT false` (`migrations/2026-08-25_wp_free_of_charge.sql`). Deploy-order-safe via `_isMissingFreeOfChargeCol`/`_stripFreeOfCharge` in `db.js` (folded into `_stripOptional` + all three `submitWP`/`updateWP`/`updateWPDirect` retry conditions) — ships safely before the migration runs.
    - **⚠️ AN EXPLICIT FLAG, NOT "`awarded_cost = 0` MEANS FREE". Do not "simplify" this by relaxing the `> 0` test for everyone.** A literal 0 is not a signal of intent in this data: on AVR101, **WP 35 (Architectural Design) and WP 45 (Gravel Bedding and Lean Concrete Labor Works) already store `awarded_cost = 0` with no vendor** — an importer default on rows whose cost was simply never entered. Inferring "0 = free" would have instantly booked those as savings. Only a person ticking the box can tell the two apart. The migration says the same thing and deliberately does not back-fill.
    - **Semantics — unlike `not_to_be_awarded`, this flag does NOT stand alone.** NTBA means there was never an award, so it is independent of `award_status`; free-of-charge *describes an award*, so it only counts once `award_status === 'Awarded'`. `isMoneyAwarded` is now: `not_to_be_awarded` → true; else Award Status must be `Awarded` AND (`free_of_charge` OR `total_awarded > 0`). `effectiveAwardedCost` returns 0 for either flag. `buybackValue` returns 0 for a free WP (nothing to recover from a ₱0 award).
    - **Because both money helpers are the single choke point (#28), the whole ledger picked this up with no per-consumer change** — Cost Overview KPIs, `charts.js` awarded bars + cumulative/forecast lines, Top 5, the Budget-tab per-trade table, Action Center, vendor analytics. **The awarded COUNT basis is unchanged** (`isResolved` already counted it, since Award Status is `Awarded`) — only the *money* side moved.
    - **It waives the Awarded COST requirement but never the VENDOR one** — somebody is providing the scope free and we record who. Enforced in `wp-form.html` `save()` (`!freeOfCharge && !getNum('f-contract')`) and in `review.html`'s `_xlRequired` (`col.k === 'awarded_cost' && free_of_charge` → not required; `contractor` still is). Co-award per-vendor amounts are skipped for a free award.
    - **UI:** a **No-Cost Award** checkbox in `wp-form.html`'s Budget & Contract (`#f-free-of-charge`, `onFreeOfChargeChange()`) which sets Awarded Cost to `0` + disables it, and hides Buyback. **Mutually exclusive with Not to be Awarded** — ticking NTBA unchecks + hides this one (NTBA wins: no award at all beats an award at zero), and `onNotToBeAwardedChange()` calls `onFreeOfChargeChange()` on its way out so the cost field's state is re-applied. **`onFreeOfChargeChange`'s else-branch only re-ENABLES the cost field, never rewrites its value** — a non-free WP can legitimately load with a stored 0 (see above), and blanking that on open would silently edit data the user never touched. Also a `No-Cost Award` `type:'bool'` grid column in `review.html`, and a green **"No-Cost Award — Awarded at ₱0, vendor provides this free"** row in the WP detail panel on both dashboards so a ₱0 award does not read as missing data.
    - **Action Center Data Gaps excludes it from "missing cost"** (`index.html`, `gapsAll`) — otherwise every free award would sit in the gap list permanently. It can still appear there for a **missing vendor**, which is correct.
    - **NOT covered (flag if wanted):** the CSV template/importer and the Excel monitoring-sheet importer have no `free_of_charge` column (source sheets do not carry it), the Status-Tracker award modal does not offer it (full authoring is the WP form), and `wp_view_public` needs the column adding by hand — **the migration only RAISES A NOTICE about this**, it does not rewrite the view (rolling that definition forward blind risks dropping a column). Until that is done, the `viewer` role computes a different awarded set than every other role.

29. **`review.html` grid: bool-column cell editor showed a spurious raw `true`/`false` option (pre-existing bug, surfaced 2026-08-03 by the new Not to be Awarded column).** The cell editor's "keep legacy/off-list values" logic (`_xlBeginEdit`, ~line 1277) unshifts `String(cur)` as an extra `<option>` whenever the current value isn't already in the dropdown's option list — correct for `select`-type columns (a stored value the current option set dropped), but wrong for `type:'bool'` columns: their option list is the DISPLAY strings `['','Yes','No']`, while `cur` is the raw JS boolean `true`/`false`, so `String(cur)` (`"true"`/`"false"`) never matches and gets unshifted as a bogus 4th option every time — any bool cell with a real value showed `false`/`true`, `— none —`, `Yes`, `No` instead of just the three. This silently affected `requires_approval` too (rarely re-edited directly, so unnoticed) — it wasn't introduced by the new column, just made obvious because every row of a brand-new bool column defaults to `false`. **Fixed by excluding `col.type==='bool'` from that unshift** — a bool column's 3-state set (`blank`/`Yes`/`No`) is always complete, there's no such thing as an off-list boolean. If you add another `type:'bool'` column, this is already handled; don't reintroduce a per-column bypass.

30. **Mobile UI pass (2026-08-03): topbar title clash, dead expand buttons, review.html default view, onboarding-modal footer.**
    - **Topbar title was crushed to 1 character on phones (`index.html` + `project.html`).** Root cause was two-fold: (a) the `<div>` wrapping `.page-title`/`.page-sub` had no `min-width:0`, so as a flex item its automatic min-size was its full un-truncated content width — `.topbar-left`'s `overflow:hidden` (mobile only) then hard-clipped it instead of letting `text-overflow:ellipsis` engage; (b) even after fixing that, `.topbar-right` (`flex-shrink:0`, never shrinks) was crowded with up to 6 icon buttons (refresh, Export Excel, Export PDF, guide "?", theme toggle, avatar) — on a 375px phone their combined footprint left almost nothing for the title regardless of the ellipsis fix. Fixed both: the title wrapper now has `min-width:0;flex:1 1 auto` (project.html's wrapper also needed to stay overflow:visible, not hidden, since it hosts the `#hdr-switcher` popover — only `min-width:0` was added there); the actual truncating text moved to a dedicated `.proj-switcher-text` span (`overflow:hidden;text-overflow:ellipsis;white-space:nowrap;min-width:0`) since `.page-title`'s own ellipsis CSS doesn't apply cleanly once project.html repurposes it as an `inline-flex` row with a caret child. **And** `#btn-export`, `#btn-export-xlsx`, `#btn-guide` are now `display:none` below 768px (dashboard.css) — see next bullet for where they went. Verified via a throwaway static harness (real `dashboard.css`/`db.js`, deleted after) at 375px: topbar-right dropped from an estimated ~350px to a measured 118px, and the title box went from ~0px usable width to 153px (rendering "AVR101 — Avida R…" instead of "A").
    - **Export Excel / Export PDF / the "?" guide button moved into the profile (avatar) dropdown on mobile only**, per user request, instead of just hiding them outright. `renderUserBar()` (db.js) now builds an `exportItems` block by checking for `#btn-export-xlsx`/`#btn-export` in the DOM (so it adapts per page with no per-page code — project.html gets both, index.html gets PDF only) and injects "Export Excel"/"Export PDF" `<a class="user-menu-mobile-only">` rows that just call `.click()` on the real (now hidden) button — reusing its exact handler, including its own `window.__hideBudget` viewer guard, so nothing needed duplicating. The builder ALSO checks `window.__hideBudget` itself before adding anything, so a `viewer` never sees an export entry that would silently no-op (both pages set `__isViewer`/`__hideBudget` before calling `renderUserBar`, so the flag is always ready in time). `.user-menu-mobile-only{display:none!important}` at `≥768px` hides these dropdown rows on tablet/desktop, where the topbar buttons are the primary path — **the `!important` is required**: the rows carry an inline `display:flex` (matching the other dropdown rows' inline-styled layout) which would otherwise always beat a plain media-query rule regardless of viewport (caught by testing the real `db.js` output, not just a hand-copied snippet). The standalone `.btn-guide` topbar button is simply hidden on mobile (no dropdown replacement needed) since the dropdown's existing "Restart tour" link already opens the same tour.
    - **Chart "fullscreen" expand button did nothing on mobile.** `initExpandableCharts()` (ui.js) used only the real Fullscreen API — iOS Safari has no `Element.requestFullscreen` at all (video-only), and some Android/PWA-standalone browsers silently reject the request or never fire `fullscreenchange`, so the button looked broken. Added a CSS-simulated fallback: `.panel-fake-fullscreen` (dashboard.css — `position:fixed;inset:0;z-index:500`, mirrors `.panel:fullscreen`'s styling) is applied whenever `requestFullscreen`/`webkitRequestFullscreen` don't exist at all, whenever the request's promise rejects, or (safety net) whenever fullscreen still hasn't actually engaged ~350ms after asking. That 350ms fallback timer is cancelled the moment a real `fullscreenchange` for the panel does land (`fakeFallbackTimer`, cleared in `syncState()`), so a native fullscreen that's merely slow to engage can't end up ALSO stuck in the fake mode. Esc exits fake mode via a dedicated `keydown` listener (real fullscreen already had this via the browser). `document.body.style.overflow` is toggled to `hidden` while faked, matching the existing mobile-drawer pattern.
    - **Top 5 KPI card expand caret was a dead click target on mobile.** `initTop5Accordion()` (ui.js) has always no-op'd below 768px (mobile already stacks each card full-width, so there's nothing to expand) — but the `.t5-caret` icon was still rendered with a hover-affordance, looking clickable while doing nothing. `.top5-row .t5-caret{display:none}` + `cursor:default` on the title now hide it below 768px (dashboard.css) — no JS change needed since `initTop5Accordion` already gated on the same breakpoint.
    - **Chart expand button clipped past the panel's right edge on mobile, THREE rounds the same day — the third was a real functional regression, not cosmetic.** Round 1: the panels with a Monthly/Quarterly/WP/Budget/# toggle row (the period-chart panels on both dashboards) already filled their `.panel-title` row edge-to-edge on a phone; that row's toggle-button group is `flex-shrink:0` (never shrinks) and had no wrap room to spare, so appending the expand icon as a normal flex child rendered up to ~11px past the panel's right edge (confirmed via a throwaway harness loading the real `dashboard.css`: 374px button-right vs. a 363px panel-right). Fix: let the title row wrap — no longer clipped, but a user screenshot showed the icon then landing on its own orphaned line with nothing around it, reading as randomly placed. Round 2: the button stopped being a flex child of `.panel-title` — `initExpandableCharts()` (ui.js) anchors it via `position:absolute;top:18px;right:18px` to the PANEL itself instead, independent of however crowded or wrapped the title row gets; the title row still wraps (so a `flex-shrink:0` toggle group drops to its own row 2, getting the FULL row width there instead of fighting the corner icon for room) plus reserves 28px `padding-right` on row 1 (so the title's own shrinking text-line never runs under the icon — padding alone doesn't stop a `flex-shrink:0` group from overflowing into it, hence the wrap too). To make the icon a positioning reference, that round set `panel.style.position='relative'` **inline via JS** — which turned out to be the cause of Round 3. **Round 3 (the actual regression, caught by a user report: "when expanded the only thing you can do is un-expand"):** `.panel.panel-fake-fullscreen{position:fixed}` (the CSS mobile-fullscreen fallback, see the bullet above) is a class rule with no `!important` — but Round 2's JS-set inline `panel.style.position='relative'` **always beats a class rule regardless of specificity** (inline wins over stylesheet short of `!important`), so the panel could never actually become `position:fixed` once expanded — it just sat in normal document flow at an enormous height (`calc(100vh - 96px)`), pushing all the OTHER page content down instead of covering the screen as a real overlay. The toggle buttons technically still worked, but there was no true fullscreen state to interact with — scrolling a giant in-flow chart with the rest of the dashboard shoved out of view reads exactly like "stuck, only the shrink button does anything." **Fixed at the CSS level, not JS:** `position: relative` moved onto the base `.panel` class itself (dashboard.css) — `initExpandableCharts()` no longer touches `panel.style.position` at all — so `.panel.panel-fake-fullscreen`'s higher-specificity class selector can now correctly override the bare `.panel` rule's `position:relative` with its own `position:fixed`, the way CSS cascade is supposed to work. A second, smaller instance of the exact same inline-beats-class trap was caught in the same pass: several panels (e.g. `#dash-period-panel`) carry an inline `margin-top`/`margin-bottom`, which `.panel-fake-fullscreen{margin:0}` (also no `!important`) couldn't override either — `top/right/bottom/left:0` resolved correctly but the panel's own margin still offset its border box away from those edges, leaving thin gaps at the top/bottom instead of true edge-to-edge. Fixed by adding `!important` to that `margin:0`. **Lesson generalized:** when a CSS class is meant to override arbitrary per-instance state (especially on elements this app authors with inline styles throughout, e.g. `style="margin-top:16px"`), either put the base property in a class too (so specificity decides it) or add `!important` on the override — never reach for `element.style.X` from JS to satisfy a class-based override, because inline JS-set styles silently and unconditionally outrank it. Verified geometrically (not just visually) with a throwaway harness reproducing the real inline margin: `position:fixed`, zero margin gap, full viewport coverage, and toggle-button clicks still land on the button (not swallowed) while the fake-fullscreen class is active.
    - **`review.html` filter bar: the project badge and "Trade" label landed next to each other, orphaned from the Trade `<select>` (follow-up, same day)** — in per-project context the row was `[Project group (hidden)] [GPR101 badge] [Trade label] [Trade select] [search] [view switch]`, all as separate flex siblings with one shared `flex-wrap`. On a phone the wrap broke the row wherever space ran out — landing "GPR101" and the bare "Trade" text label together on one line while the actual dropdown wrapped alone to the next, reading as two mismatched badges. Fixed by grouping each label with its own control as a single flex-wrap unit (`.rv-filter-group`, mirroring the Project group's existing pattern), plus a `≤520px` layout that stacks the badge / Trade group / search / view-switch into four full-width rows instead of an unpredictable wrap. Verified with a harness: badge on its own row, "Trade" label+select together on the next row, both confirmed unchanged at desktop width.
    - **`review.html` opened into the Grid (Excel) view on mobile** — a full spreadsheet is unusable on a phone screen; the desktop default makes sense there but not on mobile. The `requireLogin` callback now calls `setReviewView('cards')` once, right after the initial `loadAll()`, when `matchMedia('(max-width:767px)')` matches at load time. Desktop/tablet are untouched (still default to Grid); a user can still switch views manually on either.
    - **Onboarding "Dashboard Guide" modal footer wrapped badly on mobile** (`onboarding.js`) — `.ob-foot` crammed 4 items (progress dots, the "Don't show on login" checkbox, Back, Next) onto one `nowrap` row; the checkbox label had no `white-space:nowrap` so it wrapped to 2 lines and visually collided with the buttons. The existing `@media(max-width:520px)` block now also sets `.ob-foot{flex-wrap:wrap;row-gap:10px}` with explicit `order` on each child — dots (own centered row) → checkbox (own centered row, `white-space:nowrap`) → Back/Next (`flex:1` each, split the row 50/50) — plus a smaller `.ob-overlay` padding and `.ob-x` close-button size so the modal itself uses more of a small screen's width.
    - **Dashboard Guide's Back/Next footer moved per slide (2026-08-12)** — same root cause already fixed once for `vendor-guide.js`: `.ob-body{overflow-y:auto;flex:1;min-height:0}` had no fixed height, so slides with more content (e.g. the 5-item "Handy to know" tips slide) grew the modal taller than shorter slides (the 3-card capability-matrix slide), shifting `.ob-foot`'s Back/Next buttons up and down as the user paged through — reported as "moves the next per slide which makes it difficult to navigate easily." Fixed the same way: `.ob-body{height:340px;overflow-y:auto;flex-shrink:0}` (desktop) / `height:min(340px,50vh)` (the `≤520px` mobile block) so the body is a fixed-height scroll region and the footer never moves. Verified via a throwaway `_preview_onboarding.html` harness (deleted before commit) that force-opened the tour outside the real auth flow.
    - **"?" button now opens a small menu (Dashboard Guide / What's New) instead of jumping straight into the tour (2026-08-12)** — the patch notice's "What's New" entry (added to the profile/avatar dropdown, see the Patch Notice section) wasn't reachable from the **"?"** button users actually look to for help; they had to know to check the avatar menu instead. `injectButton()` now wraps `#btn-guide` in a positioned `div`, injects a sibling `#btn-guide-menu` popover, and `toggleGuideMenu()` builds its two links **on every open** (not once at inject time) — `Dashboard Guide` always, `What's New` only when `window.PatchNotice && PatchNotice.open` exists (patch-notice.js loads AFTER onboarding.js in the script order, so this can't be checked at button-creation time, only at click time once every script has run). Closes on outside click (`onGuideMenuOutsideClick`, added/removed from `document` only while open) or after either item is clicked. Same self-contained pattern as the rest of this file — CSS uses the existing `--surface`/`--border`/`--text-*` vars, dark-mode automatic.

31. **`project.html` sidebar project-list names were hard-clipped instead of ellipsis-truncated (2026-08-03).** `renderSidebarProjects()`'s per-project `<a class="nav-item">` is a flex row (icon + text `<span>`), but the `<span>` had `overflow:hidden;text-overflow:ellipsis;white-space:nowrap` with no `min-width:0` — a flex item's default `min-width` is `auto`, not `0`, so it refuses to shrink below its content's natural width regardless of `overflow`/`text-overflow`. The text just ran past the sidebar's edge and got hard-clipped by `.sidebar{overflow-x:hidden}` (no "…"), worse on longer project names and the ~6px scrollbar eating into the already-narrow 240px sidebar. **Fixed by adding `flex:1;min-width:0`** to the span (matches the working pattern already used elsewhere, e.g. index.html's period-chart title span). `index.html`/`admin.html`'s own sidebar project lists don't have this bug — they truncate by slicing the string in JS (`.slice(0,12)`/`.slice(0,14)`) rather than relying on CSS ellipsis, so they were never exposed to this. If you add another CSS-ellipsis text span inside a flex `.nav-item`, it needs `min-width:0` explicitly — `flex-shrink:1` alone is not enough.
    - **`admin.html`'s sidebar had a DIFFERENT bug (same investigation, fixed same day):** `renderSidebarProjects()` there set `navProj.style.maxHeight='240px'; navProj.style.overflowY='auto'` on the project list — a second scrollbar nested inside `.sidebar`'s own `overflow-y:auto`, the exact "two scrollbars side by side" anti-pattern the sidebar-scrolling rule elsewhere in this doc warns against (`project.html`/`index.html` were already fixed for it; `admin.html`'s own copy of this feature had drifted). Removed the inline max-height/overflow so the list grows naturally in the single sidebar scroller, and made `#sidebar-proj-search-wrap` `position:sticky;top:0` (background `var(--mw-black)`) so the search box stays reachable while scrolling, matching `project.html`'s pattern.

32. **"Work Packages by Period" Forecast can read HIGHER than Cumulative Planned — a real bug, not Change Orders (found + audited 2026-08-12, chart footnote added same day).** In `charts.js`'s `wpCountByPeriodMonthly`/`wpCountByPeriod` (the Overview period chart) AND `sCurve` (WP-count mode), **Cumulative Planned only counts WPs that have a planned `awarding_date`** — a WP with none is never bucketed into any period and is permanently excluded from that line's ceiling. **Forecast (`_wpForecastLine` / `sCurve`'s inline `fdate`) counts EVERY not-yet-resolved WP, including ones with no planned date at all** — those fall into the `idx = futureIdx` / `nextME` "overdue or no plan → next period" branch. So whenever not-yet-resolved WPs are missing `awarding_date`, Forecast's ceiling exceeds Planned's ceiling by exactly that count — the dashed line visibly climbs above the solid gray one, which reads as nonsensical until you know why. **Live-audited the portfolio (2026-08-12):** of 1,734 total WPs, 81 not-yet-resolved WPs have no `awarding_date` — **all 81 from a single project, NIA101** (Site Surveys, Security Services, Fogging/Misting, Garbage Disposal, Vertical Equipment, etc. — general-requirements-type WPs that apparently never got a planned award date entered). Planned's ceiling = 1,653; Forecast's ceiling = 1,734; gap = 81, matching exactly. **Resolution taken: flagged, not code-fixed** — per explicit decision, this is a data-entry gap to surface and correct at the source rather than special-case in the chart math (which would only mask the next project that has the same gap). Added a 5th **Action Center** section, **"Missing Planned Award Date"** (`renderActionsTab` in `index.html`) — lists every not-resolved WP with no `awarding_date`, with a per-project breakdown line, so any project with this gap (not just NIA101) surfaces automatically. **The underlying chart logic in `charts.js` was deliberately left unchanged** — if a future request wants the charts themselves hardened against this (e.g. excluding no-date WPs from Forecast too, or folding them into Planned's own ceiling the same way Forecast already does), that's a separate follow-up; two options were scoped but not built: (a) exclude no-date WPs from Forecast so both ceilings converge on 1,653 (honest to what's actually planned, understates the true pipeline), or (b) also bucket no-date WPs into Planned's cumulative line at the same "next period" slot Forecast uses (both ceilings converge on 1,734, treats "no plan" as implicitly due now — same assumption Forecast already makes, but changes what the historical Planned bars represent).
    - **In-chart footnote (2026-08-12), per explicit request "instead of just in the action center":** the Action Center flag alone requires knowing to go look for it, so both period-chart panels now show the same warning right where the mismatch is visible. `window.wpMissingPlanCount(wps)` (`db.js`, shared) is the single source of truth for the count — both pages' render functions call it. `index.html`'s `_renderMissingPlanNote('idx-dash-period-note', wps)` (called at the top of `_idxDashRenderPeriod`) writes into a `<div id="idx-dash-period-note">` under the canvas; when the signed-in user is `super_admin` the note is clickable (`window.switchTab('actions')`, underlined, cursor pointer) since only that role can reach the Action Center (profile-menu gated) — every other role gets the same text with no click affordance, since clicking would silently do nothing for them. `project.html` has no Action Center to link to at all, so its own `_renderMissingPlanNote('dash-period-note', wps)` (called at the top of `renderDashPeriod`) is informational text only. Both hide the note entirely (`display:none`) when the count is 0. **Scoped to the two "Work Packages by Period" panels only** — not the Budget-mode view of the same chart (the note's wording is WP-count-specific: "excluded from Planned, but counted in Forecast" describes the WP-count Forecast/Planned pair, not the Budget-mode series) and not `sCurve` (confirmed during this pass that `project.html` no longer has an S-Curve panel at all — the `Charts.sCurve` call site referenced elsewhere in this file no longer exists there; if it's reintroduced, wire the same footnote pattern to it too, since `sCurve`'s WP-mode forecast has the identical no-date-WP gap in `charts.js`).

33. **Buyback recovery is no longer silently folded into the headline Savings/Loss figure (2026-08-12, explicit request).** Previously `effectiveAwardedCost()` (buyback-netted) fed straight into `helpingMetrics().awardedCost`, so a buyback WP's recovered value invisibly inflated Savings/Loss with no indication why. **`helpingMetrics()` now ALSO returns `buybackTotal`** (sum of `buybackValue(w)` over the same money-awarded set) **without changing `awardedCost` itself** — `awardedCost`/Actual Award/CTC/EAC still use the real, buyback-netted effective spend (correct for cash-flow figures, deliberately left alone). **New shared `window.fmtSavingsBuyback(hm)`** (`db.js`) computes the RAW savings (`budgetAwarded − rawAwardedCost`, i.e. `combinedSavings − buybackTotal`) as the primary number and returns `buybackTotal` as a separate annotation — `"+₱180.43M (+₱19.00M Buyback)"` / `"+0.9% (+0.1% Buyback)"`, HTML built with a smaller muted `<span>`. Wired into the **Cost Overview Savings/Loss + % Savings/Loss** cards on both `index.html` (Overview tab and Budget tab) and `project.html` (Overview tab — no separate Budget tab there), and into the **Top 5 cards'** Savings/Loss/%/%WT columns (`buildRankTable` in `db.js`, all three call sites on both pages pass a new `bb` field per row = `window.buybackValue(w)` when `w.buyback`, else 0; the annotation renders inline next to the Savings/Loss cell only when `bb > 0.5`). **The per-row `variance(w)` used by the Top 5 tables (High Value/Savings/Overbudget) was ALSO changed to use RAW `total_awarded` instead of `effectiveAwardedCost`** — this is a real ranking change, not just a display one: a WP that only looked like a saving because of its buyback recovery may now rank lower or even flip into the Overbudget table. **Deliberately NOT changed**: Actual Award, CTC, EAC, the Budget-tab per-trade table, the Action Center's "Awarded Over Budget" threshold, and vendor analytics — those all still use `effectiveAwardedCost`/buyback-netted figures unchanged, so Savings/Loss no longer exactly equals `totalBudget − ActualAward` when buyback WPs exist (an intentional, scoped inconsistency — the whole point was to make the buyback contribution VISIBLE rather than silently reconciled away). **Verified live (2026-08-12): 0 buyback WPs currently exist in production**, so this change is a no-op on today's displayed numbers (`buybackTotal=0` everywhere) — confirmed correct via the identity `rawSavings + buybackTotal == combinedSavings(today's live figure)` computed against the real dataset. It activates the moment a buyback WP is saved.

34. **Action Center tables reworked — collapsible sections, sortable columns, Data Gaps filter, sticky headers (2026-08-12).** `renderActionsTab()` (`index.html`) gained module-level UI state — `_acCollapsed`/`_acSortState`/`_acGapFilter`/`_acLastWPs` — with every toggle/sort/filter click simply re-invoking `renderActionsTab(_acLastWPs)` against the cached WP set rather than patching individual DOM nodes (simpler, and cheap enough at Action Center's row counts). **Collapse**: `panel(section, icon, title, sub, extra, body)` gained a `section` key and a clickable header (rotating chevron) — state in `_acCollapsed[section]`, `window._acToggle(section)`. **Sort**: every table header is now a clickable `acTh(section, key, label, align)` cell — click toggles asc/desc (`window._acSort(section, key)`), a ▲/▼ arrow + red text mark the active column; `_acApplySort(section, rows, keyFns)` is a generic sort applier taking a `{colKey: rowObj => sortableValue}` map per section (string values compared via `localeCompare`, numbers/dates via subtraction — dates are pre-converted to `.getTime()` in the keyFn) — falls through to each table's existing default sort (e.g. Overdue by days-desc) when no column has been clicked yet. **Data Gaps filter**: a new `<select>` (All / Cost / Vendor / Planned Date, each option showing its own count) above that table, state in `_acGapFilter`, `window._acSetGapFilter(v)` — filters `gapsAll` (the full set, still used for the summary tile so the KPI stays independent of the table's current filter) down to `gaps` (what the table renders). **Sticky headers**: each table's `<thead>` is `position:sticky;top:0;background:var(--surface,#fff)` and the table's own wrapper is `overflow:auto;max-height:420px` — this is a per-table scroll region (not page-level), so headers stay visible while scrolling a table with hundreds of rows (Overdue Awards alone had 312 rows in the live audit) without needing the whole page to scroll. `tbl()`'s signature changed from a `heads` array to a pre-built `theadHtml` string, since each section now builds its own header cells via `acTh()` rather than a generic array-map.
    - **Data Gaps and "Missing Planned Award Date" folded into ONE section (2026-08-12, same day, per explicit request "can't we just fold the ... table into the Data Gaps table?"):** `gapsAll` is now built by concatenating two filters — `award_status==='Awarded' && !not_to_be_awarded && (missing cost or vendor)` and `!isResolved(w) && !awarding_date` — each row tagged `{noCost, noVendor, noPlanDate}` (exactly one kind is ever true per WP, since the two source filters are mutually exclusive: one requires Awarded, the other requires NOT resolved). The filter dropdown gained a **Planned Date** option alongside Cost/Vendor (the old **Both** option — Cost AND Vendor together — was dropped since a 4th combinable dimension made "Both" ambiguous; filtering is single-dimension now). The summary tile row dropped 5→4 tiles. **`window.wpMissingPlanCount(wps)`, the SEPARATE helper the period-chart footnotes call (Known Issue #32), was intentionally left untouched** — it's a different consumer with its own independent counting logic, not wired to `gapsAll`/`renderActionsTab` in any way, so this merge has zero effect on the chart footnotes.

35. **Two readability bugs, one pass (2026-08-22).** (a) **The sidebar "Search projects…" input rendered its typed text in near-black on the dark sidebar** — `index.html`'s `#nav-proj-search` and `project.html`'s `#sidebar-proj-search` both carried `color:inherit`, but neither the input's wrapper nor `.sidebar` sets a `color`, so it inherited the BODY's dark `--text-primary` instead of the sidebar's light text. The placeholder still looked fine (the UA derives its own grey), which is why it read as "only what I type disappears". Fixed with an explicit `color:#fff;caret-color:#fff` on both inputs — `admin.html`'s copy of the same control already had `color:#fff` and was never affected. **Don't use `color:inherit` on a control inside `.sidebar`** — that class styles its children individually rather than setting an inherited colour on the container. (b) **The Cost-Overview Savings/Loss KPI clipped its buyback annotation mid-word** (`+₱56.92M (+₱14.23M Buybac`) — `.ov-cost-grid .metric-value` is `white-space:nowrap;overflow:hidden` (deliberately, so a billion-scale headline can't wrap), so the inline `(+₱X Buyback)` span appended by `fmtSavingsBuyback()` (`db.js`) simply ran off the card. The money annotation is now `display:block` at `0.5em` on its own line under the headline (parentheses dropped, since a separate line no longer needs them); the `%` sub-line's annotation is unchanged (that line isn't nowrap-clipped). Applies to both dashboards' Overview and `index.html`'s Budget tab automatically — they all render through the one shared helper.

36. **⚠️ AMENDED BY #43:** the sub-line count now equals the Backlog table's row count exactly (the two no longer diverge). The reconciliation rule itself stands. **The Cost Overview cards MUST reconcile: `Procurement BCB − Actual Award − Balance to Award = Savings / Loss` (2026-08-25, both dashboards).** This is the identity the PMO reads the four cards against, and it is the constraint that fixes `Balance to Award`'s definition. It holds only when **`balanceAmt = totalBudget − hm.budgetAwarded`** (i.e. `ctc`), because Savings is `budgetAwarded − awardedCost` and the `totalBudget` terms cancel:
    ```
    totalBudget − awardedCost − (totalBudget − budgetAwarded)  ==  budgetAwarded − awardedCost
    ```
    - **⚠️ A change on 2026-08-25 briefly summed the BACKLOG's BCB instead** (`Σ approved_budget_bcb` over `!isResolved` rows) so the amount would agree with the Backlog table's row count. **That BROKE the identity and was reverted the same day.** The two bases differ by the BCB of every WP that is **Awarded but carries no cost** — portfolio-wide ~**₱88M**, so the cards visibly stopped adding up (₱32.59B − ₱22.89B − ₱9.22B gave ₱480M against a displayed ₱391.76M). **Do not reintroduce a backlog-summed Balance to Award.**
    - **The original mismatch it was trying to fix is corrected on the QUANTITY side instead.** The "N WPs remaining" sub-line now counts **`!isMoneyAwarded(w)`** (WPs whose money is not yet committed) rather than `!isResolved(w)` (rows in the backlog), so the amount and the count describe the SAME set. **Change one of those two lines and you must change the other.**
    - **That count is deliberately ≥ the Backlog table's row count**, and the difference is exactly the awarded-but-unpriced WPs — a data gap worth seeing rather than hiding. Live at the time of writing: portfolio **518 uncommitted vs 453 backlog rows** (65 awarded with no cost); AVR101 **7 vs 1** (6 awarded with no cost — WP 8 ₱5.09M Lot Rental for Staging Area, WP 35 ₱1.52M, WP 105 ₱0.84M, WP 66 ₱0.63M, WP 45 ₱0.16M, WP 85 ₱0.14M, all six also missing a vendor). **Action Center → Data Gaps** lists them.
    - **Verified against production, not by inspection:** recomputing every term from the raw rows gives `BCB − Award − Balance` = **₱391.76M** portfolio (= Savings ₱391.76M) and **−₱39.32M** on AVR101 (= Savings −₱39.32M). Re-run that check after touching any of the four cards.
    - **With a buyback WP present the identity is off by `buybackTotal`** — `Actual Award` is buyback-netted while Savings displays the RAW figure with buyback annotated separately (Known Issue #33), so the closed form is `BCB − Award − Balance = Savings + Buyback`. The card's own "(+₱X Buyback)" annotation makes that visible. There are currently 0 buyback WPs in production.
    - **`ctc`/`eac` were never changed** — an unpriced WP still has to be budgeted for, so Estimate at Completion keeps counting it.

37. **Vendor Management is CONTRIBUTORS ONLY, and that rule lives in THREE places (2026-08-25).** While the vendor app is still being worked on, read-only roles (`viewer`, `viewer_budget`) are kept out of it entirely. Enforced at: (a) the **sidebar link** — `#nav-vendors` on `index.html`/`project.html`, hidden when `window.__isViewer` (`admin.html` needs no guard, it already redirects anyone who isn't admin/super_admin/manager); (b) **`vendors.html` itself**, which bounces those roles to `index.html` — hiding a link does NOT stop a direct URL, so this one is the actual enforcement; (c) **`login.html`'s `showDashboardChooser`**, which skips straight to `showProjectPicker` for those roles — with Vendor Management withheld there is only one dashboard left, so offering a one-card chooser would be a dead end. **If you widen (or narrow) the audience, change all three** — drift means a viewer either sees a card that bounces them or is offered a chooser with nothing to choose. Writes were already denied server-side by RLS; this is about not showing officers an unfinished app.
    - **Deliberately NOT announced in the patch notice** either — see the header comment in `assets/js/patch-notice.js`, which records Vendor Management and the Planners need-by dates as built-but-withheld so the next round's author doesn't think they were forgotten.

38. **The Portfolio never caches an EMPTY work-package fetch (2026-08-25, `index.html`).** `wpm_wps_idx_<uid>` holds the WP set for 5 minutes. A transient empty read (Supabase free-tier cold start, a dropped connection) used to be pinned for that whole TTL, and every project card and KPI then rendered `0` and `—` — indistinguishable from a portfolio that genuinely has no work packages. Reported 2026-08-25: the Portfolio showed 0 awarded and no budget across all 21 projects while each project's own page showed its full data (**`project.html` has no such cache, which is exactly why the two disagreed**).
